The 2026-10-26 CD-GDD-ALIGN approval predates these amendments and the 2026-10-27 re-review (delegated rulings, e.g. R1); CD-GDD-ALIGN is to be re-run on this revision *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*.

## Summary

Save/Load & Versioned Format defines how a campaign of The Synaptic Sea is persisted and restored: a versioned, self-verifying save container (a readable JSON header, binary block payloads and a SHA-256 trailer) written as a rolling ring of autosave generations, the autosave schedule and its fairness gate, Save & Quit, and the MVP death stub that restarts from the last verified autosave. It also owns the PROFILE store that survives a world restore, the load pre-check and its failure classes, and the block-authoring contract every state-owning system must meet. Read it if you design a system that owns persisted state, a death or restart flow, or anything that must survive a quit, a crash or a version bump.

> **Quick reference** — Layer: `Core` · Priority: `MVP` · Key deps: `Command & Simulation Layer (designed; hard upstream); Life-Support Graph (designed; block owner, carries S-1), Player Controller & Input (designed; block owner and the death trigger), Pixel-Perfect Render Pipeline & Camera (designed; consumer of LOADING); Ship Section & Layout Data Model (designed; `layout.state`, the restore_after root); Ship Body & Interior Physics (designed; `body.state`); Emitter State & Light-Exposure Query, Item & Module Database (designed; block owner / header-field provider); Suit & Equipment, Sanity & Hallucination Rules, Attention, Interaction, Modular Ship Assembly, Web Map & Transit, Horror Entities (designed; block owners); Chart Table, HUD & Suit Layer, Debug Overlay (designed; UI and diagnostic consumers)`


## Overview

Save/Load & Versioned Format is the persistence layer of The Synaptic Sea. In one sentence: it is the host-owned writer, verifier and migrator of the world's saved state, and the owner of what happens when the crew dies in the MVP. The Command & Simulation Layer (the kernel) captures the true simulation as an in-memory snapshot at a tick boundary and can restore one; everything that touches a disk is this system's: the container format and its header, the rolling ring of autosave **generations** (complete, self-verifying files), the rules for when an autosave is written and when it is deferred, the pre-check that judges a file before the kernel is ever touched, the orchestration of format and schema migrations, the per-campaign PROFILE store that a world restore never rolls back, and the **death stub** — on crew death, the game restarts from the last autosave.

**How the player meets it.** Almost never, by design. There is no save button, no slot list, no screenshot thumbnail, no "saving" ring, no sound and no death screen. The player meets four things: an autosave they do not perceive, written on a sim-time interval and at moments of consequence (and held back while a crewmate is down, for ten seconds after one gets up, while a suit is in a critical state or a warning the world owes is still open, so a save does not record a moment the game could have warned about) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*; **Save & Quit** on the pause panel; **Continue** on the main menu; and, at the worst moment of a run, the death stub — a breath of black and then control again at the weld, the dock or the door where the last autosave stood, in a world that has not forgiven or forgotten anything, with one plain ship-log line naming the record it came from (UI-11) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*. The cost of a death is the sim time since the last autosave and nothing else. Rare plain-spoken strings (a corrupt record, an incompatible build) are the only text this system ever sources; their final copy belongs to UX.

**What the game loses without it.** The index puts it plainly: a no-win-state game that breaks saves on every module change cannot be played long, and versioning is cheapest on day one. Concretely: the kernel's registry contract (every owner registers a versioned `persist` block) would have no host, so the kernel's save-equivalence guarantee (G4) could never be run against a real file; the Graph, the Controller and the Pipeline already point at a death stub that would otherwise be improvised separately by every MVP GDD; the PROFILE scope that carries Pillar 5's "never knowledge" across a world restore would have no store; and a single corrupt file would end a campaign in a game that has no win state to fall back on.

**What it deliberately is not.** It registers **zero state blocks and zero step hooks** (SL-17): the ring's bookkeeping must not roll back with the world it describes. No domain system calls it: the consumer rule stands — a system that wants state to persist registers a block with the kernel and never talks to Save/Load directly. It serialises **data models, never scene trees** (kernel H7, H10), and it never reads, derives or stores perception: a save is the true SIM state (Pillar 1), and it restores every persisted counter and seed exactly (kernel G4, D.10) while never varying by perception; whether perception draws replay identically after a restore is Sanity's decision, not Save/Load's (OQ-37, Pillar 4) *(Amended 2026-10-26 — CD-GDD-ALIGN)*. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* **Release verification is file-stage only** until the kernel answers K-4 (OQ-6): the reproduction compare (SL-12) runs in the test harness and in dev builds, and in a release build the only proof that a restore reproduced the snapshot is the file stage (trailer and block hashes) plus each owner's closed `<BLOCK>_RESTORE_*` reasons. In the MVP the "never knowledge" half of Pillar 5 is **mechanism only**: the PROFILE store is specified and tested with a synthetic block, but no PROFILE block ships; the failure contract proper (what is kept, what is lost, the cryo-bay re-wake) is Failure Contract & Re-wake's at Vertical Slice A and replaces the death stub through the same restore path.

**MVP slice vs later.** One place to confirm what ships at MVP and what waits.

| Area | MVP | Later |
|---|---|---|
| Autosave triggers *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* | Interval + `QUIT` + `NEW_GAME` initial generation + the `EVENT_TRIGGERS` set, stated once here and in Tuning Knobs: weld and cut completion (`ASSEMBLY_WELD`, `ASSEMBLY_CUT`) and module cut (`SALVAGE_CUT` (registered), from `last_salvage_T()`), whose owners' pure reads are designed and registered; shipping them is a user ruling (D17, OQ-44); a RAGGED cut's `ASSEMBLY_CUT` is withheld until the breach is capped (D54); pod arrival (`POD_ARRIVED`) and berth (`BERTHED`) join when the registry merge fixes their names. An event whose owner read is absent never fires | Further events as their owners land (SL-4, OQ-24); the choice to ship events at all is recorded in OQ-44 |
| Manual save | Save & Quit only | Pinned main-menu slot if playtest demands (OQ-18) |
| PROFILE store | Mechanism + synthetic block; zero shipped blocks | Knowledge Codex, scars (VS, Alpha) (SL-9) |
| Death *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* | Death stub: restart from last autosave, plus one plain ship-log line on the first `RUNNING` frame (UI-11, user ruling D15) | Failure Contract & Re-wake (VS-A) (SL-7) |
| Co-op | Payload separable; `saveload.request_save` reserved | VS-B (SL-17) |
| Compression | None, `codec` 0 | VS-A review (SL-19) |
| API | No `save_now`; no `load(generation)` outside dev builds | Revisit with the Chart Table UX spec (SL-21) |

**Vocabulary (fixed).** The index and the kernel's Interactions row call the stub "reload last save". This GDD fixes the terms **"restart from the last autosave"**, **"death stub"** and **"generation"**; the older wording is a stale token routed to the consistency check (OQ-14). Ownership boundaries: block shapes and intra-block migrations are the owners'; snapshot mechanics, `state_hash`, restore ordering and `FAULTED` are the kernel's; the re-wake, the lights-out beat and the cryo-bay fiction are Failure Contract & Re-wake's; world construction is Layout's, Web Map's and Authoring's; the menu's look is the Chart Table's.

**Design-level only.** Which thread writes the file, which call sequence makes a rename safe on Windows, which hash library computes the file digest and whether payloads are compressed are implementation choices (→ ADR candidates: container format and trailer; atomic write, backup and recovery; hash choice; death-stub handshake). The engine reference holds no file-I/O, serialization or hashing module (its `modules/` folder covers animation, audio, input, navigation, networking, physics, rendering and ui), so every engine behaviour assumed here is tagged **[UNV]** and assigned to the concept's "save round-trip with version" spike. *(Framing: technical infrastructure with a thin but real player surface, selected as the recommended option for a `Core`/`Persistence` system under `autonomous` automation; logged.)*

## Player Fantasy

*Indirect — the player never experiences a save system; they experience what it guarantees: **trust without a safety net**.*

Persistence has no fantasy of its own. The promise comes from Pillar 5 — "losing the home-ship or a crewmate costs ship and body, never knowledge" — and from the user's binding rule for death: "This is like project zomboid, death is a bitch. It happens." The emotion this system must produce is that the player believes, without ever checking, that (1) the ship they welded is still there tomorrow exactly as they left it, (2) a death will cost them the minutes since the last autosave and nothing else, and (3) the game never records a moment it could have warned them about: it will not save while a crewmate is down, for ten seconds after one gets up, while a suit is in a critical state, while a warning the world owes them is still open, while a breach they cut is still uncapped, or (if Attention publishes it) during a breach phase (SL-5); and if a death follows a restore within ten seconds, the restore steps back past that save (SL-8) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*. It does **not** promise to refuse every doomed moment: a death that arrives with no tell and gives the player more than ten seconds after the restore is the world's, not the save's (user ruling, OQ-40). They never feel *saved*; they feel *committed*. Death and quitting never buy back more than the minutes since the last autosave. *(Amended 2026-10-26 — CD-GDD-ALIGN)* *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*

> You never see the save. You see a ship you welded together last week, still breathing, still yours. You go one room deeper than is wise because if it kills you, it costs the minutes since the last autosave — not the hull, not the campaign. When you die, there is no screen: a breath of black, and you are standing where the world last stood still, in a world that has not forgiven or forgotten anything, including the room that killed you. The save never lies about the world and never rescues you from a mistake; it only refuses to record a moment it could have warned you about. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*

**The player never...** (each absence is a rule elsewhere in this document):

| The player never... | Because | What they feel instead |
|---|---|---|
| sees a save happen as an event | capture stays inside the kernel's snapshot budget (`SNAPSHOT_BUDGET_MS`, cited from the kernel, not restated) and the write is off the critical path (SL-10) | the world runs continuously; the ship "just exists" |
| chooses which save to load after a death | the death stub restores the newest verified generation (SL-7, SL-8) | death is consequence, not a menu |
| sees a death screen, a thumbnail or a sting | SL-7, SL-21; art bible 7.4 "No death screen" | the loss lands in the body of the game, not in an interface |
| buys back a mistake at will | no save-anywhere in the MVP (SL-4) | weight on every door and every weld |
| wakes into a moment the game had already warned about *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* | the autosave gate defers while any seat is `DOWNED` or `DEAD`, for `W_MIN_WARNING_S × TICK_RATE` ticks after a recovery, while a seat is `CRITICAL`, while a Life-Support Graph warning window or a self-inflicted breach is still open, and (if Attention publishes it) outside a calm breach phase (SL-5); a death that follows a restore within `W_MIN_WARNING_S × TICK_RATE` ticks steps back past it (SL-8); what the gate cannot see beyond that is not promised *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* | a death that was warned about was theirs to avoid; one that came with no tell is the world's, not the save's |
| loses the campaign to one bad file | a rolling ring of `AUTOSAVE_GENERATIONS` 3 and a refuse-don't-corrupt pre-check (SL-3, SL-11) | the long tail is safe to invest in |

**Honesty about the MVP (Pillar 5, second half).** "Never knowledge" is **mechanism-only** in the MVP: the PROFILE store exists and is tested with a synthetic block (SL-9), but no PROFILE block ships, and the kernel states that the MVP death stub "is a placeholder, not Pillar 5's failure contract". In the MVP the only knowledge that survives a death lives in the player's head — which, because the world is deterministic (SL-23), is genuinely reusable: the scrubber that failed at minute four fails at minute four again. That is a real, small, honest version of Pillar 5, and this section does not claim more. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* The same determinism would also replay a *threat* draw (the ambush at the same corner) after a restore, which works against Pillars 2 and 3; by user ruling (D16, OQ-47) threat draws (Attention, Horror) re-key per restore epoch while the Graph's physical failures and every persisted counter replay exactly *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*.

**The anchor moment (MVP, solo; numbers from SL-4, SL-5, SL-8 and the Controller's `DOWN_TO_DEATH_TICKS` 1800, at `TICK_RATE` 60).**

1. **T = 432 000** (two hours of sim time). An interval autosave is written (kind `AUTO`). The player sees and hears nothing: there is no per-write mark of any kind (UI-1 retired). The pause panel shows no save fact at all, so a player who opens it learns nothing about when the game last wrote or deferred; the newest record's playtime (2:00:00) appears only on the main-menu Continue screen (UI-2, UI-4). *(Amended 2026-10-26 — CD-GDD-ALIGN)* *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*
2. **T = 437 400.** The player completes their first weld; an event autosave is written (kind `EVENT`; 5 400 ticks since the last write, above `AUTOSAVE_MIN_GAP_TICKS` 1800). Again invisible.
3. The player pod-hops to a derelict and pushes one room too far. At **T = 446 400** their suit runs out of air mid-channel and `loco_state` becomes `DOWNED`. The next interval autosave falls due at T = 448 200 (437 400 + `AUTOSAVE_INTERVAL_TICKS` 10 800); the gate refuses it: the save system will not record the trap (and had the suit been `CRITICAL` before the fall, the gate would already have deferred it softly, SL-5) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*.
4. **T = 448 200** (`DOWN_TO_DEATH_TICKS` 1800 = 30 s after the fall; a player who chose the solo `give_up` action would reach `DEAD` sooner, `GIVE_UP_MIN_TICKS` after the fall at the earliest, user ruling D7) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*: `DEAD`. On that same tick's boundary the death stub requests a restore of the newest verified generation (the one from T = 437 400). No beat, no lights-out, no screen.
5. The kernel enters `LOADING`; the Pipeline shows layer-1 black (`TRANSIT_HOLD`). The black hold is bounded by `LOADING_TARGET_MS` (1000, provisional); under 120 ms reads as a flash, over about 2 s reads as a crash (Game Feel).
6. The first `RUNNING` frame is a hard camera cut to the avatar standing at the weld they just finished, at the **pre-load zoom** (the Pipeline keeps `k_z` across an in-session load), with one plain ship-log line from the HUD (placeholder: "Restored from the record at 2:01:30.", the restored generation's playtime, `437 400 div 60` = 7 290 s; UI-11) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*. The new section is welded on. The pod is docked. The derelict still holds the room that killed them, and the physical world's keyed draws (the Graph's, set by step and subject) play out the same way if they walk the same path.
7. **The cost, in one sentence:** 10 800 ticks — three minutes of play — and nothing else. No item loss, no stat penalty, no progression tax.

The anchor emotion is the half-second of black followed by recognition: *I am back at the weld. The ship is exactly as I made it. I know what is in that room now.* That is Zomboid's "death is a bitch" scaled to a three-minute window, with Pillar 5's kernel — the next attempt knows more — carried entirely by the player and the deterministic world. The typical loss is at most one `AUTOSAVE_INTERVAL_TICKS`; **each death discards at most** 20 400 ticks (5.7 minutes), and 24 000 ticks after one step-back (6.7 minutes), **on a healthy writer and when the `DOWNED` episode that ended in death is the first since the last capture** (a crew revived and downed again adds up to 1 799 ticks per recovery, F-7) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* (Formulas, `loss_ticks`, `discard_per_restore`; the figures include the post-`DOWNED` refractory, user rulings) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*. While writes keep failing the rewind grows with the failure (the backoff bounds the retry rate, not the loss), and the net progress lost across a step-back chain is larger than either number (`net_progress_lost`, F-7). Event saves lower the typical loss; the interval is the lever for the interval path. These numbers are the tuning surface of how much death hurts: playtest knobs, not designs of consequence. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*

**Design test (Pillar 5, applied).** *A save that lets the player buy back a mistake vs. a save that only refuses to record a moment the game could have warned the player about *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* → the refusal.* Concept test: "safe farming vs. scarcity that pushes inward → scarcity." Every dispute below resolves the same way:

| Debate | Resolves to | Rule |
|---|---|---|
| Save-anywhere vs. autosave + Save & Quit | autosave + Save & Quit | SL-4 |
| Unconditional step-back after a second quick death vs. the newest verified generation | the newest verified; step back only when it fails verification, or when it is `RISKY` and the death came within `DEATH_LOOP_WINDOW_TICKS` of restoring it, or when the fall came within `W_MIN_WARNING_S × TICK_RATE` of restoring it, `RISKY` or not (the fairness step-back, user ruling X7) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* | SL-8 |
| Autosave while `DOWNED` vs. defer | defer | SL-5 |
| Let the player pick a generation after a death vs. the stub picks | the stub picks | SL-7, SL-8 |

Secondary test (Pillar 1 applied to persistence): *a restore that re-presents the world as it was vs. one that re-presents what the player saw → as it was.*

**Under hallucination (Pillar 4).** The save is the one artefact in the game that is pure ground truth. That deepens the horror if, and only if, the player can never use it as a free window onto truth. Pillar 4: "No lie is unfalsifiable: the truth is always available … and checking costs time, risk or trust." The rules (all enforced in SL-24 and its criteria):

| # | Rule | Why it deepens rather than breaks the fantasy |
|---|---|---|
| H1 | A save stores SIM truth only: never a `PERCEPTION_OUT` block, never a "what the player saw" field, no thumbnail. | The record of the ship is incorruptible — the dead derelict's flight recorder, not a photograph of your madness. |
| H2 *(Amended 2026-10-26 — CD-GDD-ALIGN)* | Every persisted counter and seed (the owners' `rng_derive` counters, kernel G4 and D.10) is restored exactly, and Save/Load never varies by perception. Whether a **perception** draw (sanity drift, the onset of a false system state) replays identically after a restore or is re-keyed is **Sanity & Hallucination Rules' decision**, not this GDD's (OQ-37); any re-keying must not be steerable by the player, so quit and death offer no reroll oracle. Physical-world determinism (the scrubber that fails at minute four) is unchanged: that is Pillar 1. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings: threat draws (Attention, Horror) are re-keyed per restore epoch by user ruling D16, OQ-47; they are not perception draws, and the Graph's physical failures and every persisted counter still replay exactly. The re-key keys off Save/Load's persisted `restore_epoch`, SL-23.)* *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review, delegated ruling R1: the epoch advances on every restore, death stub or load, and is durable before the first `RUNNING` tick; whether a Save & Quit followed by Continue should instead be exempt is a USER DECISION, OQ-50.)* | Binding perception replay into a Save/Load rule would turn hallucinations into fixed scripts the player can memorise across deaths, flattening dread (P2) and Discovery, which P4's "checking costs time, risk or trust" depends on. Save/Load states only what it owns: exact counters, no perception input, and no restore path that hands out a free reroll. |
| H3 | The sanity level is WORLD-scoped in the MVP and rolls back with the world. | Death is not a sanity cure: a player already low wakes still low; no "die to clear your head" exploit. |
| H4 | No clean frame: the first `RUNNING` frame after `LOADING` must already be presented through the Perception Filter for the restored sanity level; the black hold covers any rebuild. | One unfiltered frame would be a free oracle. This is a **routed request** to Perception Filter, Sanity and Pipeline (all three now designed; the ordering request stands, OQ-11), not Save/Load's to implement. |
| H5 *(Amended 2026-10-26 — CD-GDD-ALIGN)* | Every mark this system sources (the failed-write notice, the main-menu newest-record line, its strings) is an inviolable channel: it never carries flesh tones or violet, never flickers with sanity and is never faked by a hallucination class. There is **no per-write mark** in the MVP, so nothing on screen tells the player when the game saves or defers a save. | One more thing the player can trust absolutely. A per-write glyph was retired (UI-1): held by the gate, its absence would have signalled that danger was building, a hidden Attention meter against P3. |
| H6 | Save/Load never reads, derives or varies by perception: no sanity-dependent autosave timing, no hallucinated save list. | A lie that reached the save menu would be unfalsifiable inside the session; Pillar 4 requires at least one channel of truth to remain. |

**Reference techniques — adopt / reject.**

| Reference | Technique | Decision | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| Project Zomboid | Continuous autosave; Save & Quit only; no manual slots | **Adopt** | The user's stated touchstone (SL-4) |
| Project Zomboid | Permadeath of the character while the world persists; a new character | **Reject for the MVP** | The user rule is restart from the last autosave; the new-character form is Failure Contract & Re-wake's (VS-A) |
| Darkest Dungeon and roguelikes | No manual save; autosave on meaningful events | **Adopt** (event triggers, names provisional) | Weight on every decision |
| Roguelike "suspend save" (consumed on resume) | A quit-save deleted when resumed | **Reject** | A death after a Continue would lose more than the last autosave; contradicts SL-8 |
| Souls-likes | Authored bonfire-style rest points; a "YOU DIED" screen; a corpse run | **Reject** | Authored spatial rest points violate "no authored story"; a death screen is forbidden (art bible 7.4); a corpse run is a progression penalty |
| Most RPGs and immersive sims | Named manual save-anywhere; quicksave | **Reject** (MVP) | A reload becomes a free hallucination oracle and the cost of death vanishes; the one fallback is a pinned main-menu-only slot (OQ-18) |
| Rogue-lite meta progression | Unlocks earned by dying | **Reject** | Anti-pillar "nothing levels up"; would invert Pillar 5 (death becomes profitable) |
| Pandorum, *Beyond the Aquila Rift* | Waking into wrongness | **Route to Failure Contract & Re-wake** | The re-wake fiction is VS-A; using it in the stub would break SL-7 |
| Satisfactory and factory games | Saves as long-lived investment; patch-safe migrations | **Adopt the principle** | "Versioning is cheapest on day one"; golden saves (SL-14) |
| The save-thumbnail convention | A screenshot in the load list | **Reject** | It would capture the Perception Filter's lie (Pillar 1; SL-21) |

**Anti-pillar guard rails.**

| Anti-pillar / pillar | Guard rail for Save/Load |
|---|---|
| Not a game you win | No "final save", no completion flag, no ending state in the header; saves never close a campaign; `SaveInfo` carries no percentage and no progress meter |
| Not a freeform builder | No ship-blueprint export or import, no "save ship design"; the composite ship is Layout + Graph + Body data only |
| Not intentional PvP | Host-owned saves only; no guest-authoritative block; a friendly-fire death (VS-B) is Failure Contract's, never a save that records blame |
| Not an authored story | No named chapters, acts or authored save locations; generation labels come from SIM facts (the depth label is provisional from Web Map) |
| Not a combat game | The death stub attaches no penalty, no visible deaths counter and no difficulty tier; the cost is the autosave interval, nothing else |
| P2 Dread Before Chaos | The autosave gate is a Pillar 2 obligation, not a convenience: a save inside a `DOWNED` state, inside an open Graph warning window, or (if Attention publishes it) in a non-calm breach phase is deferred |
| P3 The Web Watches | No audio cue for saving (any sound is attributable by the web) unless Audio justifies one; Attention restores exactly; no meter is stored for display |

**Tone and fixed terms.** The few strings this system sources are plain, factual and low-affect, in a ship-log register: never apologetic, never cute, no exclamation marks, no emoji, no colour from the sane/insane palette. Placeholder examples (final copy is UX's and the narrative director's): "Newest record unreadable. Continuing from the one before it." / "This save was made by an incompatible build." / "Restored from the record at 2:01:30." (the UI-11 line, user ruling D15) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*. Fixed terms: "restart from the last autosave", "death stub", "generation"; player-facing labels "Continue", "Save & Quit", "New campaign". Not used affirmatively anywhere in this document or in a string it sources: "reload last save", "respawn", "save point", "game over", "you died", "progress saved", "quicksave", and "slot" for the (invisible) ring.

*(Framing: indirect — "what it lets you stop worrying about"; selected over the direct "the ship remembers you" framing, which would put infrastructure in the player's face, and over the diegetic cryo-bay framing, which belongs to Failure Contract & Re-wake; logged.)*

## Detailed Design

### Core Rules

**Conventions.** Rules are numbered `SL-n` with names; decisions and routed amendments keep their brief ids (D1–D18, S-1, K-1, K-2, K-3, K-4, K-5). Kernel rules are cited by id (CR-8, CR-15, CR-18, H1–H10, D.11, D.12), as are the Graph's (LS-21), the Controller's (PC-17) and the Pipeline's (C5, `TRANSIT_HOLD`). **(provisional — X undesigned/in flight)** marks a name or contract owned by a system whose GDD does not exist or is still being written; it is repeated at every mention, and none of those names is a contract until its owner confirms it. **[UNV]** marks an engine behaviour not verified against Godot 4.7.2 (the engine reference has no file-I/O, serialization or hashing module); every [UNV] is assigned to the concept's "save round-trip with version" spike. "→ ADR candidate" marks an implementation choice deliberately left open. Registry names are used verbatim and never restated differently: `TICK_RATE` 60, `DOWN_TO_DEATH_TICKS` 1800 (Controller), `HOST_PEER_ID` 1, `W_MIN_WARNING_S` 10 (Graph), and the formulas `state_hash` (D.12) and `rng_derive` (D.10). **Kernel GDD values (Tuning Knobs / D.11), not registry entries — kernel registry candidates:** `SNAPSHOT_BUDGET_MS` 16.7 (K:515), `MAX_BLOCKS` 128 (K:722) and the `snapshot_bytes` formula (K:501); they are cited by name and never restated with another value. All durations are integer ticks read from `kernel.clock.T`; wall clock is metadata only (SL-23).

**SL-1 — Container and header.** One file holds one **generation** (or, for the PROFILE store, one profile image — SL-9). Layout is the contract:

| Offset | Size | Field | Rule |
|---|---|---|---|
| 0 | 8 | magic | A fixed 7-byte ASCII prefix followed by one byte, the container-layout id (1 for `FORMAT_VERSION` 1). The layout-id byte names the header/payload encoding only; it never locates the trailer (format law below) |
| 8 | 4 | `header_len` | Unsigned, little-endian; `0 < header_len ≤ MAX_HEADER_BYTES` and `12 + header_len + 32 ≤ file size`, checked **before any allocation** (a corrupt 4 GiB length is never allocated) |
| 12 | `header_len` | JSON header | UTF-8 object (fields below) |
| 12 + `header_len` | rest − 32 | payload region | The block payloads, concatenated in block-table order (SL-2) |
| end − 32 | 32 | trailer | SHA-256 of every preceding byte (magic through the last payload). It lives outside the header because a header cannot contain a hash of itself; a missing or short trailer is truncation, detected for free |

**Format law (every layout, forever).** For every file bearing the 7-byte magic prefix, in every container layout, the last 32 bytes are SHA-256 over all preceding bytes, `[0, size − 32)`. The layout-id byte names the header/payload encoding only. A reader can therefore verify integrity before it judges the layout id, so one flipped bit in the layout-id byte is reported as corruption (and falls back, SL-11) rather than as "made by a newer build" (which must not).

*(Orchestrator ruling: the trailer is additive to the layout in R1 — the file must be self-verifying (R3) and a header cannot hash itself. Payload region and file wrapper stay separable so the same payload can be sent to a guest at VS-B session start (CR-18) without the wrapper.)*

Header fields (closed list for format 1; unknown extra keys are ignored when `min_reader_version` is satisfied, missing required keys refuse, except `state_hash`, whose presence rules are in its row) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*:

| Field | Type | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| `format_version` | int ≥ 1 | Container version; `FORMAT_VERSION` 1 today |
| `min_reader_version` | int | Oldest reader that may open the file. In the MVP it equals `format_version` on every write (an older build can never read a newer file's blocks); the field exists so a later additive header change can lower it deliberately |
| `file_kind` | enum | `WORLD` (a generation) or `PROFILE` (SL-9) |
| `game_version`, `build_id` | string | File metadata written by the service; never read by the sim (H9) |
| `catalogue_version` | int | Item/module content epoch — the Item & Module Database's name (designed; IDB-26 `meta.json` key, IDB-27, its Save/Load row; history OQ-13). `catalogue_hash` (hex) is diagnostic only |
| `registry_epoch` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* | hex u64 | A digest of the writing build's sorted (`block_id`, `schema_version`, `added_in_format`) triples over its `persist` blocks. It lets the reader tell "the registry moved" from corruption: a mandatory-set or schema failure on a file whose `registry_epoch` differs from ours is INCOMPATIBLE-dev, never CORRUPT and never quarantined (SL-11, SL-14, T-5) |
| `hash_algo` | int | Names the 64-bit hash the kernel used for `state_hash` and per-block hashes at capture (2 = H64, the ratified default: first 8 bytes of SHA-256 via `HashingContext`, read little-endian; the header hex form is little-endian too, F-10, R-17 / user ruling X13; 0 xxHash64 and 1 FNV-1a 64 are reserved ids, not written), so a later hash change needs no `format_version` bump (SL-16) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings, consistency follow-up)* |
| `codec` | int | 0 = none; 1 reserved (zstd). No compression in the MVP (SL-19) |
| `tick_rate` | int | Must equal the kernel's `TICK_RATE`; a save is bound to the rate that wrote it |
| `T`, `world_seed` | hex u64 | Copied from `kernel.clock`; pre-check asserts they equal the block's values |
| `state_hash` | hex u64 | Kernel `state_hash(PERSIST, T)` of the WORLD blocks at capture (D.12, cited); absent in `PROFILE` files, and *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* absent from WORLD files written by a **release** build until K-4 lands (OQ-6): a release build has no consumer for it (the ratified H64 is native, so cost is no longer the reason; SL-16) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings, consistency follow-up)*. Absence means the reproduction compare records no verdict (`NONE`); a dev or test build always writes it |
| `campaign_id` | hex, 32 chars | 128-bit random; must equal the directory name |
| `generation` | int, < 2^53 | Strictly increasing per campaign; **the ordering truth** (SL-3) |
| `restore_epoch` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review, delegated ruling R1)* | int, 0 ≤ v < 2^32 | The campaign's restore counter (SL-23): the value live when this file was last written, raised by 1 on every restore by an atomic header-only rewrite of the restored generation. A header int (never hex), `WORLD` files only (absent in `PROFILE` files). Missing, non-integral or ≥ 2^32 refuses (`SL_PRECHECK_HEADER_FIELD_MISSING` / `_TYPE` / `_NOT_INT`). It is sim-visible through `kernel.clock`, so it is a behaviour-changing field and AC-SL-13 tags it MUST_REFUSE when damaged |
| `save_kind`, `trigger` | enum, string | `AUTO` / `EVENT` / `QUIT`; `trigger` names the event (names provisional until the registry merge: `POD_ARRIVED`; `ASSEMBLY_WELD` / `ASSEMBLY_CUT` are registered, SL-4); the initial generation is `EVENT` with trigger `NEW_GAME` |
| `risky` | bool | Written while a soft gate condition was closed, including a Save & Quit that bypassed one (SL-5, SL-6) |
| `risky_cause` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* | string[] | The closed set of soft inputs that were true at the write: `GRAPH_TELL`, `ATTENTION_TELL`, `BREACH_PHASE`, `SEAT_STATE`, `VITALS`, `ASSEMBLY_BREACH`; empty exactly when `risky` is false. A member outside the set, or a non-empty list with `risky` false, is refused (`SL_PRECHECK_HEADER_FIELD_TYPE`). SL-8 reads it: a `QUIT` generation keeps the RISKY-window step-back only when `VITALS` is in it |
| `created_unix` | int | Wall clock, metadata only (SL-23) |
| `seats` | int[] | PlayerIds present, for validation only (SL-20) |
| `crew_count`, `depth_label` | int, string | `SaveInfo` inputs computed from SIM blocks at capture; `depth_label` (provisional — derived from the `ring` of the pod's node in `web.view`; Web Map & Transit publishes a ring, not a depth-label field) |
| `blocks[]` | records | `{block_id, schema_version, persist_scope, offset, length, payload_sha256 (64 hex), block_state_hash (hex, optional)}` per block; entries are contiguous, ascending, non-overlapping and sum exactly to the payload region |

**64-bit rule (S-1).** Every header value that can exceed 2^53 (`T`, `world_seed`, `state_hash`, per-block hashes, `campaign_id`) is an exact-width lowercase hex string (16 characters for 64-bit, 32 for 128-bit), never a JSON number; hex decoding is exact-width — a wrong length, uppercase or non-hex character is a refusal — and the contract is "8 bytes in, 8 bytes out" with no general string-to-int parse. Header numbers that stay numbers (`format_version`, `schema_version`, `generation`, lengths, offsets, counts, `hash_algo`, `codec`, `tick_rate`) arrive as floats from the parser (the Graph GDD's technical-director note, LS-21) and must be integral and below 2^53 before conversion. A `world_seed` ≥ 2^63 would read as a negative signed integer in GDScript [UNV]; it is stored as the hex of its 8 bytes. **File bytes are not canonical:** dictionary iteration is insertion-ordered (kernel D.12), so two saves of identical state can differ byte for byte. State equality is `state_hash` (or the owners' canonical bytes), never file bytes; the file SHA-256 guards *these bytes* against accidental damage; golden saves are immutable artefacts regenerated only deliberately.

**SL-2 — Interchange form and trust model (S-1, K OQ 7, D1).** Each block payload is the owner's `snapshot()` dictionary encoded with `var_to_bytes(dict)` (the form that does not encode objects) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* (the earlier `allow_objects = false` argument is not a Godot 4 call shape; the object-allowing forms are the separate `var_to_bytes_with_objects` and `bytes_to_var_with_objects`, which are forbidden) [UNV], or the owner's own canonical byte form where it publishes one (the Graph's `canonical_bytes()`, LS-21), as raw bytes inside the container. JSON appears **only** in the header. Decoding uses `bytes_to_var(bytes)` only, never `bytes_to_var_with_objects`; the object-allowing decode, `Resource` and `.tres` paths are forbidden outright (a script path can ride inside a resource; the Graph states "never a `Resource` for saves"). This is the answer to the kernel's Open Question 7. S-1 is carried verbatim: **64-bit integers cannot round-trip JSON text** (Godot's JSON parses numbers as float — the technical director's sourced note in LS-21), so no hashed, 64-bit or packed data ever travels as a JSON number; a payload that stored a packed array as a JSON number list is refused by the Graph's decoder (`GRAPH_RESTORE_BAD_TAG`). A dev-only `dump-save` command renders any block as JSON with 64-bit values as strings; it is diagnostics, never a load path. After decoding, a bounded walk (`MAX_DECODE_DEPTH`, `MAX_DECODE_NODES`; a packed array counts as one node) checks types before any `request_restore`, because `bytes_to_var` (the form without objects) stops object instantiation but does not by itself bound allocation or nesting [UNV]: allowed are null, bool, int, float, String, Array, Dictionary and the packed arrays (the H7 set, kernel CR-14); `Vector2i`, float vectors, `Color`, `Rect2`, `Object`, `Callable`, `Signal`, `RID`, `NodePath` and `StringName` are forbidden (kernel H7, K:125, enumerates no types: the allowed set is this GDD's reading of it, and K:839's JSON round trip cannot carry a `Vector2i`, which stringifies, so allowing it would make format 1 depend on K-3 landing); integer vectors travel as two ints or a packed int array (a shape note for the Body author — Ship Body & Interior Physics, designed, whose tables are integer-only (BDY-5); revisited only if K-3 changes K:839, OQ-9); dictionary keys are `String` or `int`; `snapshot()` uses untyped containers until the spike resolves typed-container encoding [UNV]. The kernel text that calls the interchange form "JSON-safe primitives" (CR-8) and its acceptance criterion that round-trips through JSON conflict with S-1 and with 64-bit fields; this GDD does not claim they are already amended — routed amendment **K-3** (OQ-5): the interchange test becomes a `var_to_bytes` round-trip plus a separate JSON-dump assertion that 64-bit values arrive as strings.

**SL-3 — Generation ring.** `AUTOSAVE_GENERATIONS` 3 files (safe 2–5), each complete and self-verifying, at ring positions `gen_0.sav … gen_{N−1}.sav`. **Order is the header `generation` number — never the file name, the modification time or `T`** (after a step-back `T` rolls back while generation numbers keep rising; wall clock is untrusted). A new generation's number is `1 + max(generation seen in the ring, in `quarantine/` (read from the file name), among `.demoted` files and in the manifest)`. It is written into the first ring position that is free by F-9 (no `.sav` at all, or only a `.demoted`; an unreadable `.sav` occupies its slot) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* and is not claimed by an abandoned write job (SL-10) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*; otherwise into the position of the **oldest** generation; the newest generation's file is never renamed or touched, so a window with no newest file cannot exist. *(Orchestrator ruling: R11 states the old newest is renamed to `.bak` during a swap; the three specialists converged on writing into the oldest position, where `.bak` holds the replaced oldest generation. R11's guarantee — never delete a generation before its successor verified — is kept and strengthened; the sequence is R11's "ADR candidate" and is stated in SL-10.)* To the player the ring is one logical autosave ("Continue"); it exists for corruption fallback and the RISKY rule (SL-8), not for choice, so the MVP API offers **no `load(generation)`** (a dev-build-only call may exist). `campaign.json` is a **hint, never truth**: a rebuildable cache of the ring (order, last write status, `suspect` counters); if missing or corrupt it is rebuilt by scanning generation headers and causes no refusal; the ring's truth is the set of generation headers and file names.

**SL-4 — Autosave triggers and manual save (D3).** Triggers: **interval** (`AUTOSAVE_INTERVAL_TICKS` 10 800 = 3 min of sim time, safe 5 400–18 000 *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*, measured from `kernel.clock.T`), **events** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — consistency pass)* (pod-hop arrival = Web Map & Transit's `POD_ARRIVED{tick, leg_epoch, at}` (`web_events`, provisional names until merge); weld or cut completion = Assembly's shape change, polled through the pure read `last_shape_change_T()` (MSA-33; event names `ASSEMBLY_WELD` / `ASSEMBLY_CUT` registered 2026-10-27, OQ-24); section extraction is Assembly's detach, served by the same `last_shape_change_T()`; a module cut is served by Interaction's pure read `last_salvage_T()` (registry `last_salvage_T`; IA-25; int64 tick or NONE; Boarding & Salvage BS-21), polled under SL-5's gate and `AUTOSAVE_MIN_GAP_TICKS`; dock completion = Web Map & Transit's `BERTHED{tick, derelict_id}` (`web_events`, provisional until merge; `UNBERTHED` is not a trigger); an event trigger activates only when its owner publishes a notification or pure read Save/Load may consume), **Save & Quit** (SL-6) and the **initial generation** after `new_game` (SL-18). Events coalesce (kind only, never queued). **A RAGGED cut's `ASSEMBLY_CUT` event is withheld while Assembly's pure read `ragged_breach_open()` is true** (user ruling D54; the read is registered, source Modular Ship Assembly) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings, consistency follow-up)*: the event stays latched, is not due, and is taken once the breach is capped; the `ASSEMBLY_WELD` event and a SEALED cut's `ASSEMBLY_CUT` are never withheld by it, and the open window also defers the interval save as a soft input (SL-5 row (d)) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* A minimum gap `AUTOSAVE_MIN_GAP_TICKS` 1 800 separates any two interval/event generations; QUIT ignores it. An interval save is never due while the kernel is `PAUSED` (`T` cannot advance). The MVP set is interval + QUIT + initial + the `EVENT_TRIGGERS` set stated once in the MVP table and in Tuning Knobs (`ASSEMBLY_WELD`, `ASSEMBLY_CUT`, `SALVAGE_CUT` (registered) — the label for `last_salvage_T()` — and `POD_ARRIVED` / `BERTHED` once their names merge); an event whose owner read is absent never fires, and further events join as their owners land. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* Shipping events at all trades a shorter typical loss against a learnable checkpoint grammar (the bonfire pattern this document rejects): the user confirmed that trade (Resolved 2026-10-27 — user ruling (design-review decisions): ship the weld, cut and module-cut event autosaves, D17, OQ-44) and metric M5 (Edge Cases) measures it *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* **Manual save is none in the MVP except Save & Quit** from the pause panel: save-anywhere is rejected because it makes every reload a free hallucination oracle (Pillar 4) and erases the cost of death (the user rule). A pinned main-menu-only manual slot is the single playtest fallback and is recorded in OQ-18, never an in-session save. The Chart Table's API (provisional) therefore has no `save_now()` in the MVP; if one ever appears it is marked "(provisional, reserved)" at every mention. The due-computation is Formula `autosave_due` (Formulas).

**SL-5 — Autosave gate.** A due autosave waits for the gate, re-evaluated every `AUTOSAVE_RETRY_TICKS` 300 from the tick it first became due.

| Class | Condition | Source | Overridden by the cap? |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Hard** | Kernel state is `RUNNING` or `PAUSED` | kernel | Never |
| **Hard** | No restore pending; no write in flight (an abandoned job, SL-10, does not count) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* | Save/Load | Never |
| **Hard** | No bound seat has `controller.state.loco_state` of `DOWNED` or `DEAD` | Controller (`DOWN_TO_DEATH_TICKS` 1800, cited) | Never |
| **Hard, expiring** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* | **Post-`DOWNED` refractory.** No bound seat has made a `DOWNED` → non-`DOWNED` transition within the last `W_MIN_WARNING_S × TICK_RATE` ticks (600 at the defaults) | Controller `loco_state` transitions (SL-13) | Never by the cap; it expires on its own after 600 ticks. A `QUIT` is not bound by it (SL-6) |
| **Soft** *(Amended 2026-10-26 — CD-GDD-ALIGN)* | **(a) Graph tell — required at MVP.** Any loaded descriptor of subject kind NODE, EDGE or SEAM (the kinds that carry `warn_since_step` and that the Graph's fairness gate governs; COMPARTMENT and OPENING descriptors such as ATMO_COLD or SEAL_STRAIN open no window, report `warn_elapsed` 0 and never count) with `cause` ENDOGENOUS, `sev` ≥ 1 and `warn_elapsed` < `W_MIN_STEPS` (the Graph's fairness gate is still closed, D.11); the whole open window defers. A WORN descriptor (mode 10) counts: it is the Graph's first warning before an endogenous FAILED, and it is read from the Graph's SIM descriptor, never from the Perception Filter's corruptible module-body channel | Life-Support Graph `read_view.failures()` (LS-19; descriptor fields of LS-14) — **designed and locked**; not provisional, no `GATE_INPUT_ABSENT` | After `AUTOSAVE_MAX_DEFER_TICKS` 7 200 |
| **Soft** *(Amended 2026-10-26 — CD-GDD-ALIGN)* | **(b) Attention tell and breach phase — `attention.view` (designed); optional (absent = open).** An active Attention tell with fewer than `W_MIN_WARNING_S × TICK_RATE` ticks remaining (`gate_tell_mode` FAIR; STRICT defers for the whole tell), or a breach phase that is not calm | Attention SIM read view `attention.view` (designed — Attention System; `tell_active`, `tell_remaining` 0 … `TELL_MAX_TICKS` 1 800, `breach_phase` {CALM, BREACH, SUBSIDING}; `breach_phase` is Save/Load-only (a TELL reads CALM); `tell_remaining` is read by Save/Load, SIM readers and the DIAGNOSTIC Debug Overlay only); an absent view still counts as open, logged once per process as `GATE_INPUT_ABSENT` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — consistency pass)* | After the cap |
| **Soft** | A bound seat is in a state listed in `GATE_SOFT_STATES` (default `CHANNELLING`, `STAGGERED`, `IN_POD`) | Controller | After the cap |
| **Soft** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* | **(c) Critical suit.** Any bound seat's `vitals_state` is `CRITICAL` or worse (the worst of Suit's three rungs) | Suit & Equipment `view(player_id).vitals_state` (SE-33, designed; the `vitals_state` field of the registered `suit.state` summary; one more SL-13 read, required at MVP like the Graph's; *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* an absent `vitals_state` counts as OK (not blocking) and logs `GATE_INPUT_ABSENT{VITALS}` once per process) | After `AUTOSAVE_MAX_DEFER_TICKS`; then `RISKY`, no glyph |
| **Soft** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* | **(d) Open self-inflicted breach.** Assembly's pure read `ragged_breach_open()` is true (a RAGGED cut still has an uncapped stub; its descriptors are exogenous, so row (a) never sees them) | Modular Ship Assembly `ragged_breach_open()` (MSA-33; registered, source Modular Ship Assembly); an absent read counts as no breach (not blocking), logged once per process as `GATE_INPUT_ABSENT` | After the cap; then `RISKY` |

When a due save has waited `AUTOSAVE_MAX_DEFER_TICKS` and only **soft** conditions are closed, it is written anyway and its header is tagged `RISKY`, with `risky_cause` listing every soft input that was true *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*. A hard condition is never overridden: a `DOWNED` or `DEAD` world is never written by an autosave or by a Save & Quit (SL-6), and the refractory is never shortened by the cap. *(Orchestrator ruling: R4 says the cap "writes anyway"; read literally that would record the very trap the gate exists to avoid, and would write a state the death stub is about to discard. Three specialists proposed three splits; the ruling is that DOWNED and DEAD are hard, the cap overrides soft conditions only, and the derived loss ceiling in Formulas holds. `GATE_SOFT_STATES` is a systems-designer refinement: a restore drops channel progress and turns `STAGGERED` into `IDLE` (PC-17), and a mid-transit state is Web Map's to restore.)* **Consequence stated plainly:** in the solo MVP a downed seat dies after `DOWN_TO_DEATH_TICKS` 1 800 (or sooner on a `give_up`), well before the cap, so a `RISKY` generation is reachable through chained Graph warning windows held past the cap, a `CRITICAL` suit or an uncapped self-inflicted breach held past the cap, the Attention view, or a transit/channel state held past the cap or at a Save & Quit (SL-6); none of them involves a downed seat. The step-back of SL-8 is no longer near-dead code *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*: its fairness route fires on a non-`RISKY` generation, so it runs on every death that follows a restore within `W_MIN_WARNING_S × TICK_RATE` ticks. The gate also exists to avoid a fairness regression the save would otherwise cause: a generation captured mid-tell restores with only the remainder of the warning (the Graph persists `warn_since_step`, so the elapsed part is kept and the player wakes into a fault with less than `W_MIN_WARNING_S` of it left to perceive), so the Graph's guarantee (`W_MIN_WARNING_S`, endogenous transitions only) would be silently shortened by the save system and a death-stub restore would put the player into a fault with a shortened warning. *(Amended 2026-10-26 — CD-GDD-ALIGN)* **The Graph half (a) prevents this at the MVP.** It is built from the designed Graph read view, not from the undesigned Attention system: `failures()` publishes `cause`, `sev` and `warn_elapsed` per descriptor, and `W_MIN_STEPS` (= `W_MIN_WARNING_S × TICK_RATE / GRAPH_STEP_TICKS`, the Graph's, cited and never restated) says when its gate opens, so a due save waits at most one window (at most `W_MIN_WARNING_S × TICK_RATE` ticks, 600 at the defaults) per descriptor. The Attention half (b) reads the designed `attention.view` but stays optional: without the Attention view the gate is Graph + controller + kernel conditions and logs `GATE_INPUT_ABSENT` once per process. `gate_tell_mode` governs half (b) only; for half (a) FAIR and STRICT collapse into one behaviour (defer for the whole open window), because the window *is* the guarantee. Windows may chain (one closes as another opens); the cap bounds the chain, so `AUTOSAVE_MAX_DEFER_TICKS` and `loss_ticks` are unchanged. **Dormant sections:** a dormant section freezes its `warn_since_step` values in place (Graph dormant-section rule), so a frozen mid-window descriptor never closes; LS-19 names no loaded-section predicate, so the filter "loaded descriptors only" is a routed one-line request to the Graph owner (OQ-38). Until it is answered, a frozen dormant window can defer a save to the cap and tag it `RISKY`, an accepted rare exposure (it needs a derelict unloaded within one window of a new tell). The gate is Formula `autosave_gate` (Formulas); its inputs are injectable for tests. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* **What the gate sees, and what it still does not (Resolved 2026-10-27 — user ruling (design-review decisions): OQ-40 and OQ-41 (b), the recommended package adopted).** The gate defers what the world has *already warned about*, and since the ruling it also covers the three cases the review found it blind to. (1) **A suit already critical before any `DOWNED`** is a third soft input, `vitals_block` (row (c); capped, then `RISKY`, no glyph; it also enters `quit_risky`). (2) **A recovery onto a still-critical seat:** after any `DOWNED` → non-`DOWNED` transition no autosave fires for `W_MIN_WARNING_S × TICK_RATE` ticks (the refractory, a hard-type block that expires on its own and that the cap does not override; 600 ticks at the defaults). (3) **A doom with no Graph or Attention tell** (hostile contact, a breach, lethal friendly fire) is not deferred, but it can no longer be restored into unfairly: if the fall follows the restore by fewer than `W_MIN_WARNING_S × TICK_RATE` ticks the stub steps back past that generation whether or not it is `RISKY` (SL-8, the fairness route). A self-inflicted RAGGED breach is the fourth soft input (row (d), OQ-41 (b)). **Still not covered, stated plainly:** a doom that leaves the player more than 600 ticks of acting time after the restore is the world's, not the save's; a STRAINED-yet-doomed seat (31 s of reserve and a 40 s exit) is caught by the step-back and not by the gate (Suit SE-33); and Assembly's optional `pending_any()` soft gate for the one-tick pending window (OQ-41 (a)) was not ruled and stays covered by the `REBASE_BLOCKS` interim (SL-12). The earlier idea of accepting the exposure because the `RISKY` step-back bounds it stays withdrawn, because a doomed save is by definition not `RISKY`.

**SL-6 — Save & Quit.** From the pause panel: `request_snapshot` while `PAUSED` (`PAUSED → SAVING → PAUSED`, `T` unchanged), write a generation of kind `QUIT`, and `request_shutdown` only after the write verifies (`WRITTEN`). The call is host-local (CR-15) and is never named a command in the MVP. Quitting is **never deferred** and bypasses the **soft** gate conditions only: if a soft condition was closed at request time the generation is written immediately and tagged `RISKY` (alternatives rejected: refusing to quit traps the player; quitting without writing silently throws away up to one interval). It does **not** bypass the seat-state hard condition: **a `QUIT` requested while any bound seat is `DOWNED` or `DEAD` writes nothing.** `save_and_quit` returns `SL_QUIT_NO_WRITE_DOWNED`, `save_status` is unchanged, and shutdown proceeds after the hold-to-confirm "quit without saving" (art bible HR-14; the look is the Chart Table's); Continue then restores the newest verified generation, which is exactly what the death stub would have done within `DOWN_TO_DEATH_TICKS`. The invariant this buys — **no file on disk ever holds a `DOWNED` or `DEAD` seat** — is testable (Acceptance Criteria). A `QUIT` while a seat is `DEAD` is unreachable in the solo MVP (the stub fires on the `DEAD` tick); the `DEAD` clause is for multi-seat futures. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* **QUIT-then-Continue exposure (resolved, user ruling X7)** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*. A `QUIT` has the same blind spots as an autosave (SL-5). A `QUIT` made with a critical suit is `RISKY` with `risky_cause` containing `VITALS` (the vitals input is in `quit_risky`), so Continue followed by a death inside `DEATH_LOOP_WINDOW_TICKS` steps back past it (SL-8); a `QUIT` made a tick before `DOWNED`, or with no tell at all, is covered by the fairness route (a fall within `W_MIN_WARNING_S × TICK_RATE` of Continue steps back past an `AUTO` or `EVENT` generation; *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review, delegated ruling G2)* a deliberate `QUIT` generation without `VITALS` in its `risky_cause` is exempt, because the player chose that moment and a step-back would discard a deliberate save and the session before it; a residual exposure, OQ-52). The post-`DOWNED` refractory does not bind a `QUIT`: a deliberate quit is never refused for it (the seat-state hard condition is the only one that refuses), and a `QUIT` inside the refractory is written at once, tagged `RISKY` only through the soft inputs (AC-SL-129). **QUIT inside an open Graph window.** A `QUIT` made while a Graph warning window is open is written at once and tagged `RISKY`, so Continue restores a fault with less than `W_MIN_WARNING_S` of its warning left. This is a listed exposure with a metric (M4, Edge Cases), and it is **not** clamped: forcing the quit to wait out the window runs the wrong way, trapping the player at the menu to protect a window they can see on screen. The other hard conditions also apply: the kernel-state condition, and an in-flight write (the `QUIT` waits for it), but not the post-`DOWNED` refractory, which binds interval and event saves only *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*; in `FAULTED` there is no write path (SL-22). If the write fails, the quit is held, `save_status` reads `FAILED`, and the player may retry or quit without saving by hold-to-confirm. The write waits at most `SAVE_QUIT_TIMEOUT_MS` (measured on the monotonic millisecond source, which is an I/O timeout only, SL-23; polled each frame, never a blocking join, SL-10); on timeout the pause panel stays and `save_status` reads `FAILED`, the job is not abandoned, and one that completes later is simply a `QUIT` generation *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*. A window-close request while `RUNNING` calls `save_and_quit()` from `RUNNING` (`RUNNING → SAVING → RUNNING`, K:164) and then `request_shutdown`, under the same rules (it saves the *current* state, so it is no rollback and no oracle); an OS kill (the task manager, power loss) falls back to the newest generation. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* **Close mechanism.** Alt-F4 and the title-bar close are not kills: the OS delivers the same window-close request, so they take the `save_and_quit()` path. The composition root sets `auto_accept_quit = false` so the engine does not exit on the request [UNV: the close notification and this flag are confirmed by the spike]. Save/Load is one Node built by the composition root and owned by it (not an autoload; the worker never touches it); it handles the engine's close notification **without blocking**: it records a `close_pending` intent and polls the writer each frame from `process_mode = ALWAYS` (in `RUNNING`, `PAUSED`, `LOADING` and `SAVING` alike), and `quit()` is called deferred, only after the `QUIT` generation reads `WRITTEN` (or the write was deduped, or the state is a boot-flow screen or `FAULTED`, where nothing is written). **Per state:** `RUNNING` and `PAUSED` run `save_and_quit()`; `LOADING` defers the intent until the kernel is `RUNNING`; `SAVING` lets the in-flight job reach `WRITTEN` or `FAILED`, then dedupes against it (state hash equal, no second write) or writes the `QUIT` generation; any bound seat `DOWNED` writes nothing and holds the close for the hold-to-confirm "quit without saving" exactly as a pause-panel quit does (`SL_QUIT_NO_WRITE_DOWNED`); `FAULTED` and boot-flow screens write nothing. **A failed or timed-out write holds the close; it does not fall through to an exit:** `save_status` reads `FAILED`, the close request is not acted on, and Save/Load publishes the host-local read `quit_held` (bool) so the Chart Table can present its hold-to-confirm (a request to the Chart Table owner, OQ-48). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — consistency pass, ASK-CT-11, EC-CT-38)* A window-close request while `PAUSED` under the open Chart Table (SOLO) runs `save_and_quit()` from `PAUSED` (`PAUSED → SAVING → PAUSED`) under the same rules and then `request_shutdown`; the Chart Table calls nothing and is never a second save path. On a boot-flow screen (main menu, UI-8, UI-9: no SIM state exists and the kernel is neither `RUNNING` nor `PAUSED`) Quit and window close write nothing: the process ends through boot code with no kernel call, because the kernel lists `request_shutdown` only from `RUNNING`, `PAUSED` and `FAULTED` (whether a boot-state shutdown call is wanted is the kernel owner's, ASK-CT-11); from `FAULTED`, Quit keeps `request_shutdown` and writes nothing (SL-22). If the state hash equals the newest generation's (same `T`, nothing changed) the write is skipped and shutdown proceeds. *(Orchestrator ruling: R4/R24 are silent on whether the gate applies to QUIT; three specialists agreed it must not block a deliberate quit, and proposed that it bypass every condition; the stage-5 checkpoint made the seat-state hard condition absolute, because a `DOWNED` generation would have broken the Player Fantasy's promise for up to `DOWN_TO_DEATH_TICKS`. Accepted exposure, from the creative director: Save & Quit before a risky door, quit, relaunch and Continue is a manual save in disguise; the friction of quit-and-relaunch is the only cost (the Zomboid comparison does not carry over, because a Zomboid death is final and a death here restarts from an autosave); determinism means it buys a retry of the player's inputs and, in a deterministic physical world, a look at one visible unknown for at most the minutes since the last autosave (Discovery is aesthetic priority 1, so this is measured, not waved through: triggers M1 to M6 in Edge Cases and AC-SL-94). The interval cannot be the remedy for quit-scumming, because a `QUIT` generation is written at the quit, not on the interval: the response is to accept the exposure for the MVP, keep metric M3 running and escalate to Failure Contract & Re-wake, never deleting the QUIT generation, which would contradict SL-8; OQ-25.)* *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*

**SL-7 — Death stub (the MVP answer to the Graph's Open Question 1).** "Crew death" in the MVP = every bound seat `DEAD` (single player: PlayerId 1); multi-seat semantics are Failure Contract's and Co-op's. `DEAD` is reached by the `DOWN_TO_DEATH_TICKS` timer or by the solo `give_up` action (user ruling D7), and from `DOWNED` in the pod as well as on foot (statuses run in the pod, user ruling D60); the stub cannot tell the three apart and does not need to *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*. Save/Load learns of `DEAD` through the observation channel of SL-13 and, **in the same tick boundary**, settles the writer **without waiting on it** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*: a write job that has already reached `WRITTEN` or `FAILED` is simply a candidate or not, and **a job still busy at the `DEAD` boundary is abandoned** (SL-10, abandon semantics). On the timer path it can only be a hung write: the hard gate has refused every new capture since `DOWNED`, a job started before `DOWNED` has had `DOWN_TO_DEATH_TICKS` (30 s) to settle, and the `DOWNED`-entry command of SL-10 already limited its remaining work to `DEATH_STUB_WRITE_WAIT_MS`. After a solo `give_up` (user ruling D7) it need not be hung: `DEAD` can arrive `GIVE_UP_MIN_TICKS` (the Controller's, 48 at the defaults, 0.8 s) after `DOWNED`, long before `DEATH_STUB_WRITE_WAIT_MS` (5 000 ms) can elapse, so a healthy job that was in flight when the seat went down may be abandoned; its generation lands as `STALE_EPOCH` (quarantined, never a candidate) and the stub restores the previous verified generation. That costs at most one more interval, inside the ceilings of F-7, and it keeps a capture taken in the seconds before a fall out of the ring *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* Abandonment bumps `write_epoch`, logs `SL_WRITE_ABANDONED_AT_DEATH`, takes the job out of `writer_busy` and leaves the selection to the already-verified candidates; the job's late result can only become `STALE_EPOCH`. There is no join and no blocking wait on the main thread at the `DEAD` tick. Save/Load then bumps `write_epoch` (SL-10), computes `restore_epoch` (SL-23), selects a generation (SL-8), pre-checks and stages it (SL-11), from the hot in-memory copy of the newest verified generation where one exists (SL-11), and only then calls `request_restore(payload)`, which the kernel honours at that tick's boundary services (the Controller's `DEAD` row and its Edge Case "`DEAD` entered during a camera cut" both state this hand-off). Sequence: `DEAD` → (same boundary: in-flight write settled or abandoned, `write_epoch`++ and `restore_epoch` computed (SL-23), generation chosen by SL-8, pre-checked and staged) → `request_restore(payload, restore_epoch)` → kernel `LOADING` (the command queue is flushed, every command `Dropped(RELOAD)`) → the Pipeline shows layer-1 black (`TRANSIT_HOLD`, which the Pipeline lists "kernel `LOADING` entered mid-session (death stub, load)" as an entry condition) → the kernel's restore and the verification window (SL-12) → first `RUNNING` frame is a camera `CUT` at the **pre-load `k_z`**. **No beat, no lights-out, no death screen, no dim-out and no sound** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: answers Adaptive Audio's flag F3 — the stub renders no dim-out of any kind, neither sim-driven nor an overlay, and plays no sound; the down sheet's last frame holds, and what sustained cues do at the `LOADING` cut is Audio's own rule, AAV-36)*: the art bible's 2.10 sequence (sources going dark one by one, then the cryo-bay lamp) is written for the cryo re-wake and belongs to Failure Contract & Re-wake (VS-A); the Pipeline's traceability row that says the sequence is "built once in the death stub" is a routed text fix (OQ-14). *(Orchestrator ruling: the brief proposed that the death stub counts as a re-wake for camera purposes and resets zoom to `k_auto`; the locked Pipeline text classes the death stub with in-session loads and says zoom resets "only on re-wake", so the stub **keeps `k_z`**. If Failure Contract wants the reset at VS-A, that is the Pipeline owner's call then.)* `DEAD` during a camera cut is **handled**, not impossible: the request is made at the tick's boundary, the Presenter holds the down sheet's last frame and no second cut is issued for the death (only `DEAD` during a *cut channel* is impossible, since `DOWNED` precedes `DEAD` by 1 800 ticks on the timer, or by at least `GIVE_UP_MIN_TICKS` after a solo `give_up`, and clears the channel) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*. If no candidate passes the load pipeline, the outcome is `SL_LOAD_NO_LOADABLE_SAVE`: Save/Load publishes `SL_LOAD_NO_LOADABLE_SAVE` and `save_status`; the Chart Table shows it as a boot-flow screen — the kernel is not `RUNNING` and no pause is requested (CT-25) — with New campaign or Quit (the message text is UX's; how a running session whose death stub found nothing is torn down to the boot flow is OQ-42, and from that state the player must be able to reach New campaign or Quit, AC-SL-134) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — consistency pass)* *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*; the dead world is never autosaved. The stub attaches no penalty beyond the lost interval. **The one plain line** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*: on the first `RUNNING` frame the HUD shows one ship-log line naming the record that was restored (UI-11), through its `FIRST_FRAME_LINE` channel, keyed on `restore_reason = DEATH_STUB` and carrying the restored generation's playtime. Resolved 2026-10-27 — user ruling (design-review decisions): option (b) of OQ-46 (D15, D84). It is a HUD line, not a screen: it has no death wording, no sound and no dim-out, so the art bible's no-death-screen rule (7.4) stands. Failure Contract & Re-wake replaces this rule at VS-A through the same `request_restore` path and PROFILE store (SL-9). *(Superseded 2026-10-27 — design review, withdrawn in the re-review: the earlier ruling that the stub waits for the in-flight write before it bumps the epoch is gone. There is no wait at the `DEAD` boundary: a job that finished by the boundary is a candidate, a job still busy is abandoned (which bumps `write_epoch`), and `restore_epoch` is computed after, before selection, SL-23.)* *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*

**SL-8 — Generation selection and demotion.** *Default:* restore the **newest** generation that is not demoted and passes the pre-check (SL-11) — the user's rule, "restart from the last autosave", verbatim. Save/Load restores an *older* generation only when (a) the newest fails the pre-check (fall back newest-first, within `LOAD_MAX_ATTEMPTS`; a reproduction mismatch is never a fallback reason, SL-12), or (b) the **last restored** generation `g_r` (set by the death stub and by Continue alike) is the newest non-demoted verified generation (no newer one exists) **and** one of two routes fires, where `T_downed` is the start of the `DOWNED` episode that ended in `DEAD` (observed through the `loco_state` read SL-13 already grants) and a restore returns `T` to `T(g_r)`: the **RISKY-window route** — `g_r` is tagged `RISKY`, is not a `QUIT` generation unless its `risky_cause` contains `VITALS`, and `T_downed − T(g_r) ≤ DEATH_LOOP_WINDOW_TICKS` 3 600 — or the **fairness route** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* (Resolved 2026-10-27 — user ruling (design-review decisions): X7 (b), D14) — `T_downed − T(g_r) < W_MIN_WARNING_S × TICK_RATE` (600 ticks at the defaults), **independent of `RISKY`**, and of the kind except that a `QUIT` generation without `VITALS` in its `risky_cause` is exempt *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review, delegated ruling G2)* (OQ-52). The fairness route covers a capture taken one tick before `DOWNED` and a doom from hostile contact, a breach or lethal friendly fire that no tell announced: a generation that would wake the player into a fall they had under `W_MIN_WARNING_S` to perceive is skipped, which is the unfairness the Graph's `W_MIN_WARNING_S` exists to prevent. The window bounds the player's *acting* time: measured at death it would shrink by `DOWN_TO_DEATH_TICKS`, whose owner range is 600–3 600, so at the Controller's upper bound the rule could never fire. The "no newer non-demoted verified generation" precondition is the loop test: if one was written after the restore the gate opened and the player got out, so there is no loop and the default already picks the newer file. In case (b) `g_r` is **demoted** (renamed `gen_k.demoted`, file kept, never read as a candidate) and selection proceeds as the default; demotion is what keeps (b) coherent — without it the next death would pick the same RISKY newest again and the ring would oscillate (the fairness route demotes a non-`RISKY` generation the same way and for the same reason). If nothing non-demoted verifies, a second pass over demoted generations (newest first) runs before `SL_LOAD_NO_LOADABLE_SAVE`; if the only candidate is `g_r` itself (a loop is detected on either route), restore it and log `SL_STEPBACK_NONE_AVAILABLE`. *(Orchestrator ruling: the brief's unconditional "second death within the window steps back two generations" is not adopted — it reinterprets the user's binding rule. "Step back on a repeated death from a non-RISKY save" is the open question flagged for the user (OQ-1), now narrowed: the user ruled the fairness route only (X7 (b)), and the general form stays playtest-gated. Demotion by rename is a systems-designer addition: it extends the file layout, survives the loss of the manifest, and extends R18; a late-landing write from an abandoned timeline is handled separately in SL-10.)* The selection is Formula `generation_select` (Formulas), which takes `T_downed`. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* **Why `QUIT` is excluded from the RISKY-window route unless the cause is `VITALS`:** a `QUIT` generation is `RISKY` only because the player chose to quit with a soft condition closed (SL-6); an unrelated death shortly after Continue must not step back past a deliberate save and discard its minutes. The exclusion is keyed on the stored `risky_cause` header field *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*: a `QUIT` made with a critical suit keeps the step-back, a `QUIT` made with a tell, a breach phase or a channel open does not. The fairness route shares the RISKY-window route's `QUIT` exclusion *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review, delegated ruling G2)*: a fall within `W_MIN_WARNING_S × TICK_RATE` of a restore is the unfair case for an `AUTO` or `EVENT` file, but a `QUIT` generation is a deliberate save and is stepped back past only when `VITALS` is in its `risky_cause`; the earlier "`QUIT` included" reading made a quit, Continue and a fall within 599 ticks discard the deliberate quit-save and the session before it, a loss F-7 never derived (OQ-52). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* **Who performs the demotion, and what a kill does.** The demotion applies **in memory at once** (the ring marks `g_r` demoted before `request_restore`, so the selection never re-picks it); the file rename `gen_k.sav` → `gen_k.demoted` is queued to the **worker** as a `DEMOTE` job, serialised with write jobs on the same queue (a write never races a rename in its own ring) and never run on the main thread. *Crash row:* a kill between `request_restore` and the rename leaves `gen_k.sav` un-demoted. That exposure is accepted and recorded instead of guarded by a `.demote_intent` marker: on relaunch Continue restores that generation (it is the newest again), and a death inside the same window demotes it again, so the cost is at most one extra restore of the stretch the player had already stepped back from (AC-SL-146).

**SL-9 — PROFILE store (K OQ 13, D5).** A per-campaign `profile.sav` beside the generation files, in the same container (`file_kind = PROFILE`; no `state_hash`; the trailer and per-block hashes still apply). The kernel produces one snapshot; Save/Load **splits it by `persist_scope` at write time**: WORLD blocks to the generation, PROFILE blocks to `profile.sav`. An in-session restore (the death stub, a load of the already-open campaign) passes **only WORLD blocks** to `request_restore`, so PROFILE blocks are never rolled back (CR-18); a cold open (process start, Continue) loads each scope whole from its own file, because partial restore within a scope is unsupported. `profile.sav` is rewritten, by the same atomic procedure (SL-10), when a capture's PROFILE per-block hashes differ from those last written (no subscription needed), on QUIT, and on an owner change notification (mechanism provisional, as SL-13), which triggers a profile-only capture with no new generation; a floor `PROFILE_MIN_WRITE_GAP_TICKS` bounds the write rate. `profile.sav` keeps a permanent `.bak` (Pillar 5 makes it more precious than any one world generation). A new campaign starts with an empty profile; the death stub and the VS re-wake ("new crew, same world") leave PROFILE untouched, so knowledge survives death in memory by construction and the disk file matters across process restarts. **Zero PROFILE blocks ship in the MVP; the mechanism is specified and tested now** with a synthetic PROFILE block (the kernel's acceptance criterion for `PROFILE` blocks is the model). What is kept at re-wake is Failure Contract's policy. `scope` (SHARED / PER_PLAYER) and `persist_scope` (WORLD / PROFILE) are orthogonal axes: `suit.state` and the Sanity blocks `sanity.state` and `sanity.ledger` are `PER_PLAYER` × `WORLD` in the MVP and **roll back with the death stub**; only knowledge, lore and scars are PROFILE, and none ships. The routed thread's wording "PER_PLAYER / PROFILE" conflated the two axes and is corrected here; the Sanity row in the kernel's Interactions table gives the level `persist` but no `persist_scope`; the designed Sanity GDD and the registry now state `persist_scope WORLD` for both Sanity blocks (OQ-21 closed), and the boot assertion still catches any such gap mechanically (SL-15).

**SL-10 — Write procedure and write epoch (D9; behaviour only → ADR candidate "atomic write, backup and thread model").** Capture is synchronous inside the kernel's `SAVING` state (the kernel owns capture cost against `SNAPSHOT_BUDGET_MS`, cited; Save/Load adds, on the main thread, only the encoding of the captured plain data and a small hand-off — Formula `write_budget`, bounded by `SAVE_ENCODE_BUDGET_MS` and `WRITE_HANDOFF_MAX_FRACTION`, both logging thresholds); the payload is an immutable byte value handed to a **worker** that never touches the kernel or any block [UNV: worker threads with file access]. The worker writes `gen_k.tmp` (named `gen_<write_epoch>_<k>.tmp`, SL-10 continued), flushes, re-reads and re-hashes it, renames the position's existing file (the oldest generation, if any) to `.bak`, renames `.tmp` into place, verifies again, deletes `.bak` and rewrites `campaign.json` (state table T-4 in States). **Rules:** every `store_*` return is checked (they return `bool` since Godot 4.4 — sourced from the engine reference); any false return, open error, hash mismatch or rename failure leaves every previous generation exactly as it was, reports to diagnostics and to `save_status`, and the next trigger retries with bounded exponential backoff (`AUTOSAVE_FAIL_BACKOFF_MAX_TICKS`; `AUTOSAVE_FAIL_ESCALATE_COUNT` consecutive failures raise the HUD notice). Never overwrite in place; never delete a generation before its successor verified; **never write while the kernel is `FAULTED`**. The design assumes only that a rename of a closed file to an *absent* name is all-or-nothing; atomic replace on Windows, sharing violations from scanners and indexers and OneDrive-redirected folders are [UNV] and are spike items. "Verified" means readable and hash-correct through the operating system's cache — it does not prove the bytes reached the media (whether a flush implies an fsync is [UNV]); the ring and the recovery scan (T-6) are the answer to power loss, not the verify step. **Write epoch (`write_epoch`)** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review, delegated ruling R1)*: this in-memory, per-process counter was called the "session epoch" and is renamed so it cannot be confused with the persisted `restore_epoch` of SL-23; wherever this GDD says "the epoch" in a write context (`gen_<write_epoch>_<k>.tmp`, `STALE_EPOCH`, "epoch bump", "epoch++"), it means `write_epoch`. Every `request_restore` increments `write_epoch`, which each write job carries; a job that observes an advanced `write_epoch` before its first rename aborts and removes its `.tmp`; a job that had already completed its swap lands a verified generation of the abandoned timeline, which is moved to `quarantine/` as `STALE_EPOCH` and is never a candidate (otherwise a later death would restore the ghost of the previous timeline). Recovery on opening a campaign runs the crash-point scan of T-5: any `*.sav`, `*.bak` or `*.tmp` that verifies is accepted and ordered by `generation`; a file that cannot be proven obsolete is never deleted.

**SL-10 (continued) — writer thread contract, polling and abandonment** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*. *Hand-off.* The worker receives only the per-block `PackedByteArray`s (immutable, caller-owned, *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*), file paths and integers (position, generation, `write_epoch`, retry counts): no kernel reference, no block, no Node and no `Callable` that touches the scene tree, and it never calls `call_deferred` into Save/Load or waits on the main thread. It publishes a status record (`state` ∈ the T-4 write states, `write_epoch`, `generation`, `position`, result code) under a `Mutex` [UNV: the status publication pattern is confirmed by the spike]; the main thread only **polls** that record and never joins the worker, because `Thread.wait_to_finish` and `WorkerThreadPool.wait_for_task_completion` take no timeout, so a join at the `DEAD` boundary would hang the frame on a hung write and could deadlock a worker that reports back through `call_deferred` [UNV: confirmed by the spike, AC-SL-90]. *Polling.* A tick-boundary poll in `RUNNING`;
