a frame poll from the Save/Load Node with `process_mode = PROCESS_MODE_ALWAYS` [UNV: confirmed in `PAUSED` and `LOADING`] in `PAUSED`, `LOADING`, `SAVING` and during the quit wait, where no tick runs and a tick-boundary poll would never fire. *`DOWNED` entry.* When a bound seat enters `DOWNED` with a job in flight, the main thread sets the job's `finish_or_abort` flag in the status record: the worker completes only its required steps, takes no rename retries beyond the first, skips the manifest rewrite (a hint) and aborts at its first failure, removing its `.tmp`; the remaining work must settle within `DEATH_STUB_WRITE_WAIT_MS` of the command, measured on the monotonic millisecond source (an I/O timeout only, SL-23), else the job is abandoned at that moment. *`DEAD`.* A job still busy at the `DEAD` boundary is abandoned with no wait (SL-7). *General deadline.* A job that has not reported `WRITTEN` or `FAILED` within `WRITE_JOB_TIMEOUT_MS` of its hand-off is abandoned in any kernel state, `DOWNED` or not (`SL_WRITE_ABANDONED_TIMEOUT`, `save_status` reads `FAILED`, `fail_n` is incremented and the backoff of F-2 applies), so a worker that hangs during healthy play cannot hold the hard gate until someone dies; `DEATH_STUB_WRITE_WAIT_MS` stays the shorter limit that starts at `DOWNED` entry. *Abandon semantics.* To abandon a job the main thread increments `write_epoch`, logs `SL_WRITE_ABANDONED_AT_DEATH` (or `SL_WRITE_ABANDONED_TIMEOUT` when a deadline expired), takes the job out of `writer_busy` (an abandoned job never counts toward the hard gate, so one hung worker cannot disable every later autosave) and records its ring position as **claimed**: F-9 skips claimed positions until the job reports, and if no unclaimed position other than the newest generation's remains, the write ends `FAILED` with `SL_WRITE_NO_POSITION` and backs off. Temporary names carry `write_epoch` (`gen_<write_epoch>_<n>.tmp`), so an abandoned job's `.tmp` and a later job's can never be the same file. The worker re-reads `write_epoch` from its status record immediately before each ring-touching rename and aborts (removing its `.tmp`) if it is stale; a job that lands a generation despite that (the check-then-rename race, or a worker that resumed after being stuck past the check) reports its old `write_epoch`, and the main thread moves the file to `quarantine/` as `STALE_EPOCH`, never a candidate (SL-3). The worker is never killed. *Owner.* Capture and every `request_*` call stay on the main thread; the single Save/Load Node (SL-6, close mechanism) owns the worker handle and the poll. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* **Thread model, reaping, exit, lock.** *Model:* one `Thread` per job (no `WorkerThreadPool`), its handle held on a `RefCounted` `WriteJob` object that also carries the status record, so the thread's lifetime is the job's. *Reaping:* each poll (tick-boundary or frame) calls `wait_to_finish()` on a job whose thread reports `!is_alive()`; a job that is not alive and has published no terminal `WRITTEN` or `FAILED` status is a worker killed by a GDScript error: `SL_WRITE_WORKER_DIED`, `save_status` reads `FAILED`, `fail_n` is incremented, the ring position is released and the `.tmp` is left to T-6, instead of the 30 s `WRITE_JOB_TIMEOUT_MS` hold of the hard gate. *Cap:* at most `MAX_LIVE_ABANDONED_JOBS` (default 2) abandoned workers may be alive at once; at the cap a new write ends `FAILED` with `SL_WRITE_TOO_MANY_ABANDONED` and backs off until one reports. *Exit:* process exit never blocks on a busy or abandoned worker (no `wait_to_finish` on a live thread); a `QUIT` write that is itself busy follows SL-6's timeout, and an abandoned worker still running at exit is left to the OS, its `.tmp` handled by T-6 on the next open. *Single instance:* the campaign directory is opened under an exclusive lock (`campaign.lock`, created with an exclusive open and holding the process id; the mechanism and its behaviour under OneDrive and antivirus are [UNV], spike rows in AC-SL-30 and AC-SL-90); a second process that cannot take it opens the campaign **READ_ONLY**: it writes no generation, runs no T-6 deletion or quarantine move and offers New campaign and Quit only (AC-SL-147), because two processes would collide on `gen_` and `.tmp` names and T-6 would delete the other process's work.

**SL-11 — Load pipeline, pre-check and fallback classes.** All pre-check steps run **before the kernel is touched**, so a bad file never produces `FAULTED`. The pre-check is a pure function of `(bytes, registry view, build constants)` — no clock, no file handle — and is unit-testable over in-memory byte arrays. Pipeline: candidates (ring files plus reconciled leftovers, sorted by header `generation` descending, minus demoted and quarantined) → pre-check (table T-5, a closed `SL_PRECHECK_*` reason set) → stage the decoded WORLD blocks (plus PROFILE on a cold open) → `request_restore` (the kernel orders restore by `restore_after`, runs every `restore()` before any `post_restore()`, CR-8) → verification (SL-12) → `LOADING → RUNNING` with `restore_reason` published (SL-21). **Failure classes decide what happens next:** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* **Option A for the death stub; hot copy decoded; writer defects.** (1) *Retries.* The `READ_RETRY_*` retries are frame-polled and apply on **Continue only**; the stub makes **one synchronous attempt per candidate** at the `DEAD` boundary (it must finish inside that tick boundary, which a frame-polled retry cannot), so a locked file there falls through to the next candidate or is served from the hot copy; the `DEAD`-tick work is checked against `DEAD_TICK_HITCH_MS` (AC-SL-88), a threshold separate from the black hold, because the work runs on the frozen pre-black frame that Game Feel 2 says reads as a crash. (2) *Hot copy, decoded.* After a generation reaches `WRITTEN` the **worker** decodes and type-walks it and holds **both the newest and the immediately preceding verified generation** as immutable decoded data (the staged WORLD blocks, handed to `request_restore` read-only; an owner's `restore()` copies what it keeps, OQ-53), so a step-back after a fast death (a solo `give_up` 48 ticks after `DOWNED`) selects the predecessor without a disk read; the `DEAD` tick only selects and hands over, and `t_read + t_decode + t_walk` leave the main thread on the hot path (F-8). A decoded copy that is not ready falls through to the disk chain; copies are dropped on a restore and when their generation is demoted or quarantined; the RAM held is two decoded payloads (a few MiB at the MVP slice, measured by the spike). (3) *Writer defects.* A decode or walk failure at load (T-5 step 19) on a file that passed write-time verification (T-4 `VERIFYING`) is a **writer defect**, not corruption: `SL_WRITER_DEFECT` is logged, the file is marked `suspect`, the next candidate is tried, and the ring is never quarantined wholesale.

| Class | Examples | Outcome |
|---|---|---|
| **CORRUPT** (integrity / structure) | bad magic, truncation, trailer or block hash mismatch, malformed header, decode failure, scope or seat mismatch, caps exceeded, mandatory block missing (with a `registry_epoch` equal to ours) | The file is a **quarantine candidate** (moved to `quarantine/`, never deleted); the loader falls back to the next candidate automatically |
| **INCOMPATIBLE** (intact, not ours) | `format_version` or layout unknown, `min_reader_version` above ours, `tick_rate` mismatch, unknown non-retired block, unsupported `schema_version`, a mandatory block missing from a file whose `registry_epoch` differs from ours (**INCOMPATIBLE-dev** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*: "made by an incompatible development build", never quarantined) | **The loader stops** and shows "made by an incompatible build"; files stay in place, never quarantined; the campaign directory is **read-only** for the older build (it never writes a generation into a campaign whose newest header has `format_version` above its own). A silent fallback would roll the player back to an older loadable generation and then overwrite newer files |
| **MISSING** | no candidate passes | `SL_LOAD_NO_LOADABLE_SAVE` (SL-7 / menu) |

*(Orchestrator ruling: this reorders R10 and overrides R12's "a generation that fails restore twice moves to `quarantine/`" in two ways. First, the trailer SHA-256 moves ahead of any JSON parse or version judgement so a flipped bit in `format_version` is reported as corruption (and falls back) rather than as "made by a newer build" (which must not). Second, the quarantine rule applies to the CORRUPT class and to `QUARANTINE_AFTER_FAILURES` repeated owner-reported restore failures; a **reproduction** mismatch (SL-12) is a code defect — the file hashed fine — so it marks the generation `suspect` in the manifest and keeps the file, because quarantining healthy player files for our own bug would hide them.)* Owner-level failures reported during `request_restore` (`<BLOCK>_RESTORE_*`, for example `GRAPH_RESTORE_UNKNOWN_DEF`) put the kernel in `FAULTED` with the previous state not intact; the kernel's only exit is an explicit user restore, so the loader's automatic fallback is **limited to pre-check failures**, and after `FAULTED` the menu's Continue re-runs the pipeline on the remaining candidates (SL-22). The pre-check makes that path nearly unreachable by catching everything it can first. Unknown block ids on the build's `RETIRED_BLOCKS` list are stripped with a diagnostics note (a removed block does not strand old saves); any other unknown id refuses, so the kernel's `RESTORE_UNKNOWN_BLOCK` is unreachable — reaching it is a Save/Load defect. A missing block is filled by `default_state()` only when its registration declares `added_in_format` greater than the file's `format_version`; otherwise the file is refused. `LOAD_MAX_ATTEMPTS` (= `AUTOSAVE_GENERATIONS`) bounds one load. For the death stub, the pre-check, decode and staging run **at the `DEAD` tick boundary, before `request_restore(payload)` is issued** (the pre-check is pure and needs no kernel); their cost lands in the `DEAD` tick, where the Presenter holds the down sheet's last frame, and **not** in `LOADING`, which holds only the kernel's restore and the verification window of SL-12. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* **Primary path: the hot copy.** After a generation reaches `WRITTEN` the main thread keeps, as an immutable byte value, the complete file image the worker assembled and returned (magic, header, payload region and trailer, T-4 `CAPTURING`) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* (the newest verified generation only; replaced by the next `WRITTEN`, dropped on a restore, and dropped when its generation is demoted or quarantined); at the `DEAD` tick the pre-check runs on those RAM bytes, so the death stub does no file I/O when the newest verified generation is the selection, and the decode and type walk (`t_decode + t_walk`, F-8) are the only main-thread cost. **Fallback: the disk chain**, used when no hot copy exists (a cold process, a demoted newest, an abandoned job) or the selection is not the hot generation: candidates are read and pre-checked from disk (a few ms for a ~90 KiB file; cold-cache and scanner latency [UNV]) in the `DEAD` tick. **Step-back pre-stage** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*: only the one candidate a step-back would select (SL-8 (b)) is pre-staged, read and pre-checked on the worker at `DOWNED` entry when `g_r` is `RISKY` and the loop precondition can hold, or when `T_downed − T(g_r) < W_MIN_WARNING_S × TICK_RATE` (the fairness route is decidable at `DOWNED` entry, because `T_downed` is then known); after a solo `give_up` the pre-stage may not finish in the few ticks left, and the disk chain in the `DEAD` tick covers it; it is discarded and re-staged on a `DOWNED → IDLE` recovery, because the gate may have opened in between and written a newer generation. The spike measures the cost of both (OQ-23, AC-SL-88).

**SL-12 — Verification: file stage, reproduction stage, and the verification window.** Verification has two stages (Formula `save_verification`, Formulas). The **file stage** is always on and runs before the kernel is touched: trailer SHA-256, every `payload_sha256`, structure. The **reproduction stage** proves the restore reproduced the snapshot: after the last `post_restore()` and **before the first `RUNNING` tick**, recompute `state_hash(PERSIST, T)` (D.12, cited, never restated) over the restored WORLD blocks and compare it with the header's `state_hash`. **Validity guard (needs no kernel hook):** the compare counts only if `kernel.clock.T` equals the header `T` at compare time; otherwise `SL_VERIFY_WINDOW_MISSED` is logged and **no verdict is recorded** (a hash taken after a tick has run would differ for the wrong reason). Its mode is **EXACT** unless one of five closed **REBASED causes** holds: (1) a migration ran, (2) a retired block was stripped, (3) `default_state()` filled a missing block, (4) the header's `hash_algo` is unsupported by this build (the compare is skipped with `SL_VERIFY_ALGO_SKIPPED`; the state hash is a bug detector, and refusing on it would make a kernel hash change a save-breaking event, contradicting SL-16), or (5) a member of the Save/Load-owned build constant `REBASE_BLOCKS` is present in the file. In REBASED mode *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: the baseline is now read back — the next restore of the same generation, with the same `generation`, `build_id`, `registry_epoch` and the first 8 hex characters of the trailer SHA-256 *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review: a byte-different file with the same generation number is a different baseline)*, compares its recomputed value with the baseline recorded last time and reports `BASELINE_MATCH` or `BASELINE_DIVERGED` (F-4), so a REBASED load is no longer vacuous against a non-deterministic restore; a first restore records, a later one checks, and a manifest rebuilt from headers has no baseline and re-records)* the recomputed value is recorded as the generation's baseline **in `campaign.json` only** (a hint — SL-14 forbids writing back to the file) and `SL_VERIFY_REBASED{cause}` is logged; it is not a failure. In EXACT mode a mismatch is `SL_VERIFY_NOT_REPRODUCED{blocks}` (naming the diverging blocks when the kernel exposes per-block hashes): **release** builds mark the generation `suspect` in `campaign.json` and **play on**; **dev and test** builds halt on the first occurrence (it is a K:839 / G4 regression). A reproduction mismatch **never falls back to another generation**: the file stage has already proved the bytes, so a mismatch after a successful restore is deterministic code (`restore(snapshot) ≠ identity`) that reproduces on every generation, and walking the ring would strand the player on an intact ring with `SL_LOAD_NO_LOADABLE_SAVE` for our own defect. `SUSPECT_THRESHOLD` is a diagnostics escalation only. *(Orchestrator ruling — findings the first rulings did not settle, as amended at the stage-5 checkpoint. (1) R10 says the compare runs after every restore in every build; applied literally, the first schema bump would fail every older generation (a migrated block's canonical bytes differ from the stored ones), hence the EXACT/REBASED modes and their closed cause set. (2) **Restore-idempotence is the kernel's rule, not a Save/Load request:** K:839 already requires, for every registered block, that the snapshot → interchange → restore → snapshot round trip yields byte-identical canonical bytes, and G:1096 is the Graph's instance of it; a registration flag that let a block opt out would be a CR-8 field invented by Save/Load and would make verification permanently vacuous, so none exists. The Controller's restore is a non-identity (PC-17: `octant`, `run` and `channel_ref` reset and `CHANNELLING` / `STAGGERED` become `IDLE`; the Controller draft's Body text zeroed `sub` and set `previous := current` in `post_restore`, P:913, while P:285 persists `sub` — answered for `body.state` by Ship Body & Interior Physics BDY-31: `post_restore` preserves `sub`, and `body.state` is restore-idempotent at block level *(Amended 2026-10-27 — consistency pass)*), and the Controller's G4 claim (P:1045) cannot hold while the control run carries sub-pixel residue that the restored run zeroes and the live Adapter's resync command is absent from a replayed stream. That is routed to the Controller/Body author as a **CONFLICT against K:839 and G4** naming those three specifics — PC-17 restore normalisation; `sub = 0` and `previous := current` in `post_restore` against a persisted `sub`; the P:1045 replay claim against the residue and the absent resync command (OQ-9) — and the resolution is the owners': express the normalisation in `snapshot()` or move it out of restore. **Interim, Save/Load-owned and provisional** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: one reading of PC-17 across this rule, SL-15 item 10, OQ-9, OQ-39 and the knob row)*: the build constant `REBASE_BLOCKS` lists the blocks whose presence selects REBASED mode. **Default `[interaction.state, assembly.state]`.** `controller.state` and `body.state` are **not** listed: the Controller answered that PC-17 normalises in `snapshot()` and that `restore()` is a whole-block replace, and BDY-31 makes `body.state` restore-idempotent at block level, so the automated AC-SL-67 (a mid-move `controller.state` fixture compared in EXACT mode) is the gate that proves it, and a story whose fixture fails it re-adds the block with a dated debt entry. `interaction.state` and `assembly.state` still clear transient fields in `post_restore` (IA-36, MSA-4), so a generation saved mid-channel or inside Assembly's pending window would verify `NOT_REPRODUCED` and halt a dev build on a routine quit mid-weld; they are listed **now**, so no merge halts dev builds, and the **exit** is that the owners move the clears into `snapshot()` (OQ-39, routed), after which Save/Load empties the list and the default becomes `[]`. A non-empty list is a known kernel-contract debt: while it holds, every real MVP file contains a listed block and verifies REBASED, which makes the reproduction stage diagnostic-only for real saves (the recorded baseline above still catches a restore that is not deterministic), and a CI check fails the first outside build while the list is non-empty (SL-14). The through-disk G4 criteria run on the kernel's stand-in blocks, `graph.state`, `controller.state` and `body.state`, and AC-SL-67 is a merge gate for the Controller, Interaction and Assembly stories. (3) **K-4** (provisional): the kernel text gives Save/Load no hook between the last `post_restore()` and the first `RUNNING` tick. The recommended mechanism is that `request_restore(payload, expected_state_hash)` is compared inside `LOADING` and reported to the requester as a **non-fatal** verdict (not a `FAULTED` transition, because release plays on); the alternative is a restore-complete callback to the requester; restoring into `PAUSED`, verifying and resuming is rejected (it conflicts with the Pipeline's `LOADING → RUNNING` `CUT`, with single-player-only pause in CR-16 and with VS-B's reuse of the restore path). Until the kernel owner answers (OQ-6), the reproduction stage runs in the test harness and in dev builds, which drive ticks and can call `state_hash` between restore and tick one, and in release only under the validity guard, diagnostic-only. This GDD states the requirement — the compare happens before tick one — and routes the mechanism.)* Integrity here is against accidental corruption, not tampering (host-owned saves, no PvP, no leaderboard).

*(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* **Per-build verification table.** The build flag is derived from the engine feature tags: `release` = `OS.has_feature("template_release")`; `dev` = any other run (editor or `template_debug`); `test` = `dev` plus the harness flag [UNV: the feature-tag names are confirmed by the spike; the engine reference has no OS module]. The table replaces the three inconsistent descriptions of release behaviour in SL-1, SL-16 and F-4.

| Build | Header `state_hash` written at capture | Reproduction compare on load | A file from the other kind of build |
|---|---|---|---|
| `release` | Absent until K-4 lands (SL-1, SL-16); written after | Not run before K-4 (verdict `NONE`); after K-4 it runs under the EXACT / REBASED rules of this paragraph with the same `REBASE_BLOCKS` | A file that carries `state_hash` loads, the compare following the row above; a file without it loads with verdict `NONE` |
| `dev` | Always written | Runs; EXACT mismatch halts, REBASED records and later checks the baseline | A release file (no `state_hash`) gives verdict `NONE`: never CORRUPT, never quarantined, never a halt |
| `test` | Always written | Runs as `dev`, with the injectable halt handler | As `dev` |

`REBASE_BLOCKS` makes a real MVP save REBASED in every build that compares; that is the stated, temporary diagnostic-only state of this paragraph, not a release-only effect. The outside playtest (AC-SL-94) must run on a `release` export, so testers hit the shipped behaviour.

**SL-13 — Observation channel for a control-plane caller (provisional — kernel owner).** Save/Load is a host-local control-plane caller (CR-15), not one of the kernel's five subscriber classes (PRESENTATION, INPUT_SURFACE, PROJECTOR, REPLICATION, DIAGNOSTIC). Its reads are limited to: `kernel.clock` (`T`, `world_seed`); `controller.state.loco_state` per bound seat (its `DOWNED` → non-`DOWNED` transitions give the refractory's `last_recover_T`, SL-5) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*; the Life-Support Graph's `read_view.failures()` for the Graph half of the soft gate (designed, LS-19; required at MVP; a SIM read with no perceived variant, so it is not a `PERCEPTION_OUT` view) and the optional Attention SIM view `attention.view` for the Attention half (designed; absent = open); Suit's `view(player_id).vitals_state` per bound seat for the critical-suit input (designed, SE-33, a SIM read) and Assembly's pure read `ragged_breach_open()` for the open-breach input (registered, source Modular Ship Assembly) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings, consistency follow-up)*; the event-trigger reads of SL-4 (the `POD_ARRIVED` notification, Assembly's pure read `last_shape_change_T()` and Interaction's pure read `last_salvage_T()`); and the Controller's end-of-tick change notification for `controller.state` (for the death stub) and for any PROFILE block (for SL-9). It **never** reads a `PERCEPTION_OUT` view (CR-12, H5) and **never writes a block** (H3, H4). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review, delegated ruling R1)* It reads no epoch: `restore_epoch` is Save/Load's own persisted value (SL-23) and reaches the sim as an argument of `request_restore`, never as a read. Which subscriber class or read access a persistence service has is not stated by the kernel's interaction table; it is routed to the kernel owner (OQ-7), and until it is answered every observation is a tick-boundary read-view poll made in the same boundary in which the request will be issued.

**SL-14 — Versioning and migration (D7).** Two levels. **Container** `format_version` (from 1, `FORMAT_VERSION`) is migrated by Save/Load; **block** `schema_version` is migrated by the *owner* inside `restore(data, stored_version)` as a chain v → v+1 → …, each step a registered pure function `(old dictionary) → new dictionary`. **Save/Load never rewrites a block's data itself** — owners are the only writers of their shape. Save/Load verifies, in the pre-check (T-5 step "schema chain"), that the chain is complete for every stored block (a gap is `SL_PRECHECK_SCHEMA_UNSUPPORTED`, never a mid-restore failure). A migrated payload is **not written back** to the old generation file; the next autosave writes the new shape, so a downgrade followed by an upgrade cannot half-migrate a file. A persisted WORLD block is **mandatory** for a file iff its registration's `added_in_format ≤ file.format_version`; the MVP mandatory set (SL-25) is the *expected result* of that rule, not a hand-kept list. An **additive bump** — a new block added after the first outside build — is `format_version + 1`, `min_reader_version` = the new version, container migration = identity, the new block filled by `default_state()`; defining it keeps the friction of adding a block constant. `added_in_format`, `RETIRED_BLOCKS` and the mandatory flag live in one data file cross-checked against the live registry at boot (→ ADR candidate). **Policy:** the machinery exists from format 1 and two goldens are kept *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*: a **stand-in golden** in `tests/fixtures/saves/v1-standin/`, regenerated freely while the registry is still moving (it exercises the machinery, not any shipped shape), and a **frozen golden** in `tests/fixtures/saves/v1/`, generated from the real registry at the first outside build and immutable from then on, with a schema-drift fingerprint check in CI (the committed shapes' `registry_epoch` digest may change only together with a `schema_version` or `format_version` bump) — the Graph's acceptance criterion names the convention; the **obligation** to migrate begins with the first build given to anyone outside the project (the three-person MVP playtest); earlier development saves are disposable and are refused with "made by an incompatible development build" *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: the signal is the header `registry_epoch` — a file whose epoch differs from ours and fails a mandatory-set or schema step is INCOMPATIBLE-dev and never quarantined, so registering one more block does not turn every dev save into a quarantined CORRUPT file)*. That policy is Save/Load's own; the Graph's citation of a Layout open question ("pre-release saves are disposable") is answered from the Layout side *(Amended 2026-10-27 — consistency pass)*: a renamed `room_key` or `template_id` needs a migration step in the owner once the first build has been given outside the project; before that, saves are disposable (this rule; OQ-12 closed). After the policy begins, a rename of a `def_id` or a `room_id` needs a migration step in its owner. The header's `catalogue_version` (designed — Item & Module Database, IDB-26, IDB-27) lets the owner detect a content-epoch change. A header `catalogue_version` above or below the running catalogue's is never a pre-check refusal (it is not a T-5 step): the load proceeds and each holder's `restore()` decides, with `GRAPH_RESTORE_UNKNOWN_DEF` as the model — this answers the Item DB's OQ 7 and its edge case on a save written under a higher `catalogue_version`. An unknown `def_id` at restore stays the owning block's named failure (`GRAPH_RESTORE_UNKNOWN_DEF`), and a `renamed_from` alias honoured only at restore is the Item DB's to offer. `format_epoch` (the brief's separate field) is **dropped**: `format_version`, `min_reader_version` and `added_in_format` cover it.

**SL-15 — Block authoring contract (R23; layered on CR-8, not a kernel amendment).** Save/Load's main deliverable to every block owner. A `persist` block must:

1. carry a namespaced `block_id`; declare `class`, `persist`, an explicit `persist_scope` (WORLD or PROFILE) and `scope`; a `PERCEPTION_OUT` block is never `persist`;
2. declare `schema_version` ≥ 1, bumped on any serialized-shape change, and `supported_versions` (the kernel's pre-check uses the list only "if owners declare"; Save/Load requires it) containing the current version;
3. return from `snapshot()` plain data only (H7: primitives, arrays, dictionaries, packed arrays; no `Vector2i` — integer vectors travel as two ints or a packed int array), in deterministic order, with entity references by stable ID and **no** derived caches, object references, `Resource` or `Node`; untyped containers; no `StringName` keys;
4. encode 64-bit data per S-1 (owner values above 2^53 never become float-typed JSON numbers; they travel inside the binary payload);
5. implement two-phase `restore(data, stored_version)` (decode and validate into scratch, swap on success) returning a value `(ok, reason, detail)` with `reason` from the block's own closed `<BLOCK>_RESTORE_*` set (the Graph's `GRAPH_RESTORE_*` is the model); a non-conforming return (null, wrong type, a reason outside the declared set) is `SL_RESTORE_NONCONFORMING` and is a hard failure — GDScript has no exceptions, so a runtime script error yields a garbage return [UNV];
6. treat out-of-range values as a hard failure, **never clamp** ("a corrupt save must not be fixed into a different world");
7. provide `default_state()` and, for a block added after format 1, `added_in_format`;
8. in `post_restore()` resolve cross-block IDs, rebuild caches, check invariants, and set `previous := current` for every moving read view (Pipeline Interactions row 1, PC-17) so the first frame after a load does not interpolate from a stale pose;
9. track dirty keys explicitly in its mutators — Godot 4.7 packed-array element writes do not call the property setter (sourced) — so any cached hash the block exposes stays honest;
10. be **restore-idempotent**: `snapshot(restore(snapshot()))` equals `snapshot()` in canonical bytes — the kernel's own rule (K:839: the round trip yields byte-identical canonical bytes for every registered block, whose byte-identity assertion K-3 retains while it changes only the transport; the Graph's instance is G:1096). A block whose restore normalises transient fields does not conform (the Controller resolved this by normalising in `snapshot()`, PC-17; `interaction.state` and `assembly.state` are the remaining cases, OQ-39, and the routed CONFLICT of OQ-9 is closed for the Controller and Body); until their owners move the clears into `snapshot()` the build constant `REBASE_BLOCKS` (SL-12) names such blocks, and it must be empty at the first outside build *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*;
11. register its migration steps per version and a golden save per shipped version (SL-14);
12. key `PER_PLAYER` data by PlayerId inside its own dictionaries, and pass the generic round-trip test every block has (`snapshot → var_to_bytes → bytes_to_var → restore → snapshot` equal, plus a JSON-dump assertion that 64-bit values arrive as strings — K-3).

Enforcement is by **boot assertions in the Save/Load service**, not by the kernel; a violation fails Save/Load's own boot — the composition root refuses to start, naming the block and the rule — never a runtime surprise. Whether that surfaces as the kernel's `BOOTING → FAULTED` is the composition root's mechanism (routed to the kernel owner; `FAULTED`'s triggers are kernel-owned, K:159):

| Reason | Condition |
|---|---|
| `SL_BOOT_NO_SUPPORTED_VERSIONS` | a `persist` block declares no `supported_versions`, or the list lacks its own current `schema_version` |
| `SL_BOOT_RESTORE_AFTER_UNREGISTERED` | a `restore_after` target is not a registered block id |
| `SL_BOOT_PERCEPTION_PERSIST` | `class PERCEPTION_OUT` with `persist = true` (CR-12) |
| `SL_BOOT_SCOPE_MISSING` | `persist = true` without `persist_scope` (catches the Sanity row gap mechanically) |
| `SL_BOOT_PER_PLAYER_UNKEYED` | a `PER_PLAYER` block whose fixture snapshot does not key its data by PlayerId |
| `SL_BOOT_NO_ADDED_IN_FORMAT` | a persisted block has no `added_in_format` entry in the format data file |
| `SL_BOOT_RETIRED_BUT_REGISTERED` | an id is on `RETIRED_BLOCKS` and also registered |
| `SL_BOOT_STRINGNAME_KEY` / `SL_BOOT_TYPED_CONTAINER` | the fixture snapshot contains a `StringName` key or a typed container |
| `SL_BOOT_SAVELOAD_REGISTERS_BLOCK` | Save/Load itself registered a block or hook (SL-17) |
| `SL_BOOT_KNOB_RANGE{knob}` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* | a tick or byte knob lies outside its safe range (OQ-28, decided): checked **before** any invariant, so no invariant ever divides by an out-of-range knob |
| `SL_BOOT_KNOB_INVARIANT{id}` | a Tuning Knobs invariant I1–I8 or I10–I13 fails against the data file *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*; I9 is a boot refusal in a **release** build and a logged diagnostic in dev and test *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* |

**SL-16 — Hash roles (D8; R10, R15).** Four distinct integrity layers, no overlap. (1) The **file trailer** SHA-256 over every preceding byte and (2) the per-block **`payload_sha256`** over each stored payload — Save/Load's own, fixed, native [UNV], verified before the kernel is touched; they localise damage and guard accidental corruption and truncation, not tampering. (3) The header **`state_hash`** and the optional per-block **`block_state_hash`** are the *kernel's* 64-bit hash over the owners' canonical bytes (D.12), named by `hash_algo`, so the ratified hash (H64 = first 8 bytes, little-endian, of SHA-256 via `HashingContext`, `hash_algo` 2; R-17, user ruling X13, registry `state_hash`) and the per-block cached hash (**K-1**, a kernel ADR candidate — cited, never owned) can change with no `format_version` bump *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings, consistency follow-up)*; `block_state_hash` is present only if the kernel exposes a per-block hash, is diagnostic and is never a refusal reason. (4) The post-restore **reproduction compare** of SL-12 detects a restore that does not reproduce the snapshot — a bug, not corruption. The kernel's D.11 worked example assumed the hash cost is a debug-build cost; this design makes it a release cost on every capture and restore, and the ratified H64 is native, so the remaining cost question is whether SHA-256 over 100–300 KiB stays near the 1.5 ms that example allowed [UNV] — the spike (AC-SL-89) measures it, and K-1 is the mitigation if it does not (OQ-2, OQ-17) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings, consistency follow-up)*. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* Godot's `HashingContext` offers only MD5, SHA-1 and SHA-256 [UNV: 4.5 – 4.7 are post-cutoff, confirm in the spike], so a scripted `hash_algo` 0 (xxHash64) or 1 (FNV-1a 64) would be a per-byte loop, estimated at 15 to 150 ms over ~90 KiB (the range between the first review and the game-designer's estimate) [UNV]; a hitch that appears only on writes would tell the player when the game saved, the signal UI-1 was retired for. **Ratified** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings, consistency follow-up)*: the default is the **native truncated SHA-256** (`hash_algo` 2, H64), which `HashingContext` computes natively (R-17, user ruling X13). The capture-time `state_hash` is still **skipped in release builds** until K-4 lands, because it has no release consumer before then (not because of hash cost), and is written in dev and test builds (SL-1).

**SL-17 — Zero blocks, zero hooks (D13).** Save/Load registers no state block and no step hook: ring bookkeeping lives in `campaign.json`, because a WORLD block would roll back on restore and desync the file set from the world. The kernel's acceptance criterion that the registry holds exactly one block, `kernel.clock`, stays true with Save/Load booted. The MVP save request is a host-local API call (CR-15), so CR-16's "save-request state" is not needed. A `saveload.request_save` command type (`origin PLAYER`, `allowed_when_paused`, `actor_binding NONE`) for VS-B guests is **reserved and (provisional — Co-op Session & Replication, VS-B)** at every mention; nothing in the MVP depends on it.

**SL-18 — New game entry (D14; routed amendment K-2).** Save/Load owns only `new_game(world_seed)`: create the campaign directory and a random `campaign_id`, set `world_seed` into `kernel.clock`, and write the **initial generation** (kind `EVENT`, trigger `NEW_GAME`) at the first tick boundary after the world is constructed, so a death before the first interval restores a fresh world rather than nothing. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* The world is built by the kernel's `init_world(world_seed)` owner step, which is **adopted** (kernel CR-18, K-2): every owner that registers `init_world(world_seed)` runs it in `LOADING`, in `restore_after` order, under one `WriteScope`, after every `default_state()` and before `post_restore()`, and never on a load from a save. The MVP world build is Web Map & Transit's `init_world` (WM-7), which builds the home-ship, the two hand-made derelicts and the pod through Layout's `build_section`; Save/Load owns none of it and only observes its end, and AC-SL-75 re-checks the ordering against CR-18. World content is Web Map's, Layout's and Authoring's. If world construction fails, or the baseline cannot be written after `AUTOSAVE_FAIL_ESCALATE_COUNT` attempts, the empty campaign directory is removed (it holds no verified generation, so the no-deletion rule is intact) and the menu receives `SL_NEW_GAME_NO_BASELINE`: a campaign with no verified generation would make the first death unrecoverable.

**SL-19 — Directory layout, compression, settings (D15–D17).** `user://saves/<campaign_id>/` holds `campaign.json` (the manifest hint), `gen_0.sav … gen_{N−1}.sav`, transient `.tmp`/`.bak`, `gen_k.demoted` files (SL-8), `profile.sav` and its `.bak`, and `quarantine/` (files moved there carry a reason — `CORRUPT`, `RESTORE_FAILED`, `STALE_EPOCH` — and their **generation number in the file name** (`g<generation>_<REASON>_<k>.sav`; the physical reason encoding stays OQ-27, the generation number is fixed now so `gen_next` can read it without opening the file) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* — and are never deleted automatically; the folder's size is reported in diagnostics against `SL_QUARANTINE_SIZE_ALARM_BYTES`, a diagnostics threshold only; deletion happens only through the explicit campaign delete under hold-to-confirm). `campaign_id` is 128-bit random hex; one campaign exists in the MVP, many are permitted (Alpha host-owned worlds). Starting a new campaign over an existing one is destructive and defaults to retiring the old campaign (renaming it out of the active location); deletion is a separate explicit action under hold-to-confirm (HR-14, art bible; the UI is the Chart Table's). **No compression in the MVP** (typical size ≈ 100–300 KiB, kernel D.11 expectation); header `codec` 0 with 1 reserved; a size alarm at `SAVE_SIZE_ALARM_BYTES` 2 MiB (diagnostics only), re-evaluated at VS-A (600–1 200 Graph nodes). **Settings and key bindings are never in a snapshot**: Settings & Input Rebinding (Alpha) owns a per-client `ConfigFile`; Save/Load may expose only the atomic-write helper. Steam Auto-Cloud is Alpha and constrains the path layout only.

**SL-20 — Identity (D12).** The header `seats` list names the PlayerIds present, **for validation only**; `PER_PLAYER` blocks are keyed by PlayerId inside their own dictionaries; the binding table (`peer_id → PlayerId`) is never saved (kernel CR-7) and a session rebuilds it. Single-player is PlayerId 1 bound to `HOST_PEER_ID` 1. A file whose `PER_PLAYER` keys are not a subset of `seats`, or whose `seats` is empty, duplicated or out of range, is refused (`SL_PRECHECK_SEATS_INVALID`). "Keyed by peer ID" is a stale token; a reconnecting peer with a different transport id changes nothing in a save.

**SL-21 — Published reads, `SaveInfo`, `restore_reason` and the host-local API (R7, R20, D11, D18).** Save/Load publishes **host-local reads**, never commands and never blocks: `save_status` ∈ {`IDLE`, `WRITING`, `WRITTEN`, `FAILED`} (`WRITTEN` is sticky until the next write begins and carries `written_at_T`; no HUD element displays it per write, *(Amended 2026-10-26 — CD-GDD-ALIGN)*); `fail_escalated` (bool) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — consistency pass, HUD OQ-HS-21)* — true from the `AUTOSAVE_FAIL_ESCALATE_COUNT`-th consecutive failed write until the next `WRITTEN`, because `save_status` reads `FAILED` on every failed write and the HUD counts nothing (HS-46); `SaveInfo` for the newest generation — campaign id, generation, kind (`AUTO`/`EVENT`/`QUIT`), `T`, playtime (`T div TICK_RATE`, whole seconds, integer floor — OQ-30), `game_version`, crew count, `RISKY`, depth label (provisional — derived from the node `ring` of `web.view`; Web Map publishes no depth-label field) — **computed from SIM blocks at capture** (`RISKY` stays on the read for diagnostics and the Debug Overlay; no player-facing line shows it, user ruling D82 *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*); and `restore_reason` ∈ {`NEW_GAME`, `LOAD_GAME`, `DEATH_STUB`, `FAULT_RECOVERY`}, valid for the first `RUNNING` frame after `LOADING`. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review, delegated ruling R1)* Also published, diagnostic only: `restore_epoch` (u32, SL-23); the sim reads the value only through `kernel.clock`. **`restore_reason` is a routed request, not an existing flag:** the Pipeline GDD has no such field (a search finds zero hits), so the brief's statement that the Pipeline was already amended is false; this GDD offers the flag as *provided* and requests it of the Pipeline, HUD & Suit Layer (designed, hud-and-suit-layer.md; the request stands) and Failure Contract & Re-wake (provisional — undesigned); the HUD's UI-11 line is the MVP consumer of `DEATH_STUB` (user ruling D15) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*, and no consumer may use `DEATH_STUB` to alter hallucination content (SL-24). **There is no screenshot thumbnail** (it would capture the Perception Filter's lie, Pillar 1), **no audio cue** (any sound is attributable by the web, Pillar 3, unless Audio justifies one) and **no death screen** (art bible 7.4). There is **no per-write autosave mark** in the MVP (UI-1 retired): the HUD & Suit Layer draws only the failed-write notice (UI-10, tied to write health, never to the gate; its shape and placement are the HUD's, HS-46), and the Chart Table's **main-menu Continue screen** (`/ux-design`) shows one recency fact, `SaveInfo.playtime` of the newest generation, read once at process start; the pause panel shows no save fact (UI-2), because a value that changes between writes would let a player who pauses for free infer that a write is overdue and a tell is open. *(Amended 2026-10-26 — CD-GDD-ALIGN)* *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* The MVP host-local API (consumer: Chart Table, designed): `new_game(world_seed)`, `continue_campaign()`, `save_and_quit()`, read-only `campaign_info()` (newest `SaveInfo` plus `newest_failed_verification` (bool) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — consistency pass)*: true when the newest generation by header `generation` failed the file stage at the last ring scan (opening the campaign, T-5) and a load would fall back, so the Chart Table can show UI-7 on the main menu (ASK-CT-5); otherwise the UI-7 line is the HUD's on the first `RUNNING` frame) and `list_saves()`, and `retire_campaign(campaign_id)` / `delete_campaign(campaign_id)` for the destructive actions; no `save_now()` and no `load(generation)` outside a dev build. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* Also published: `quit_held` (bool, SL-6). After a death-stub restore the HUD shows one plain ship-log line on the first `RUNNING` frame, keyed on `restore_reason = DEATH_STUB` (UI-11); Resolved 2026-10-27 — user ruling (design-review decisions): option (b) of OQ-46 *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*. **Restore timing (ASK-CT-7)** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — consistency pass)*: the kernel's restore and every `post_restore()` run as one blocking call inside `LOADING`, never chunked across frames; the pre-check and staging run before it (SL-11). Save/Load imposes no minimum black frame of its own and publishes no progress: the black hold is the Pipeline's `TRANSIT_HOLD` (`t_hold_pad` ≥ 120 ms, F-8), and the one presented black frame the Chart Table assumes (CT-42) is the Pipeline's to guarantee.

**SL-22 — `FAULTED` recovery (D10).** The kernel in `FAULTED` never writes or autosaves over an existing save, and a snapshot or autosave in `FAULTED` hands no payload; on a capture error the existing files are untouched. **Release:** `FAULTED` → main menu → "Continue" runs the full load pipeline (SL-11) on the newest verified generation through `request_restore` (the kernel's only exit besides shutdown), with `restore_reason = FAULT_RECOVERY`; the faulting session is never autosaved. A generation whose restore fails `QUARANTINE_AFTER_FAILURES` times (default 2, safe 1–3) because the *owner* reported a restore failure moves to `quarantine/` (never deleted); a reproduction mismatch only marks `suspect` (SL-11). **Dev build:** halt with diagnostics; the optional in-memory pre-restore snapshot the kernel allows is diagnostics only, never an automatic revert. Whether Save/Load may issue the recovery restore automatically in release (rather than on "explicit user action") is a kernel-owner question (K-4; OQ-20).

**SL-23 — Determinism, save-scum and wall clock.** Every persisted counter and seed is restored exactly (the kernel's `rng_derive` with counters inside the owners' blocks, G4 and D.10; the Graph keys its random draws by `(step_no, subject_id)` from `world_seed`), so the **physical** world replays the same way (Pillar 1). Whether a **perception** draw (a false system state's onset, sanity drift) replays identically after a restore, or is re-keyed, is Sanity & Hallucination Rules' decision; Save/Load makes no promise either way, and routes the constraint that any re-keying must not be steerable by the player — no reroll oracle through quit or death (OQ-37). *(Amended 2026-10-26 — CD-GDD-ALIGN)* **Threat draws and `restore_epoch` (user ruling D16, OQ-47; OQ-49 closed)** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review, delegated ruling R1)*: Attention and Horror re-key their threat draws per restore, while the Graph's physical failures and every persisted counter and seed replay exactly (the first sentence of this paragraph stands, AC-SL-68). Resolved 2026-10-27 — delegated ruling R1 (re-review; user delegated to best judgment): **Save/Load owns `restore_epoch`**, a persisted unsigned 32-bit integer that starts at 0 at `new_game` and is a key of every generation header (SL-1), so it survives a process kill, a lost `campaign.json` and a step-back. (1) *Advance.* On every restore, whether the death stub (`DEATH_STUB`), any load (`LOAD_GAME`, which includes a Continue after Save & Quit and a Continue in a fresh process) or a `FAULT_RECOVERY`, Save/Load sets `restore_epoch := min(2^32 − 1, E_seen + 1)`, where `E_seen` is the largest `restore_epoch` in any header of the campaign directory that parses (ring files, `.bak`, `.demoted`, `quarantine/`) and in the in-memory value; the ring scan at open (T-5, T-6) and every `WRITTEN` keep `E_seen` current, so `DEAD` adds no disk read. Reloading the same autosave twice therefore gives e and then e + 1, in one process or across a fresh start, and a step-back, a demotion, a quarantine or a lost manifest cannot lower it. A stub that finds nothing loadable (`SL_LOAD_NO_LOADABLE_SAVE`) restores nothing and does not advance it. (2) *Durable before play.* Save/Load durably writes the new value into the header of the generation it restores, by an atomic header-only rewrite: the payload region and `blocks[]` are copied unchanged (`payload_sha256`, `state_hash` and `generation` do not change), the header is re-serialised, the trailer recomputed, and the result goes through the SL-10 `.tmp`, verify and rename sequence on the worker. The write is issued at the staging step of the `DEAD` boundary (or of the load), runs while the kernel is in `LOADING`, and the end-of-`LOADING` hand-off (T-8 step 7, K-4, provisional) waits for its `EPOCH_DURABLE` report, so the epoch is durable **before the first `RUNNING` tick** and the wait lies inside the Pipeline's black hold. If the write fails, or `RESTORE_EPOCH_WRITE_MAX_MS` (placeholder 500, a monotonic I/O timeout) passes, play proceeds with the in-memory value, `SL_RESTORE_EPOCH_NOT_DURABLE` is logged and the next generation written carries it; a kill before that write replays the same epoch, a repeat and not a new draw (the exposure of OQ-25). Every newly written header carries the live value. (3) *Reach into the sim.* `request_restore(payload, restore_epoch)` hands the value to the kernel, which exposes it read-only, sim-visible and pure in `kernel.clock` as `(T, world_seed, restore_epoch)` before the first `restore()`. The kernel owns the one derivation for threat draws, `counter_eff = rng_counter + RESTORE_KEY_STRIDE × restore_epoch` (`RESTORE_KEY_STRIDE` 1 048 576, owned by the kernel; the stream-name hash stays cached and unchanged); Attention and Horror call that derivation and define none of their own, and the name `threat_epoch()` is retired in favour of `restore_epoch`. The Graph's physical failures and every persisted counter never read it. (4) *Determinism is defined given the pair:* the same (save bytes, `restore_epoch`) replays identically, and tests pin the epoch (AC-SL-142, AC-SL-145). (5) *What it prices, stated plainly:* the player can neither pick nor predict the new value; a death re-keys the next stretch (the price, metric M2); and because every restore advances it, a Save & Quit followed by Continue also re-keys the *threat* draws while the physical world and every persisted counter come back exactly, a re-key whose only cost is the relaunch (metric M3). That reading of "quit buys nothing" is recorded as a USER DECISION at OQ-50. A process kill replays the older stretch exactly (OQ-25). The former rule "advances on a death-stub restore only" is superseded by (1). Save/Load stores true SIM state only (SL-24). Every interval, gap and window in SL-4, SL-5 and SL-8 is in ticks read from `kernel.clock.T`; `created_unix` and `game_version` are file metadata written by the service and never read by the sim (H9), so the tests are deterministic. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* **Wall clock, and the one exception.** The wall clock is never read in a decision about sim time. `SAVE_QUIT_TIMEOUT_MS`, `RENAME_RETRY_DELAY_MS`, `READ_RETRY_DELAY_MS` and `DEATH_STUB_WRITE_WAIT_MS` are millisecond **I/O timeouts** that must run while `T` is frozen (`PAUSED`, `SAVING`, `LOADING`), so they read the engine's **monotonic** millisecond counter through an injected source; a timeout never changes sim state, schedules an autosave or enters a gate input. The Graph's note that "single-slot autosave is Save/Load's and the user's decision" is answered by SL-3's ring; the determinism argument is unaffected. The kernel's `rng_derive` note that a 63-bit owner-defined key is a valid `counter` appears only in OQ-3 as a routed kernel amendment (D.10), not as a rule here. A process kill after a mistake rewinds by at most one interval plus the deferral cap — the same price as dying, minus the death floor — and is an accepted, unpreventable exposure (no write can protect against it), recorded for playtest.

**SL-24 — Truth-only persistence (Pillars 1 and 4).** Save/Load never reads, derives, stores or varies by perception: no `PERCEPTION_OUT` block is ever written (a boot assertion and a pre-check step refuse one), no "what the player saw" field exists, no thumbnail, no sanity-dependent autosave timing, no hallucinated save list (H1, H6). The first `RUNNING` frame after a load must already be presented through the Perception Filter for the restored sanity level — the black hold covers any rebuild (H4); the ordering is a **routed request** to Perception Filter, Sanity and Pipeline (all three designed; the request stands), recorded as a cross-system requirement with no owner yet (OQ-11). The failed-write notice and the main-menu newest-record line are inviolable channels (H5): their look is UX's, but they never take flesh or violet, never flicker with sanity and are never a hallucination class; there is no per-write glyph *(Amended 2026-10-26 — CD-GDD-ALIGN)*. Because Suit vitals and the Sanity level roll back with the world, a reload is also never a sanity cure (H3).

**SL-25 — What is saved: provisional block inventory and the mandatory set.** The kernel's snapshot covers every `persist` block at one `T`; Save/Load splits it by `persist_scope` (SL-9). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — consistency pass)* Every owner GDD below is designed and every name is the registered `block_id` (the registry entry name in brackets where it differs); `web.state` keeps its "provisional name until merge" tag:

| `block_id` | Owner | `persist_scope` × `scope` | Status |
|---|---|---|---|
| `kernel.clock` | Kernel | WORLD × SHARED, `SIM`; canonical keys exactly `T` and `world_seed` | designed; mandatory |
| `layout.state` (registry: `layout_state_block`) | Ship Section & Layout Data Model | WORLD × SHARED, `repl_hint SHARED_SUMMARY`, schema 1, `restore_after []` | designed (LY-1); mandatory; **the `restore_after` root of `graph.state`, `emitter.state` and `body.state`**: room identity must be stable across versions (a renamed `room_id` breaks Graph restore with `GRAPH_RESTORE_UNKNOWN_ROOM`; migration stance OQ-12) |
| `graph.state` | Life-Support Graph | WORLD × SHARED, `repl_hint SHARED_SUMMARY`, `schema_version` 1, `restore_after [layout.state]` | designed; mandatory; zero floats; largest block (≤ 32 KB at MVP size, per the Graph's cost criterion) |
| `controller.state` | Player Controller & Input | WORLD × PER_PLAYER (the Controller author confirms the scope value) | designed; mandatory; `carry_ref` already saved here |
| `body.state` (registry: `body_state_block`) | Ship Body & Interior Physics (BDY-5) | WORLD × SHARED, `repl_hint SHARED_SUMMARY`, schema 1, `restore_after [layout.state]` | designed; mandatory; restore-idempotent at block level (BDY-31), so not in `REBASE_BLOCKS` |
| `emitter.state` | Emitter State & Light-Exposure Query | WORLD × SHARED, `SHARED_SUMMARY`, schema 1, `restore_after: [layout.state, graph.state, body.state, controller.state]` per EM-1 (designed; supersedes the three-block list in the sibling brief, which omitted `body.state`) | designed (EM-1) |
| `interaction.state` | Interaction System (IA-36) | WORLD × SHARED (per-seat records keyed by PlayerId), `repl_hint SHARED_SUMMARY`, schema 1, `restore_after [layout.state, controller.state, graph.state, emitter.state]` | designed; `post_restore` drops door attempts (`attempts[]`) and in-progress channels and clears `last_result` (IA-36) — a restore normalisation, OQ-39; in `REBASE_BLOCKS` until the owner moves the clear into `snapshot()` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* |
| `assembly.state` | Modular Ship Assembly (MSA-4) | WORLD × SHARED, `repl_hint SHARED_SUMMARY`, schema 1, `restore_after [layout.state, graph.state, interaction.state, body.state]` | designed; per-seat `pending` only is persisted (`last_result` is runtime-only, outside `snapshot()` and every hash scope), plus the empty-in-MVP `cascade_deferred` record; no channel progress and no world history; `post_restore` verifies the as-restored `pending` then clears it (OQ-39); in `REBASE_BLOCKS` until the owner moves the clear into `snapshot()` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* *(Amended 2026-10-27 — consistency pass after design review)* |
| `suit.state` | Suit & Equipment | WORLD × PER_PLAYER (keyed by PlayerId; rolls back on the death stub), `repl_hint SHARED_SUMMARY` | designed; schema 1, `restore_after []`; its `view(player_id).vitals_state` is a runtime soft-gate read (SE-33) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* |
| `sanity.state` | Sanity & Hallucination Rules | WORLD × PER_PLAYER (keyed by PlayerId), `repl_hint SHARED_SUMMARY`, schema 1, `restore_after []` | designed; closed `SANITY_RESTORE_*` reasons (SCHEMA_UNSUPPORTED, SHAPE, LEVEL_RANGE, STAGE_RANGE, COUNTER_RANGE, LIE_KIND_UNKNOWN, LIE_TIME_ORDER, SUBJECT_UNRESOLVED) |
| `sanity.ledger` | Sanity & Hallucination Rules | WORLD × PER_PLAYER, `repl_hint HOST_ONLY`, schema 1, `restore_after [sanity.state, graph.state, layout.state]` (+ `horror.state`, designed) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* | designed; lie records and RNG counters inside; read by nothing outside sanity/ except the Debug Overlay |
| Sanity perception output | Sanity | `PERCEPTION_OUT`, **never persisted**, rebuilt from the restored `sanity.state` and the persisted `sanity.ledger` (the active lies are SIM state a seed cannot reproduce) | designed |
| `attention.state` | Attention System | WORLD × SHARED, `repl_hint SHARED_SUMMARY`, schema 1, `restore_after [graph.state, emitter.state]` | designed; closed `ATTENTION_RESTORE_*` reasons, never clamps |
| `web.state` (provisional name until merge): derelict records, seeds and deltas, pod and leg state | Web Map & Transit | WORLD × SHARED, `repl_hint SHARED_SUMMARY`, schema 1, `restore_after [layout.state, graph.state, body.state]`; about 2 KB | designed; seeds plus deltas keep it small; the loaded and berthed sets are derived, never persisted; unloaded derelicts persist as data (Graph `dormant` sections) |
| `horror.state` | Horror Entities | WORLD × SHARED, `repl_hint SHARED_SUMMARY`, schema 1, `restore_after [layout.state, graph.state, emitter.state, attention.state]`; RNG counter (`horror.tell`) and the mark ring inside | designed; closed `HORROR_RESTORE_*` reasons |
| PROFILE blocks | Knowledge Codex, Sanity scars, Failure Contract | PROFILE | VS / Alpha; **zero in the MVP** |
| (none) | Save/Load; Failure & Trail; Item & Module Database; 2D Lighting & Occlusion | — | register no block (2D Lighting & Occlusion is a pure renderer, LO-29) |

The MVP **mandatory set** — the expected result of SL-14's derivation, now that those GDDs are designed — is `kernel.clock`, `layout.state`, `graph.state`, `controller.state`, `body.state`, `emitter.state`, `interaction.state`, `assembly.state`, `suit.state`, `sanity.state`, `sanity.ledger`, `attention.state`, `web.state` (provisional name until merge) and `horror.state`: every MVP-registered WORLD block *(Amended 2026-10-27 — consistency pass)*. **Not in a snapshot:** the pending command queue (kernel data), the binding table, the command log, diagnostics, any `PERCEPTION_OUT` block, camera and zoom state (the Pipeline's; never saved), the Controller Adapter's local state (`last_acked`, hold timers, run latch, device, pre-play tokens), derived caches of any block, and settings and bindings.

**Reconciliation notes (how the specialists' proposals and the rulings were merged).**

| # | Question | Resolution | Source of the pick |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | File layout | R1 plus a 32-byte SHA-256 trailer; layout id in the magic's last byte | technical-director, lead-programmer; additive to R1 |
| 2 | Ring write sequence | New generation into the oldest ring position; `.bak` = replaced oldest; order by header `generation` | all three specialists; R11's guarantee kept |
| 3 | Gate hard vs soft | `DOWNED`/`DEAD`, kernel state, restore pending and writer busy are hard; the soft inputs are the Graph tell, the Attention tell, the breach phase, the seat soft states (including transit), a critical suit and an open self-inflicted breach (F-2 `soft_block`) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*; cap overrides soft only; QUIT bypasses soft conditions only (refused with `SL_QUIT_NO_WRITE_DOWNED` while any seat is `DOWNED`/`DEAD`) and tags `RISKY` | systems-designer, with QUIT per all three; refines R4 |
| 4 | Death-loop guard | Default newest verified; step back on a pre-check failure, on the RISKY-window route or on the fairness route (user ruling X7 (b)); demotion by `.demoted` rename *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review: was "R5 verbatim")* | systems-designer; extends R5 and R18 |
| 5 | Fallback and quarantine | CORRUPT falls back and quarantines; INCOMPATIBLE stops and leaves files; reproduction mismatch marks `suspect` | lead-programmer, systems-designer; overrides R12's "twice-failed" |
| 6 | Pre-check order and names | Trailer SHA before the layout id, JSON and version judgement (SL-1 format law: the layout-id byte is inside the SHA-covered range); one closed `SL_PRECHECK_*` set (22 pre-kernel steps, plus the post-restore compare) | lead-programmer; reorders R10 |
| 7 | Post-restore compare | EXACT/REBASED with a closed five-cause set (including `REBASE_BLOCKS`); a mismatch plays on (release) or halts (dev), never the next candidate; `T == header T` validity guard; restore-idempotence is K:839 (CONFLICT routed, OQ-9); K-4 mechanism provisional (OQ-6) | systems-designer, lead-programmer; rulings landed in OQ-9, SL-12 and OQ-6 (K-4); refines R10 |
| 8 | Timeline-fork rule (quarantine every generation newer than the restored one) | Folded into demotion (RISKY step-back) and `STALE_EPOCH` (late-landing write); no separate rule | orchestrator |
| 9 | Per-block hash | Two families: `payload_sha256` (transport, always) and optional kernel `block_state_hash` (K-1 compatible) | lead-programmer |
| 10 | MVP API | No `save_now()`, no `load(generation)`; `continue_campaign`, `new_game`, `save_and_quit`, reads | lead-programmer, technical-director; the brief's API list narrowed |

### States and Transitions

Save/Load owns no kernel state and no state block (SL-17). It has **host-local** state — `last_save_T`, `pending_event` (kind only; events coalesce), `first_due_T`, `restore_epoch`, the writer's state, `restored_gen_id` (the generation this session began from) and per-generation `suspect` counters in `campaign.json` — all re-derived on process start (`last_save_T := T` of the restored generation, `pending_event := none`, `first_due_T := none`), none of it sim state, none of it able to alter the sim **except `restore_epoch`**, which is persisted in the generation headers (SL-23) and is the one Save/Load value the sim sees, through `kernel.clock` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review, delegated ruling R1)* (an autosave-transparency criterion in Acceptance Criteria). The kernel's states and transitions are the kernel's and are cited, never restated; the tables below describe what Save/Load may do in each, the lifecycle of a generation file, the writer, the pre-check, crash recovery and the death-stub run.

**T-1. Kernel-state interplay, from Save/Load's seat.**

| Kernel state | What Save/Load may do | What it must not do | Rule |
|---|---|---|---|
| `BOOTING` | Run the block-contract boot assertions on the frozen registry | Issue any request; read a file as a world | SL-15 |
| `LOADING` | Wait; publish `restore_reason`; hold the epoch; verification window (dev/test; release diagnostic-only per SL-12, K-4 provisional) | Issue a second request (the kernel ignores and reports it); autosave | SL-11, SL-12 |
| `RUNNING` | Poll the gate and triggers; `request_snapshot` when an autosave fires; `request_restore` on crew death | `request_pause` / `request_resume` (not its calls) | SL-4, SL-7 |
| `PAUSED` | Save & Quit `request_snapshot`; `request_restore`: dev-build `load(generation)` only (no MVP caller) | Start an interval autosave (`T` cannot advance, so none is due) | SL-6, SL-11 |
| `SAVING` | Wait. A restore requested now waits for the boundary | Queue a second snapshot | SL-10 |
| `FAULTED` | Show recovery; on explicit user action `request_restore` of the newest verified generation; `request_shutdown` | **Any write**, any autosave, any overwrite | SL-22 |
| `SHUTTING_DOWN` | Nothing — the QUIT write completed (`WRITTEN`) before the request was issued | Begin any write | SL-6 |

**T-2. Autosave scheduler (host-local).**

| State | Entry | Exit | Transitions | Who may cause them |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `IDLE` | Boot; a write ended; a restore reset `last_save_T` | `due` becomes true (interval elapsed, or an event is pending and `AUTOSAVE_MIN_GAP_TICKS` has elapsed) or a QUIT is requested | → `DUE_WAITING`; → `FIRING` (QUIT; soft conditions bypassed; refused with `SL_QUIT_NO_WRITE_DOWNED` while any seat is `DOWNED`/`DEAD`) | Clock `T`; event notifications (provisional); the pause panel |
| `DUE_WAITING` | A save is due; `first_due_T` recorded | Gate open on a retry tick; or `AUTOSAVE_MAX_DEFER_TICKS` elapsed with only soft conditions closed | → `FIRING` (`RISKY` if a soft condition was closed); stays while a hard condition is closed (a seat-state or in-flight hard condition never expires; the post-`DOWNED` refractory expires on its own after `W_MIN_WARNING_S × TICK_RATE` ticks, SL-5) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* | Gate inputs re-read every `AUTOSAVE_RETRY_TICKS` |
| `FIRING` | `request_snapshot` issued at a boundary | Capture handed to the writer (T-4 `CAPTURING`) | → `IDLE` on `WRITTEN`; → `BACKOFF` on `FAILED` | Kernel (`SAVING`); the writer |
| `BACKOFF` | A write failed | The backoff interval elapses (bounded exponential, capped by `AUTOSAVE_FAIL_BACKOFF_MAX_TICKS`) | → `DUE_WAITING` (the save is still due, because `last_save_T` is unchanged by a failed write) | Clock |

**T-3. Generation lifecycle (per file).** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: `gen_k.tmp` here reads `gen_<epoch>_<k>.tmp`, see the T-4 note.)*

| State | Meaning | Entry | Exit | Transitions | Who may cause them | On disk |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `TMP` | Being written | The worker opens `gen_k.tmp` | Re-read hash verifies, or a failure | → `VERIFIED`; → removed (failure) | Writer | `gen_k.tmp` only; previous generations untouched |
| `VERIFIED` | Complete and self-checked, not yet in place | Re-read and re-hash match | The position swap starts | → `NEWEST` | Writer | the verified `.tmp` |
| `NEWEST` | Highest `generation` among non-discarded files | Swap complete and manifest rewritten | A successor reaches `NEWEST` | → `OLDER`; → `DEMOTED`; → `QUARANTINED` | Writer; SL-8; the load pipeline | `gen_k.sav` |
| `OLDER` | A ring member below the newest | A successor became newest | Chosen as the position for reuse, demoted or quarantined | → overwritten by a successor; → `DEMOTED`; → `QUARANTINED` | Writer; SL-8; the load pipeline | `gen_k.sav` |
| `DEMOTED` | Stepped back from (SL-8); excluded from default selection, retained | A death on the RISKY-window route or the fairness route (SL-8) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* | Its ring position is rewritten | → removed only when the position is reused | SL-8 | `gen_k.demoted` |
| `BAK` | The replaced oldest file during a swap | The position's file is renamed to `.bak` | Manifest verified, then removed; or promoted back on a failed swap | → removed; → `gen_k.sav` | Writer; boot recovery | `gen_k.bak` |
| `QUARANTINED` | Failed as CORRUPT, failed owner restore `QUARANTINE_AFTER_FAILURES` times, or landed `STALE_EPOCH` | The pre-check, the kernel or the `write_epoch` rule names it | Never leaves automatically | — | Load pipeline; epoch rule | `quarantine/…` with a reason |
| `SUSPECT` | Passed the file stage but failed the reproduction stage (`SL_VERIFY_NOT_REPRODUCED`) | Verification | A later generation reproduces (counter reset) | stays in the ring (release plays on; dev/test halts on the first occurrence); counter only, and `SUSPECT_THRESHOLD` is a diagnostics escalation | Verification (SL-12) | unchanged; counter in `campaign.json` |

**T-4. Write-procedure states.** A failure at any state leaves every previous generation exactly as it was. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* Temporary names carry `write_epoch`: read every `gen_k.tmp` below as `gen_<write_epoch>_<k>.tmp` (SL-10), and the recovery scan of T-6 matches that pattern.

| State | `save_status` | Work (runs where) | Failure outcome on disk |
|---|---|---|---|
| `IDLE` | `IDLE` (or sticky `WRITTEN`/`FAILED`) | none | n/a |
| `CAPTURING` | `WRITING` | Kernel `SAVING`, synchronous snapshot; split by `persist_scope`; `SaveInfo` computed; per-block `var_to_bytes` only (main thread, tick halted); the **worker** hashes each block, builds the header (`payload_sha256`, `block_state_hash`), assembles header, payload region and trailer, and returns the complete image, which becomes the hot copy after `WRITTEN` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* | A capture error takes the kernel to `FAULTED`; no file touched |
| `WRITING_TMP` | `WRITING` | Worker writes `gen_k.tmp`; **every `store_*` return is checked**; flush; close | Any false return or open error: `.tmp` removed best-effort, `FAILED` |
| `VERIFYING` | `WRITING` | Worker re-opens the `.tmp` and runs the offline subset of the pre-check (every T-5 step up to and including per-block `payload_sha256`: no registry needed), then the read-side **decode and bounded type walk** (T-5 step 19) over the same bytes *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* | Mismatch: `.tmp` removed, `FAILED`; a decode or walk failure is `SL_WRITE_UNENCODABLE` naming the block, previous generations intact |
| `SWAPPING_OUT` | `WRITING` | If the position holds a file — the oldest generation, or a `.demoted` occupant, which is treated exactly like an oldest occupant — rename it to `.bak` (a stale verified `.bak` is deleted first; an unverified one aborts the write, `SL_WRITE_STALE_BAK`); the `.bak` is deleted after `POST_VERIFY` | Rename failure (retry `RENAME_RETRY_COUNT`): `FAILED`, the position's file still in place |
| `SWAPPING_IN` | `WRITING` | Rename `.tmp` → `gen_k.sav` (the target is absent, so no rename-onto-existing; Windows overwrite semantics are never relied on [UNV]) | Failure: roll back by renaming `.bak` back; `FAILED` |
| `POST_VERIFY` | `WRITING` | Hash `gen_k.sav` again; on success delete `.bak` | Mismatch: promote `.bak` back, `FAILED` |
| `MANIFEST` | `WRITING` | Rewrite `campaign.json` by the same temp-and-rename (a hint) | A failure here is a diagnostic, **not** a save failure |
| `WRITTEN` | `WRITTEN` | Main thread observes completion at a tick boundary; `last_save_T` updated | n/a |
| `FAILED` | `FAILED` | Diagnostics entry; the HUD read set; `BACKOFF` | Previous generations intact |
| (`write_epoch` bump) | unchanged | Any state; a job that sees a newer `write_epoch` before its first rename aborts and removes its `.tmp`; one already past `SWAPPING_IN` lands `STALE_EPOCH`. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* An **abandoned** job (SL-10: busy at `DEAD`, past `DEATH_STUB_WRITE_WAIT_MS` after `DOWNED` entry, or past `WRITE_JOB_TIMEOUT_MS` of its hand-off) leaves `writer_busy` at once and its ring position stays claimed until it reports | the late generation is quarantined, nothing else changed |

**T-5. Pre-check: ordered decision list.** Runs entirely before the kernel is touched; stop at the first failing step. `SL_PRECHECK_*` is Save/Load's closed refusal set; every refusal is logged with the generation number and step name. **Class** is the failure class of SL-11. The trailer is checked **before** the layout id is judged (the format law of SL-1), so a flipped bit in the layout-id byte is CORRUPT and falls back, never INCOMPATIBLE.

| # | Check | Refusal reason | Class → next action |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Open and read succeed *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: retried `READ_RETRY_COUNT` times, `READ_RETRY_DELAY_MS` apart, frame-polled and never a blocking sleep, **on Continue only; the death stub makes one synchronous attempt per candidate at the `DEAD` boundary** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*, so one antivirus scan of the newest file does not silently fall back to an older generation)* | `SL_PRECHECK_FILE_UNREADABLE` | unreadable after the retries (transient) → next candidate; file left in place |
| 2 | Size floor: size ≥ 12 + 2 + 32 (non-empty, not truncated below the minimum) | `SL_PRECHECK_FILE_TOO_SMALL` | CORRUPT → quarantine candidate; next |
| 3 | Size ≤ `MAX_SAVE_BYTES` | `SL_PRECHECK_FILE_TOO_LARGE` | CORRUPT → quarantine candidate; next |
| 4 | The 7-byte magic prefix matches | `SL_PRECHECK_BAD_MAGIC` | CORRUPT → quarantine candidate; next |
| 5 | Trailer: the last 32 bytes equal SHA-256 over `[0, size − 32)` (format law, SL-1) | `SL_PRECHECK_FILE_SHA_MISMATCH` | CORRUPT → quarantine candidate; next |
| 6 | Layout-id byte known | `SL_PRECHECK_LAYOUT_UNKNOWN` | INCOMPATIBLE → stop |
| 7 | `header_len` within bounds (`0 < header_len ≤ MAX_HEADER_BYTES`, `12 + header_len + 32 ≤ size`) | `SL_PRECHECK_HEADER_LEN_INVALID` | CORRUPT → next |
| 8 | Header parses as a JSON object | `SL_PRECHECK_HEADER_NOT_JSON` | CORRUPT → next |
| 9 | Required keys present (including `registry_epoch`, `risky_cause` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* and, in `WORLD` files, `restore_epoch` as an int below 2^32 *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review, delegated ruling R1)*; `state_hash` per its SL-1 row), types right, integers integral and below 2^53, hex exact-width | `SL_PRECHECK_HEADER_FIELD_MISSING` / `_TYPE` / `_NOT_INT` / `_HEX_MALFORMED` | CORRUPT → next |
| 10 | `format_version` supported (known, or has a registered container migration) and `codec` implemented (0 in the MVP) | `SL_PRECHECK_FORMAT_UNSUPPORTED` | INCOMPATIBLE → stop ("made by a newer build" / "incompatible development build") |
| 11 | `min_reader_version` ≤ our `FORMAT_VERSION` | `SL_PRECHECK_MIN_READER_EXCEEDED` | INCOMPATIBLE → stop |
| 12 | Header `tick_rate` equals `TICK_RATE` | `SL_PRECHECK_TICK_RATE_MISMATCH` (the kernel's own `TICK_RATE_MISMATCH` is the unreachable backstop) | INCOMPATIBLE → stop |
| 13 | Block table contiguous; Σ lengths equals the payload region; each length ≤ `MAX_BLOCK_BYTES`; count ≤ the kernel's `MAX_BLOCKS` (cited, not restated) | `SL_PRECHECK_BLOCK_TABLE_INVALID` / `_PAYLOAD_LENGTH_MISMATCH` / `_BLOCK_TOO_LARGE` / `_BLOCK_COUNT_EXCEEDS_MAX` | CORRUPT → next |
| 14 | No duplicate `block_id` | `SL_PRECHECK_DUPLICATE_BLOCK_ID` | CORRUPT → next |
| 15 | Each `payload_sha256` matches the stored bytes | `SL_PRECHECK_BLOCK_HASH_MISMATCH` (names the block) | CORRUPT → next |
| 16 | Each `block_id` registered, or on `RETIRED_BLOCKS` (stripped with a note — not a refusal) | `SL_PRECHECK_UNKNOWN_BLOCK`; note `SL_PRECHECK_RETIRED_BLOCK_STRIPPED` | INCOMPATIBLE (a newer or foreign build wrote it) → stop |
| 17 | Scope: no block whose registration is `PERCEPTION_OUT` or non-`persist`; a WORLD file holds no PROFILE-scope block and `profile.sav` holds no WORLD block | `SL_PRECHECK_PERCEPTION_BLOCK_PRESENT` / `SL_PRECHECK_SCOPE_MISMATCH` | CORRUPT (never written by us; presence = defect or tamper) → next |
| 18 | Schema chain: each stored `schema_version` ∈ the owner's `supported_versions`, and the migration chain to current is complete | `SL_PRECHECK_SCHEMA_UNSUPPORTED` (names block and stored version) | INCOMPATIBLE → stop |
| 19 | Decode and type walk: each payload decodes (objects disallowed) to the type its registration declares — a Dictionary, or the owner's published canonical byte form (the Graph's) — and the type walk passes (SL-2) | `SL_PRECHECK_BLOCK_DECODE_FAILED` / `_BLOCK_TYPE_FORBIDDEN` / `_BLOCK_DECODE_LIMITS` | CORRUPT → next |
| 20 | Clock block: `kernel.clock` decodes with exactly the keys `T` and `world_seed`; header `T` and `world_seed` equal them | `SL_PRECHECK_CLOCK_BLOCK_INVALID` / `SL_PRECHECK_HEADER_CLOCK_MISMATCH` | CORRUPT → next |
| 21 | Mandatory set present; a missing non-mandatory block only if its `added_in_format` exceeds the file's `format_version` (then `default_state()` and a note) | `SL_PRECHECK_MANDATORY_MISSING` (names the block) | CORRUPT → next when the header `registry_epoch` equals ours; **INCOMPATIBLE-dev** → stop, no quarantine, when it differs *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* |
| 22 | Seats and campaign: `seats` non-empty, unique, in range; `PER_PLAYER` keys ⊆ `seats`; `campaign_id` equals the directory's | `SL_PRECHECK_SEATS_INVALID` / `SL_PRECHECK_CAMPAIGN_MISMATCH` | CORRUPT → next |
| 23 | *(after `request_restore`, inside `LOADING`; K-4 provisional)* Reproduction compare (SL-12), counted only if `kernel.clock.T` equals the header `T` | `SL_VERIFY_NOT_REPRODUCED` / `SL_VERIFY_BASELINE_DIVERGED` / `SL_VERIFY_WINDOW_MISSED` | suspect mark; play on (release) / halt (dev); never the next candidate; `WINDOW_MISSED` records no verdict *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: `BASELINE_DIVERGED` is the REBASED-mode counterpart, F-4)* |

By name: the **integrity** steps are open/read, size floor, `MAX_SAVE_BYTES`, magic prefix, trailer, and per-block `payload_sha256`; the **compatibility** steps are layout id, `format_version`, `min_reader_version`, `tick_rate`, registered/retired and schema chain; the **structure and meaning** steps are `header_len` bounds, JSON parse, fields, block table, duplicates, scope, decode and type walk, clock block, mandatory set and seats/campaign. The **offline subset** is every step up to and including per-block `payload_sha256`: it needs no registry, and the writer re-runs it in `VERIFYING` (T-4).

**T-6. Crash recovery (what is on disk → what the recovery scan does).** Recovery runs when a campaign is opened, before the first load; it is deterministic and never deletes a file it cannot prove obsolete. Every write step is an injection point for the deterministic fault-injection test harness. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* A `.tmp` is matched by the `write_epoch` pattern `gen_<write_epoch>_<k>.tmp` (SL-10); an orphan `.tmp` from an earlier epoch is deleted unless it passes the roll-forward test of the second row. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review, delegated ruling R1)* The scan also computes `E_seen` (SL-23) from every header that parses, and two verifying files with the same `generation` (a header-only epoch rewrite caught between its rename steps leaves the `.bak` and the new file side by side) are ordered by `restore_epoch`, the higher winning; their payloads are identical. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* A kill before the queued `DEMOTE` rename (SL-8) leaves the generation un-demoted; recovery changes nothing.

| Crash point | On disk | Recovery |
|---|---|---|
| During `WRITING_TMP` | the position's file (previous generation) intact; a partial `gen_k.tmp` | Delete the `.tmp`; the ring is unchanged |
| After `VERIFYING`, before `SWAPPING_OUT` | the position's file intact; a complete `.tmp` | If the `.tmp` passes its trailer and its `generation` exceeds every generation in the ring, roll forward (run the swap); otherwise delete it |
| Between `SWAPPING_OUT` and `SWAPPING_IN` | `gen_k.bak` (the replaced oldest), a complete `.tmp`, no `gen_k.sav` | `.tmp` valid → promote it into the position and delete the `.bak`; invalid → promote the `.bak` back |
| Between `SWAPPING_IN` and `POST_VERIFY` done | `gen_k.sav` (new) and `gen_k.bak` (replaced oldest) | `gen_k.sav` verifies → delete the `.bak`; else promote the `.bak` |
| During `MANIFEST` | all generations fine; manifest stale or partial | Rebuild the manifest by scanning headers |

Candidates after recovery are every `*.sav` plus `*.bak` leftovers, deduplicated by header `generation` (preferring `.sav`); a `.tmp` is never a candidate except through the roll-forward row. The design assumes only that renaming a closed file to an *absent* name is all-or-nothing [UNV]; the ring (at least two verified generations during any swap, since `AUTOSAVE_GENERATIONS` ≥ 2) is the answer to a failed rename.

**T-7. `save_status` values.**

| Value | Entered | Exit | Consumer |
|---|---|---|---|
| `IDLE` | Boot | Any write starts | Chart Table pause panel, Debug Overlay (designed; `DIAGNOSTIC` P-STATE read); no HUD per-write consumer *(Amended 2026-10-26 — CD-GDD-ALIGN)* |
| `WRITING` | `CAPTURING` begins | `WRITTEN` or `FAILED` | Never drawn for an autosave. The pause panel may show a plain in-panel state only during the player's own Save & Quit (SL-6); diagnostics *(Amended 2026-10-26 — CD-GDD-ALIGN)* |
| `WRITTEN` | Swap and manifest complete | The next write begins (carries `written_at_T`) | Chart Table **main-menu Continue screen** (the newest record's playtime, a SIM fact read once at process start; the pause panel shows none, UI-2) and Debug Overlay; no HUD display; *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* `STATUS_WRITTEN_HOLD_TICKS` is retired *(Amended 2026-10-26 — CD-GDD-ALIGN)* |
| `FAILED` | Any write-procedure failure | The next write begins | HUD notice on the non-diegetic suit panel, drawn only while `fail_escalated` (SL-21); diagnostics |

**T-8. Death-stub run (single player).** Pre-check, decode and staging run at the `DEAD` boundary, before the kernel is touched (SL-11); `LOADING` holds only the kernel's restore and the verification window.

| Step | Tick boundary | Actor | Result |
|---|---|---|---|
