| 1 | `DEAD` entered | Controller | Notifies at the end of the tick (the kernel's notify phase) |
| 2 | Same boundary | Save/Load | *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* Settle the writer without waiting (SL-7, SL-10): a job already `WRITTEN` or `FAILED` is a candidate or not; a job still busy is **abandoned** (`SL_WRITE_ABANDONED_AT_DEATH`) and takes no part in the selection; then `write_epoch`++ and `restore_epoch` computed and its header-only rewrite queued to the worker (SL-23); `generation_select` (SL-8); pre-check (T-5) and staging from the hot RAM copy where it is the selection, otherwise from disk, with per-class fallback to the next candidate. Their main-thread cost (decode and type walk on the hot path; file I/O as well on the disk fallback) lands in this `DEAD` tick, where the Presenter holds the down sheet's last frame. No candidate passes → `SL_LOAD_NO_LOADABLE_SAVE`, the kernel is never touched (SL-7) |
| 3 | Same boundary | Save/Load | `request_restore(payload, restore_epoch)` of the staged WORLD blocks, honoured at the tick's boundary services *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review, delegated ruling R1)* |
| 4 | The tick's boundary services | Kernel | `RUNNING → LOADING`; queue flushed, each command `Dropped(RELOAD)` |
| 5 | `LOADING` | Pipeline | `TRANSIT_HOLD`: layer-1 black, fade in 120 ms; zoom presses discarded |
| 6 | `LOADING` | Kernel | Restores every block in `restore_after` order, every `restore()` before any `post_restore()`; an owner-reported failure here is `FAULTED` (SL-22) |
| 7 | End of `LOADING` | Save/Load (K-4, provisional) | `restore_reason = DEATH_STUB` published; the hand-off waits for the `EPOCH_DURABLE` report of the header-only `restore_epoch` rewrite (or `SL_RESTORE_EPOCH_NOT_DURABLE` after `RESTORE_EPOCH_WRITE_MAX_MS`, SL-23) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review, delegated ruling R1)*; the verification window (dev/test; release diagnostic-only, SL-12); the kernel enters `RUNNING` |
| 8 | First `RUNNING` frame (`LOADING → RUNNING`) | Pipeline | Camera `CUT` at the pre-load `k_z`; the first frame already passes through the Perception Filter for the restored sanity (H4, routed, OQ-11); the HUD shows the one ship-log line of UI-11 (`restore_reason = DEATH_STUB`) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* |

**T-9. Campaign directory states.**

| State | Entry | Exit | Transitions | Who may cause them |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `EMPTY` | `new_game` created the directory | The initial generation is `WRITTEN`; or world construction / baseline write fails | → `ACTIVE`; → removed (`SL_NEW_GAME_NO_BASELINE`) | `new_game` |
| `ACTIVE` | At least one verified generation | A newer-format header is observed; the user retires or deletes it | → `READ_ONLY`; → `RETIRED`; → deleted | Load pipeline; the user (hold-to-confirm) |
| `READ_ONLY` | An older build found `format_version` above its own in the newest header | A newer build opens it | The older build never writes into it | Load pipeline (SL-11) |
| `RETIRED` | The user started a new campaign over this one | The user deletes it | Renamed out of the active location, files intact | Chart Table (provisional) |

### Interactions with Other Systems

**What Save/Load exposes and consumes, in names fixed elsewhere.** It registers **no hooks, no mutators, no commands (the VS-B `saveload.request_save` is reserved and provisional), no blocks and no dirty keys** (SL-17), and it never writes a block (H3, H4). It calls the kernel's host-local control plane — `request_snapshot`, `request_restore`, `request_shutdown` (CR-15); `request_pause` / `request_resume` are the pause panel's, not its own. It reads `kernel.clock`, `controller.state.loco_state` per bound seat, the Graph's `failures()` read view and an optional Attention SIM view (SL-13). It publishes the host-local reads `save_status`, `SaveInfo` and `restore_reason` and the API of SL-21. All three rules for every consumer in the kernel's Interactions section hold: a system that wants state to persist registers a block and never talks to Save/Load directly. **Every interface with an undesigned or in-flight system below is provisional — it lists only what that system must supply or may rely on, never a guess of its internals.**

#### Upstream and contract-level systems (designed)

Each row quotes the dependency GDD's own Interactions row so the two documents can be checked against each other (bidirectional consistency).

| System | Data in | Data out | Interface owner | Names fixed by the other GDD |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **Command & Simulation Layer** (hard; designed) | `kernel.clock` (`T`, `world_seed`); per-block snapshots and versions at capture; `state_hash(PERSIST, T)` for the header and verification (D.12, cited); capture time against `SNAPSHOT_BUDGET_MS` (cited); `MAX_BLOCKS` (cited) | Control-plane calls only; WORLD blocks (PROFILE on a cold open) to restore; the registration expectations `supported_versions` and `added_in_format` | Kernel: mechanism (snapshot, hash, restore order, `FAULTED`) · Save/Load: file, header, ring, pre-check, container migration, orchestration of owner migrations | CR-8 registry fields, `restore_after`, `post_restore`, CR-15, CR-18; the kernel's edge cases for missing block (`default_state()`), unknown block (`RESTORE_UNKNOWN_BLOCK`), version mismatch, `TICK_RATE_MISMATCH`, restore during `SAVING`; "in `FAULTED` the kernel never writes or autosaves over an existing save" |
| **Life-Support Graph** (block owner; designed) | `graph.state` snapshot: `SIM`, persist `WORLD`, `scope` `SHARED`, `repl_hint` `SHARED_SUMMARY`, `schema_version` 1, `restore_after: [layout.state]`; zero floats; packed arrays; `canonical_bytes()` (LS-21) | `restore(data, stored_version)` (supports exactly version 1 in the MVP), `post_restore()` | Graph: shape, two-phase restore, the closed `GRAPH_RESTORE_*` reasons (BAD_VERSION, BAD_TAG, BAD_LENGTH, BAD_ENUM, OUT_OF_RANGE, ID_ORDER, COUNTER, BAD_EDGE_ENDPOINT, SEAM_MISMATCH, UNKNOWN_DEF, UNKNOWN_ROOM, INVARIANT) · Save/Load: S-1, the golden-save convention, the harness | S-1; Save/Load never reorders `restore_after` (the kernel's topological order does); Graph cost: snapshot ≤ 0.5 ms and ≤ 32 KB, restore + `post_restore` ≤ 2 ms (cited from the Graph's performance criterion); the soft-gate read: `read_view.failures()` descriptor fields `cause`, `sev`, `warn_elapsed` (LS-14, LS-19) and the constants `W_MIN_STEPS` and `GRAPH_STEP_TICKS` (D.11; cited, never restated) *(Amended 2026-10-26 — CD-GDD-ALIGN)* |

The kernel's own Interactions rows for the first five (rows 4, 5, 8, 10, 13) do not mention Save/Load except through the registry; they are consistent with the table above. **Edge type.** The index lists Save/Load & Versioned Format as a dependency of Suit & Equipment, Modular Ship Assembly, Web Map & Transit, Attention and Sanity (index rows I:82, I:85, I:88, I:89, I:91). One position: each such edge is a **design-time dependency on this GDD's contracts** — the block authoring contract (SL-15), the PROFILE/WORLD scope rule (SL-9), the death-stub semantics (SL-7) and the event/notification contract (SL-4) — and **not a runtime call**: none of them ever calls Save/Load (the consumer rule), which is the sense of the kernel's note that a Graph → Save/Load edge is redundant. The Graph and the Controller were designed before this GDD, so a design-time edge from them is moot and the Graph's S-1 obligation is carried here (SL-2) instead; for the systems not yet designed, the edge is real and is labelled **"design-time (contract)"**. Routed to /map-systems as a label clarification plus a uniformity note — either every undesigned block owner carries the labelled edge or the kernel's registry edge is treated as transitive, the index's call (OQ-15). The ruling-stage suggestion that the index rows for Web Map, Attention and Sanity are wrong is withdrawn: those systems' blocks are written to SL-15, so the edge is correct and only its label was ambiguous.

## Formulas

All sim-time quantities are **integer ticks** read from `kernel.clock.T` and converted to seconds only in examples, at `TICK_RATE` 60 (registry, cited). Anything hashed or stored in a header is an integer or an exact-width hex string (S-1); a millisecond budget below is a float used only for logging thresholds, is never hashed or replicated, and so is outside the kernel's determinism rule. **Cited, never restated:** `state_hash` (kernel D.12, registry), `rng_derive` (kernel D.10, registry), `snapshot_bytes` and `capture_time_ms` (kernel D.11), `SNAPSHOT_BUDGET_MS` and `MAX_BLOCKS` (kernel GDD values, not registry entries — kernel registry candidates), `DOWN_TO_DEATH_TICKS` (registry, Controller), `W_MIN_WARNING_S` (registry, Graph). Owned constants are named in Tuning Knobs. Ten formulas follow; the first six are the registry candidates of the rulings (R29, with `generation_select` and `save_verification` amended at the stage-5 checkpoint), the last four are derived bounds and encodings the criteria need.

### F-1. `autosave_due`

The `autosave_due` formula is defined as:

`elapsed = T − last_save_T; interval_due = elapsed ≥ AUTOSAVE_INTERVAL_TICKS; event_due = (pending_events ≠ ∅) AND elapsed ≥ AUTOSAVE_MIN_GAP_TICKS AND NOT (ragged_breach_open() AND ASSEMBLY_CUT ∈ kinds(pending_events)); due = interval_due OR event_due; kind = EVENT if event_due else AUTO` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `T` | int | 0 … 2^63 − 1 | `kernel.clock` (read at the tick boundary) | Current sim tick |
| `last_save_T` | int | 0 … `T` | Save/Load session memory: the capture `T` of the newest durable generation; reset to the restored generation's `T` on every restore | Basis of the loss window; unchanged by a failed write, so a failed save stays due |
| `event_pending` | bool | {0, 1} | Owner notifications and pure reads (SL-4): `POD_ARRIVED` (`web_events`), `last_shape_change_T()` (Assembly), Interaction's `last_salvage_T()` (BS-21) — `POD_ARRIVED` provisional until merge; coalesced; cleared when a capture at or after the event's tick is `WRITTEN` | At least one autosave-worthy event (pod-hop arrival, weld or cut completion, dock, section extraction) since the last write. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* `event_pending` is the predicate `pending_events ≠ ∅`, where `pending_events` is a **set of `(kind, tick)` entries**, not one coalesced kind: a later event adds an entry and never overwrites an earlier one, and an entry leaves the set only when a capture at or after its tick is `WRITTEN` |
| `event_kind`, `ragged_breach_open()` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* | enum, bool | {`ASSEMBLY_WELD`, `ASSEMBLY_CUT`, …}; {0, 1} | Assembly pure reads (`last_shape_change_T()` names the kind, MSA-33; `ragged_breach_open()` is true while a RAGGED cut still has an uncapped stub; registered, source Modular Ship Assembly) | The ruled withhold (D54): a RAGGED cut's `ASSEMBLY_CUT` is not due while the breach is open; `event_pending` stays latched and the event is taken once it is capped. `ASSEMBLY_WELD` and a SEALED cut (no stubs, so the read is false) are never withheld by their own entry. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* While a RAGGED cut's entry is pending and the breach is open, **no entry of any kind** fires a capture, so a cut followed by a weld cannot capture inside the open breach (the weld entry waits with the cut); event order no longer matters |
| `AUTOSAVE_INTERVAL_TICKS` ★ | int | 5 400 … 18 000, default 10 800 *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* | Tuning Knobs | Interval trigger (3 min) |
| `AUTOSAVE_MIN_GAP_TICKS` ★ | int | 900 … 3 600, default 1 800 *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* | Tuning Knobs | Floor between any two `AUTO`/`EVENT` generations; strictly below the interval |
| `elapsed` | int | 0 … unbounded | calculated | Sim ticks since the last durable capture |
| `due`, `kind` | bool, enum | {0, 1}; {`AUTO`, `EVENT`} | calculated | Input to F-2; `kind` is stored in the header and `SaveInfo` (an event and the interval due on the same tick produce **one** save, kind `EVENT`) |

**Output Range:** boolean `due` plus `kind`. `T < last_save_T` cannot occur after the reset-on-restore rule; if observed, `elapsed := 0`, `last_save_T := T` and `SL_AUTOSAVE_CLOCK_REGRESSION` is logged once. No interval save is ever due while the kernel is `PAUSED` (`T` cannot advance). The `QUIT` kind is a separate path (SL-6, F-2) and never passes through `due`.

**Example:** `last_save_T = 432 000` (the anchor moment, Player Fantasy). A weld completes at `T = 437 400`: `elapsed = 5 400 ≥ 1 800`, so `event_due`, `due = 1`, `kind = EVENT`. The next interval save is then due at `437 400 + 10 800 = 448 200`. Latch case: a second event at `T = 438 700` has `elapsed = 1 300 < 1 800`, so `event_pending` stays set and `due` becomes true at `T = 439 200`. **Withhold case** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*: a RAGGED cut applies at `T = 440 000` with `last_save_T = 437 400` (the anchor weld's save) and two uncapped stubs: `elapsed = 2 600 ≥ 1 800` but `ragged_breach_open()` is true, so `event_due = 0` and `event_pending` stays latched; the last stub is capped at `T = 441 800` (`elapsed = 4 400`), `event_due = 1`, and the capture fires on the next retry-grid tick. If the interval falls due at 448 200 with the breach still open, `assembly_breach_block` defers it (F-2) and it fires no later than 455 400, tagged `RISKY` with `risky_cause = {ASSEMBLY_BREACH}`; a SEALED cut at the same `T` has no stubs and is due at once. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* **Cut then weld:** with the RAGGED cut of `T = 440 000` pending and two stubs open, a weld completes at `T = 440 900`; `pending_events = {(ASSEMBLY_CUT, 440 000), (ASSEMBLY_WELD, 440 900)}`, `ragged_breach_open()` is true, so `event_due = 0` at every grid tick until the last stub is capped at `T = 441 800`, then one capture fires and clears both entries (AC-SL-146).

### F-2. `autosave_gate`

The `autosave_gate` formula is defined as:

`refractory_block = (last_recover_T ≠ none) AND (T − last_recover_T) < W_MIN_WARNING_S × TICK_RATE; hard_block = (EXISTS seat IN bound_seats: loco_state(seat) IN {DOWNED, DEAD}) OR kernel_state NOT IN {RUNNING, PAUSED} OR writer_busy OR restore_pending OR refractory_block; graph_tell_block = EXISTS d IN graph.failures(): d.subject_kind IN {NODE, EDGE, SEAM} AND d.cause == ENDOGENOUS AND d.sev ≥ 1 AND d.warn_elapsed < W_MIN_STEPS; attention_tell_block = tell_active AND (gate_tell_mode == STRICT OR tell_remaining < W_MIN_WARNING_S × TICK_RATE); tell_block = graph_tell_block OR attention_tell_block; vitals_block = EXISTS seat IN bound_seats: vitals_state(seat) ≥ CRITICAL; assembly_breach_block = ragged_breach_open(); soft_block = tell_block OR breach_phase ≠ CALM OR (EXISTS seat: loco_state(seat) IN GATE_SOFT_STATES) OR vitals_block OR assembly_breach_block; waited = T − due_first_T; next_retry_T = (fail_n == 0 ? 0 : last_fail_T + min(AUTOSAVE_RETRY_TICKS × 2^min(fail_n − 1, 16), AUTOSAVE_FAIL_BACKOFF_MAX_TICKS)); fire = due AND waited mod AUTOSAVE_RETRY_TICKS == 0 AND T ≥ next_retry_T AND NOT hard_block AND (NOT soft_block OR waited ≥ AUTOSAVE_MAX_DEFER_TICKS); risky = fire AND soft_block; risky_cause = the members of {GRAPH_TELL, ATTENTION_TELL, BREACH_PHASE, SEAT_STATE, VITALS, ASSEMBLY_BREACH} whose input is true at the fire tick (empty iff NOT risky); quit_write = kernel_state IN {RUNNING, PAUSED} AND NOT (EXISTS seat: loco_state(seat) IN {DOWNED, DEAD}); quit_risky = quit_write AND soft_block` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `loco_state(seat)` | enum | the nine Controller states (`IDLE`, `WALK`, `RUN`, `CARRY_WALK`, `CHANNELLING`, `STAGGERED`, `DOWNED`, `DEAD`, `IN_POD`) | `controller.state` read per bound seat (SL-13) | `DOWNED` and `DEAD` are **hard** and never in `GATE_SOFT_STATES` |
| `vitals_state(seat)` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* | enum | {OK, STRAINED, CRITICAL, FAILED} | Suit & Equipment `view(player_id).vitals_state` (SE-33, designed; the worst of the breath, warmth and integrity rungs) | `vitals_block` is true at CRITICAL or worse (Suit's cap follows it: CRITICAL is WALK_ONLY, FAILED is NONE). It is capped like every soft input and never opens a hard block |
| `last_recover_T` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* | int tick or none | 0 … `T` | Save/Load session memory: the tick of the latest `DOWNED` → non-`DOWNED` transition of any bound seat, observed through `loco_state` (SL-13); none at boot and after every restore | Anchor of the refractory: `refractory_block` is true for `W_MIN_WARNING_S × TICK_RATE` ticks after it (600 at the defaults). A hard-type block that expires by itself, never shortened by the cap; it does not bind `quit_write` |
| `ragged_breach_open()` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* | bool | {0, 1} | Modular Ship Assembly pure read (MSA-33; registered, source Modular Ship Assembly) | True while a RAGGED cut still has an uncapped stub; its descriptors are exogenous, so `graph_tell_block` never sees them. An absent read counts as no breach (not blocking), logged once per process as `GATE_INPUT_ABSENT` |
| `kernel_state` | enum | the seven kernel states | kernel (read) | Capture is valid only in `RUNNING` or `PAUSED` |
| `writer_busy`, `restore_pending` | bool | {0, 1} | Save/Load host-local state | One write in flight at a time; never capture into a world about to be replaced. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* An abandoned job (SL-10) does not count toward `writer_busy` |
| `graph.failures()` descriptors `d` (`subject_kind`, `cause`, `sev`, `warn_elapsed`) | enum, enum, 0 … 4, int steps | `subject_kind` {NODE 1, EDGE 2, COMPARTMENT 3, SEAM 4, OPENING 5}; `cause` {ENDOGENOUS, EXOGENOUS}; `sev` 0 … 4; `warn_elapsed` ≥ 0 | Life-Support Graph `read_view.failures()` (LS-19, LS-14; designed and locked) | *(Amended 2026-10-26 — CD-GDD-ALIGN)* The Graph's published warning state. Only loaded NODE, EDGE and SEAM descriptors (COMPARTMENT and OPENING descriptors carry no `warn_since_step`, so `warn_elapsed` is 0 and counting them would defer on every cold or thin room), endogenous, with `sev` ≥ 1 and a closed fairness gate, count; WORN counts; exogenous causes bypass the Graph's gate and owe no Graph-side warning. The Graph's own window, never restated here |
| `W_MIN_STEPS`, `GRAPH_STEP_TICKS` | int steps, int ticks | 100 at the defaults (`W_MIN_WARNING_S` × `TICK_RATE` / `GRAPH_STEP_TICKS`); 6 | Graph D.11 (cited) | `d.warn_elapsed < W_MIN_STEPS` is "the gate is closed"; `graph_tell_remaining = MIN over the counted d of (W_MIN_STEPS − d.warn_elapsed) × GRAPH_STEP_TICKS`, 0 … `W_MIN_WARNING_S × TICK_RATE` ticks, is a diagnostic and test output and does not enter `fire` |
| `tell_active`, `tell_remaining` | bool, int ticks | {0, 1}; 0 … `TELL_MAX_TICKS` (1 800, Attention's, cited) | Attention SIM read view `attention.view` (designed) | Remaining warning of the soonest active **Attention** tell; an absent view counts as open and logs `GATE_INPUT_ABSENT` once per process (the Graph half is unaffected) |
| `gate_tell_mode` | enum | {`FAIR`, `STRICT`} | Tuning Knobs | Governs the **Attention half only**: `FAIR` defers only while the remaining warning is below the Graph's guarantee; `STRICT` defers for the whole tell. For the Graph half the two collapse into one behaviour, defer for the whole open window, because the window is the guarantee |
| `W_MIN_WARNING_S` | int s | 10 (registry, range 4 … 20) | Graph (cited) | The fairness minimum; `W_MIN_WARNING_S × TICK_RATE` = 600 ticks at the defaults |
| `breach_phase` | enum | {`CALM`, `BREACH`, `SUBSIDING`} (a TELL reads `CALM`) | `attention.view` (designed) | Only `CALM` opens the soft gate |
| `GATE_SOFT_STATES` | set | subset of {`CHANNELLING`, `STAGGERED`, `IN_POD`} | Tuning Knobs | Restores drop channel progress and turn `STAGGERED` into `IDLE` (PC-17); a mid-transit state is Web Map's to restore |
| `due_first_T` | int | 0 … `T` | session memory: the first tick `due` was true since the last fire | Anchor of the deferral clock and the retry grid; **not** reset by gate flicker. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* Nor is it reset by a `FAILED` write: **only a `WRITTEN` capture or a restore clears it**, so the loss bound counts from the first moment the save was due, and a failed write retries on the grid under `next_retry_T` without restarting the deferral clock (the two readings gave different loss bounds) |
| `AUTOSAVE_RETRY_TICKS` ★, `AUTOSAVE_MAX_DEFER_TICKS` ★ | int | 60 … 900 and ≤ `AUTOSAVE_MAX_DEFER_TICKS` / 4, default 300; `4 × AUTOSAVE_RETRY_TICKS` … (`AUTOSAVE_INTERVAL_TICKS` − `AUTOSAVE_RETRY_TICKS`), static range 1 800 … 17 700, default 7 200 *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* | Tuning Knobs | Re-evaluation period and the cap on **soft** deferral; `AUTOSAVE_MAX_DEFER_TICKS mod AUTOSAVE_RETRY_TICKS = 0` so the cap lands on the retry grid |
| `fail_n`, `last_fail_T`, `next_retry_T` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* | int, int ticks, int ticks | 0 … unbounded; 0 … `T`; 0 … `T` + `AUTOSAVE_FAIL_BACKOFF_MAX_TICKS` | Save/Load session memory: consecutive failed writes (reset to 0 by a `WRITTEN`), the tick of the last failure, and the earliest tick the next attempt may fire | The backoff of OQ-26 inside the formula; `AUTOSAVE_FAIL_BACKOFF_MAX_TICKS mod AUTOSAVE_RETRY_TICKS = 0` (I12) keeps `next_retry_T` on the retry grid. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* The exponent is clamped at 16 (`2^min(fail_n − 1, 16)`), because an unclamped `2^(fail_n − 1)` overflows an integer after about 55 consecutive failures, which would cancel the backoff and make every grid tick retry (AC-SL-146) |
| `fire`, `risky`, `risky_cause`, `quit_write`, `quit_risky` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* | bool; set | {0, 1}; the closed set of SL-1 | calculated | `fire` requests `request_snapshot` at this boundary; `risky` is stored in the header; `quit_write = 0` makes `save_and_quit` return `SL_QUIT_NO_WRITE_DOWNED` (SL-6) |

**Output Range:** booleans, plus the `risky_cause` set. A seat-state **hard** condition is never overridden by the cap or by a quit: a `DOWNED` or `DEAD` world is never written, so no generation file ever decodes to such a seat (the refractory is never shortened by the cap, and a quit is not bound by it) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*. A seat-state hard block never expires (a revived crew reopens the gate once the refractory has run out, 600 ticks later); only soft conditions are bounded, by `AUTOSAVE_MAX_DEFER_TICKS`, and the refractory bounds itself. An interval save is never taken while an endogenous Graph warning window is open and the cap has not elapsed, so no unforced generation holds a Graph tell with fewer than `W_MIN_WARNING_S` of it elapsed *(Amended 2026-10-26 — CD-GDD-ALIGN)*. A due save is postponed at most `AUTOSAVE_MAX_DEFER_TICKS` beyond `due_first_T` unless a hard condition holds. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* Since the user ruling (X7, Resolved 2026-10-27 — user ruling (design-review decisions): OQ-40 and OQ-41 (b)) the formula carries the critical-suit input (`vitals_block`, also in `quit_risky`), Assembly's open-breach input (`assembly_breach_block`) and the post-`DOWNED` refractory (`refractory_block`, a hard-type block that expires after 600 ticks and that the cap never overrides); the fairness step-back is in F-3. After a failed write the next attempt also waits for `next_retry_T`; the cap bounds only the soft deferral, not the failure backoff.

**Example (`FAIR`):** `last_save_T = 0`, `due_first_T = 10 800`. A tell is active with 700 ticks remaining: `700 ≥ 600`, so `tell_block = 0`; the crew is `WALK`, `breach_phase = CALM`; `fire` at `T = 10 800`, kind `AUTO`, not risky. The same state under `STRICT` is blocked at 10 800, 11 100 and 11 400 and fires at 11 700 when the tell ends. **Graph example** *(Amended 2026-10-26 — CD-GDD-ALIGN)*: `W_MIN_STEPS` = 10 × 60 / 6 = 100. A cable descriptor (`subject_kind` EDGE, `cause` ENDOGENOUS, `sev` 1) has `warn_elapsed` = 47 when the interval save falls due at `T = 10 800`: `graph_tell_block = 1`, `graph_tell_remaining = (100 − 47) × 6 = 318` ticks, no fire. At the next retry tick `T = 11 100` the 300 ticks are 50 steps, so `warn_elapsed` = 97 and it is still blocked; at `T = 11 400` it is 147, the window has closed, and the save fires, kind `AUTO`, not risky. A restore from a save taken at 11 400 therefore never lands inside the first 10 s of that tell. **Cap example:** a soft block from 10 800 onward fires at `T = 18 000` (`waited = 7 200`) with `risky = 1`. **Hard example:** the crew goes `DOWNED` at 17 900: at `T = 18 000` the cap has elapsed but `hard_block = 1`, so nothing is written; `DEAD` follows at 19 700 and the stub restores the generation from `last_save_T = 0`. **Backoff example** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*: a write fails at `T = 18 000` (`fail_n` 1): `next_retry_T = 18 300`; it fails again at 18 300 (`fail_n` 2): `next_retry_T = 18 900`; the sixth consecutive failure waits 4 800 ticks; a `WRITTEN` resets `fail_n` to 0. **Vitals and refractory examples** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*: the suit is `CRITICAL` from `T = 10 000`; the interval save falls due at 10 800 with `due_first_T = 10 800`: `vitals_block = 1`, no fire at 10 800, 11 100 and so on, and the cap fires it at `T = 18 000` with `risky = 1`, `risky_cause = {VITALS}`. A seat gets up from `DOWNED` at `T = 17 700`: `last_recover_T = 17 700` and `refractory_block = 1` until 18 300; at the cap tick 18 000 `hard_block = 1` and nothing is written; at the next grid tick 18 300 `T − last_recover_T = 600`, no longer `< 600`, and the save fires (`risky = 1` if a soft input is still true).

### F-3. `generation_select`

The `generation_select` formula is defined as:

`C = [g_0, g_1, …] (non-demoted candidates that pass the pre-check, by header generation descending); window_hit = g_r.risky AND (g_r.save_kind ≠ QUIT OR VITALS ∈ g_r.risky_cause) AND (T_downed − g_r.T) ≤ DEATH_LOOP_WINDOW_TICKS; fair_hit = (T_downed − g_r.T) < W_MIN_WARNING_S × TICK_RATE AND (g_r.save_kind ≠ QUIT OR VITALS ∈ g_r.risky_cause); loop_hit = g_r ≠ none AND g_r == g_0 AND (window_hit OR fair_hit); selected = g_1 if (loop_hit AND |C| ≥ 2) else g_0; demote = g_r if (loop_hit AND |C| ≥ 2) else none` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `C` | list | 0 … `AUTOSAVE_GENERATIONS` (plus transient `.bak` leftovers) | header scan after the pre-check (SL-11) | Candidates; a second pass over demoted generations (newest first) runs only if `C` is empty |
| `g_r` | generation or none | — | session memory: the generation the session was last restored from; none on a campaign with no restore yet | The last restored generation |
| `g.risky`, `g.T`, `g.save_kind` | bool, int, enum | {0, 1}; 0 … 2^63 − 1; {`AUTO`, `EVENT`, `QUIT`} | header | `RISKY` tag from F-2; capture tick (a restore returns `T` to `g.T`); the kind, so a deliberate `QUIT` generation is never stepped back past by an unrelated death *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* |
| `T_downed` | int | `g_r.T` … `T_dead` | start tick of the `DOWNED` episode that ended in `DEAD`, observed through the `loco_state` read (SL-13) | Bounds the player's *acting* time after the restore |
| `DEATH_LOOP_WINDOW_TICKS` ★ | int | 1 800 … 10 800, default 3 600 | Tuning Knobs | The window of the RISKY route *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* |
| `g.risky_cause`, `fair_hit` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* | string[], bool | the closed set of SL-1; {0, 1} | header; calculated | `risky_cause` keeps a `QUIT` generation's RISKY-window step-back only when it contains `VITALS`. `fair_hit` is the fairness route (user ruling X7 (b)): a fall less than `W_MIN_WARNING_S × TICK_RATE` ticks (600 at the defaults, strict `<`) after the restored `T`, **independent of `risky`**, and of the kind except that a `QUIT` generation without `VITALS` in its `risky_cause` is exempt *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review, delegated ruling G2)*; it never exceeds the RISKY window (invariant I13) |
| `g_r == g_0` | bool | {0, 1} | calculated | The loop test: if a newer non-demoted verified generation exists, the gate opened after the restore and the player got out; there is no loop |
| `selected`, `demote` | generation or none | `C` or none | calculated | `selected = none` only if `C` is empty and the second pass is empty: `SL_LOAD_NO_LOADABLE_SAVE` |

**Output Range:** `g_0`, `g_1` or none. The result is always the newest non-demoted generation that passes the pre-check, except when `loop_hit` forces `g_1`. A load makes at most `LOAD_MAX_ATTEMPTS` (default `AUTOSAVE_GENERATIONS`) pre-check attempts. If `loop_hit` and `|C| = 1`, the only candidate is restored anyway and `SL_STEPBACK_NONE_AVAILABLE` is logged. A reproduction mismatch (SL-12) is never an input to this formula.

**Example:** ring G7 (`T = 40 000`, `RISKY`), G6 (`T = 29 000`), G5 (`T = 18 000`); the stub restores G7, so `g_r = G7`. The crew is `DOWNED` at `T = 41 200` and `DEAD` at 43 000: `T_downed − g_r.T = 1 200 ≤ 3 600`, `g_r == g_0`, so `loop_hit`; G6 is selected and G7 is renamed `gen_k.demoted`. A second death before any new autosave selects G6 again, never G7 (it is demoted). Variants: `T_downed = 44 000` gives `4 000 > 3 600`, no loop, G7 is restored again; G7 untagged and `T_downed − g_r.T` = 599 is a fairness-route loop (G6 selected, G7 demoted) and at 600 or more G7 is restored every time (the user rule); G7 `RISKY` of kind `QUIT` with `risky_cause` not containing `VITALS` gives no RISKY-window loop at 1 200 and steps back at 599 by the fairness route, and with `VITALS` in its `risky_cause` it steps back at 1 200 exactly like an `AUTO` generation *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*.

### F-4. `save_verification`

The `save_verification` formula is defined as:

`file_ok = SHA256(bytes[0 : size − 32]) == bytes[size − 32 : size] AND all(payload_sha256 match) AND structure_ok; causes = {MIGRATED, RETIRED_STRIPPED, DEFAULT_FILLED, ALGO_UNSUPPORTED} ∩ observed ∪ {REBASE_BLOCK if REBASE_BLOCKS ∩ blocks_in_file ≠ ∅}; mode = EXACT if causes = ∅ else REBASED; window_ok = (kernel.clock.T == header.T) at compare time; baseline = the REBASED value recorded for (generation, build_id, registry_epoch) in campaign.json, or none; recomputed = state_hash(PERSIST, T); verdict = NONE if NOT window_ok OR (mode == EXACT AND header.state_hash absent), else (if mode == REBASED: REBASED_RECORDED if baseline == none, BASELINE_MATCH if recomputed == baseline, else BASELINE_DIVERGED), else MATCH if recomputed == header.state_hash, else NOT_REPRODUCED` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `bytes`, `size` | bytes, int | 46 … `MAX_SAVE_BYTES` | the candidate file | The whole generation; the last 32 bytes are the trailer (SL-1 format law) |
| `payload_sha256` | 64 hex chars | exact width | header block table | Per-block transport hash, Save/Load's own, native [UNV] |
| `structure_ok` | bool | {0, 1} | pre-check steps (T-5) | Block table contiguous, lengths sum to the payload region |
| `causes` | set | the closed set of five | load pipeline | The five REBASED causes of SL-12: migration ran, retired block stripped, `default_state()` filled, `hash_algo` unsupported, a `REBASE_BLOCKS` member present |
| `REBASE_BLOCKS` ★ | list of `block_id` | default `[interaction.state, assembly.state]` (the Controller normalises in `snapshot()`, PC-17, and `body.state` is restore-idempotent, BDY-31, so neither is listed; the list becomes `[]` when the Interaction and Assembly owners move their clears into `snapshot()`, OQ-39) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* | Tuning Knobs | Interim, known kernel-contract debt (OQ-9, OQ-39); must be empty at the first outside build |
| `state_hash(PERSIST, T)` | u64 | 0 … 2^64 − 1 | kernel D.12 (cited, never restated), recomputed over the restored WORLD blocks | The reproduction primitive; `hash_algo` names the 64-bit hash |
| `header.T`, `header.state_hash` | hex u64 | 16 lowercase hex chars | header (S-1) | Capture tick and capture-time hash |
| `verdict` | enum | {`NONE`, `REBASED_RECORDED`, `BASELINE_MATCH`, `BASELINE_DIVERGED`, `MATCH`, `NOT_REPRODUCED`} | calculated | `NONE` logs `SL_VERIFY_WINDOW_MISSED` and records nothing; `REBASED_RECORDED` stores the recomputed value as the baseline in `campaign.json` only and logs `SL_VERIFY_REBASED{cause}`; `NOT_REPRODUCED` logs `SL_VERIFY_NOT_REPRODUCED{blocks}`; `BASELINE_MATCH` / `BASELINE_DIVERGED` (REBASED mode, a later restore of the same generation) compare against the recorded baseline, and `BASELINE_DIVERGED` logs `SL_VERIFY_BASELINE_DIVERGED` and is handled exactly like `NOT_REPRODUCED` (suspect in release, halt in dev and test, never another candidate) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* |

**Output Range:** `file_ok` boolean; `verdict` one of six values. `file_ok = 0` is decided before the kernel is touched and sends the loader to the next candidate (class CORRUPT). `NOT_REPRODUCED` never selects another candidate: release marks the generation `suspect` and plays on, dev and test halt on the first occurrence. Undetected accidental damage: `2^−256` at the file stage and `2^−64` at the reproduction stage (the width of D.12's hash). In `REBASED` mode the compare cannot prove equality with the capture; it proves only that a second restore of the same generation lands on the same state (the baseline), and protection is the file stage plus each owner's closed `<BLOCK>_RESTORE_*` reasons. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*

**Example:** a 90 020-byte file (F-6) with `REBASE_BLOCKS` empty, no migration: the trailer equals SHA-256 of the first 89 988 bytes (`file_ok`), `mode = EXACT`, `kernel.clock.T = header.T = 437 400`, the recomputed hash `0x9f1c2b770a54e3d1` equals the header's (illustrative): `MATCH`. The same file with the default `REBASE_BLOCKS` holds `interaction.state`, so `causes = {REBASE_BLOCK}`: the first load is `REBASED_RECORDED`, a later restore of the same generation is `BASELINE_MATCH` (a different recomputed value would be `BASELINE_DIVERGED`), no failure *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*. A build whose Graph migrates v1 to v2: `causes = {MIGRATED}`, `REBASED_RECORDED`, and the loader does not walk the ring. A flipped bit in the Graph payload: `file_ok = 0` at the `payload_sha256` step, the kernel is never touched, the next candidate is tried.

### F-5. `write_budget`

The `write_budget` formula is defined as:

`main_added_ms = t_file_encode + t_handoff; main_hitch_ms = capture_time_ms + main_added_ms; worker_ms = t_sha + t_write + t_flush + t_reread + t_resha + t_swap + t_manifest; durable_ms = main_hitch_ms + worker_ms; checks: main_hitch_ms ≤ SNAPSHOT_BUDGET_MS, t_handoff ≤ WRITE_HANDOFF_MAX_FRACTION × SNAPSHOT_BUDGET_MS, t_file_encode ≤ SAVE_ENCODE_BUDGET_MS, durable_ms ≤ WRITE_DURABLE_ALARM_MS, worker_ms ≤ DOWN_TO_DEATH_TICKS × 1000 / TICK_RATE (logging only); a job past WRITE_JOB_TIMEOUT_MS is abandoned (SL-10)` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `capture_time_ms` | float ms | within `SNAPSHOT_BUDGET_MS` (kernel GDD value 16.7, logging threshold only) | kernel D.11 (cited; Save/Load neither restates nor adds to it) | Σ `t_snapshot(b)` + `t_hash` inside `SAVING`; with the stored `state_hash`, the hash is a release cost on every capture (OQ-17). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* `t_hash` is a main-thread cost: ≈ 1.5 ms [UNV, spike AC-SL-89] for the ratified native H64 (truncated SHA-256); a scripted xxHash64 or FNV-1a loop would be 15 … 150 ms [UNV] and is not used; a release build skips the capture-time hash until K-4 lands (SL-16), so there `t_hash` = 0 *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings, consistency follow-up)* |
| `t_file_encode`, `t_handoff` | float ms | 0 … ~10 [UNV]; 0 … ~2 | Save/Load, main thread, tick halted | Per-block `var_to_bytes` of the captured plain data only; moving the blocks to the worker. The SHA work, header and trailer are worker work (T-4); `t_file_encode` is Save/Load's term and is not the kernel's `t_encode(b)` (D.11, OQ-53) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* |
| `t_sha`, `t_resha` | float ms | ~0.3 … 2 at 60–300 KiB [UNV] | worker, native hashing | Per-block `payload_sha256`, header assembly and the trailer SHA-256 at write (moved off the main thread, *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*), and the verify re-read |
| `t_write`, `t_flush`, `t_reread` | float ms | 2 … 100 (AV and indexers add 50 … 500) [UNV] | worker | Disk dependent |
| `t_swap`, `t_manifest` | float ms | 1 … 20 [UNV] | worker | The two renames of the swap, `POST_VERIFY`, and the manifest rewrite |
| `WRITE_HANDOFF_MAX_FRACTION` | float | 0.01 … 0.2, default 0.05 | Tuning Knobs | Share of `SNAPSHOT_BUDGET_MS` the hand-off may take |
| `SAVE_ENCODE_BUDGET_MS`, `WRITE_DURABLE_ALARM_MS` | float ms | 2 … 16, default 4; 200 … 2 000, default 500 | Tuning Knobs | Logging thresholds, never truncating |
| `DOWN_TO_DEATH_TICKS` | int | 1 800 (registry, cited) | Controller | A write that settles inside the `DOWNED` window cannot race a death on the timer path; after a solo `give_up` the window is only `GIVE_UP_MIN_TICKS`, and a job still busy then is abandoned (SL-7) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* |

**Output Range:** `main_hitch_ms`, the main-thread sum, is the quantity the player can feel as a stutter, and a stutter that appears only on writes is itself a Pillar 3 leak: it tells the player when the game saved and when the gate deferred, the signal UI-1 was retired for. The check `main_hitch_ms ≤ SNAPSHOT_BUDGET_MS` therefore gates the hash choice (SL-16), and `main_added_ms` adds to — is not part of — the kernel's capture *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*. `worker_ms` is bounded in effect, not in principle: a job that has not settled `DEATH_STUB_WRITE_WAIT_MS` after `DOWNED` entry, or is still busy at `DEAD`, is abandoned (SL-10), so stuck I/O costs a stale file and not a stuck game. The 30 000 ms logging ceiling is `1 800 × 1 000 / 60` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*. All components are [UNV] estimates the spike replaces; every threshold logs and none truncates.

**Example (MVP slice: 14 blocks, 86 016 B payload, SSD, native truncated SHA-256)** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*: `capture_time_ms = 4.6` (Graph 0.5 + thirteen small blocks at 0.2 = 3.1 of snapshots, plus a 1.5 hash), `t_file_encode = 2.0`, `t_handoff = 0.3` (≤ 0.05 × 16.7 = 0.835 ✓): `main_hitch_ms = 6.9 ≤ 16.7` ✓. `worker_ms = 0.3 + 3 + 4 + 1 + 0.3 + 4 + 3 = 15.6`; `durable_ms = 22.5 ≤ 500` ✓. A slow HDD with a scanner: `worker_ms = 300`, `durable_ms = 306.9` ✓, and the job settles far inside `DEATH_STUB_WRITE_WAIT_MS`. **Scripted-hash counter-example:** with `hash_algo` 0 or 1 as a per-byte loop at 15 … 150 ms [UNV], `capture_time_ms` becomes 18.1 … 153.1 and `main_hitch_ms` 20.4 … 155.4, which fails `main_hitch_ms ≤ SNAPSHOT_BUDGET_MS`; that is why the ratified H64 is native (SL-16, R-17); a release build skips the capture-time hash until K-4 lands, for want of a consumer *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings, consistency follow-up)*.

### F-6. `save_bytes_header`

The `save_bytes_header` formula is defined as:

`header_len = H0 + B × h_b; file_bytes = 8 + 4 + header_len + Σ P_b + 32; alarm = file_bytes > SAVE_SIZE_ALARM_BYTES; refuse = file_bytes > MAX_SAVE_BYTES OR (EXISTS b: P_b > MAX_BLOCK_BYTES) OR header_len > MAX_HEADER_BYTES`

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 8, 4, 32 | int B | fixed | the container (SL-1) | Magic with layout id, `u32 header_len`, the SHA-256 trailer |
| `H0` | int B | ~600 (500 … 700) | measured at the spike; the fixed header keys of SL-1 (versions, hex fields, `campaign_id`, `SaveInfo` inputs) | Fixed header size |
| `h_b` = `BLOCK_RECORD_BYTES` ★ *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: one named constant shared by F-6, I7 and the `MAX_HEADER_BYTES` floor)* | int B | 240 (measured 180 … 240; the JSON record is about 190 B without `block_state_hash` and about 225 B with it and a 14-character id, so 240 budgets longer ids and larger offsets) | Tuning Knobs, measured at the spike | One block-table record (`block_id`, `schema_version`, `persist_scope`, `offset`, `length`, 64-hex `payload_sha256`, optional hex `block_state_hash`) |
| `B` | int | 1 … `MAX_BLOCKS` (kernel GDD value 128, cited); **14 for the MVP mandatory set** (SL-25) | registry | Blocks in the file |
| `P_b` | int B | ≤ `MAX_BLOCK_BYTES` | the encoded payload | Not the kernel's canonical byte length (`snapshot_bytes`, D.11, cited): the two differ by encoding overhead (≈ 1.0 for packed arrays, 1.0 … 1.6 for dictionary blocks [UNV]) |
| `MAX_SAVE_BYTES` ★, `MAX_BLOCK_BYTES` ★, `MAX_HEADER_BYTES` ★, `SAVE_SIZE_ALARM_BYTES` ★ | int B | defaults 64 MiB, 32 MiB, 64 KiB, 2 MiB | Tuning Knobs | The trust caps and the diagnostics alarm |

**Output Range:** `file_bytes` from ≈ 0.8 KB (only `kernel.clock`, `B = 1`) up to `MAX_SAVE_BYTES`; the MVP expectation is the kernel's 100 … 300 KiB payload (D.11, provisional) plus ≈ 2 KiB of overhead. At `B = 128` the header is `600 + 128 × 240 = 31 320 B` (30.6 KiB), under half of `MAX_HEADER_BYTES` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*. **`refuse` is a write-side rule:** Save/Load never produces a file its own read side would refuse (`SL_WRITE_TOO_LARGE{block}`, the ring intact, play continues). `alarm` is a diagnostics entry (`SL_SIZE_ALARM`, once per campaign open), not a failure.

**Example** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: the MVP mandatory set, B = 14)*: Graph 32 768 B (the Graph's cost ceiling, cited) plus thirteen blocks of 4 096 B = 86 016 B of payload; `header_len = 600 + 14 × 240 = 3 960`; `file_bytes = 8 + 4 + 3 960 + 86 016 + 32 = 90 020 B` (87.9 KiB), header share 4.4 %, far below the 2 MiB alarm. The payload bytes are raw (no base64 inflation; base64 exists only in the dev dump, S-1).

### F-7. `loss_ticks`

The `loss_ticks` formula is defined as:

`loss_ticks = T_dead − T_g; loss_min = GIVE_UP_MIN_TICKS + 1; loss_min_timer = DOWN_TO_DEATH_TICKS + 1; R_g = AUTOSAVE_RETRY_TICKS × ceil(W_MIN_WARNING_S × TICK_RATE / AUTOSAVE_RETRY_TICKS); loss_max = AUTOSAVE_INTERVAL_TICKS + AUTOSAVE_MAX_DEFER_TICKS + R_g + DOWN_TO_DEATH_TICKS; loss_max_stepback = AUTOSAVE_INTERVAL_TICKS + AUTOSAVE_MAX_DEFER_TICKS + R_g + DEATH_LOOP_WINDOW_TICKS + DOWN_TO_DEATH_TICKS; loss_mean ≈ AUTOSAVE_INTERVAL_TICKS / 2 + DOWN_TO_DEATH_TICKS + E[defer]; discard_per_restore = loss_ticks; net_progress_lost = T_peak − T_g_final over one restore chain; net_progress_lost_max = (AUTOSAVE_INTERVAL_TICKS + AUTOSAVE_MAX_DEFER_TICKS + R_g) + loss_max; loss_max_k = loss_max + k × (DOWN_TO_DEATH_TICKS − 1) for k ≥ 0 revive-and-fall-again recoveries since the last capture` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `T_dead` | int | 0 … 2^63 − 1 | `kernel.clock` at the `DEAD` tick | Death tick |
| `T_g` | int | 0 … `T_dead` | header `T` of the restored generation | Capture tick of the generation the stub restores; the sim time the death discards is `T_dead − T_g` |
| `AUTOSAVE_INTERVAL_TICKS` ★, `AUTOSAVE_MAX_DEFER_TICKS` ★, `DEATH_LOOP_WINDOW_TICKS` ★ | int | knob ranges | Tuning Knobs | Interval, soft-defer cap, death-loop window |
| `DOWN_TO_DEATH_TICKS` | int | 1 800 (registry, owner range 600 … 3 600, cited) | Controller | `DOWNED` to `DEAD` on the timer path *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* |
| `GIVE_UP_MIN_TICKS` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* | int | the Controller's: `ceil(H_give_up × TICK_RATE)` = 48 in hold mode at its default; in tap mode the two taps, at least 2 ticks apart, so the true floor is about 3 ticks there; the floor is the Controller's value under the player's confirm settings (cited, never restated; registered in the registry, value 48) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — consistency pass after design review)* | Controller | The shortest `T_dead − T_downed` the solo `give_up` action (user ruling D7) can produce; the loss floor uses it, not `DOWN_TO_DEATH_TICKS + 1` |
| `R_g` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* | int ticks | `W_MIN_WARNING_S × TICK_RATE` … that + `AUTOSAVE_RETRY_TICKS` − 1; 600 at the defaults | calculated | The refractory (SL-5) rounded up to the retry grid: the longest it can hold a fire back after a recovery at the cap. `W_MIN_WARNING_S` 10, `TICK_RATE` 60 and `AUTOSAVE_RETRY_TICKS` 300 give `300 × ceil(600 / 300)` = 600. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* With I3 requiring `W_MIN_WARNING_S × TICK_RATE` mod `AUTOSAVE_RETRY_TICKS` = 0, `R_g` equals the refractory exactly at every legal setting |
| `E[defer]` | float ticks | 0 … `AUTOSAVE_MAX_DEFER_TICKS` | the offline simulation of the systems-designer's plan (a playtest output) | Mean wait between `due_first_T` and the fire |
| `k` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* | int | 0 … unbounded (a playtest reports it) | counted | Revive-and-fall-again recoveries since the last durable capture; each can add up to `DOWN_TO_DEATH_TICKS − 1` ticks to the loss |
| `T_peak`, `T_g_final` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* | int | 0 … `T_dead` | `kernel.clock` | The furthest `T` a restore chain reached before its last restore, and the `T` of the generation that last restore returned to; `net_progress_lost` is what a chain costs against the furthest point reached, `discard_per_restore` what one death rewinds |

**Output Range:** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* `loss_ticks` lies in `[49, 20 400]` at the defaults when no event trigger fires (events can only lower it), in `[1 801, 20 400]` when the player waits out the timer, and in `[49, 24 000]` after one step-back. **Derivation of the floor:** the gate hard-blocks capture while any seat is `DOWNED`, and `DEAD` needs at least `GIVE_UP_MIN_TICKS` after `T_downed` (the solo `give_up` action, user ruling D7; the Controller's, 48 ticks = 0.8 s in hold mode at its default) or `DOWN_TO_DEATH_TICKS` on the timer; the latest possible capture is the boundary of tick `T_downed − 1`, so `loss_ticks ≥ GIVE_UP_MIN_TICKS + 1 = 49` at the Controller's defaults (1 801 without the give-up; about 4 in tap mode). **Ceiling:** the last durable generation is at `T0`; the next save is due at `T0 + I`; a continuous soft block postpones it to the cap at `c = T0 + I + D`. With no recovery in play the crew enters `DOWNED` on that very tick (a hard block, so the cap does not fire) and `DEAD` follows after 1 800 ticks: `I + D + 1 800` = 19 800. **The refractory term (user ruling X7 (c)):** if a seat got up from a `DOWNED` episode at `c` (the latest recovery the ceiling covers), the refractory of `W_MIN_WARNING_S × TICK_RATE` ticks (SL-5) holds the fire back to the first retry-grid tick at or after `c + 600`, which is `c + R_g` with `R_g` = `300 × ceil(600 / 300)` = 600 (600 is a multiple of the 300-tick grid); a second fall on that very tick is a hard block again and `DEAD` follows 1 800 ticks later: `T_dead − T0 = I + D + R_g + DOWN_TO_DEATH_TICKS` = 10 800 + 7 200 + 600 + 1 800 = **20 400** (5.7 min). *Scope of the ceilings:* they cover a death whose `DOWNED` episode is the first since the last capture opportunity and a death that follows one recovery completed at or before the cap tick; a recovery that completes after the cap tick starts a new gate-closed stretch (hard block until the revive, then the refractory), and a crew that is repeatedly downed and revived keeps the gate closed for as long as it keeps falling (a seat-state hard block never expires, F-2), so the bound is per recovery *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*: `loss_max_k = loss_max + k × (DOWN_TO_DEATH_TICKS − 1)`, because each recovery can hold the gate closed for up to `DOWN_TO_DEATH_TICKS − 1` ticks of `DOWNED` before it (22 199 for k = 1, 23 998 for k = 2 at the defaults, before any step-back); no bound is claimed beyond the k a playtest reports (the headline 20 400 and 24 000 are the k = 0 figures). **Step-back ceiling:** a `RISKY` generation is written at the first grid tick at or after the refractory (at most `T0 + I + D + R_g`), is restored, the crew survives for the whole window and goes `DOWNED` at `T(g_r) + W`, dies 1 800 ticks later, and the predecessor at `T0` is restored: `(T0 + I + D + R_g + W + 1 800) − T0` = 18 600 + 3 600 + 1 800 = **24 000** (6.7 min). The fairness route's window (`W_MIN_WARNING_S × TICK_RATE` = 600) is never longer than `W`, which boot invariant I13 asserts, so the fairness route adds nothing to this ceiling: a fall within 600 ticks of a restored generation written no later than `T0 + I + D + R_g` costs at most `I + D + R_g + 600 + 1 800` = 21 000. The coincidences in the defaults (`AUTOSAVE_MIN_GAP_TICKS` = `DOWN_TO_DEATH_TICKS` = 1 800; the refractory and the fairness window both 600) are coincidences, not derivations, and none may be computed from another. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* **Healthy writer only.** These bounds hold while `fail_n = 0`: each failed write adds its retry delay (up to `AUTOSAVE_FAIL_BACKOFF_MAX_TICKS`) with no bound on the number of failures, so on a persistently failing disk the discard per restore is bounded only by how long the disk keeps failing (the HUD notice at `AUTOSAVE_FAIL_ESCALATE_COUNT` is the player's warning, SL-21). **Net progress.** `discard_per_restore` is what one death rewinds; `net_progress_lost` is what a restore *chain* costs against the furthest point reached: a `RISKY` generation written at the end of the refractory (`T0 + I + D + R_g` = 18 600), a first death up to `loss_max` after it (20 400, so `T_peak − T0` = 39 000), then a second death inside the window that steps back to `T0`, loses `(I + D + R_g) + loss_max` = 18 600 + 20 400 = 39 000 ticks (10.8 min) of reached progress at the defaults (the review's estimate was about 41 400 and the pre-ruling derivation 37 800; the derivation here governs and AC-SL-55 asserts it). **Corners.** With the narrowed safe ranges (`I` ≤ 18 000, `D` ≤ `I − R`) `loss_max` reaches 18 000 + 17 700 + 600 + 1 800 = 38 100 ticks (10.6 min); `LOSS_CEILING_TICKS` (Tuning Knobs, 24 000 by this derivation) makes any configuration whose `loss_max_stepback` exceeds it a boot refusal in a release build (I9). The Player Fantasy and Game Feel therefore promise "each death loses at most", not a bound on the whole campaign. With event saves enabled the ceiling only falls, but event saves change the loss model (a death after an event loses less than a death after an interval), so every AC-SL-55 scenario is also run with the shipped `EVENT_TRIGGERS`.

**Example (defaults, 60 Hz):** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* `loss_min = 48 + 1 = 49` (0.8 s; `loss_min_timer = 1 801`, 30.0 s); `loss_max = 10 800 + 7 200 + 600 + 1 800 = 20 400` (5.7 min); `loss_max_stepback = 10 800 + 7 200 + 600 + 3 600 + 1 800 = 24 000` (6.7 min); `loss_mean = 5 400 + 1 800 + 0 = 7 200` (120 s, timer path; each `give_up` shortens a death by up to `DOWN_TO_DEATH_TICKS − GIVE_UP_MIN_TICKS` = 1 752 ticks). The anchor moment: generation `T_g = 437 400`, `T_dead = 448 200`, `loss_ticks = 10 800` (180 s), inside the range. `net_progress_lost_max = (10 800 + 7 200 + 600) + 20 400 = 39 000` (10.8 min). **Revive-and-fall-again chain** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*: `loss_max_k` with k = 1 is 20 400 + 1 799 = 22 199 ticks (6.2 min) and with k = 2 is 20 400 + 3 598 = 23 998 (6.7 min); I9 guards the k = 0 figure and a release build reports `loss_max_k` for k = 1 and 2 as a diagnostic, not a refusal. **Fault loss window (user ruling D55, accepted):** a kernel `FAULTED` or a process kill rewinds to the newest generation, `T_fault − last_save_T`, which is at most `AUTOSAVE_INTERVAL_TICKS` = 10 800 with an open gate (mean about 5 400) and at most `AUTOSAVE_INTERVAL_TICKS + AUTOSAVE_MAX_DEFER_TICKS` = 18 000 when a soft input defers the save to the cap (the ceiling); the refractory can hold a due save back by up to `R_g` = 600 ticks after a recovery at the cap, so on that narrow path a kill or fault rewinds up to 18 600; the accepted figures are those of the path without a recovery, and Assembly's fault window (D.16, a defect inside an Assembly apply with no seat falling) is unchanged.

### F-8. `load_budget`

The `load_budget` formula is defined as:

`precheck_ms = t_read + t_sha + t_hdr + t_decode + t_walk; loading_ms = Σ_b (t_restore(b) + t_post(b)) + t_verify; restart_ms = LOAD_ATTEMPTS × precheck_ms + max(loading_ms, t_hold_pad, t_epoch); require restart_ms ≤ LOADING_TARGET_MS; hot-path hitch = the main-thread part of precheck_ms, checked against DEAD_TICK_HITCH_MS`

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `t_read` | float ms | 1 … 100 [UNV]; 0 on the hot-copy path (SL-11) | disk | Read one candidate file |
| `t_sha` | float ms | 0.3 … 2 [UNV] | native hashing | Trailer SHA-256 and the per-block hashes |
| `t_hdr`, `t_decode`, `t_walk` | float ms | 0.1 … 1; 1 … 10; 2 … 30 (about 10 … 30 over the 30 000 … 60 000 nodes of a Graph-sized payload) [UNV] *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* | Save/Load, main thread | Header parse, payload decode with objects disallowed, the bounded type walk (SL-2) |
| `t_restore(b)`, `t_post(b)` | float ms | Graph ≤ 2 combined (the Graph's cost criterion, cited); others 0.1 … 1 | kernel `LOADING`, owner diagnostics | Per-block restore and `post_restore` |
| `t_verify` | float ms | 1.5 (D.11 example) … 20+ [UNV] | kernel D.12 | The reproduction compare, dev/test and the guarded release case (SL-12) |
| `t_epoch` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review, delegated ruling R1)* | float ms | 1 … `RESTORE_EPOCH_WRITE_MAX_MS` [UNV] | worker | The header-only `restore_epoch` rewrite (SL-23), concurrent with `loading_ms`; the end-of-`LOADING` hand-off waits for it, so it sits inside the hold unless it is slower |
| `t_hold_pad` | float ms | ≥ 120 | Pipeline (cited: the `TRANSIT_HOLD` fade) | The black hold; load work hides inside it |
| `LOAD_ATTEMPTS` | int | 1 … `LOAD_MAX_ATTEMPTS` (default `AUTOSAVE_GENERATIONS`) | calculated | Candidates whose pre-check ran; the kernel restore happens once, because an owner-reported failure inside `LOADING` is `FAULTED` and not retried in line |
| `LOADING_TARGET_MS` ★ | int ms | 500 … 2 000, default 1 000 (provisional) | Tuning Knobs | A logging threshold, never truncating |

**Output Range:** `restart_ms` is the time the player perceives from `DEAD` (or Continue) to control. For the death stub `precheck_ms` is a hitch inside the `DEAD` tick, where the Presenter holds the down sheet's last frame (SL-11), and `max(loading_ms, t_hold_pad, t_epoch)` is the black hold; in practice the hold dominates. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* On the hot path `t_read`, `t_decode` and `t_walk` are 0 on the main thread (the worker did them, SL-11), so the `DEAD`-tick hitch there is `t_sha + t_hdr` or less and is checked against `DEAD_TICK_HITCH_MS`, separately from the hold. A black hold under 120 ms reads as a flash and over about 2 s as a crash (Game Feel).

**Example** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*: read 3, SHA 0.5, header 0.2, decode 4, walk 12 → `precheck_ms = 19.7`; Graph restore + post 2, thirteen other blocks 13 × 0.2, hash 1.5 → `loading_ms = 6.1`; `t_hold_pad = 120`: `restart_ms = 1 × 19.7 + max(6.1, 120) = 139.7 ms` ≤ 1 000 ✓ (on the hot-copy path `t_read` = 0 and `precheck_ms` = 16.7). Worst case, two candidates fail the pre-check before the third passes: `3 × 19.7 + 120 = 179.1 ms`. If a scripted 64-bit hash costs 60 ms [UNV], `loading_ms = 64.6` and the hold still dominates; the capture side, not the restore, is where the scripted hash hurts (F-5).

### F-9. `ring_position`

The `ring_position` formula is defined as:

`gen_next = 1 + max(generation over the ring, the generation number in every `quarantine/` file name, the .demoted files and campaign.json); pos = min{p IN 0 … AUTOSAVE_GENERATIONS − 1 : slot p holds no `.sav` file at all (missing, or its file was moved to `quarantine/`) or holds only a `.demoted` file, AND p is not claimed by an abandoned job} if that set is non-empty, else argmin over the unclaimed p of generation(slot p), excluding the newest generation's slot; if no such p remains, SL_WRITE_NO_POSITION (FAILED, backoff)` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `generation` | int | 1 … 2^53 − 1 | header (a JSON number, below 2^53) | Strictly increasing per campaign; the ordering truth, never `T`, never the file name, never modification time |
| `AUTOSAVE_GENERATIONS` ★ | int | 2 … 5, default 3 | Tuning Knobs | Ring size `N` |
| slot `p` | file | `gen_p.sav`, `gen_p.demoted` or empty | the campaign directory | A slot is **free** only if it holds no `.sav` (missing, or the file was moved to `quarantine/`) or holds a `.demoted` file *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*: **any other `.sav`, readable or not, occupies it**, so an unreadable but possibly newest file (a transient sharing violation, a file not yet classified) is never targeted or overwritten; a slot **claimed** by an abandoned write job (SL-10) is never chosen until that job reports |

**Output Range:** `gen_next` is monotonic over the life of the campaign even after step-backs (which roll `T` back) and even when the newest file is quarantined and `campaign.json` is lost, because every quarantine file name carries its generation number (SL-19) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*; `pos` is in `[0, N − 1]` and the newest generation's file is never renamed or touched, so a window with no newest file cannot exist.

**Example:** `N = 3`; `gen_0.sav` = G7, `gen_1.demoted` = G6 (stepped back from), `gen_2.sav` = G5, and `quarantine/` holds G9 (`STALE_EPOCH`). `gen_next = 1 + max(7, 6, 5, 9) = 10`; slot 1 is the first free position, so G10 is written to `gen_1.sav` and the `.demoted` occupant is renamed `.bak` and deleted after `POST_VERIFY` (T-4).

### F-10. `hex64`

The `hex64` formula is defined as:

`hex64(x) = lowercase hexadecimal of the 8 bytes of x (two's complement, LEAST significant byte first, little-endian), exactly 16 characters; unhex64(h) accepts only 16 characters of [0-9a-f] and returns the same 8 bytes` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `x` | 64-bit value | 0 … 2^64 − 1 (a signed GDScript int above 2^63 − 1 reads negative [UNV]) | `T`, `world_seed`, `state_hash`, per-block hashes | Any header value that can exceed 2^53 (S-1) |
| `h` | string | exactly 16 chars (32 for the 128-bit `campaign_id`) | header | Uppercase, a wrong width or a non-hex character is a refusal (`SL_PRECHECK_HEADER_HEX_MALFORMED`); there is no general string-to-int parse |

**Output Range:** a bijection between 8-byte values and 16-character strings; `unhex64(hex64(x)) = x` bit for bit for every `x`, including values a float cannot hold.

**Example:** `world_seed = 2^64 − 1` → `"ffffffffffffffff"` (the signed value is −1 [UNV]); `T = 2^53 + 1 = 9 007 199 254 740 993` → `"0100000000002000"`; `"FFFFFFFFFFFFFFFF"` and `"ffffffffffffffff0"` are refused. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* **One endianness everywhere:** the H64 truncation (first 8 bytes of SHA-256, read little-endian into the integer, SL-16) and this hex form are both little-endian, so an H64 hex string is exactly the first 16 hex characters of the SHA-256 digest and the byte order never flips between hashing and printing. **Fixed vectors** (value → hex): 0 → `0000000000000000`; 1 → `0100000000000000`; 2^53 + 1 → `0100000000002000`; 2^63 − 1 → `ffffffffffffff7f`; 2^63 → `0000000000000080` (the signed value is −2^63 [UNV]); 2^64 − 1 → `ffffffffffffffff`. **Implementation note:** build and parse the string from the 8 bytes (`PackedByteArray.hex_encode()` and `String.hex_decode()`, [UNV]); `String.hex_to_int` and `PackedByteArray.encode_s64` are not used for header hex, because a value of 2^63 or more reads or overflows as a negative signed integer.

## Edge Cases

Each case names the exact outcome and, where one exists, the diagnostic id. Diagnostics (`SL_*`) are log and `save_status` entries; they are not a second refusal set — the closed refusal set is `SL_PRECHECK_*` (T-5) and the boot set is `SL_BOOT_*` (SL-15). Cases are grouped by theme; a rule id in parentheses names where the behaviour is defined. **Reading rule for every case:** the kernel is never touched by a file the pre-check refuses, and the previous generations are never changed by a failed write.

### Zero, maximum and out-of-range values

- **If `world_seed` is 2^63 or above, or `T` exceeds 2^53**: the header stores each as exactly 16 lowercase hex characters and the round trip is bit-exact (F-10); a signed GDScript read of a `world_seed` above 2^63 − 1 is negative [UNV] and must still round-trip. Any other width, an uppercase letter or a non-hex character is refused (`SL_PRECHECK_HEADER_HEX_MALFORMED`, CORRUPT). Rationale: S-1 — a float parse loses everything above 2^53.
- **If a header number that must stay a number (`generation`, a length, an offset) arrives at or above 2^53 or with a fraction**: the float parse has already rounded it, so the pre-check refuses (`SL_PRECHECK_HEADER_FIELD_NOT_INT`, CORRUPT). Rationale: a silently rounded `generation` would corrupt ring order.
- **If `header_len` is 0, a corrupt 4 GiB, or larger than the file allows**: refused at the `header_len` bounds step before any allocation (`SL_PRECHECK_HEADER_LEN_INVALID`, CORRUPT). Only a re-signed file can reach this step, because the trailer step comes first; the fuzz includes re-signed files for exactly that reason.
- **If the encoded file would exceed `MAX_SAVE_BYTES`, a block exceeds `MAX_BLOCK_BYTES`, or the header exceeds `MAX_HEADER_BYTES` at write time**: no file is produced (`SL_WRITE_TOO_LARGE{block}`), `save_status` reads `FAILED`, the ring is untouched and play continues; nothing is truncated. Rationale: the write side never produces a file its own read side would refuse (F-6).
- **If the size crosses `SAVE_SIZE_ALARM_BYTES`**: the write proceeds and `SL_SIZE_ALARM` is logged once per campaign open. Rationale: it is a prompt to review compression at VS-A, not a failure.
- **If the header `codec` is not 0**: refused at the `format_version` step (`SL_PRECHECK_FORMAT_UNSUPPORTED`, INCOMPATIBLE). Rationale: the field is reserved with no decode path in the MVP, which also rules out decompression bombs.
- **If `T < last_save_T` is observed**: `elapsed := 0`, `last_save_T := T`, and `SL_AUTOSAVE_CLOCK_REGRESSION` is logged once. Rationale: unreachable after the reset-on-restore rule, so it is a defect signal; dev builds assert.
- **If `kernel.clock.T` cannot be read as an integer**: `SL_BAD_CLOCK`, no write and no restore. Rationale: every interval and window is in ticks (SL-23); with no clock, no decision is safe.

### Simultaneous rules and same-tick collisions

- **If a Graph warning window opens on the tick the interval falls due, or a second window opens as the first closes** *(Amended 2026-10-26 — CD-GDD-ALIGN)*: the save waits (`graph_tell_block`, F-2), re-evaluated on the retry grid; chained windows are bounded by `AUTOSAVE_MAX_DEFER_TICKS`, after which the save fires tagged `RISKY`. Rationale: the Graph's `W_MIN_WARNING_S` guarantee must not be shortened by a restore, and the cap keeps `loss_ticks` unchanged.
- **If the only open Graph window belongs to a dormant section**: its `warn_since_step` is frozen (Graph dormant-section rule) so it cannot close; until the Graph owner answers OQ-38 the save can wait to the cap and be tagged `RISKY`. Rationale: a rare, bounded exposure that is recorded rather than hidden.
- **If an event and the interval fall due on the same tick**: one save, kind `EVENT` (F-1). Rationale: the ring must not be flushed by two generations a frame apart.
- **If a due autosave falls inside the post-`DOWNED` refractory** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*: it waits (`refractory_block`, F-2) and fires on the first retry-grid tick at or after `last_recover_T + W_MIN_WARNING_S × TICK_RATE`; the cap does not shorten it, and a `QUIT` is not bound by it (SL-6). Rationale: a recovery reopens the gate onto a seat that may still be in the state that downed it (user ruling X7 (c)).
- **If a seat's `vitals_state` is `CRITICAL` when the save falls due and stays so past the cap** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*: the save defers to `AUTOSAVE_MAX_DEFER_TICKS` and is then written `RISKY` with `risky_cause` containing `VITALS`; nothing is drawn. Rationale: SL-5 row (c) is capped like every soft input, so a suit that never recovers cannot suppress saving forever.
- **If an `ASSEMBLY_CUT` event is latched while `ragged_breach_open()` is true** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*: the event is not due and stays latched, the interval save is deferred as a soft input meanwhile, and the event is taken once the breach is capped (F-1, F-2; user ruling D54). Rationale: a restore from a save inside a self-inflicted decompression can reload the player into an unwinnable breach that the autosave hides; the interval path is bounded by the cap, by which time the decompression has resolved either way.
- **If a due autosave and the `DEAD` tick share a boundary**: no snapshot is taken; `DEAD` is a hard condition and the restore wins (SL-7). Rationale: a snapshot of a dead world is the trap the gate exists to prevent.
- **If no `DOWNED` episode was observed before `DEAD`**: `T_downed := T_dead` for `generation_select`, and the hard condition still closes the gate at that boundary. Rationale: the formula must not depend on an observation Save/Load may not have made.
- **If `request_restore` arrives while the kernel is `SAVING`**: the kernel waits for the boundary (K:651) and the capture completes; the death stub then settles the writer (SL-7): the new generation is a candidate only if its write has reached `WRITTEN` by that boundary, otherwise its job is abandoned. The combination should not occur at all, because `DOWNED` precedes `DEAD` by 1 800 ticks on the timer (at least `GIVE_UP_MIN_TICKS` after a `give_up`) and the hard gate refuses captures while a seat is down; seeing it is a defect signal. Rationale: no main-thread wait on a write job, so a hung worker cannot hold the death stub *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*.
- **If a Save & Quit and an interval autosave are due at the same tick**: the `QUIT` path wins and the pending autosave state is discarded (`due_first_T` is session memory only); only an in-flight write delays it. Rationale: the quit writes the current state anyway.
- **If Continue is pressed while the kernel is `LOADING`**: the second call is ignored (`SL_LOAD_REENTRANT`), as the kernel also ignores and reports a second request. Rationale: one restore at a time, one `restore_epoch` step.
- **If the death stub fires before the initial `NEW_GAME` generation is `WRITTEN`**: the stub finds the baseline job `WRITTEN` (then it is the candidate) or settles it as in SL-7: a job still busy is abandoned, there is no candidate, and the escalation of SL-18 applies (`SL_NEW_GAME_NO_BASELINE`, the empty directory removed, the menu); a `FAILED` baseline takes the same path *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*. Rationale: the 30 s `DOWNED` floor (0.8 s after a solo `give_up`, user ruling D7) makes the case nearly unreachable, but a campaign with no verified generation must never reach a first death.

### Writing, crashing and recovering (save/restore)

- **If the process is killed during `WRITING_TMP`**: the position's file is intact and a partial `gen_k.tmp` remains; the next open deletes the `.tmp` and the ring is unchanged (T-6). Rationale: an unverified `.tmp` is never a candidate.
- **If the process is killed between `SWAPPING_OUT` and `SWAPPING_IN`**: `gen_k.bak` and a complete `.tmp` exist with no `gen_k.sav`; recovery promotes the `.tmp` if it verifies and deletes the `.bak`, otherwise promotes the `.bak` back (T-6). Rationale: at least two verified generations exist during any swap because `AUTOSAVE_GENERATIONS` is at least 2.
- **If a `store_*` call returns false, a file will not open, or disk is full or locked**: the `.tmp` is removed best-effort, previous generations are untouched, `save_status` reads `FAILED` (`SL_WRITE_FAILED{step}`), `last_save_T` is unchanged so the save stays due, and the retry backs off to at most `AUTOSAVE_FAIL_BACKOFF_MAX_TICKS`; after `AUTOSAVE_FAIL_ESCALATE_COUNT` consecutive failures the HUD notice is raised. Rationale: sourced — `FileAccess.store_*` returns `bool` since 4.4 (breaking-changes), so every return is checked.
- **If a rename fails with a sharing violation (antivirus, indexer)**: the worker retries `RENAME_RETRY_COUNT` times, `RENAME_RETRY_DELAY_MS` apart, then rolls back by renaming the `.bak` back and reports `FAILED`. Rationale: the design assumes only that renaming a closed file to an absent name is all-or-nothing [UNV].
- **If a write job outlives a restore**: a job that sees an advanced `write_epoch` before its first rename aborts and removes its `.tmp` (`SL_WRITE_ABORTED_RESTORE`); a job already past `SWAPPING_IN` lands a verified generation of the abandoned timeline, which is moved to `quarantine/` as `STALE_EPOCH` and is never a candidate. An **abandoned** job (SL-10) leaves `writer_busy` at once, its `.tmp` carries the old `write_epoch` in its name and its ring position stays claimed until it reports; a later autosave goes ahead on another position *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*. Rationale: otherwise a later death would restore the ghost of the previous timeline.
- **If two files carry the same `generation` (a kill between renames)**: prefer `.sav` over `.bak`, then verified over unverified; the loser is removed only after the winner verifies. Rationale: order is the header number, so the choice is deterministic.
- **If `campaign.json` is missing, corrupt or disagrees with the files**: it is rebuilt by scanning headers (`SL_MANIFEST_REBUILT`) and a load is never refused for it; demoted and quarantined generations stay excluded because they are encoded in file names, while `suspect` counters and REBASED baselines are lost. Rationale: the manifest is a hint, never truth (SL-3).
- **If `AUTOSAVE_GENERATIONS` shrinks between builds (3 → 2)**: the writer only uses positions `0 … N − 1`; a file at a position beyond `N` stays on disk as a candidate by generation order and is removed only by the campaign delete. If it grows, new positions fill lazily as the first free position. Rationale: never delete a file that cannot be proven obsolete.
- **If a restore moves `T` backwards**: `last_save_T := T(g)`, `due_first_T` and `event_pending` are cleared and `restored_gen_id := g`; generation numbers keep rising and the first new autosave is `AUTOSAVE_INTERVAL_TICKS` after `T(g)`. Rationale: timers are sim time and the sim just went back.

### Hostile, damaged and foreign files (pre-check)

- **If a file is empty, shorter than the size floor, or truncated anywhere**: `SL_PRECHECK_FILE_TOO_SMALL` or `SL_PRECHECK_FILE_SHA_MISMATCH` (CORRUPT); the file is a quarantine candidate and the loader falls back. Rationale: a missing or short trailer is truncation, detected for free.
- **If one bit flips in the last byte of the magic (the layout-id byte)**: the trailer no longer matches, so the file is CORRUPT (`SL_PRECHECK_FILE_SHA_MISMATCH`), not INCOMPATIBLE, and the loader falls back on an intact ring. Rationale: the layout id is judged only after the trailer (SL-1 format law); a flipped bit must not stop the loader.
- **If one bit flips in `format_version`**: the same — corruption, falling back — never "made by a newer build". Rationale: the integrity steps precede the compatibility steps.
- **If the newest generation is genuinely newer (a re-signed `format_version` above ours, or `min_reader_version` above ours)**: INCOMPATIBLE — the loader stops with "made by an incompatible build", files stay in place, and the campaign directory is `READ_ONLY` for the older build; New campaign creates a different directory. Rationale: a silent fallback would roll the player back and then overwrite newer files (SL-11).
- **If an older generation is loadable but the newest is INCOMPATIBLE**: no fallback occurs and no older generation is overwritten. Rationale: same-campaign files share the incompatibility in practice, and a rollback is data loss.
- **If a block id is unknown and not on `RETIRED_BLOCKS`**: INCOMPATIBLE, the loader stops (`SL_PRECHECK_UNKNOWN_BLOCK`), so the kernel's `RESTORE_UNKNOWN_BLOCK` is unreachable — reaching it would be a Save/Load defect. If it is on `RETIRED_BLOCKS`, it is stripped with `SL_PRECHECK_RETIRED_BLOCK_STRIPPED` and the mode becomes REBASED (cause 2). Rationale: a removed block must not strand old saves; an unknown one is a newer or foreign build.
- **If a non-mandatory block is missing and its `added_in_format` exceeds the file's `format_version`**: `default_state()` fills it with a note and the mode becomes REBASED (cause 3); otherwise `SL_PRECHECK_MANDATORY_MISSING` (CORRUPT). Rationale: K:645 leaves the decision to Save/Load.
- **If a `PERCEPTION_OUT` block, or a block of the wrong `persist_scope`, appears in a file**: `SL_PRECHECK_PERCEPTION_BLOCK_PRESENT` or `SL_PRECHECK_SCOPE_MISMATCH` (CORRUPT). Rationale: Save/Load never writes one, so presence is a defect or tampering, and a PROFILE block inside a world file could roll knowledge back.
- **If a payload contains an `Object` marker, a forbidden type, excessive nesting or too many nodes**: `SL_PRECHECK_BLOCK_TYPE_FORBIDDEN` or `_BLOCK_DECODE_LIMITS` (CORRUPT); no object is instantiated and the kernel is never touched. Rationale: the object-disallowed decode stops instantiation but does not itself bound allocation (SL-2).
- **If a Graph payload stores a packed array as a JSON-style number list (an S-1 violation that survives the container)**: it passes the container checks, because the pre-check validates declared types, and the Graph's two-phase `restore()` refuses it (`GRAPH_RESTORE_BAD_TAG`) with the live Graph block byte-identical; the kernel enters `FAULTED` (blocks restored earlier in `restore_after` order are not rolled back, K:650), the menu offers Continue on the remaining candidates (SL-22), and the generation's failure counter increments. Rationale: owner-level validation cannot run before the kernel is touched; the 1,000-mutation fuzz (re-signed files) measures how rare this path is.
- **If header `T` or `world_seed` differs from the `kernel.clock` block although every hash is valid (a re-signed file)**: `SL_PRECHECK_HEADER_CLOCK_MISMATCH` (CORRUPT). Rationale: the header copy exists for exactly this cross-check.
- **If a file's `campaign_id` differs from its directory name (a file copied between campaigns, or a directory renamed)**: `SL_PRECHECK_CAMPAIGN_MISMATCH` (CORRUPT), quarantine candidate. Rationale: a copied directory under the same name from another machine is untrusted data under the same caps and loads if it passes; a renamed one does not.
- **If the header `hash_algo` is not implemented by this build**: the file stage still runs (SHA-256 is Save/Load's own), the compare is skipped (`SL_VERIFY_ALGO_SKIPPED`) and the mode is REBASED (cause 4); it is never a refusal. Rationale: a kernel hash change must not break saves (SL-16).
- **If stale `.tmp` or `.bak` files exist when a campaign opens**: the recovery scan of T-6 reconciles them before the first load; nothing it cannot prove obsolete is deleted. Rationale: a crash can leave any of them.
- **If `seats` names a PlayerId the session has not bound**: allowed; the session rebuilds its binding table (CR-7) and the owners' restores decide. A `PER_PLAYER` key outside `seats`, or an empty or duplicated `seats`, is `SL_PRECHECK_SEATS_INVALID`. Rationale: identity is the seat, never the transport peer.

### Death stub and generation selection (degenerate strategies live here too)

- **If a write job is still busy at the `DEAD` boundary (a hung worker)** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*: it is abandoned (`SL_WRITE_ABANDONED_AT_DEATH`), `write_epoch` advances, the stub selects among the already-verified candidates, and a later autosave runs on a fresh job with epoch-named temporary files; if the stuck worker ever resumes, its late rename is quarantined as `STALE_EPOCH` (AC-SL-130). Rationale: a join on a hung write would hang the frame, and a blocking wait with no timeout is not available on the engine's thread API.
- **If the only candidate is `RISKY` and a loop is detected**: it is restored anyway and `SL_STEPBACK_NONE_AVAILABLE` is logged. Rationale: the user rule is restart from the last autosave; there is nothing older.
- **If a `RISKY` generation is restored, the crew dies inside the window, and the predecessor is chosen**: the `RISKY` file is renamed `gen_k.demoted`; a second death before any new autosave restores the predecessor again, never the demoted one (the three-death scripted fixture). Rationale: without demotion the ring would oscillate between a `RISKY` newest and its predecessor.
- **If the newest generation is not `RISKY` and the crew dies repeatedly at the same spot** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*: the newest is restored every time (the user rule) unless a fall comes within `W_MIN_WARNING_S × TICK_RATE` ticks of the restore, when the fairness route steps back whether or not the generation is `RISKY`; `deaths_per_restored_gen` is counted in diagnostics for playtest. Rationale: the general step-back from a non-`RISKY` generation is the question left to playtest (OQ-1); only the fairness window was ruled (X7 (b)).
- **If every candidate fails the pre-check**: `SL_LOAD_NO_LOADABLE_SAVE` is published before the kernel is touched; the Chart Table shows it as a boot-flow screen (no pause is requested, CT-25; in-session teardown, OQ-42) with New campaign or Quit; no file is deleted and the dead world is never autosaved. Rationale: the game stays inside the player's control and the files stay available for recovery.
- **If only demoted generations remain loadable**: a second pass restores the newest demoted one. Rationale: a stepped-back-from file is still better than nothing.
- **If the stub fires on a campaign with no restore yet (the first life)**: no loop is possible (`g_r` is none) and the newest generation, the `NEW_GAME` baseline at worst, is restored. Rationale: the formula needs a restored generation to detect a loop.
- **If the crew goes `DOWNED` within `W_MIN_WARNING_S × TICK_RATE` (600) ticks of a restore, or later** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*: within 599 ticks of `T(g_r)` the fairness route steps back (F-3: the generation is demoted, the predecessor is restored, `SL_STEPBACK_NONE_AVAILABLE` if there is none); from 600 ticks on, even inside `AUTOSAVE_MIN_GAP_TICKS`, the same non-`RISKY` generation is restored again and the loop rule does not apply. Rationale: under ten seconds of acting time is the unfair case; beyond it only a `RISKY` generation is suspected of being unwinnable.
- **If deaths follow a restore by under 600 ticks again and again** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*: each death steps one generation further back (the generation stepped back to becomes `g_r` and the test repeats), bounded by the ring and ending at `SL_STEPBACK_NONE_AVAILABLE` on the last candidate.
