Rationale: every step costs the player a death and more lost time, so it is a price and not a prize; see the deliberate-fast-death case under degenerate strategies.
- **If the player ends the `DOWNED` wait with the solo `give_up` action** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* (user ruling D7): `DEAD` follows at the apply tick, at least `GIVE_UP_MIN_TICKS` after `T_downed`; the stub, `T_downed` and the selection are identical to the timer path, and a write job still busy is abandoned (SL-7). The loss floor is `GIVE_UP_MIN_TICKS + 1` (F-7) and metric M2 counts the death, with `death_via_give_up` logged as a separate column. Rationale: the Controller owns the action; Save/Load sees only `DEAD`.
- **If a seat reaches `DEAD` in the pod** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* (statuses run in the pod, user ruling D60): the path is identical: the hard gate was closed while the seat was `DOWNED`, the stub restores the newest verified generation, and `web.state` restores the leg and the pod. `IN_POD` is a soft state (SL-5), so a generation written earlier in the leg restores the in-leg state. Rationale: Save/Load keys on `loco_state` only.
- **If `DEAD` is entered during a camera cut**: the request is made at that tick's boundary, the Presenter holds the down sheet's last frame, and no second cut is issued (the Controller's edge case). Rationale: only `DEAD` during a *cut channel* is impossible, because `DOWNED` precedes it by 1,800 ticks on the timer (at least `GIVE_UP_MIN_TICKS` after a solo `give_up`, user ruling D7) and clears the channel *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*.
- **If an owner's `restore()` or `post_restore()` fails inside `LOADING`**: the kernel enters `FAULTED` with the prior state not intact; the loader does not retry in line; the menu's Continue re-runs the pipeline on the remaining candidates (SL-22). Rationale: K:170 allows only an explicit user restore out of `FAULTED`.
- **If a reproduction mismatch is detected (EXACT mode)**: release marks the generation `suspect` and plays on; dev and test halt; the loader never walks to another generation. Rationale: the bytes are proven, the defect is in the code, and it would reproduce on every generation.
- **If the compare window is missed (`kernel.clock.T` ≠ header `T`)**: `SL_VERIFY_WINDOW_MISSED`, no verdict, play on. Rationale: a hash taken after a tick has run would differ for the wrong reason (SL-12).

### Save & Quit and window close

- **If a Save & Quit is requested while any bound seat is `DOWNED`**: nothing is written — `save_and_quit` returns `SL_QUIT_NO_WRITE_DOWNED`, `save_status` is unchanged, and shutdown proceeds after the hold-to-confirm "quit without saving"; Continue then restores the newest verified generation. Rationale: no file on disk may ever hold a `DOWNED` or `DEAD` seat; the death stub would have restored the same file within `DOWN_TO_DEATH_TICKS`.
- **If a Save & Quit is requested while a seat is `DEAD`**: unreachable in the solo MVP (the stub fires on the `DEAD` tick); the clause exists for multi-seat futures. Rationale: SL-6.
- **If a Save & Quit is requested with only a soft condition closed (a tell, a breach phase, `CHANNELLING`, `STAGGERED`, `IN_POD`, a `CRITICAL` suit, an open self-inflicted breach)** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*: the generation is written immediately and tagged `RISKY` with its `risky_cause`; inside the post-`DOWNED` refractory with a healthy suit it is written and is not `RISKY`. Rationale: refusing to quit traps the player; quitting without writing silently discards up to one interval.
- **If a Save & Quit finds the state hash equal to the newest generation's (same `T`, nothing changed)**: the write is skipped (`SL_QUIT_DEDUPED`) and shutdown proceeds. Rationale: a duplicate generation would only push a useful one out of the ring.
- **If the Save & Quit write fails or times out (`SAVE_QUIT_TIMEOUT_MS`)**: the quit is held, `save_status` reads `FAILED`, the pause panel stays, and the player may retry or quit without saving by hold-to-confirm. Rationale: never silently lose progress; never trap the player.
- **If the window is closed while a write is in progress** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*: the quit waits up to `SAVE_QUIT_TIMEOUT_MS` (frame-polled, SL-6), then **holds**: `save_status` reads `FAILED`, `quit_held` is published and the player leaves only through the hold-to-confirm "quit without saving"; the next open reconciles by the crash table (T-6). Rationale: one outcome for a failed or slow quit, the one SL-6 and AC-SL-45c already state; an exit after a timeout would silently discard up to one interval.
- **If the window is closed while the kernel is `PAUSED` under the open Chart Table (SOLO)** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — consistency pass)*: Save/Load's handler runs `save_and_quit()` from `PAUSED` (`PAUSED → SAVING → PAUSED`) under SL-6's rules, then `request_shutdown`; the Chart Table calls nothing (EC-CT-38). **On a boot-flow screen** nothing is written and the process ends through boot code (ASK-CT-11). Rationale: one save path, the same rules everywhere.
- **If a quit is requested while the kernel is `FAULTED`**: no write is attempted (K:151); the quit proceeds to the menu or shutdown. Rationale: the faulting session is never saved over an existing generation.
- **If the window is closed while a seat is `DOWNED`, while the kernel is `LOADING`, or while it is `SAVING`** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*: `DOWNED` writes nothing and holds the close for the hold-to-confirm "quit without saving" (`SL_QUIT_NO_WRITE_DOWNED`); `LOADING` defers the close intent until the kernel is `RUNNING` and then handles it as a `RUNNING` close; `SAVING` lets the in-flight job reach `WRITTEN` or `FAILED` and then dedupes against it or writes the `QUIT` generation (SL-6, close mechanism; AC-SL-44b). Rationale: every state has one answer and none of them is a silent exit.
- **If a Save & Quit is made while a Graph warning window is open** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*: the generation is written at once and tagged `RISKY`; Continue restores a fault with less than `W_MIN_WARNING_S` of its warning left. It is not clamped (a clamp would trap the player at the menu to protect a window they can see) and it is counted by metric M4. Rationale: SL-6.

### PROFILE store

- **If `profile.sav` is absent and zero PROFILE blocks are registered (every MVP build)**: this is valid on a cold open — an empty profile. Rationale: nothing to restore, nothing to refuse.
- **If `profile.sav` is absent and one or more PROFILE blocks are registered**: `SL_PRECHECK_MANDATORY_MISSING` for the profile file (CORRUPT class); `profile.sav.bak` is tried first, and if it is also unusable the world still loads, the profile is reset to `default_state()` with `SL_PROFILE_RESET`, and the damaged file is quarantined, never deleted. Rationale: Pillar 5 says knowledge is not lost with the ship, and a corrupt knowledge file must not strand the world.
- **If a PROFILE write is pending when a world restore occurs**: the profile is flushed first (its file is not part of the restore) and the restore then proceeds; a world restore never reads `profile.sav` into WORLD blocks. Rationale: CR-18 — partial restore within a scope is unsupported, and PROFILE blocks are never rolled back by a world restore.
- **If the process is killed during a PROFILE write**: the crash table (T-6) applies to `profile.sav` and its permanent `.bak`; the ring is unaffected. Rationale: a profile is rewritten by the same atomic procedure.

### Hallucination interplay (Pillars 1 and 4)

- **If a save is captured while a hallucination is active**: the file holds the true SIM state — the sanity level and the owner's counters — and never the perception output (`PERCEPTION_OUT`) or a field describing what the player saw. Rationale: a save is ground truth (H1); the rebuilt output after load derives from level and seed.
- **If the crew reloads after dying to a scrubber that "looked fixed" because of a false system state** *(Amended 2026-10-26 — CD-GDD-ALIGN)*: Save/Load restores every persisted counter and seed exactly (kernel G4, D.10), reads no perception and varies nothing by it; whether the false state's onset replays at the same moment or is re-keyed is Sanity & Hallucination Rules' decision (OQ-37). The physical fact underneath, that the scrubber fails at minute four, is unchanged and is Pillar 1. Rationale: binding perception replay here would let the player memorise hallucinations across deaths (P2, P4); any re-keying must not be steerable by quit or death. Threat draws (the ambush replayed at the same corner) are re-keyed per restore epoch by user ruling D16 (OQ-47, SL-23): the physical fact replays, the ambush does not *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*.
- **If the sanity level was low at death**: the restore returns the generation's sanity level (WORLD-scoped) — death does not cure sanity (H3), so no "die to clear your head" exploit exists. Rationale: Suit vitals and the Sanity level roll back with the world.
- **If the first `RUNNING` frame after `LOADING` is presented unfiltered**: this violates H4; the requirement is routed to the Perception Filter, Sanity and Pipeline (OQ-11), and the black hold covers any rebuild. Rationale: one clean frame would be a free oracle.
- **If an autosave would vary its timing, or a save list its contents, with sanity**: not allowed and not built; Save/Load never reads perception (SL-24). Rationale: a lie that reached the save menu would be unfalsifiable inside the session.

### Co-op hooks and later tiers

- **If a guest later joins (VS-B)**: the snapshot payload is separable from the file wrapper (SL-1) and is the guest's initial transfer (CR-18); a guest's save request is the reserved `saveload.request_save` command (provisional — Co-op Session & Replication), validated on the host; no guest-authoritative block exists. Rationale: host-owned saves only.
- **If one of several seats is `DEAD` while others live (multi-seat futures)**: the hard condition keeps autosave closed until Failure Contract & Re-wake resolves the seat (provisional — Failure Contract undesigned), so the deferral is unbounded in that case. Rationale: recorded as a gap for VS-A/VS-B, not designed here.
- **If a friendly-fire death occurs (VS-B)**: the save records the `DEAD` seat's state like any death and never records blame (Anti-pillar: no PvP). Rationale: blame is Failure Contract's.
- **If a peer reconnects under a different transport id**: nothing in any save changes — blocks are keyed by PlayerId (SL-20). Rationale: "keyed by peer id" is a stale token.
- **If `TICK_RATE` differs between the build that wrote a file and the build reading it**: INCOMPATIBLE at the `tick_rate` step (`SL_PRECHECK_TICK_RATE_MISMATCH`); the kernel's `TICK_RATE_MISMATCH` is the unreachable backstop. Rationale: a save is bound to the rate that wrote it.

### Versioning and migration

- **If a golden v1 save is loaded by a build whose Graph migrates v1 to v2**: the mode is REBASED (cause 1), the load succeeds, the recomputed hash becomes the baseline in `campaign.json`, and the next autosave writes the new shape; the old file is not rewritten. Rationale: a downgrade followed by an upgrade must not half-migrate a file (SL-14).
- **If a save from a development build predates the first build given to anyone outside the project**: it may be refused with "made by an incompatible development build" — migration is an obligation only from that build on (SL-14). Rationale: the policy is Save/Load's own; the Layout open question that the Graph cites is unverified (OQ-12).
- **If a new persisted block is registered while dev saves exist** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*: every older dev save now lacks a mandatory block and carries a different `registry_epoch`, so each is INCOMPATIBLE-dev ("made by an incompatible development build"), not CORRUPT, and none is quarantined (T-5 step 21). Rationale: otherwise each new block would quarantine every dev save as damaged and hide it from the owner.
- **If a new persisted block is added after the first outside build**: `format_version` increments, `min_reader_version` equals the new version, the container migration is the identity and the new block is filled by `default_state()` (SL-14). Rationale: the friction of adding a block stays constant.

### Degenerate strategies and accepted exposures

- **If the player kills the process (task manager, power loss) after a mistake** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: Alt-F4 and the title-bar close are not kills; they raise the window-close request and take the Save & Quit path, SL-6)*: the game falls back to the newest generation, a rewind of at most `AUTOSAVE_INTERVAL_TICKS + AUTOSAVE_MAX_DEFER_TICKS` = 18 000 ticks (mean about 5 400 ticks, 10 800 at most when no soft input defers the save, 18 600 on the narrow recovery-at-the-cap path, F-7; the user accepted this fault loss window, D55) — the same price as dying, minus the death floor (49 ticks with the give-up, 1,801 on the timer) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*. Accepted and unpreventable (no write can protect against a kill); measured by M3 below (OQ-25). Rationale: both rewinds cost at most the minutes since the last autosave and nothing else.
- **If the player Save & Quits before a risky door, relaunches and Continues** *(Amended 2026-10-26 — CD-GDD-ALIGN)*: it works as a retry of the player's inputs, and because the physical world is deterministic it can also reveal what is behind the door for up to the minutes since the last autosave. Accepted for the MVP as a bounded exposure (the user's death rule is binding; the Zomboid comparison does not carry over, because a Zomboid death is final), **but with a measurable trigger, not an open-ended "if playtest shows"**: metric M3 below. The interval is not the remedy, because a `QUIT` generation is written at the quit: the response is to keep the metric and escalate to Failure Contract & Re-wake, never deleting the `QUIT` generation (OQ-25) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*. Rationale: SL-6.
- **If the player dies on purpose to rewind a bad decision or to scout a room** *(Amended 2026-10-26 — CD-GDD-ALIGN)*: the cost is at least `GIVE_UP_MIN_TICKS + 1` = 49 ticks with the solo `give_up` (1,801 on the timer) plus the time back to the last autosave, the restore also returns vitals and sanity, and in a deterministic world the physical death can reveal one visible unknown (Discovery, aesthetic priority 1) for about 3 to 5.7 minutes of sim time; threat draws re-key per restore epoch (D16), so a scouted ambush does not replay *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*. Accepted for the MVP; metric M2 below is the trigger *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*. Rationale: Pillar 5's design test — safe farming versus scarcity — resolves to scarcity, as long as scouting deaths stay rare.
- **If the player dies deliberately fast to step the ring back** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*: a fall within `W_MIN_WARNING_S × TICK_RATE` ticks of a restore demotes the restored generation and restores its predecessor (the fairness route, SL-8), so a player can choose to rewind further. Accepted: each step costs a death and strictly more lost time, depth is bounded by `AUTOSAVE_GENERATIONS`, and it buys only the older state, never a better one than a normal death could reach; metrics M1 and M2 see it (`deaths_per_restored_gen` and `scout_death_rate`). Rationale: the fairness route is the ruled price of never waking into an unfair fall (X7 (b)).
- **If the player treats event saves as checkpoints (event-scum)** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*: a weld, cut, module cut, pod arrival or berth writes a generation (`EVENT_TRIGGERS`), so a player can learn that finishing a weld "saves" and plan a risky sortie around it, the bonfire pattern this document rejects. Accepted for the MVP with metric M5 as the trigger; the response is a data change (remove the offending trigger from `EVENT_TRIGGERS`) before touching the interval. Rationale: OQ-44, confirmed by the user (D17) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*.
- **If a save file is edited by hand and re-signed**: it loads if it passes the pre-check; integrity guards accidental damage, not tampering (host-owned, no PvP, no leaderboard). Rationale: documented scope of SL-16; a stricter trust model is a security-audit question before multiplayer.

**Playtest triggers for the accepted exposures** *(Amended 2026-10-26 — CD-GDD-ALIGN)*. Pillar 5's "loss costs ship and body" and Discovery (aesthetic priority 1) pull in opposite directions here, so the exposure is accepted with numbers attached. The constants below are playtest-protocol values, not data-file knobs, and are provisional starting values to be calibrated at the first outside build (AC-SL-94). `T_enter` is, at the MVP, the derelict-level first boarding `FIRST_BOARDED{derelict_id, T}` published by Web Map & Transit (WM-28; the dock room), not a compartment-level first entry, which no designed system tracks; a DIAGNOSTIC-only compartment-entry log (Debug Overlay, from Body section changes; never persisted) is the recorded refinement if M2 proves too coarse (OQ-25) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — consistency pass)*. Wall-clock gaps come from `created_unix` file metadata and the process-start log, read by the playtest harness only, never by the sim (H9).

| Metric | Definition | Starting threshold | Named response |
|---|---|---|---|
| **M1** `deaths_per_restored_gen` | Deaths whose restored generation is the same `generation`, counted per tester | Median across testers ≥ 3, or any tester ≥ 5 | Players are stuck on the same save, so death is too dear: lower `AUTOSAVE_INTERVAL_TICKS` by up to 3 600 ticks (not below 5 400, OQ-19); if M1 trips again, ask the user OQ-1 (non-`RISKY` step-back) |
| **M2** `scout_death_rate` | Deaths with `T_dead − T_enter` ≤ `SCOUT_DEATH_WINDOW_TICKS` 5 400 (provisional protocol value: the 1 800-tick `DOWN_TO_DEATH_TICKS` timer floor plus 3 600 ticks of looking; with the solo `give_up` the real floor is `GIVE_UP_MIN_TICKS`, so the window admits up to 5 352 ticks of looking, and the log records `death_via_give_up` as a separate column) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*, divided by all deaths; evaluated for a tester with ≥ 4 deaths | More than 0.25 of that tester's deaths, in at least 2 of 3 testers | Death is being used as a free look: raise `AUTOSAVE_INTERVAL_TICKS` by up to 3 600 ticks (not above 18 000) and raise `LOSS_CEILING_TICKS` by the same amount on purpose, because the defaults sit exactly on the ceiling and I9 refuses a release build above it; if M2 trips again in the next round, escalate to Failure Contract & Re-wake at VS-A |
| **M3** `quit_relaunch_rate` | Save & Quit followed by Continue within `QUIT_RELAUNCH_WINDOW_S` 120 s of wall time, divided by all `QUIT` generations | More than 0.30 of `QUIT` generations, or 3 or more such pairs inside one 30-minute session | *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* Quit-and-relaunch is a disguised manual save. Raising the interval has **no effect** on a `QUIT` generation, so it is not the response: accept the exposure for the MVP, keep the metric, and on a repeat trip escalate to Failure Contract & Re-wake at VS-A *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review, delegated ruling R1: because every restore advances `restore_epoch`, a quit-and-relaunch also re-keys the next threat draws, so M3 now also measures use of that reroll; USER DECISION at OQ-50)*; never delete the `QUIT` generation (SL-8) and never add save-anywhere (OQ-18 allows only the pinned main-menu slot, and only for M1) |
| **M4** `quit_in_tell_rate` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* | `QUIT` generations written with a Graph or Attention tell open, divided by all `QUIT` generations; read from the playtest build's write log (the gate inputs at each write, a DIAGNOSTIC log, never persisted) | More than 0.10 | The player quits inside a warning window and Continue restores a shortened warning (SL-6): ask the Graph owner for the loaded-descriptor query (OQ-38) and, if it persists, route to Failure Contract & Re-wake; never clamp the quit |
| **M6** `risky_generation_rate` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* | `RISKY` generations divided by all generations, with per-cause counts over `risky_cause` (`GRAPH_TELL`, `ATTENTION_TELL`, `BREACH_PHASE`, `SEAT_STATE`, `VITALS`, `ASSEMBLY_BREACH`) from the write log; the sheet also logs E[loss \| death] (the mean loss over the deaths that actually happened), not the unconditioned mean of F-7 | More than 0.25 of all generations, or any single cause above 0.10 | The gate has seven soft inputs, and if `VITALS` or `ASSEMBLY_BREACH` is common the effective checkpoint spacing quietly became `I + D` and step-backs fire often: lower `AUTOSAVE_MAX_DEFER_TICKS` once (not below 1 800, I2, I3), ask the Suit owner for the `CRITICAL` threshold (SE-33) or Assembly for the breach-window length; on a repeat trip escalate to Failure Contract & Re-wake at VS-A |
| **M5** `event_scum_rate` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* | Event-kind generations followed by a death, or by a Save & Quit and Continue, within `SCOUT_DEATH_WINDOW_TICKS` of the event, divided by all event-kind generations; evaluated for a tester with ≥ 4 events | More than 0.30 for at least 2 of 3 testers | Players are using events as checkpoints: remove the offending trigger from `EVENT_TRIGGERS` (a data change, OQ-44) before touching the interval |

If M1 trips together with M2 or M3, the interval cannot repair both and the response goes straight to Failure Contract & Re-wake. The ladder is deliberately short: one retune of the one lever (OQ-19), then the owner of the failure contract.

## Dependencies

**Status at authoring time (2026-10-26, a Glob of `design/gdd/`).** Designed: Command & Simulation Layer, Life-Support Graph, Player Controller & Input, Pixel-Perfect Render Pipeline & Camera, Emitter State & Light-Exposure Query, Item & Module Database. Draft in flight on disk (Status In Design): Failure & Trail System. No file at all: Ship Section & Layout Data Model, Ship Body & Interior Physics, Suit & Equipment, Attention, Modular Ship Assembly, Web Map & Transit, Sanity & Hallucination Rules, Horror Entities, Chart Table, HUD & Suit Layer, Perception Filter, Failure Contract & Re-wake, Co-op Session & Replication. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — consistency pass)* **Status refreshed 2026-10-27:** every system named above is now designed on disk except Failure Contract & Re-wake and Co-op Session & Replication; Failure & Trail System is Designed (2026-10-26); Interaction System, Boarding & Salvage, 2D Lighting & Occlusion and Debug Overlay are designed as well. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review, delegated ruling R9)* **Status re-confirmed 2026-10-27:** Failure Contract & Re-wake and Co-op Session & Replication remain **undesigned — provisional contract**, listed with the interface this GDD assumes (SL-7 stub replacement through `request_restore`; SL-20 seats); that is not a blocker.

**Upstream (Save/Load depends on):** exactly one **runtime** system — the Command & Simulation Layer — matching the index (`Depends On: Command & Simulation Layer`; bottleneck, nine dependents). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* The roughly fifteen block-owner rows below marked Hard are **design-time (contract)** edges: the owner depends on this GDD's block contract (SL-15) and Save/Load restores their blocks through the kernel, but Save/Load never calls them and they never call it.

| System | Direction | Strength | Interface | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **Command & Simulation Layer** | Save/Load depends on the kernel | **Hard** | CR-15 control plane (`request_snapshot`, `request_restore`, `request_shutdown`), CR-8 registry contract (`persist`, `persist_scope`, `schema_version`, `restore_after`, `post_restore`, `default_state`), CR-18 snapshot semantics, D.12 `state_hash`, `kernel.clock` | Designed |
| **Life-Support Graph** | Block owner; Save/Load carries its S-1 obligation (no runtime edge for the block; the index's Graph → Save/Load edge was removed). Save/Load also **reads** the Graph's `read_view.failures()` for the Graph half of the autosave tell condition *(Amended 2026-10-26 — CD-GDD-ALIGN)* | **Hard — design-time (contract)** for MVP load (the largest block); the tell read is a **runtime soft-gate input, required at MVP** | `graph.state`, S-1, `restore_after [layout.state]`, `GRAPH_RESTORE_*`, golden-save convention; LS-13, LS-14, LS-19 and D.11 (`cause`, `sev`, `warn_elapsed`, `W_MIN_STEPS`, `GRAPH_STEP_TICKS`) | Designed |
| **Player Controller & Input** | Block owner and the death trigger | **Hard** at runtime for the death stub; **design-time (contract)** for the block | `controller.state`, `loco_state` / `DEAD`, PC-17 restore rules, the boundary-time hand-off, `GIVE_UP_MIN_TICKS`, the `DOWNED` → non-`DOWNED` transition *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* | Designed |

| **Ship Section & Layout Data Model** (`layout.state`) | Block owner; the `restore_after` root of the Graph | **Hard — design-time (contract)** for load; this GDD's top risk | `layout.state` (schema 1, `restore_after []`), stable `room_id` | Designed (LY-1, LY-6) |
| **Emitter State & Light-Exposure Query** (`emitter.state`) | Block owner | Hard — design-time (contract) per block once registered | `restore_after [layout.state, graph.state, body.state, controller.state]` | Designed (EM-1) |

| **Derelict Authoring (Handmade Kits)** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* | Supplies the world content `init_world` builds; asked for an autosave at the weld | Soft | SL-4, SL-14, SL-18 | Designed |
| **Debug Overlay** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* | DIAGNOSTIC reader: one registered `save_load` channel; no block, no command | Soft | the Debug Overlay row in Interactions; registry `diag_panel_contract` | Designed |
| **Failure Contract & Re-wake** (VS-A) | Replaces the death stub | Soft | The same `request_restore` path and PROFILE store; `restore_reason` | Undesigned — provisional |
| **Co-op Session & Replication** (VS-B) | Reuses the snapshot payload; a guest save request is a command defined later | Soft | Separable payload (CR-18); `saveload.request_save` (reserved, provisional) | Undesigned — provisional |
| **Godot 4.7.2 file I/O, JSON, `var_to_bytes`, `HashingContext`, threads** | Engine | **Hard** | None covered by the engine reference except two sourced facts: `FileAccess.store_*` return `bool` (since 4.4) and packed-array element writes skip the property setter (4.7) | [UNV] — the concept's spike "save round-trip with version" |

**Downstream (systems that depend on Save/Load):** nine in the index — Suit & Equipment, Modular Ship Assembly, Web Map & Transit, Attention System, Sanity & Hallucination Rules (MVP, designed in this run), and Knowledge Codex & Logs, Failure Contract & Re-wake, Co-op Session & Replication (VS-B), Host-Owned World & Guest Carry-over (later tiers). Every downstream row is a **design-time** dependency on the contracts of this GDD (SL-15, SL-9, SL-7, SL-4) and never a runtime call; for the designed Graph and Controller the edge is moot (S-1 is carried in SL-2, PC-17 in OQ-9), and for the undesigned block owners it is real and labelled "design-time (contract)" (OQ-15). Debug Overlay, Settings & Input Rebinding and the Chart Table consume reads or the host-local API only.

**Bidirectional consistency.** The kernel's Dependencies table lists Save/Load as a **Hard** downstream registrant with "control plane, snapshot/restore semantics, `Dropped(RELOAD)`" on its side and "file format, migrations, death stub" on ours — agreed. The kernel's Interactions row 3, the Graph's Interactions and Dependencies rows, the Controller's `DEAD` row and Interactions row 15, and the Pipeline's Interactions row 17 and traceability row are quoted in Interactions with the places they need a text fix. The designed Item DB's Dependencies row for Save/Load (`catalogue_version`, `catalogue_hash` (diagnostic), stable `def_id` strings, `renamed_from[]`, `replaced_by`) agrees on content and on the field name, `catalogue_version`, which this GDD uses (history: OQ-13). The designed Emitter GDD's EM-1 adds `body.state` to `restore_after` (the sibling brief omitted it); the designed Failure & Trail GDD registers nothing. No sibling draft contradicts a rule here.

**Data Save/Load takes, and what it refuses.** It takes decoded plain data (primitives, arrays, dictionaries, packed arrays), the kernel's snapshot and hash, the Controller's `loco_state`, optional SIM read views and owner notifications. It refuses: object decoding and `Resource` / `.tres` loads in any form; `Vector2i` and the other non-H7 types (SL-2); any `PERCEPTION_OUT` input; any 64-bit value as a JSON number; any write to a block or registration of a block or hook (SL-17); any dependence on a transport peer id (SL-20) or on wall-clock in logic (SL-23); any file that fails the pre-check (SL-11).

## Tuning Knobs

Knobs live in one data file read at boot (never hardcoded, coding standard); the boot invariants below are asserted against it. **★ marks a cross-system fact** — a value or name other GDDs cite or must not contradict — and so a registry candidate for the registry stage. Tick values convert at `TICK_RATE` 60 (cited): 10 800 = 180 s, 7 200 = 120 s, 3 600 = 60 s, 1 800 = 30 s, 300 = 5 s. "Too high" and "too low" are the failure modes a player or a developer sees. Defaults for engine-facing figures are estimates until the "save round-trip with version" spike measures them [UNV].

### Owned knobs

| Knob | Default | Unit | Safe range | Too high | Too low | Interacts with |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| ★ `FORMAT_VERSION` | 1 | int | ≥ 1; bumped only on a container change | Every bump needs a container migration and a golden fixture | 0 is invalid | `min_reader_version`, every block's `added_in_format`, SL-14 |
| ★ `AUTOSAVE_GENERATIONS` | 3 | files | 2 – 5 | Disk and quarantine clutter; a longer, slower scan; deeper save-scum history without benefit | 1 forbidden: no safe swap, one bad write loses the campaign; 2 survives exactly one corrupt file | `LOAD_MAX_ATTEMPTS`, step-back depth (F-3), invariant I4 |
| ★ `AUTOSAVE_INTERVAL_TICKS` | 10 800 | ticks | 5 400 – 18 000 *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: narrowed from 3 600 – 36 000, because the old range admitted a 20-minute ceiling; a configuration above `LOSS_CEILING_TICKS` is now a release-build boot refusal, I9)* | Death is punishing (mean loss `I/2 + 1 800`), a crash rewinds far; at 18 000 the typical worst case is ~10 min; raising the interval means raising `LOSS_CEILING_TICKS` on purpose (I9) | Death is cheap — "safe farming", Pillar 5 pressure vanishes; capture hitch every minute | `loss_ticks` (F-7), `AUTOSAVE_MAX_DEFER_TICKS`, `AUTOSAVE_MIN_GAP_TICKS`; **the main lever of how much death hurts, together with the event set** (OQ-19, OQ-44) |
| ★ `AUTOSAVE_MIN_GAP_TICKS` | 1 800 | ticks | 900 – 3 600, strictly below the interval (I1) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* | Event saves never matter (effectively interval-only) | Weld and dock bursts write back to back and flush the ring in a minute | `event_due` (F-1), invariant I1; equals `DOWN_TO_DEATH_TICKS` by coincidence only |
| ★ `AUTOSAVE_RETRY_TICKS` | 300 | ticks | 60 – 900 and ≤ `AUTOSAVE_MAX_DEFER_TICKS` / 4 (I3), and a divisor of both `AUTOSAVE_MAX_DEFER_TICKS` and `W_MIN_WARNING_S` × `TICK_RATE` (at the defaults: 60, 100, 120, 150, 200, 300 or 600) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* | A recovered crew waits long for its save (up to `R − 1` ticks late) | Gate-polling churn; a tight retry after failure | `fire` grid (F-2), invariant I3 |
| ★ `AUTOSAVE_MAX_DEFER_TICKS` | 7 200 | ticks | static range 1 800 – 17 700; constraints `4 × AUTOSAVE_RETRY_TICKS ≤ D ≤ AUTOSAVE_INTERVAL_TICKS − AUTOSAVE_RETRY_TICKS`, a multiple of the retry period (I2, I3) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* | Long unsaved stretches; ceiling `loss_max` grows linearly | Many `RISKY` generations; the step-back rule fires too often | `loss_max` (F-7), invariants I2, I3 |
| ★ `DEATH_LOOP_WINDOW_TICKS` | 3 600 | ticks | 1 800 – 10 800 | An honest later death of a `RISKY` generation is mistaken for a loop | The window cannot contain the player's acting time: the step-back never fires | `generation_select` (F-3), `T_downed`; matters only for the RISKY-window route (the fairness route uses `W_MIN_WARNING_S × TICK_RATE` and is never longer, I13) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* |
| ★ `MAX_SAVE_BYTES` | 64 MiB | bytes | 4 – 256 MiB | Memory spike on a hostile or runaway file | Legitimate VS-A saves refused | `MAX_BLOCK_BYTES` (≤), invariant I5 |
| ★ `MAX_BLOCK_BYTES` | 32 MiB | bytes | 2 – 128 MiB, ≤ `MAX_SAVE_BYTES` | One block dominates memory on decode | A large block is refused | `MAX_DECODE_NODES`, I5, I6 |
| ★ `MAX_HEADER_BYTES` | 64 KiB | bytes | 16 – 256 KiB, ≥ 600 + `MAX_BLOCKS` × `BLOCK_RECORD_BYTES` (31 320 at 128 blocks) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* | Large JSON parse on hostile input | Headers refused near 128 blocks (≈ 31 KiB) | F-6, kernel `MAX_BLOCKS` (cited), I7 |
| ★ `SAVE_SIZE_ALARM_BYTES` | 2 MiB | bytes | 1 – 8 MiB, below `MAX_SAVE_BYTES` (I6) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* | Real growth goes unnoticed before VS-A | Alarm noise | Compression review (`codec`), I6 |
| ★ `LOADING_TARGET_MS` | 1 000 (provisional) | ms | 500 – 2 000, above the Pipeline's fade | The black hold reads as a crash (over ~2 s) | Meaningless below the 120 ms fade; a spurious log | `restart_ms` (F-8), I8; Game Feel |
| ★ `QUARANTINE_AFTER_FAILURES` | 2 | restore failures | 1 – 3 | Repeated `FAULTED` loops on one bad file | One transient fault quarantines a good file | SL-22; counts **owner-reported** restore failures only, never a reproduction mismatch |
| ★ `REBASE_BLOCKS` (provisional) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* | `[interaction.state, assembly.state]` | list of `block_id` | empty list … the two defaults | A longer list weakens the reproduction stage: every real save verifies REBASED while any listed block is present | Empty while Interaction or Assembly still clear transients in `post_restore`: a routine quit mid-weld verifies `NOT_REPRODUCED` and dev builds halt | SL-12, F-4, OQ-9, OQ-39; the Controller normalises in `snapshot()` (PC-17) and `body.state` is restore-idempotent (BDY-31), so neither is listed (AC-SL-67 proves it); **emptied when the Interaction and Assembly owners move their clears into `snapshot()`**, and must be empty at the first outside build; a non-empty list is a known kernel-contract debt |
| `SL_QUARANTINE_SIZE_ALARM_BYTES` | 16 MiB | bytes | 1 – 256 MiB | Slow disk growth unreported | Alarm noise | Diagnostics threshold **only**; quarantined files are never deleted automatically; deletion is the explicit campaign delete under hold-to-confirm |
| `gate_tell_mode` | `FAIR` | enum | {`FAIR`, `STRICT`} | `STRICT` defers up to a whole Attention tell (≤ `TELL_MAX_TICKS` 1 800 ticks, Attention's, cited) and raises the loss tail | `FAIR` allows a save with exactly the guaranteed warning (600 ticks) left | `attention_tell_block` (F-2), `W_MIN_WARNING_S` (cited); **governs the Attention half only** and needs the Attention read view `attention.view` (designed; absent = open); the Graph half has no mode — it defers for the whole open window — *(Amended 2026-10-26 — CD-GDD-ALIGN)* |
| `GATE_SOFT_STATES` | {`CHANNELLING`, `STAGGERED`, `IN_POD`} | set | any subset; **never** `DOWNED` or `DEAD` | More deferral and more `RISKY` saves | Saves restore mid-transit and lose channel progress (PC-17) | F-2, invariant I10 |

| `RENAME_RETRY_COUNT` | 3 | retries | 0 – 10 | The worker is busy long on a locked file | An antivirus lock becomes a failed save | `RENAME_RETRY_DELAY_MS`; a worker stuck in a rename, flush or re-read is bounded by abandonment, not by this knob (SL-10, `DEATH_STUB_WRITE_WAIT_MS`) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* |
| `RENAME_RETRY_DELAY_MS` | 50 | ms | 10 – 250 | A slow failure report | Spinning on a locked file | worker only; monotonic millisecond source (SL-23) |

| `READ_RETRY_DELAY_MS` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* | 100 | ms | 10 – 250 | A slow fallback | Spinning on a locked file | `READ_RETRY_COUNT`; monotonic millisecond source (SL-23) |

| `SAVE_HITCH_BUDGET_MS` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* [PLACEHOLDER] | 4 | ms | 1 – 8 | A capture frame is visibly longer than its neighbours (a stutter that tells the player when the game saved) | A healthy capture frame fails the budget | F-5; AC-SL-87, AC-SL-91, AC-SL-131; the spike replaces it with 16.7 − P99(baseline CPU frame work) |

| `MAX_LIVE_ABANDONED_JOBS` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* [PLACEHOLDER] | 2 | jobs | 1 – 4 | Leaked worker threads accumulate behind a stuck disk | A single slow healthy write that was abandoned blocks the next autosave with `SL_WRITE_TOO_MANY_ABANDONED` | SL-10 thread model; `WRITE_JOB_TIMEOUT_MS`; AC-SL-90, AC-SL-147 |

The id stays in this table so the registry stage and every reader see the retirement; no consumer may reintroduce it |
| `SUSPECT_THRESHOLD` | 3 | reproduction failures | 2 – 5 | Defects stay silent | False alarms | Diagnostics escalation **only**; dev/test halts on the first occurrence regardless (SL-12) |

An event whose owner read is absent never fires. `SALVAGE_CUT` is the registered label for the module-cut read `last_salvage_T()` (BS-21); a RAGGED cut's `ASSEMBLY_CUT` is withheld while `ragged_breach_open()` is true (D54) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*; `ASSEMBLY_WELD` / `ASSEMBLY_CUT` are registered (the weld/cut poll of `last_shape_change_T`); `POD_ARRIVED` and `BERTHED` are provisional until merge | subset | any subset | Write churn (bounded by the minimum gap); a learnable checkpoint grammar (OQ-44, metric M5) | Interval-only saves (Assembly and Derelict Authoring ask for a weld save, OQ-DA-41) | SL-4, F-1 |

### Invariants (asserted at boot against the data file)

A violation fails Save/Load's own boot naming the invariant (`SL_BOOT_KNOB_INVARIANT{id}`, composition-root mechanism as in SL-15); the range check (`SL_BOOT_KNOB_RANGE{knob}`, OQ-28, decided) runs **first**, so no invariant ever divides by an out-of-range knob; I9 is a boot refusal in a release build and a logged diagnostic in dev and test *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*.

| Id | Invariant | Why |
|---|---|---|
| I1 | `AUTOSAVE_MIN_GAP_TICKS` < `AUTOSAVE_INTERVAL_TICKS` | Otherwise events could be due more often than the interval |
| I2 | `AUTOSAVE_MAX_DEFER_TICKS` + `AUTOSAVE_RETRY_TICKS` ≤ `AUTOSAVE_INTERVAL_TICKS` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* | Otherwise a deferred save collides with the next interval |
| I3 *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* | `AUTOSAVE_RETRY_TICKS` ≤ `AUTOSAVE_MAX_DEFER_TICKS` / 4 and `AUTOSAVE_MAX_DEFER_TICKS` mod `AUTOSAVE_RETRY_TICKS` = 0 **and (`W_MIN_WARNING_S` × `TICK_RATE`) mod `AUTOSAVE_RETRY_TICKS` = 0** | The cap must land on the retry grid, and so must the refractory: otherwise `R_g` rounds above the refractory (R = 400, 450, 720, 900 give 800, 900, 720, 900 against 600), `loss_max_stepback` exceeds `LOSS_CEILING_TICKS` and a release build refuses to boot on a value the knob table called safe. The boot check (AC-SL-38) enforces it |
| I4 | `AUTOSAVE_GENERATIONS` ≥ 2 | A safe swap and a fallback need two |
| I5 | `MAX_BLOCK_BYTES` ≤ `MAX_SAVE_BYTES` | A block cannot exceed its file |
| I6 | `SAVE_SIZE_ALARM_BYTES` < `MAX_SAVE_BYTES` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* | The alarm is on file bytes, so it must lie below the file cap to be able to fire (re-based from `MAX_BLOCK_BYTES`, the wrong quantity) |
| I7 | `MAX_HEADER_BYTES` ≥ 600 + `MAX_BLOCKS` × `BLOCK_RECORD_BYTES` (31 320 at the kernel's 128 and 240) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* | A full registry must fit |
| I8 | `LOADING_TARGET_MS` > the Pipeline's black-hold fade (120 ms, cited) | Otherwise the target is below what the player sees |
| I9 | `loss_max_stepback` (F-7) ≤ `LOSS_CEILING_TICKS` | A change that raises the worst case must be deliberate: a boot refusal for shipped data (release build), a logged diagnostic in dev and test *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* |
| I10 | `GATE_SOFT_STATES` ∩ {`DOWNED`, `DEAD`} = ∅ | A listed hard state would let the cap write a downed world |
| I11 *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* | `LOAD_MAX_ATTEMPTS` ≥ `AUTOSAVE_GENERATIONS` | A smaller bound strands an intact ring: a load would give up before reaching a good generation |
| I12 *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* | `AUTOSAVE_FAIL_BACKOFF_MAX_TICKS` mod `AUTOSAVE_RETRY_TICKS` = 0 | The backoff delays (`R × 2^min(n−1, 16)`, capped) must land on the retry grid, or `next_retry_T` and `waited mod R` disagree (F-2) |
| I13 *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* | `W_MIN_WARNING_S × TICK_RATE` ≤ `DEATH_LOOP_WINDOW_TICKS` | The fairness route's window must not exceed the RISKY window, or `loss_max_stepback` (F-7) would understate it. The Graph's range for `W_MIN_WARNING_S` (4 – 20 s, 240 – 1 200 ticks) lies below the window's floor of 1 800, so only a retune of either can break it |

### Cited knobs (owned elsewhere — never restated with another value)

| Knob | Value as cited | Owner and source of truth | Used by |
|---|---|---|---|
| `TICK_RATE` | 60 Hz (registry, set-once) | Kernel | Header check, every tick/second conversion |
| `DOWN_TO_DEATH_TICKS` | 1 800 (registry; owner range 600 – 3 600) | Controller | F-3, F-5, F-7; the stub's observation (SL-7) |
| `W_MIN_WARNING_S` | 10 s (registry; range 4 – 20) | Graph | `attention_tell_block` (F-2) and the `W_MIN_STEPS` derivation below |
| `TELL_MAX_TICKS` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — consistency pass)* | 1 800 ticks (registry) | Attention | `tell_remaining` range (F-2); the `gate_tell_mode` STRICT bound |
| `W_MIN_STEPS` *(Amended 2026-10-26 — CD-GDD-ALIGN)* | 100 (Graph D.11: `W_MIN_WARNING_S × TICK_RATE / GRAPH_STEP_TICKS`, calculated at boot; range 40 – 200) | Graph | `graph_tell_block` (F-2): `d.warn_elapsed < W_MIN_STEPS` |
| `GRAPH_STEP_TICKS` *(Amended 2026-10-26 — CD-GDD-ALIGN)* | 6 ticks (registry; equals the Graph's hook divisor) | Graph | `graph_tell_remaining` (F-2) |
| `HOST_PEER_ID` | 1 (registry) | Kernel | Single-player identity (SL-20) |
| `GIVE_UP_MIN_TICKS` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* | 48 at the Controller's default in hold mode (`ceil(H_give_up × TICK_RATE)`; registered in the registry) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — consistency pass after design review)* | Controller | F-7 `loss_min`; SL-7 (a busy write job is abandoned after a give-up) |
| `CONFIRM_HOLD_S`, `DELETE_HOLD_S` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* | 0.8 s (registry; range 0.6 – 1.9 s, player-adjustable) and 2 × `CONFIRM_HOLD_S` = 1.6 s (a proposed name; Default applied 2026-10-27, pending user confirmation: D81) | Controller | UI-3 (quit without saving) and UI-5 (delete) hold durations; OQ-32 |
| `SNAPSHOT_BUDGET_MS` | 16.7 ms (kernel GDD value, range 8 – 33; kernel registry candidate) | Kernel | F-5 |
| `MAX_BLOCKS` | 128 (kernel GDD value; kernel registry candidate) | Kernel | F-6, I7, T-5 block table step |
| Graph snapshot and restore cost | ≤ 0.5 ms and ≤ 32 KB; restore + `post_restore` ≤ 2 ms | Graph | F-5, F-6, F-8 |
| `TRANSIT_HOLD` fade | 120 ms | Pipeline | F-8, I8, Game Feel |
| `STEP_BUDGET_MS`, `MAX_DIRTY_KEYS` | 4 ms, 256 (registry) | Kernel | Not Save/Load's; it registers no hook and no dirty key (SL-17) |

### Considered and not made knobs

`QUARANTINE_MAX_FILES` (ruled out: quarantined files are never deleted automatically); *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: the earlier ruling that a restore-wait timeout is not a knob is reversed — `DEATH_STUB_WRITE_WAIT_MS` is now an owned knob, because the stub no longer joins the writer, SL-10)* `HOT_COPY_ENABLED` and `PRESTAGE_ON_DOWNED` (no longer alternatives but MVP mechanisms, SL-11; they are not switches, so not knobs; OQ-23 records the spike measurement); `format_epoch`, a separate `SAVE_AUDIO_CUE`, any glyph colour and any per-write glyph or its hold time (rejected or retired: SL-21, SL-24, UI-1); a `GRAPH_TELL_MODE` (not made: the Graph window is the guarantee, so the Graph half has nothing to tune); the playtest-trigger constants `SCOUT_DEATH_WINDOW_TICKS` 5 400 and `QUIT_RELAUNCH_WINDOW_S` 120 and the M1 to M6 thresholds (playtest-protocol values in Edge Cases, calibrated at the first outside build, not data-file knobs). *(Amended 2026-10-26 — CD-GDD-ALIGN)* A separate fairness-window or refractory knob *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* (not made: user ruling X7 fixes both at `W_MIN_WARNING_S × TICK_RATE`, so a second value could diverge from the Graph's guarantee; they are an expression, not a value, and I13 guards the one relation that matters).

## Visual/Audio Requirements

Save/Load owns no art and no sound. Its whole visible footprint is two things drawn by other systems from the reads it publishes (SL-21), plus one thing the player must never see. This section records what is visible, what is audible, who renders it, and which art-bible rules bind it, so the owners of those surfaces inherit the constraints instead of rediscovering them.

**What the player can see.**

| Surface | When | Rendered by | Binding rules |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Failed-write notice** *(Amended 2026-10-26 — CD-GDD-ALIGN)* (a plain, non-modal mark on the suit panel; shape and placement are not designed here) | Only after `AUTOSAVE_FAIL_ESCALATE_COUNT` (3) consecutive failed writes (published as `fail_escalated`, SL-21); persists until the next `WRITTEN` | HUD & Suit Layer (designed — hud-and-suit-layer.md; shape and placement are HS-46's), under art bible 7.0 (the HUD is a suit) | HR-1: it lives on the HUD layer, never dims, fades or flickers with world light, Attention or sanity. It takes **no Flesh** (`#D9A58C`, `#B5705C`) and **no Violet** (`#8A3FD1`, `#B07BE6`): those hues are reserved for the player's sanity leaking into the world (art bible 4.4), and a mark that could take them would be a lie the player could not tell from a leak (SL-24, H5). It is never a hallucination class: a false "failed" would be an unfalsifiable lie on the one channel that must stay true. It sits on an opaque Ground plate (HR-2) and counts inside the HUD's roughly 1 % non-Ground area cap (7.0). It is tied to write health, never to the gate: it looks and behaves identically whether the gate is deferring or not, and it says nothing about when a save was or will be taken |
| **Newest-record line** *(Amended 2026-10-26 — CD-GDD-ALIGN)* (main-menu Continue screen only) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* | While the main-menu Continue screen is open | Chart Table (designed — chart-table-web-map-and-menus.md), through `/ux-design` | One SIM fact: the playtime of the newest generation (`SaveInfo.playtime`), read once at process start. It is **not** on the pause panel: pausing is free, so a value that changed between writes would let a player infer that a write is overdue and a tell is open, the hidden gate meter UI-1 was retired to prevent. No countdown, no "time since", no per-write mark, no gate state. Ground plate; no Flesh or Violet; never a hallucination class |
| **Layer-1 black hold** during `LOADING` | From the `DEAD` tick (death stub) or the Continue press to the first `RUNNING` frame; bounded by `LOADING_TARGET_MS` (1 000, provisional) | Pixel-Perfect Render Pipeline (`TRANSIT_HOLD`, a 120 ms fade) | Plain black on layer 1. No death screen, no text, no beat: the art bible's 2.10 "last light going out" sequence is written for the cryo re-wake and belongs to Failure Contract & Re-wake (VS-A, provisional — undesigned); the death stub does **not** render it, and must stay retire-able with no presentation change beyond what VS-A adds |
| **First `RUNNING` frame after a load** | The frame after the hold | Pipeline (camera `CUT` at the pre-load `k_z`), Perception Filter (designed) | The frame must already be filtered for the restored sanity level (SL-24, H4): the black hold covers any rebuild, and a single unfiltered frame is a free oracle about a hallucination. This is a routed request (OQ-11), not a Save/Load render. After a death-stub restore the HUD adds the one plain ship-log line of UI-11 on a Ground plate (Save/Load supplies only `restore_reason` and the restored generation's playtime; no Flesh or Violet, never a hallucination class, H5) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* |
| **Menu and pause surfaces**: Save & Quit, Continue, New campaign, the hold-to-confirm "quit without saving", the corrupt-ring, no-loadable-save and incompatible-build messages | On the pause panel and main menu | Chart Table (designed — chart-table-web-map-and-menus.md), through `/ux-design` | Art bible 7.4: the pause panel is a right-third side panel on Ground over the frozen world, and **the world is not dimmed, blurred or desaturated** under it (dimming is a mask on the world, blur is a lens lie, desaturation is the sanity convention 4.4 rejected). Hold-to-confirm fills a **linear bar with a needle-mark**, never a radial wipe (7.3, HR-14). Cuts and short fades of 80 – 120 ms, no slides or bounces (7.4). Selection is the 7.2 bracket plus bezel thickening, never a Tungsten fill (7.4 menu components) |

**What the player never sees.** No save-list **thumbnail** (a screenshot would capture the Perception Filter's lie, Pillar 1), no ring, spinner or percentage (a "saving…" meter would turn persistence into infrastructure in the player's face and contradict "shown in the world, never a meter"), no **death screen** (art bible 7.4 and 2.10: the alternative "a death screen with text" was considered and rejected), no "progress saved" toast, no completion or progress indicator on a campaign (no win state). **No per-write autosave mark of any kind** *(Amended 2026-10-26 — CD-GDD-ALIGN)*: writes are held by the gate (an active tell, a breach phase that is not calm, a seat in `CHANNELLING`, `STAGGERED`, `IN_POD` or `DOWNED`), so a mark that appeared on every write would turn its absence into a HUD signal that danger is building and, once Attention publishes its breach phase, into a hidden Attention meter (P3: "shown in the world, never a meter"). The glyph and `STATUS_WRITTEN_HOLD_TICKS` are retired; only the write-health notice above remains. The campaign list shows only `SaveInfo` facts computed from SIM blocks: campaign id, generation kind, playtime, crew count and a depth label (provisional — derived from the node `ring` of `web.view`); `RISKY` is not shown (user ruling D82) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*.

**What the player can hear.** **Nothing, in the MVP.** There is no save chime, no write tick, no failure sting and no load sound. Reason: any sound is attributable by the web (Pillar 3), and silence is the correct cue for an event the player should not perceive at all. If the Audio design later justifies a cue, it enters through the Audio GDD, never as a Save/Load rule, and must not be confusable with a hallucinated sound (Pillar 4). The black hold is silent in this GDD; whether ambient audio ducks across `LOADING` is the Audio and Pipeline owners' call.

**Strings.** The few player-facing strings this system sources (corrupt ring, incompatible build, no loadable save, failed write, quit held, the death-restore record line *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*) are plain, factual and low-affect, in a ship-log register: no exclamation marks, no emoji, no second-person death phrase, no colour from the sane or insane palette (Player Fantasy, tone). Final copy is the UX designer's and the narrative director's.

**Art bible sections that apply:** 2.10 (Crew loss — explicitly not rendered here), 4.4 (the sanity palette: Flesh and Violet reserved), 7.0 (HUD-as-suit, ~1 % area cap), 7.3 (linear hold-to-confirm bar, three motions only), 7.4 (menus: world not dimmed; no death screen), 7.7 HR-1 (HUD exemption) and HR-14 (gamepad completeness, hold-to-confirm for destructive actions). Retained-screenshot evidence for each touched screen is AC-SL-92 and AC-SL-117.

📌 **Asset Spec** — Visual/Audio requirements are defined. After the art bible is approved, run `/asset-spec system:save-load-and-versioned-format` to produce per-asset visual descriptions, dimensions, and generation prompts from this section.

## Game Feel

The player never feels a save system; they feel what it guarantees (Player Fantasy: trust without a safety net). The feel of this system is therefore a set of **absences and one half-second**, each with an owner and a measurable target. Save/Load owns the timing of the two moments below; it owns no animation, sound or camera move.

**1. A save is imperceptible.** *Feel:* the world runs continuously and the ship "just exists". *Target:* the main-thread cost Save/Load adds to the kernel's capture (`t_file_encode + t_handoff`, F-5) keeps the main-thread sum `main_hitch_ms` inside one frame at 60 Hz (`SNAPSHOT_BUDGET_MS` 16.7, cited); the file write, flush, re-read and rename run off the critical path (SL-10); no frame within ±2 frames of a capture start has CPU frame work above the baseline P99 plus `SAVE_HITCH_BUDGET_MS` (AC-SL-91; an absolute 16.7 ms cap cannot hold when the frame already spends 8 to 12 ms), and the CPU-work trace around a write is within `SAVE_HITCH_BUDGET_MS` / 2 between a gate-open run and a gate-deferring run (AC-SL-131) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*. *What would feel wrong:* a stutter every three minutes that teaches the player when the game saved — it would turn every autosave into a small event and hand the player a rhythm to exploit — and, for the same reason, any per-write mark: there is none, because a mark shown on every write and held by the gate would make its absence a signal that danger is building (P2, P3) *(Amended 2026-10-26 — CD-GDD-ALIGN)*. *Owner:* the kernel owns capture cost; Save/Load owns the added encode and hand-off; no system owns a glyph, because none exists.

**2. A death is a breath of black, then recognition.** *Feel:* "death is a bitch. It happens." The loss lands in the body of the game, not in an interface: the avatar goes `DOWNED` for up to 30 seconds (`DOWN_TO_DEATH_TICKS` 1 800, the Controller's grief window and the real tension; the solo player may end it sooner with `give_up`, user ruling D7 *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*), then on the `DEAD` tick the stub restores. The player sees layer-1 black for **at least 120 ms** (the `TRANSIT_HOLD` fade; shorter reads as a flash or a glitch) and **at most about 2 s** (longer reads as a crash); `LOADING_TARGET_MS` 1 000 (provisional) is the budget inside that corridor, measured as `restart_ms` (F-8). The first `RUNNING` frame is a hard camera `CUT` at the **pre-load zoom** (`k_z` is kept), so the player's framing is unchanged and recognition is instant: *I am back at the weld. The ship is exactly as I made it. I know what is in that room now.* *What would feel wrong:* a death screen, a sting, a "you died" line (the plain record line of UI-11 is the one permitted line: it states a time, not a death, user ruling D15), a lights-out beat (2.10 is for the cryo re-wake, VS-A), a zoom reset, a menu asking which save to load, or any penalty beyond the lost minutes — each would turn consequence into ceremony or punishment. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* A restore must also not release a burst of catch-up ticks on its first frame: the tick driver's wall-time accumulator is discarded on entry to `RUNNING` (K-5, routed to the kernel owner, OQ-45; AC-SL-132).

**3. The cost of dying is a number the designer can feel.** The player pays the minutes since the last autosave and nothing else: at the defaults the mean is about 7 200 ticks (2 minutes), each death discards at most 20 400 ticks (5.7 minutes) on a healthy writer and 24 000 after one step-back (for a death whose `DOWNED` episode is the first since the last capture; each revive-and-fall-again adds up to 1 799, F-7) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*, and a step-back chain can cost 39 000 (10.8 minutes) of reached progress (F-7) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*. `AUTOSAVE_INTERVAL_TICKS` is the main lever of how much death hurts, together with the event set (OQ-19, OQ-44): too cheap and "safe farming" returns, which Pillar 5 rejects ("scarcity that pushes inward"); too dear and death becomes a quit. These are playtest-owned numbers, measured by the 3-person kill/continue playtest (AC-SL-94) against the triggers M1 to M6 in Edge Cases, not design of consequence.

**4. The refusal, not the rescue.** The autosave gate (SL-5) is felt only as an absence: the game never records a moment it could have warned the player about, because a world in a `DOWNED` state, in the ten seconds after a recovery, with a critical suit or with a Graph warning window still open, is never recorded *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*. The player does **not** feel rescued — the save never buys back a mistake (no save-anywhere, SL-4) and the physical world's draws are restored exactly, so the room that killed them kills them again if they walk the same path (SL-23); threat draws re-key per restore (D16), so the ambush does not *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*. Hallucination interplay *(Amended 2026-10-26 — CD-GDD-ALIGN)*: a reload is never a cure (H3) and Save/Load adds no window onto truth; whether a false state replays at the same moment is Sanity's decision, with the constraint that death and quit offer no reroll oracle (H2, OQ-37).

**5. Failure is quiet and honest.** A failed write shows the plain `FAILED` notice only after `AUTOSAVE_FAIL_ESCALATE_COUNT` (3) consecutive failures; a corrupt newest generation is a one-line ship-log message and a fall back to the one before it; an incompatible build refuses to touch the campaign. None of these ever frightens: the system's tone is the opposite of the world's.

**Who owns the feel.** Black-hold timing, fade and camera `CUT`: Pixel-Perfect Render Pipeline & Camera (cited). The `DOWNED` grief window (30 s, or the player's own `give_up`): Player Controller & Input. The UI-11 record line: HUD & Suit Layer *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*. The failure notice: HUD & Suit Layer (designed — hud-and-suit-layer.md); the main-menu newest-record line: Chart Table (designed). The re-wake beat that will replace the bare stub: Failure Contract & Re-wake (VS-A, provisional — undesigned). The filter on the first frame: Perception Filter (designed; OQ-11). Save/Load owns the numbers in this section that are its own: `LOADING_TARGET_MS`, `AUTOSAVE_INTERVAL_TICKS`, and `AUTOSAVE_MAX_DEFER_TICKS` (the written-hold of the status read is retired). Evidence: AC-SL-84, AC-SL-91, AC-SL-93, AC-SL-131 and AC-SL-132 (retained screenshots and frame-time logs, lead sign-off).

## UI Requirements

Save/Load draws nothing itself. It publishes host-local reads and a host-local API (SL-21); every surface below is **drawn and laid out by another system** (HUD & Suit Layer and Chart Table, both designed — hud-and-suit-layer.md, chart-table-web-map-and-menus.md) under the art bible's HUD-as-suit grammar (7.0), the menu rules (7.4) and the hard requirements HR-1 and HR-14. This section is the requirements list those UX specs inherit. The player-facing vocabulary is fixed: **Continue**, **Save & Quit**, **New campaign**; never "load game", "slot", "checkpoint", "respawn", "save point", "game over", "you died", "progress saved" or "quicksave" (Player Fantasy, tone).

| # | Surface | Data it reads or calls | Requirements | Owner of the layout |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| UI-1 | ~~**Autosave glyph** (suit panel)~~ **RETIRED** *(Amended 2026-10-26 — CD-GDD-ALIGN)* | — | Retired. No per-write glyph in the MVP. The glyph appeared on every write while writes are held by the autosave gate (an active tell, a breach phase that is not calm, a seat in `CHANNELLING`, `STAGGERED`, `IN_POD` or `DOWNED`), so a due autosave with no glyph would tell the HUD wearer that danger was building, a hidden Attention meter against P3 and against "You never see the save". Not renumbered; its replacements are the UI-2 newest-record line and the UI-10 failed-write notice | — |
| UI-2 | **Pause panel: Save & Quit** | `save_and_quit()`; `save_status` | Right-third side panel on Ground over the frozen world, which is **not** dimmed, blurred or desaturated (7.4). Shows **no save fact** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*: the newest-record line is on the main-menu Continue screen only (UI-4), because pausing is free and a line that changed between writes would show that a write is overdue, the hidden gate meter UI-1 was retired to prevent. The pause panel's content is identical across any number of autosave periods, whether the gate is open or deferring (AC-SL-133) *(Amended 2026-10-26 — CD-GDD-ALIGN)*. One action writes a `QUIT` generation then exits. While any bound seat is `DOWNED` the action writes nothing and routes to UI-3 (`SL_QUIT_NO_WRITE_DOWNED`). A held or failed write keeps the panel and shows the plain `FAILED` flag with retry and UI-3 as the two ways out (SL-6) | Chart Table |
| UI-3 | **Hold-to-confirm "quit without saving"** | Result `SL_QUIT_NO_WRITE_DOWNED`, or a held write (`SAVE_QUIT_TIMEOUT_MS`) | Destructive: fills a **linear bar with a needle-mark**, never a radial wipe (7.3, HR-14); gamepad-complete; the string states that the newest autosave will be used (Continue restores the newest verified generation); hold = `CONFIRM_HOLD_S` (0.8 s at the default), adjustable by the player, with a two-tap alternative (X10, D80; Default applied 2026-10-27, pending user confirmation) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* | Chart Table |
| UI-4 | **Main menu: Continue / New campaign** | `campaign_info()` (newest `SaveInfo`); `continue_campaign()`; `new_game(world_seed)`; `list_saves()` | One Continue for the one logical autosave — the generation ring is invisible and the player never chooses a generation. `SaveInfo` shows only SIM facts: playtime, crew count, kind, depth label (provisional); `RISKY` is not on the Continue line (Resolved 2026-10-27 — user ruling (design-review decisions): D82; the read keeps the field for diagnostics) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*. The **newest-record line** (the playtime of the newest generation) lives here, read once at process start *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*. **No thumbnail**, no completion or progress figure, no "last played" derived from wall clock beyond file metadata | Chart Table |
| UI-5 | **New campaign over an existing one** | `retire_campaign(id)`, `delete_campaign(id)` | Default action is **retire** (reversible); **delete** is a separate explicit action under hold-to-confirm on a linear bar (HR-14), held for `DELETE_HOLD_S` = 2 × `CONFIRM_HOLD_S` (1.6 s at the default; adjustable, with a two-tap alternative; Default applied 2026-10-27, pending user confirmation: D80, D81) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*. Quarantined files are removed only by delete | Chart Table |
| UI-6 | **Load screen / black hold** | `restore_reason`; kernel `LOADING` | Layer-1 black via `TRANSIT_HOLD`; no text, no tip, no progress; no death screen; the first `RUNNING` frame is a camera `CUT` (Pipeline). `restore_reason` is offered to the HUD, Pipeline and Failure Contract as a request; no MVP consumer may alter hallucination content from `DEATH_STUB` (SL-21, SL-24) | Pipeline; Failure Contract (VS-A) |
| UI-7 | **Corrupt-ring notice** | A load that fell back from a CORRUPT generation; on the main menu, `campaign_info().newest_failed_verification` (ASK-CT-5) | One plain ship-log line (placeholder: "Newest record unreadable. Continuing from the one before it."), shown once on the next `RUNNING` frame or on the menu; no alarm styling (SL-11); when it shows on the first `RUNNING` frame it takes the `FIRST_FRAME_LINE` slot and UI-11 is skipped *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* | Chart Table (main menu, when the flag is set) / HUD & Suit Layer (first `RUNNING` frame otherwise) |
| UI-8 | **`SL_LOAD_NO_LOADABLE_SAVE` screen** | Published reason; shown by the Chart Table as a boot-flow screen; the kernel is not `RUNNING` and no pause is requested (CT-25; in-session death-stub teardown, OQ-42) | A plain statement that no readable record exists, with New campaign and Quit, default focus on New campaign (D83; Default applied 2026-10-27, pending user confirmation; the same default as the Chart Table's own UI-9, OQ-CT-32, which is its to confirm; retire-before-delete, UI-5, is what makes it safe); no file is deleted and no autosave of the dead world occurs (SL-7) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* | Chart Table |
| UI-9 | **Incompatible-build message** | `INCOMPATIBLE` class; campaign `READ_ONLY` | Placeholder: "This save was made by an incompatible build."; offers Quit and New campaign, default focus on New campaign (D83, Default applied 2026-10-27, pending user confirmation) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*; never offers to overwrite or "repair" (SL-11, T-9) | Chart Table |
| UI-10 | **Failed-write notice** | `fail_escalated` (true after `AUTOSAVE_FAIL_ESCALATE_COUNT` (3) consecutive failures; `save_status` reads `FAILED` on every one) | Tied to write health only (consecutive failed writes), never to the gate, and the only autosave-related mark on the HUD in the MVP. Plain, persistent, non-modal; names no cause beyond "the game could not write its record"; cleared by the next `WRITTEN` | HUD & Suit Layer (shape and placement, HS-46) |
| UI-11 *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* | **Death-restore line** | `restore_reason` (`DEATH_STUB`); the restored generation's playtime (`SaveInfo.playtime`, `T div TICK_RATE`) | One plain ship-log line on the first `RUNNING` frame after a death-stub restore, through the HUD's `FIRST_FRAME_LINE` channel and its dwell (`UI7_DWELL_S`); placeholder "Restored from the record at 2:01:30." (final copy is the UX designer's and the narrative director's). **No death wording** ("you died", "game over", "respawn"), no sound, no dim-out, no death counter and no cost figure: it states the time of the record and nothing else, so the player can see what the rewind was. Not shown after Continue (`LOAD_GAME`; the menu already showed the playtime), `NEW_GAME` or `FAULT_RECOVERY`; skipped when UI-7 takes the slot. On a step-back it shows the older time, which is the cost made legible and reveals nothing the player did not cause. Ground plate; no Flesh or Violet; never a hallucination class (H5); the pause panel is unchanged (AC-SL-133) | HUD & Suit Layer (`FIRST_FRAME_LINE`) |

**Rules that bind every surface.** No thumbnail, no ring or percentage, no meter, no audio cue, no death screen, no palette hue from the sanity channel; all destructive actions are hold-to-confirm on a linear bar; every action is reachable by gamepad (HR-14); strings are plain, factual and low-affect with no exclamation mark; none of these surfaces is ever a hallucination class (Pillar 4: at least one channel of truth remains, and the save UI is that channel). The pause panel's physical look, the hold duration (not a number in the art bible — OQ-32) and the final copy are the Chart Table's `/ux-design` and the narrative director's, not this GDD's.

**Accessibility.** Save/Load adds no timing-critical input: the hold-to-confirm duration must be adjustable by the accessibility options (`design/accessibility-requirements.md`), hold-to-confirm (UI-3, UI-5) gets a two-tap alternative, the accessibility tier is standard (X10, D80; Default applied 2026-10-27, pending user confirmation; the file `design/accessibility-requirements.md` is to be authored later with `/ux-design accessibility`, noted here and not created) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*, and the failure notice and the UI-11 line and the newest-record line must not rely on colour alone (shape and text, per HR-10, no global hue remap).

> **📌 UX Flag — Save/Load & Versioned Format**: This system has UI requirements. In Phase 4 (Pre-Production), run `/ux-design` to create a UX spec for each screen or HUD element this system contributes to **before** writing epics: the failure notice and the death-restore line *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* (HUD & Suit Layer; the autosave glyph is retired), and the Save & Quit / hold-to-confirm / Continue (with its newest-record line) / New campaign / no-loadable-save / incompatible-build surfaces (Chart Table) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*. Stories that reference UI should cite `design/ux/[screen].md`, not the GDD directly. Retained screenshots of each screen are the evidence gate (AC-SL-92, AC-SL-117).

| Registry candidates (written from the final text by the registry stage) | `autosave_due`, `autosave_gate`, `save_verification`, `write_budget`, `generation_select`, `save_bytes_header`; constants `FORMAT_VERSION`, `AUTOSAVE_GENERATIONS`, `AUTOSAVE_INTERVAL_TICKS`, `AUTOSAVE_MIN_GAP_TICKS`, `AUTOSAVE_RETRY_TICKS`, `AUTOSAVE_MAX_DEFER_TICKS`, `DEATH_LOOP_WINDOW_TICKS`, `MAX_SAVE_BYTES`, `MAX_BLOCK_BYTES`, `SAVE_SIZE_ALARM_BYTES`, `LOADING_TARGET_MS`, `QUARANTINE_AFTER_FAILURES`, `REBASE_BLOCKS`, `MAX_HEADER_BYTES`, `AUTOSAVE_FAIL_ESCALATE_COUNT`; formulas `loss_ticks`, `load_budget`, `hex64` | Formulas, Tuning Knobs | `design/registry/entities.yaml` (registered: this GDD is the source of the six formulas, `loss_ticks`, `load_budget`, `hex64`, `save_status`, `SaveInfo`, `restore_reason` and the constants above plus `AUTOSAVE_FAIL_ESCALATE_COUNT`, and is listed in `referenced_by` of `TICK_RATE`, `DOWN_TO_DEATH_TICKS`, `HOST_PEER_ID`, `W_MIN_WARNING_S`, `state_hash` and `rng_derive`) |

## Acceptance Criteria

Every criterion is a Given/When/Then a QA tester can pass or fail without reading the rest of this document. Rule ids (SL-n), state-machine ids (T-n), formula ids (F-n), Open Question ids (OQ-n) and invariant ids (I-n) point at this document; kernel, Graph and Controller facts are cited by rule id or section name (CR-8, CR-15, CR-18, D.10 – D.12, the kernel's G4 acceptance criterion, Graph LS-21, Controller PC-17), never by line number, because line numbers drift on every edit of the owning GDD. The ids below are **stable labels, not a reading order**: criteria added after the first draft keep a letter or a higher number inside the group where they belong.

**Tags.** **(auto)** automated and deterministic — no wall clock, no real disk, no unseeded randomness unless the line says otherwise; **(lint)** a static or data-file check run in CI; **(review)** a named reviewer reads an artefact — filed on the review checklist below, **not** as a story acceptance criterion *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*; **(manual)** a person plays or inspects and a retained screenshot or sheet is the evidence; **(spike)** measured on the target machine by the concept's "save round-trip with version" spike, which replaces every [UNV] tag. Test locations follow the testing standard: `tests/unit/save-load/` (pure functions over in-memory byte arrays and the injected seams below), `tests/integration/save-load/` (a real temporary directory with fault injection), `tests/fixtures/saves/v1-standin/` (a freely regenerated stand-in golden) and `tests/fixtures/saves/v1/` (the frozen golden from the first outside build, immutable) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*, `production/qa/evidence/` (retained screenshots, sheets and spike measurements).

**Fixture constants (the GDD defaults; every example below uses them).** `TICK_RATE` 60; `AUTOSAVE_INTERVAL_TICKS` 10 800; `AUTOSAVE_MIN_GAP_TICKS` 1 800; `AUTOSAVE_RETRY_TICKS` 300; `AUTOSAVE_MAX_DEFER_TICKS` 7 200; `DEATH_LOOP_WINDOW_TICKS` 3 600; `AUTOSAVE_GENERATIONS` 3; `AUTOSAVE_FAIL_BACKOFF_MAX_TICKS` 4 800; `AUTOSAVE_FAIL_ESCALATE_COUNT` 3; `DOWN_TO_DEATH_TICKS` 1 800; `W_MIN_WARNING_S` 10 (600 ticks); `QUARANTINE_AFTER_FAILURES` 2; `MAX_SAVE_BYTES` 64 MiB; `MAX_BLOCK_BYTES` 32 MiB; `MAX_HEADER_BYTES` 64 KiB; `MAX_BLOCKS` 128 (kernel GDD value); `MAX_DECODE_DEPTH` 16; `MAX_DECODE_NODES` 250 000; `MAX_JSON_DEPTH` 8; `SAVE_QUIT_TIMEOUT_MS` 5 000; `RENAME_RETRY_COUNT` 3; `RENAME_RETRY_DELAY_MS` 50; `LOAD_MAX_ATTEMPTS` 3 (= `AUTOSAVE_GENERATIONS`); `SAVE_SIZE_ALARM_BYTES` 2 MiB; `LOADING_TARGET_MS` 1 000; `LOSS_CEILING_TICKS` 24 000; `GIVE_UP_MIN_TICKS` 48 (the Controller's, cited; `confirm_mode` HOLD, `confirm_hold_s` 0.8) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*.

**Harness seams (injected, so a test needs no wall clock, no real disk and no randomness).** A tick source (`T`); a file-system adapter with fault injection at each write state of T-4; a registry view; a kernel control-plane spy (records `request_snapshot`, `request_restore`, `request_shutdown`); a Graph `failures()` stand-in (LS-14 descriptor fixtures; the real Graph read view where `graph.state` is registered) an Attention SIM view stand-in (shaped as the designed `attention.view`), a Suit `vitals_state` view stand-in and an Assembly `ragged_breach_open()` stand-in (the registered read) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings, consistency follow-up)*; a `release_mode` flag; a `halt` handler; an entropy source (for `campaign_id`), supplying a *sequence* of values so a re-draw yields a different one; an injected **monotonic millisecond source** and a **manual-pump writer executor** (a writer whose steps run only when the test pumps them, so a stall, a hang and every interleaving of the writer with the tick are deterministic; SL-10, SL-23) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*; an allocation spy; and the seed constants `FUZZ_SEED`, `LOSS_FUZZ_SEED`, `INVARIANT_FUZZ_SEED`, `BLOCK_FUZZ_SEED`, each stored in the fixture file and printed on failure. **Stand-in blocks** are the kernel acceptance fixture's blocks, as the kernel's own G4 criterion uses them.

**Glossary.** *Generation*: one complete self-verifying save file. *Ring*: the last `AUTOSAVE_GENERATIONS` generations. *`RISKY`*: written while a soft gate condition was closed. *Demoted*: stepped back from, kept on disk as `gen_k.demoted`, never a default candidate. *Quarantine*: files that failed as CORRUPT, RESTORE_FAILED or STALE_EPOCH — never auto-deleted. *EXACT / REBASED*: whether the post-restore hash compare can prove equality (F-4). *Re-signed*: a mutated fixture whose trailer and block hashes were recomputed so it reaches the later pre-check steps.

**Pre-check coverage.** Every closed `SL_PRECHECK_*` reason of T-5 has at least one deterministic positive test; the fuzz (AC-SL-13) never counts as coverage.

| T-5 step (by name) | Reason(s) | Criterion |
|---|---|---|
| open and read | `FILE_UNREADABLE` (transient after `READ_RETRY_COUNT` retries, not quarantined) | AC-SL-98 |
| size floor, `MAX_SAVE_BYTES` | `FILE_TOO_SMALL`, `FILE_TOO_LARGE` | AC-SL-10 |
| 7-byte magic prefix | `BAD_MAGIC` | AC-SL-10 |
| trailer SHA-256 | `FILE_SHA_MISMATCH` | AC-SL-10, -11, -12 |
| layout id | `LAYOUT_UNKNOWN` (INCOMPATIBLE) | AC-SL-15, -99 |
| `header_len`, JSON parse | `HEADER_LEN_INVALID`, `HEADER_NOT_JSON` | AC-SL-99 |
| header fields | `HEADER_FIELD_MISSING`, `_TYPE`, `_NOT_INT`, `_HEX_MALFORMED` | AC-SL-02, -03, -100, -96 |
| `format_version`, `codec` | `FORMAT_UNSUPPORTED` | AC-SL-12, -15, -101 |
| `min_reader_version`, `tick_rate` | `MIN_READER_EXCEEDED`, `TICK_RATE_MISMATCH` | AC-SL-15 |
| block table | `BLOCK_TABLE_INVALID`, `PAYLOAD_LENGTH_MISMATCH`, `BLOCK_TOO_LARGE`, `BLOCK_COUNT_EXCEEDS_MAX`, `DUPLICATE_BLOCK_ID` | AC-SL-19, -102 |
| per-block `payload_sha256` | `BLOCK_HASH_MISMATCH` (names the block) | AC-SL-103 |
| registered or retired | `UNKNOWN_BLOCK`, `RETIRED_BLOCK_STRIPPED` | AC-SL-15, -17 |
| class and scope | `PERCEPTION_BLOCK_PRESENT`, `SCOPE_MISMATCH` | AC-SL-18 |
| `schema_version` ∈ `supported_versions` and migration chain | `SCHEMA_UNSUPPORTED` | AC-SL-15, -104 |
| decode and type walk | `BLOCK_DECODE_FAILED`, `BLOCK_TYPE_FORBIDDEN`, `BLOCK_DECODE_LIMITS` | AC-SL-05, -07, -105 |
| clock block and header cross-checks | `CLOCK_BLOCK_INVALID`, `HEADER_CLOCK_MISMATCH` | AC-SL-19, -105 |
| mandatory set | `MANDATORY_MISSING` (CORRUPT, or INCOMPATIBLE-dev on a `registry_epoch` mismatch) | AC-SL-17, -70, -106, -135 |
| seats, campaign | `SEATS_INVALID`, `CAMPAIGN_MISMATCH` | AC-SL-19, -107 |
| step order | first failing step wins | AC-SL-97 |
| reproduction stage (after restore) | `VERIFY_NOT_REPRODUCED`, `VERIFY_WINDOW_MISSED` | AC-SL-59, -60 |

### A. Container, interchange and trust (SL-1, SL-2, F-10, S-1)

1. **AC-SL-01 (auto) — S-1 fixture, bit-exact.** GIVEN a fixture with `world_seed = 2^64 − 1`, `T = 2^53 + 1`, and a Graph-shaped stand-in block holding `PackedInt64Array` and `PackedByteArray` fields, WHEN a generation is written and read back, THEN the header carries `world_seed` as `"ffffffffffffffff"` and `T` as `"0100000000002000"` (16 lowercase hex characters, little-endian, F-10) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*, the restored `kernel.clock` and every packed field are bit-identical, and the header contains no JSON number above 2^53. Cites: SL-1, SL-2, F-10.
2. **AC-SL-02 (auto) — hex is exact-width.** GIVEN headers whose `T` is `"FFFFFFFFFFFFFFFF"`, `"ffffffffffffffff0"`, `"ffffffffffffff"` or `"gggggggggggggggg"`, WHEN the pre-check runs, THEN each is refused `SL_PRECHECK_HEADER_HEX_MALFORMED` and the kernel spy records zero calls. Cites: F-10, T-5.
3. **AC-SL-03 (auto) — numbers stay numbers below 2^53.** GIVEN a re-signed header whose `generation` is `9007199254740993` or `1.5`, WHEN the pre-check runs, THEN it is refused `SL_PRECHECK_HEADER_FIELD_NOT_INT`. Cites: SL-1, S-1.
4. **AC-SL-04 (auto) — layout and trailer law.** GIVEN a generation file, WHEN its last 32 bytes are compared with SHA-256 over `[0, size − 32)`, THEN they are equal for every file the writer produces, for layout-id value 1 and for a synthetic future layout-id value. Cites: SL-1 (format invariant).
5. **AC-SL-05 (auto) — objects are never decoded.** GIVEN a payload containing an `Object` marker, a `Callable`, a `Resource` path or nesting beyond `MAX_DECODE_DEPTH`, WHEN the pre-check decodes it, THEN it is refused (`SL_PRECHECK_BLOCK_TYPE_FORBIDDEN` or `_BLOCK_DECODE_LIMITS`), zero objects are instantiated, and the kernel spy records zero calls. Cites: SL-2, T-5.
6. **AC-SL-06 (lint) — no object-allowing decode or Resource path** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*. GIVEN the Save/Load source, WHEN searched for the symbols `bytes_to_var_with_objects`, `var_to_bytes_with_objects`, `ResourceLoader`, `ResourceSaver`, `load(`, `preload(` on a user path, `.tres`, `.res` and `instance_from_id`, THEN there are zero hits outside the lint fixture; the lint is proven by a positive control (a fixture file containing each symbol makes it fail) and a negative control (the shipped source passes). **Gate: BLOCKING** (it guards the object-injection boundary; promoted from "recommended"). Cites: SL-2.
7. **AC-SL-07 (auto) — type walk, both directions.** GIVEN a snapshot containing only null, bool, int, float, String, Array, Dictionary (keys `String` or `int`) and `PackedByteArray`, `PackedInt32Array`, `PackedInt64Array`, `PackedFloat32Array`, `PackedFloat64Array`, `PackedStringArray`, WHEN the type walk runs, THEN it is accepted. GIVEN snapshots containing, one at a time, `Vector2i`, `Vector2`, `Vector3`, `Color`, `Rect2`, `NodePath`, `RID`, `Callable`, `Signal`, `StringName` as a value, a `Dictionary` with a float key, or a `Dictionary` with a `Vector2i` key, THEN each is refused `SL_PRECHECK_BLOCK_TYPE_FORBIDDEN`.
