GIVEN nesting depth `MAX_DECODE_DEPTH` (16) and then 17, THEN depth 16 is accepted and 17 is refused `SL_PRECHECK_BLOCK_DECODE_LIMITS`; GIVEN `MAX_DECODE_NODES` (250 000) nodes and then 250 001, THEN the same boundary holds; a single `PackedInt64Array` of 10 000 000 elements counts as one node and is accepted if within `MAX_BLOCK_BYTES`. GIVEN boot fixtures with `StringName` keys or a typed container, THEN boot fails `SL_BOOT_STRINGNAME_KEY` / `SL_BOOT_TYPED_CONTAINER`. Cites: SL-2, SL-15 items 3 and 12.
8. **AC-SL-08 (auto + lint) — the dev dump is not a load path.** GIVEN the dev `dump-save` command, WHEN it renders a block, THEN 64-bit values appear as strings (auto); AND GIVEN the source, THEN the dump module is not referenced from the load pipeline and is excluded from release builds (lint). Cites: SL-2.
9. **AC-SL-09 (auto) — file bytes are not canonical, state equality is.** GIVEN two saves of identical state whose dictionaries were built in different insertion orders, WHEN compared, THEN the file bytes may differ and `state_hash` is equal. Cites: SL-1, kernel D.12.
96. **AC-SL-96 (auto) — `hex64` round trip, 64- and 128-bit.** GIVEN x in {0, 1, 2^53 − 1, 2^53, 2^53 + 1, 2^63 − 1, 2^63, 2^64 − 1} and a 128-bit `campaign_id` of `0123456789abcdef0123456789abcdef`, WHEN `hex64`/`unhex64` (and the 32-character variant) run, THEN every value round-trips bit for bit, the strings are exactly 16 (or 32) lowercase hex characters, and a signed GDScript read of 2^63 and above is negative yet round-trips. A `campaign_id` of 31 or 33 characters, or uppercase, is refused `SL_PRECHECK_HEADER_HEX_MALFORMED`. Cites: F-10, SL-1.
96f. **AC-SL-96f (auto) — payload separability.** GIVEN a generation, WHEN only the payload region is sliced out using the header's block table and passed to a block decoder, THEN every block restores without the wrapper (the shape a VS-B guest transfer needs). Cites: SL-1, SL-17.
100. **AC-SL-100 (auto) — header field table.** GIVEN re-signed headers each missing one required key (every key in the SL-1 table, one run each) or carrying a wrong type, WHEN the pre-check runs, THEN `SL_PRECHECK_HEADER_FIELD_MISSING` / `_TYPE` names the key; GIVEN an added unknown key with `min_reader_version` satisfied, THEN the file loads; GIVEN a corrupt `block_state_hash` with everything else valid, THEN it loads and a diagnostic is logged; GIVEN a `PROFILE` file with no `state_hash`, THEN it loads; GIVEN a WORLD file written by a release build before K-4 with no `state_hash`, THEN it loads and the verdict is `NONE`, whereas a dev or test build's WORLD file with no `state_hash` is refused `SL_PRECHECK_HEADER_FIELD_MISSING`; GIVEN a file with no `registry_epoch`, THEN it is refused `SL_PRECHECK_HEADER_FIELD_MISSING` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*; GIVEN a file with no `risky_cause`, or one naming a member outside the closed set, or a non-empty `risky_cause` beside `risky` false, THEN it is refused `SL_PRECHECK_HEADER_FIELD_MISSING` / `_TYPE` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*; GIVEN any file the writer produces, THEN `min_reader_version == format_version`. Cites: SL-1, SL-16.

### B. Pre-check, failure classes and fuzz (SL-11, T-5)

10. **AC-SL-10 (auto) — damaged-file table.** GIVEN the stand-in golden v1 generation (SL-14) and these fixed inputs (all derived from it; no random source), WHEN the pre-check runs on each, THEN the reason and class are exactly as listed, the kernel spy records zero calls, and the loader falls back to the next candidate: (a) 0 bytes → `SL_PRECHECK_FILE_TOO_SMALL`; (b) truncated to 11, 12 and 45 bytes → `SL_PRECHECK_FILE_TOO_SMALL`; (c) truncated to 46 bytes and to `size − 1` → `SL_PRECHECK_FILE_SHA_MISMATCH`; (d) a 46-byte file of zero bytes → `SL_PRECHECK_BAD_MAGIC`; (e) golden bytes with the first 7 bytes replaced and the trailer recomputed → `SL_PRECHECK_BAD_MAGIC`; (f) a file of `MAX_SAVE_BYTES + 1` bytes → `SL_PRECHECK_FILE_TOO_LARGE`; (g) a valid file of exactly `MAX_SAVE_BYTES` bytes is not refused for size. All of (a)–(f) are class CORRUPT and quarantine candidates. The size floor is 46 bytes (12 + 2 + 32). Cites: T-5 size floor, magic, trailer.
11. **AC-SL-11 (auto) — a flipped bit in the layout-id byte is CORRUPT.** GIVEN an intact generation with one bit flipped in the layout-id byte, and an intact older generation, WHEN the loader runs, THEN the flipped file is `SL_PRECHECK_FILE_SHA_MISMATCH` (CORRUPT), not `SL_PRECHECK_LAYOUT_UNKNOWN`, it is a quarantine candidate, and the older generation loads. Cites: SL-1 format invariant, T-5 (trailer before layout id).
12. **AC-SL-12 (auto) — a flipped bit in `format_version` is not "newer build".** GIVEN an intact generation with one bit flipped inside the header's `format_version`, WHEN the loader runs, THEN the outcome is CORRUPT and a fallback, never `SL_PRECHECK_FORMAT_UNSUPPORTED`. Cites: SL-11, T-5 order.
13. **AC-SL-13 (auto) — 1 000-mutation fuzz: a refusal never reaches the kernel, nothing crashes.** GIVEN `FUZZ_MUTATION_COUNT` (1 000) mutations generated from the constant seed `FUZZ_SEED` (about 125 per class: payload bit flip; header bit flip; truncation; corrupt `header_len`; re-signed header edit; re-signed payload replacement with a forbidden-type tree; random-byte replacement; oversize length fields), each tagged by the generator `MUST_REFUSE` or `METADATA_ONLY` (metadata-only: edits confined to `created_unix`, `game_version`, `build_id`, `catalogue_hash`, `trigger`, or an added unknown header key, all re-signed), WHEN each mutated file is loaded, THEN (i) every `MUST_REFUSE` file is refused with exactly one reason from the closed `SL_PRECHECK_*` set, the kernel spy records zero calls and no object is instantiated; (ii) every `METADATA_ONLY` file is accepted and its decoded `state_hash` equals the golden file's; (iii) no mutation produces an exception, a crash or a third outcome; (iv) the live registry is byte-identical afterwards. A mutation whose class cannot be tagged by construction fails the harness. Cites: SL-11, T-5, Tuning Knobs (`FUZZ_MUTATION_COUNT`). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* The generator's tag set is extended to three: `MUST_REFUSE`, `METADATA_ONLY` and **`VALID_BEHAVIOUR`** (a legal, re-signed edit of a header field that changes behaviour: `risky`, `risky_cause`, `restore_epoch`, `save_kind`); a `VALID_BEHAVIOUR` file must load and its effect is asserted (the value reads back, and for `risky` / `risky_cause` the `generation_select` outcome changes accordingly). Every header key of SL-1 has at least one generated mutation of each applicable tag, and a key with none fails the harness.
14. **AC-SL-14 (auto) — block-level fuzz, never clamp.** GIVEN, per persisted block, 1 000 mutations (seed `BLOCK_FUZZ_SEED`) of its payload that pass the container checks, of which at least 100 are out-of-range value edits (a count below 0, a reference to a missing id, an enum outside its set), WHEN the block's `restore()` runs against a scratch registry, THEN each returns `(ok = false, reason, detail)` with `reason` in the block's declared closed `<BLOCK>_RESTORE_*` set, the live block is byte-identical after the failure, and no out-of-range value is accepted in a clamped form (a restore returning `ok = true` for an out-of-range edit fails). Save/Load supplies the harness; each owner supplies a fixture factory; a block with no fixture factory is a failing test (OQ-36). Cites: SL-15 items 5 and 6. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* Each mutation is tagged `MUST_REFUSE` (out-of-range, dangling reference, enum outside its set) or `VALID` (a legal in-range edit): the `ok = false` assertion applies to `MUST_REFUSE` only, a `VALID` mutation must return `ok = true` and round-trip, and at least 100 of each tag are generated, so the criterion no longer requires the refusal of a valid edit.
15. **AC-SL-15 (auto) — INCOMPATIBLE stops (parameterised).** GIVEN a campaign whose newest generation is re-signed to carry, one run per row: (1) `format_version` above ours; (2) `min_reader_version` above ours; (3) `tick_rate` ≠ `TICK_RATE`; (4) an unknown non-retired block id; (5) a stored `schema_version` outside the owner's `supported_versions`; (6) an unknown layout-id byte; (7) `codec` = 1; and two intact older generations, WHEN the loader runs, THEN the outcome is class INCOMPATIBLE with the reason of its row (`FORMAT_UNSUPPORTED`, `MIN_READER_EXCEEDED`, `TICK_RATE_MISMATCH`, `UNKNOWN_BLOCK`, `SCHEMA_UNSUPPORTED`, `LAYOUT_UNKNOWN`, `FORMAT_UNSUPPORTED`), the loader stops, no older generation is loaded or modified, no file is moved to `quarantine/`, the kernel spy records zero calls, and the campaign state is `READ_ONLY`; AND WHEN the older build then reaches an autosave due-point, THEN it writes no generation into that campaign. The message string is the Chart Table's (provisional); this criterion asserts the reason code. Cites: SL-11, T-9.
16. **AC-SL-16 (auto) — CORRUPT falls back and quarantines.** GIVEN a ring whose newest file fails its trailer, WHEN the loader runs, THEN the next generation loads, the failed file is in `quarantine/`, `quarantine_reason(file)` (the host-local diagnostics read) returns `CORRUPT`, its bytes are unchanged, it is not deleted, and it is never a candidate afterwards. The physical encoding of the reason (suffix, sidecar or manifest entry) is an ADR choice and is not asserted (OQ-27). Cites: SL-11, SL-19.
17. **AC-SL-17 (auto) — retired, missing and added blocks.** GIVEN (a) a block on `RETIRED_BLOCKS`, (b) a missing non-mandatory block with `added_in_format` above the file's `format_version`, (c) a missing mandatory block, WHEN the pre-check runs, THEN (a) is stripped with `SL_PRECHECK_RETIRED_BLOCK_STRIPPED`, (b) is filled by `default_state()` with a note, (c) is refused `SL_PRECHECK_MANDATORY_MISSING`; and an unknown non-retired id is INCOMPATIBLE, so the kernel's `RESTORE_UNKNOWN_BLOCK` is unreachable. Cites: SL-11, SL-14, T-5.
18. **AC-SL-18 (auto) — scope and perception blocks.** GIVEN a world file holding a PROFILE-scope block, a `profile.sav` holding a WORLD block, and a file holding a `PERCEPTION_OUT` block, WHEN the pre-check runs, THEN each is refused (`SL_PRECHECK_SCOPE_MISMATCH` / `SL_PRECHECK_PERCEPTION_BLOCK_PRESENT`). Cites: SL-9, SL-24, T-5.
19. **AC-SL-19 (auto) — header cross-checks.** GIVEN re-signed files with: a header `T` unequal to the `kernel.clock` block; a header `world_seed` unequal to the block's; a missing or non-integer `catalogue_version`; a duplicate `block_id`; an offset gap; a length lie; a `seats` list that is empty or duplicated; a `PER_PLAYER` block holding a PlayerId key not in `seats`; and a `campaign_id` unequal to the directory name, WHEN the pre-check runs, THEN each is refused with its named reason (`_HEADER_CLOCK_MISMATCH` for the first two, `_HEADER_FIELD_MISSING` / `_NOT_INT`, `_DUPLICATE_BLOCK_ID`, `_BLOCK_TABLE_INVALID`, `_PAYLOAD_LENGTH_MISMATCH`, `_SEATS_INVALID`, `_CAMPAIGN_MISMATCH`). Cites: SL-1, SL-20, T-5.
20. **AC-SL-20 (auto) — the pre-check is pure.** GIVEN the pre-check function, WHEN called twice with the same bytes, registry view and constants, THEN the results are identical and no clock, file handle or global is read. Cites: SL-11.
21. **AC-SL-21 (auto) — pre-check precedes the kernel.** GIVEN a file refused at any T-5 step, WHEN the loader returns, THEN the kernel control-plane spy saw no `request_restore` and the kernel never entered `FAULTED`. Cites: SL-11.
97. **AC-SL-97 (auto) — pre-check ordering.** GIVEN files each failing two steps (bad trailer plus unknown block; unknown block plus scope mismatch; missing mandatory plus bad seats; header clock mismatch plus duplicate block id), WHEN the pre-check runs, THEN the reason is that of the earlier step in T-5 order and exactly one reason is logged. Cites: T-5.
98. **AC-SL-98 (auto) — unreadable is transient.** GIVEN a candidate whose open fails twice and then succeeds (injected sharing violation, `READ_RETRY_COUNT` 3, `READ_RETRY_DELAY_MS` 100 of injected time), THEN the candidate loads and no fallback occurs; GIVEN it fails four times and an intact older generation exists, WHEN the loader runs, THEN `SL_PRECHECK_FILE_UNREADABLE` is logged once, the loader falls back, the unreadable file is **not** moved to `quarantine/` and is a candidate again on the next load; the retries are frame-polled and the blocking-sleep spy records zero calls *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*. Cites: T-5 step "open and read", SL-11. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* The unreadable file also **occupies its ring slot**: a write started during the retry interval targets another free slot or ends `FAILED` with `SL_WRITE_NO_POSITION`, and never overwrites the unreadable file; with the newest file quarantined and `campaign.json` deleted, `gen_next` still exceeds the quarantined file's generation (read from its name).
99. **AC-SL-99 (auto) — structure steps on re-signed files.** GIVEN re-signed files with `header_len` = 0, = file size, and 0xFFFFFFFF; header bytes that are not JSON; a JSON array instead of an object; JSON nested deeper than `MAX_JSON_DEPTH` 8; and an unknown layout-id byte, WHEN the pre-check runs, THEN the reasons are `HEADER_LEN_INVALID` (first three), `HEADER_NOT_JSON` (next three), and `LAYOUT_UNKNOWN` (INCOMPATIBLE, stop); and for the 0xFFFFFFFF case the allocation spy records no allocation above `MAX_HEADER_BYTES`. Cites: SL-1, T-5.
101. **AC-SL-101 (auto) — codec reserved.** GIVEN a re-signed header with `codec` = 1, WHEN the pre-check runs, THEN `SL_PRECHECK_FORMAT_UNSUPPORTED`, class INCOMPATIBLE, stop (no decompression path exists). Cites: SL-19, T-5.
102. **AC-SL-102 (auto) — read-side caps.** GIVEN re-signed headers with a block length `MAX_BLOCK_BYTES + 1`, and with 129 block records, WHEN the pre-check runs, THEN `SL_PRECHECK_BLOCK_TOO_LARGE` and `SL_PRECHECK_BLOCK_COUNT_EXCEEDS_MAX` respectively; 128 records is accepted. Cites: T-5, F-6.
103. **AC-SL-103 (auto) — block hash.** GIVEN a re-signed file with one `payload_sha256` altered, WHEN the pre-check runs, THEN `SL_PRECHECK_BLOCK_HASH_MISMATCH` names that `block_id`, the class is CORRUPT, and the kernel spy records zero calls. Cites: SL-16, T-5.
104. **AC-SL-104 (auto) — migration chain completeness.** GIVEN a block with stored `schema_version` 1 whose owner lists {1, 2, 3} but registers only the 2 → 3 step, WHEN the pre-check runs, THEN `SL_PRECHECK_SCHEMA_UNSUPPORTED` names the block and version 1; with the 1 → 2 step registered it passes. Cites: SL-14, T-5.
105. **AC-SL-105 (auto) — decode failure and clock block.** GIVEN a payload that is not a valid encoded value, a payload that decodes to an Array where a Dictionary is declared, a `kernel.clock` block with an extra key, a missing key, and a string `T`, WHEN the pre-check runs, THEN `SL_PRECHECK_BLOCK_DECODE_FAILED` (first two) and `SL_PRECHECK_CLOCK_BLOCK_INVALID` (last three). Cites: T-5.
106. **AC-SL-106 (auto) — the mandatory set is derived.** GIVEN registrations with `added_in_format` of 1 and 2 and a format-1 file lacking the second, WHEN the pre-check runs, THEN the second is filled by `default_state()` with a note; with `added_in_format` = 1 the file is refused `SL_PRECHECK_MANDATORY_MISSING`; no hand-kept list is read. Cites: SL-14.
107. **AC-SL-107 (auto) — seats.** GIVEN `seats` = [] / [1, 1] / [0] / [99999] (out of range), THEN each is refused `SL_PRECHECK_SEATS_INVALID`; GIVEN `seats` = [1, 2] with only PlayerId 1 bound, THEN the file is accepted (an unbound seat is allowed); GIVEN a `PER_PLAYER` key 3 with `seats` = [1, 2], THEN it is refused. Cites: SL-20, T-5.
135. **AC-SL-135 (auto) — a moved registry is INCOMPATIBLE-dev, not CORRUPT** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*. GIVEN a dev-build file whose `registry_epoch` differs from ours and which lacks a mandatory block, WHEN the pre-check runs, THEN `SL_PRECHECK_MANDATORY_MISSING` is class INCOMPATIBLE-dev, the loader stops with the incompatible-development-build reason, nothing is moved to `quarantine/` and the campaign directory is not written to; GIVEN the same file with our `registry_epoch`, THEN the class is CORRUPT and the file is a quarantine candidate; GIVEN one flipped bit in `registry_epoch` of an otherwise intact file, THEN the trailer step refuses it first (`SL_PRECHECK_FILE_SHA_MISMATCH`, CORRUPT). Cites: SL-1, SL-11, SL-14, T-5.

### C. Generation ring, write procedure and crash recovery (SL-3, SL-10, T-3, T-4, T-6, F-9)

22. **AC-SL-22 (auto) — ring order is the header number.** GIVEN files whose modification times and `T` values disagree with their `generation` numbers (a step-back has rolled `T` back), WHEN the loader lists candidates, THEN they are ordered by header `generation` descending and `gen_next = 1 + max(generation over the ring, quarantine/, `.demoted` files and the manifest)`. Cites: SL-3, F-9.
23. **AC-SL-23 (auto) — first free position, newest untouched.** GIVEN the F-9 example ring (G7, demoted G6, G5, quarantined G9), WHEN the next write completes, THEN G10 is at `gen_1.sav`, the `.demoted` occupant was treated as an oldest occupant (renamed `.bak`, deleted after `POST_VERIFY`), and the newest generation's file was never renamed or touched at any step. Cites: SL-3, T-4 `SWAPPING_OUT`.
24. **AC-SL-24 (auto) — kill during write.** GIVEN a failing file-system adapter that aborts the process at each write state (`WRITING_TMP`, `VERIFYING`, before `SWAPPING_OUT`, between `SWAPPING_OUT` and `SWAPPING_IN`, between `SWAPPING_IN` and `POST_VERIFY`, `MANIFEST`) and at each of the same points of a `profile.sav` write, WHEN the campaign is reopened, THEN at least one loadable generation exists, no `.tmp` is a candidate, the recovery of T-6 yields the previous or the new generation, and a file that cannot be proven obsolete was not deleted; and for the kill point after `VERIFYING` and before `SWAPPING_OUT` both outcomes are exercised: a complete `.tmp` whose `generation` exceeds every ring generation is rolled forward, a complete `.tmp` whose `generation` does not is deleted (full tiebreak test: AC-SL-110). Cites: SL-10, T-6. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* *Torn writes and power loss:* a torn-write adapter writes only the first n bytes of the `.tmp` (n at 0, 1, `header_len`, mid-payload, `size − 33` and `size − 1`) and a power-loss adapter drops the last k unflushed writes at each write state; on reopen at least one loadable generation exists, the torn `.tmp` is never a candidate and is deleted or kept per T-6.
25. **AC-SL-25 (auto) — a failed write never harms.** GIVEN each injected fault — disk full, permission denied, open error, a `store_*` returning false, re-read hash mismatch, rename failure — applied at each write state in which the step performs that kind of operation (a matrix table in the test file lists the applicable cells; an inapplicable cell is marked, not skipped silently), WHEN the write runs, THEN every previous generation is byte-identical, `save_status` reads `FAILED`, `last_save_T` is unchanged, the `.tmp` is removed best-effort, and `SL_WRITE_FAILED{step}` is logged. GIVEN consecutive failures 1 through 6 at `AUTOSAVE_RETRY_TICKS` 300 and `AUTOSAVE_FAIL_BACKOFF_MAX_TICKS` 4 800, THEN the retry delays are `min(AUTOSAVE_RETRY_TICKS × 2^(n−1), AUTOSAVE_FAIL_BACKOFF_MAX_TICKS)` = 300, 600, 1 200, 2 400, 4 800, 4 800 ticks and the HUD-notice flag is raised on failure 3 (`AUTOSAVE_FAIL_ESCALATE_COUNT`) and not before (orchestrator ruling: the schedule is written into the knob row; OQ-26). Cites: SL-10, T-2, T-4, T-7.
26. **AC-SL-26 (auto) — every `store_*` return is checked.** GIVEN the writer, WHEN a `store_*` call is stubbed to return false at each call site in turn, THEN the write ends `FAILED` and never `WRITTEN`. Cites: SL-10 (sourced: `FileAccess.store_*` returns `bool` since 4.4).
27. **AC-SL-27 (auto) — epoch rule.** GIVEN a write job still running when a restore is issued, WHEN `write_epoch` advances before the job's first rename, THEN the job aborts and removes its `.tmp`; and GIVEN a job already past `SWAPPING_IN`, THEN its generation is moved to `quarantine/` as `STALE_EPOCH` and is never a candidate. Cites: SL-10.
28. **AC-SL-28 (auto) — manifest is a hint.** GIVEN `campaign.json` deleted, corrupted, or disagreeing with the files, WHEN the campaign opens, THEN it is rebuilt from headers (`SL_MANIFEST_REBUILT`), the load is not refused, and demoted and quarantined generations stay excluded. Cites: SL-3.
29. **AC-SL-29 (auto) — write refuses what read would refuse.** GIVEN a block over `MAX_BLOCK_BYTES`, a file over `MAX_SAVE_BYTES` and a header over `MAX_HEADER_BYTES`, WHEN a save is attempted, THEN `SL_WRITE_TOO_LARGE{block}` is reported, no file is produced and the ring is untouched. Cites: F-6.
30. **AC-SL-30 (spike) — Windows rename and flush.** GIVEN each machine configuration (SSD, HDD, antivirus on, OneDrive-redirected `user://`), WHEN the write procedure runs 1 000 times per configuration with sharing violations injected at the rename step on 10 % of runs, THEN after every run (including runs killed at the injection point) at least one loadable generation exists, the number of runs that leave zero loadable generations is 0, no run depends on rename-onto-existing, and the measured `flush` → fsync behaviour and the 99th-percentile `durable_ms` per configuration are recorded in `production/qa/evidence/spike-save-windows.md`. Pass = zero data-loss runs and a recorded number for each [UNV] in SL-10 and T-6. Cites: SL-10, OQ-16.
108. **AC-SL-108 (auto) — rename retries.** GIVEN the swap rename failing 1, 2 and 3 times then succeeding (`RENAME_RETRY_COUNT` 3, delay 50 ms of injected time), THEN the write ends `WRITTEN`; failing 4 times, THEN the `.bak` is renamed back, the position's original file is in place and byte-identical, and `save_status` reads `FAILED`. Cites: SL-10, T-4.
109. **AC-SL-109 (auto) — stale `.bak`.** GIVEN a position holding a verified stale `.bak`, WHEN the write reaches `SWAPPING_OUT`, THEN the `.bak` is deleted and the write proceeds; GIVEN an unverified stale `.bak`, THEN the write aborts `SL_WRITE_STALE_BAK`, nothing is deleted, and `save_status` reads `FAILED`. Cites: T-4.
110. **AC-SL-110 (auto) — recovery tiebreaks.** GIVEN a complete `.tmp` with `generation` 12 and a ring maximum of 11, THEN it is rolled forward; with `generation` 9 it is deleted; GIVEN `gen_1.sav` and `gen_1.bak` both claiming generation 8, THEN the `.sav` is chosen and the `.bak` is removed only after the `.sav` re-verifies; if the `.sav` fails verification the `.bak` is chosen and nothing is deleted. Cites: T-6.
111. **AC-SL-111 (auto) — ring size.** GIVEN `AUTOSAVE_GENERATIONS` in {2, 3, 5}, WHEN 12 consecutive writes complete, THEN the ring holds exactly the N newest generations, the newest file was never renamed at any step, and `gen_next` is monotonic. GIVEN a ring written with 3 and then opened with 2, THEN the third position's file is still a candidate by generation order and is not deleted. Cites: SL-3, F-9.
118. **AC-SL-118 (auto) — size alarm.** GIVEN `file_bytes` > `SAVE_SIZE_ALARM_BYTES` (2 MiB), WHEN a save is written, THEN the file is written, `SL_SIZE_ALARM` is logged once for that campaign open and not on the second write; GIVEN the campaign reopened, THEN it logs again once. Cites: F-6.

### D. Autosave schedule and gate (SL-4, SL-5, F-1, F-2)

31. **AC-SL-31 (auto) — interval trigger.** GIVEN `AUTOSAVE_INTERVAL_TICKS` 10 800, `last_save_T = 0` and an open gate, WHEN `T` reaches 10 800, THEN exactly one `fire` occurs with kind `AUTO` and `risky = false`. Cites: F-1, F-2.
32. **AC-SL-32 (auto) — event trigger, coalescing and the minimum gap.** GIVEN `last_save_T = 437 400`, WHEN an event arrives at 438 700 (`elapsed` 1 300), THEN it latches and `due` becomes true at 439 200; and an event and the interval due on the same tick produce one save of kind `EVENT`. Cites: F-1, SL-4.
33. **AC-SL-33 (auto) — no interval save while paused.** GIVEN the kernel `PAUSED` for 20 000 ticks of wall time, WHEN the scheduler runs, THEN no interval save becomes due. Cites: SL-4, T-1.
34. **AC-SL-34 (auto) — autosave is suppressed while `DOWNED`.** GIVEN a seat in `DOWNED` when an interval save falls due at `T = 10 800`, WHEN the gate is evaluated at every retry tick (300-tick grid), THEN no snapshot is requested, including at the `AUTOSAVE_MAX_DEFER_TICKS` cap tick (`T = 18 000`); and once the seat is revived and the refractory (600 ticks) has run out the next retry tick writes the save, not `RISKY` (AC-SL-129) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*. Cites: SL-5, F-2.
35. **AC-SL-35 (auto) — soft deferral, the `RISKY` cap and the tell mode.** GIVEN a continuous soft block (injected Attention view or Graph fixture) from `due_first_T = 10 800` and no hard block, WHEN `waited` reaches 7 200 on the retry grid, THEN the save fires at `T = 18 000` with `risky = true`. With `gate_tell_mode = FAIR`: an Attention tell with 601 ticks remaining does not block; exactly 600 remaining does not block; 599 remaining blocks (`tell_remaining < W_MIN_WARNING_S × TICK_RATE`). With `STRICT`: an Attention tell with 700 ticks remaining at 10 800 blocks at 10 800, 11 100 and 11 400, and the save fires at 11 700 after the tell ends. Each of `CHANNELLING`, `STAGGERED`, `IN_POD` in `GATE_SOFT_STATES` blocks softly (`RISKY` if the cap elapsed); `IDLE`, `WALK`, `RUN`, `CARRY_WALK` do not block by `loco_state` alone (with every seat's `vitals_state` below `CRITICAL`) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*; a `breach_phase` other than `CALM` blocks softly; `gate_tell_mode` has no effect on a Graph descriptor (AC-SL-125). Cites: SL-5, F-2.
36. **AC-SL-36 (auto) — absent provisional view.** GIVEN no Attention read view published (the Graph view present), WHEN the gate runs, THEN `GATE_INPUT_ABSENT` is logged once per process lifetime (a death-stub restore inside the same process does not log it again) and the Graph, controller and kernel conditions alone decide; the Graph half (AC-SL-125) is unaffected and never logs `GATE_INPUT_ABSENT` *(Amended 2026-10-26 — CD-GDD-ALIGN)* (orchestrator ruling: "session" = process lifetime; OQ-29). Cites: SL-5. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* `GATE_INPUT_ABSENT` names the input, `GATE_INPUT_ABSENT{input}` with `input` ∈ {`ATTENTION_VIEW`, `VITALS`, `RAGGED_BREACH`}, once per process per input; an absent Suit view counts as OK (SL-5 row (c)).
37. **AC-SL-37 (auto) — no generation ever holds a `DOWNED` or `DEAD` seat** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*. GIVEN a scripted session of 120 000 ticks (at least ten interval periods, so the schedule really fires) generated from `INVARIANT_FUZZ_SEED` with injected `DOWNED`, revive and `DEAD` events and autosaves, quits and window-closes at scripted ticks, repeated for 5 distinct seeds, WHEN every generation file written is decoded, THEN none contains a seat whose `controller.state.loco_state` is `DOWNED` or `DEAD`, AND a coverage assertion holds for every seed: at least 8 interval generations were written, at least 3 due saves were held by the hard condition, at least one `QUIT` and one window-close were requested while a seat was `DOWNED` (and wrote nothing); a seed that misses a coverage count fails the harness instead of passing vacuously. Cites: SL-5, SL-6, F-2.
38. **AC-SL-38 (auto) — knob ranges first, then invariants, one failing data file per row** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*. *Layer 1, the range check (runs first, OQ-28 decided).* GIVEN data files with `AUTOSAVE_RETRY_TICKS` 0, `AUTOSAVE_RETRY_TICKS` 901, `AUTOSAVE_INTERVAL_TICKS` 5 399, `AUTOSAVE_INTERVAL_TICKS` 18 001, `AUTOSAVE_MIN_GAP_TICKS` 3 601 and `MAX_HEADER_BYTES` 15 KiB, WHEN Save/Load boots, THEN each refuses to start with `SL_BOOT_KNOB_RANGE{knob}` naming that knob, no division by zero occurs and no invariant is evaluated. *Layer 2, the invariants.* The invariant validator is called directly with the range check bypassed, so each row isolates one invariant: I1 `AUTOSAVE_MIN_GAP_TICKS` = `AUTOSAVE_INTERVAL_TICKS`; I2 `AUTOSAVE_MAX_DEFER_TICKS` 10 800 with the interval 10 800 and `R` 300 (D + R exceeds I; I3 still holds); I3 `R` 2 000 with D 7 200 and, separately, D 7 250 with `R` 300 (not a multiple); I4 `AUTOSAVE_GENERATIONS` = 1; I5 `MAX_BLOCK_BYTES` above `MAX_SAVE_BYTES`; I6 `SAVE_SIZE_ALARM_BYTES` = `MAX_SAVE_BYTES`; I7 `MAX_HEADER_BYTES` = 31 319 (31 320 passes at 128 blocks and 240 B); I8 `LOADING_TARGET_MS` = 120; I10 `GATE_SOFT_STATES` containing `DOWNED`, and another run containing `DEAD`; I11 `LOAD_MAX_ATTEMPTS` 2 with `AUTOSAVE_GENERATIONS` 3; I12 `AUTOSAVE_FAIL_BACKOFF_MAX_TICKS` 4 850; I13 `DEATH_LOOP_WINDOW_TICKS` 1 100 with `W_MIN_WARNING_S` 20 (1 200 > 1 100, range check bypassed) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*. THEN each fails with `SL_BOOT_KNOB_INVARIANT{id}` naming exactly that invariant and no other. I9: `DEATH_LOOP_WINDOW_TICKS` 3 601 so `loss_max_stepback` = 24 001 (and nothing else is violated) logs a diagnostic only in a dev or test build and refuses boot (`SL_BOOT_KNOB_INVARIANT{I9}`) in a release build; the default 24 000 (equality) passes in both *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*. Cites: Tuning Knobs ranges and invariants, OQ-28. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* *Rows added:* I3 with `AUTOSAVE_RETRY_TICKS` 400, 450, 720 and 900 (each a legal range value that fails `(W_MIN_WARNING_S × TICK_RATE) mod R = 0` and refuses with `SL_BOOT_KNOB_INVARIANT{I3}`) and R 60, 100, 120, 150, 200, 300 and 600 (each boots); `LOSS_CEILING_TICKS` 11 399 refused by the range check and 11 400 accepted; I9 and the k-chain: `loss_max_k` for k = 1 and 2 is reported as a diagnostic and never refuses.
39. **AC-SL-39 (auto) — timers reconstruct.** GIVEN a process restart that restores a generation with `T = 54 000`, WHEN the scheduler starts, THEN `last_save_T = 54 000`, no autosave is immediately due, and the first interval save is due at 64 800. Cites: SL-3, T-2.
40. **AC-SL-40 (auto) — autosave transparency.** GIVEN two kernels fed an identical command stream for 100 000 ticks, one autosaving at every interval and the other never, WHEN `state_hash(ALL_SIM)` is compared at the end, THEN they are equal (capture and the writer have no side effect on the sim). Cites: SL-17, kernel D.12.
96c. **AC-SL-96c (auto) — event triggers.** GIVEN `EVENT_TRIGGERS` = {`NEW_GAME`}, WHEN a `POD_ARRIVED` notification arrives, THEN no save becomes due; GIVEN `EVENT_TRIGGERS` also containing `POD_ARRIVED` (`web_events`, provisional until merge) and the owner publishing the notification, THEN the event latches and fires per F-1; GIVEN the weld/cut trigger enabled and `last_shape_change_T()` advancing past `last_save_T`, THEN the event latches likewise. An event type not in the set never schedules a save. Cites: SL-4, F-1.
114. **AC-SL-114 (auto) — clock anomalies.** GIVEN `T` < `last_save_T`, THEN `elapsed = 0`, `last_save_T = T`, `SL_AUTOSAVE_CLOCK_REGRESSION` is logged once (a second occurrence is silent), and a dev build asserts; GIVEN `kernel.clock.T` returns a non-integer, THEN `SL_BAD_CLOCK`, no write, no restore. Cites: F-1.
125. **AC-SL-125 (auto) — an open Graph warning window defers an interval save** *(Amended 2026-10-26 — CD-GDD-ALIGN)*. GIVEN a Graph fixture (the real `graph.state` read view or an LS-14 descriptor stand-in) holding one loaded descriptor with `subject_kind` EDGE, `cause` ENDOGENOUS, `sev` 1 and `warn_elapsed` 47, `W_MIN_STEPS` 100 (`W_MIN_WARNING_S` 10 × `TICK_RATE` 60 / `GRAPH_STEP_TICKS` 6), `AUTOSAVE_INTERVAL_TICKS` 10 800, `last_save_T` 0, no hard block and no Attention view, WHEN the interval falls due at `T = 10 800`, THEN `fire` is false at 10 800 (`graph_tell_block` 1, `graph_tell_remaining` 318), false at 11 100 (`warn_elapsed` 97) and true at 11 400 with `risky = false` and no `GATE_INPUT_ABSENT`; with `warn_elapsed` exactly 99 at the due tick the save is deferred and with exactly 100 it fires at once (boundary); and with a fresh descriptor opened every 5 steps for the whole span the save fires at `T = 18 000` with `risky = true`. Cites: SL-5, F-2, Graph LS-13, D.11.
126. **AC-SL-126 (auto) — which Graph descriptors do not block** *(Amended 2026-10-26 — CD-GDD-ALIGN)*. GIVEN the fixture of AC-SL-125, WHEN one field is varied at a time, THEN `cause` EXOGENOUS (the Graph's gate is bypassed), `sev` 0, `subject_kind` COMPARTMENT or OPENING (an ATMO_COLD descriptor at `sev` 2 with `warn_elapsed` 0 must not defer the save), `warn_elapsed` ≥ `W_MIN_STEPS` and a descriptor the stand-in marks as in a dormant section each leave `graph_tell_block` = 0 (the dormant case is provisional until OQ-38 is answered and is recorded, not asserted, until then); `gate_tell_mode` FAIR and STRICT give identical results for the Graph fixture; and a `save_and_quit()` with the AC-SL-125 descriptor open is written at once with `risky = true`, as in AC-SL-43. Cites: SL-5, SL-6, F-2.

128. **AC-SL-128 (auto) — a critical suit defers the save; the fairness step-back** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* *(promoted from OQ-40, user ruling X7)*. GIVEN a seat with `vitals_state` `CRITICAL` and an interval save falling due at `T = 10 800`, no other input closed, WHEN the gate is evaluated on the retry grid, THEN `vitals_block` defers it at 10 800, 11 100 and on every grid tick through 17 700, it fires at the cap `T = 18 000` with `risky = true` and `risky_cause = {VITALS}`, and a seat at `OK` or `STRAINED` does not block; GIVEN the seat enters `DOWNED` one tick after a retry tick, THEN nothing is written on any later grid tick, the cap tick included (the hard block holds), and its death restores the previous generation; GIVEN instead that the cap generation G7 (`RISKY`, `risky_cause = {VITALS}`) was written and later restored, and the seat falls within `DEATH_LOOP_WINDOW_TICKS` of `T(G7)`, THEN the stub steps back to G7's predecessor (AC-SL-50); AND GIVEN a non-`RISKY` generation G7 restored and a `DOWNED` at `T(G7) + 599`, THEN the stub steps back to G6 and demotes G7 (the fairness route), whereas at `T(G7) + 600` it restores G7 again. Cites: SL-5 row (c), SL-8, F-2, F-3.
129. **AC-SL-129 (auto) — a critical-suit QUIT and the post-`DOWNED` refractory** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* *(promoted from OQ-40, user ruling X7)*. GIVEN `save_and_quit()` with a `CRITICAL` suit, THEN the generation is `RISKY` with `risky_cause` containing `VITALS`; WHEN Continue restores it and a death follows within `DEATH_LOOP_WINDOW_TICKS`, THEN the stub steps back to the predecessor (a `QUIT` made with only a channel open does not, AC-SL-139); GIVEN a `DOWNED` → `IDLE` recovery at `T = 17 700` onto a still-`CRITICAL` suit with the interval save due since 10 800, THEN `refractory_block` keeps the gate closed through the cap tick 18 000 (nothing is written there) and the save fires at the grid tick 18 300 (`T − last_recover_T` = 600), `risky = true`; GIVEN a recovery with a healthy suit and a due save, THEN no autosave fires for 600 ticks and the first grid tick at or after the 600th fires it, not `RISKY`; GIVEN `save_and_quit()` inside the refractory, THEN it is written at once (the refractory does not bind a `QUIT`); GIVEN a restore, THEN `last_recover_T` is cleared. Cites: SL-5, SL-6, F-2.
144. **AC-SL-144 (auto) — a RAGGED breach withholds the cut event and defers the interval save** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* *(user ruling D54, OQ-41 (b))*. GIVEN a RAGGED cut applied at `T = 440 000` with `last_save_T = 437 400` and `ragged_breach_open()` true (stand-in), WHEN the gate runs, THEN `event_due` is false and `event_pending` stays latched; WHEN the read turns false at `T = 441 800`, THEN `event_due` is true and the capture fires on the next retry-grid tick, kind `EVENT`; GIVEN a SEALED cut or a weld at the same `T`, THEN the event is due at once; GIVEN the interval falling due with the read true, THEN the save defers as a soft input and fires no later than `due_first_T + 7 200`, tagged `RISKY` with `risky_cause = {ASSEMBLY_BREACH}`; GIVEN the read absent, THEN the input counts as closed (not blocking) and the absence is logged once per process. Cites: SL-4, SL-5 row (d), F-1, F-2.

### E. Save & Quit, window close (SL-6)

41. **AC-SL-41 (auto) — Save & Quit writes a `QUIT` generation then shuts down.** GIVEN the kernel `PAUSED` and all seats alive, WHEN `save_and_quit()` is called, THEN the kernel goes `PAUSED → SAVING → PAUSED` with `T` unchanged, a generation of kind `QUIT` is `WRITTEN`, and only then is `request_shutdown` issued. Cites: SL-6.
42. **AC-SL-42 (auto) — Save & Quit with a seat `DOWNED` writes nothing.** GIVEN any bound seat `DOWNED`, WHEN `save_and_quit()` is called, THEN it returns `SL_QUIT_NO_WRITE_DOWNED`, no file is written, `save_status` is unchanged, the shutdown proceeds only after the hold-to-confirm "quit without saving", and Continue afterwards restores the newest verified generation. Cites: SL-6, T-2.
43. **AC-SL-43 (auto) — Save & Quit with only a soft condition closed.** GIVEN a seat `CHANNELLING` or an injected non-calm breach phase, WHEN `save_and_quit()` is called, THEN the generation is written immediately with `risky = true`; GIVEN a seat with `vitals_state` `CRITICAL`, THEN likewise, with `risky_cause` containing `VITALS` (AC-SL-129), and with `ragged_breach_open()` true likewise with `ASSEMBLY_BREACH` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*. Cites: SL-6, F-2.
44. **AC-SL-44 (auto) — window close from `RUNNING`.** GIVEN the kernel `RUNNING`, WHEN the window-close request arrives, THEN `save_and_quit()` runs `RUNNING → SAVING → RUNNING` and `request_shutdown` follows the `WRITTEN`; with a seat `DOWNED` nothing is written. GIVEN the kernel `PAUSED` under the open Chart Table, WHEN the window-close request arrives, THEN `save_and_quit()` runs `PAUSED → SAVING → PAUSED`, `request_shutdown` follows and the Chart Table calls nothing; GIVEN a boot-flow screen, THEN nothing is written *(Amended 2026-10-27 — consistency pass)*. Cites: SL-6.
44b. **AC-SL-44b (auto) — window close in every kernel state** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*. GIVEN the close request in each of: `RUNNING`, `PAUSED`, `LOADING`, `SAVING` (an autosave in flight), a bound seat `DOWNED`, `FAULTED`, and a boot-flow screen, WHEN the handler runs with `auto_accept_quit = false`, THEN: `RUNNING` and `PAUSED` write a `QUIT` generation and `quit()` is called deferred only after `WRITTEN`; `LOADING` defers the intent until the kernel is `RUNNING`; `SAVING` lets the in-flight job settle and then either dedupes (equal state hash, no second write) or writes the `QUIT` generation; `DOWNED` writes nothing, `SL_QUIT_NO_WRITE_DOWNED` is returned and the close is held for the hold-to-confirm; `FAULTED` and the boot-flow screen write nothing; in every row the handler never blocks the frame, no `quit()` is called before its row's condition holds, and an Alt-F4 injected as the same close request takes the identical path. GIVEN a write that fails or times out on a close, THEN the close is held, `save_status` reads `FAILED`, `quit_held` is true, and no exit occurs. Cites: SL-6.
45a. **AC-SL-45a (auto) — quit dedupe.** GIVEN a state hash equal to the newest generation's, WHEN `save_and_quit()` is called, THEN the write is skipped (`SL_QUIT_DEDUPED`), no file is written and shutdown proceeds. Cites: SL-6.
45b. **AC-SL-45b (auto) — quit with a failed write.** GIVEN an injected write failure, WHEN `save_and_quit()` is called, THEN the quit is held, `save_status` reads `FAILED`, the pause-panel flag is set, and a retry is possible. Cites: SL-6.
45c. **AC-SL-45c (auto) — quit with a slow write.** GIVEN a write exceeding `SAVE_QUIT_TIMEOUT_MS` (5 000 ms of injected monotonic time), WHEN `save_and_quit()` is called, THEN at 5 000 ms the quit is held, `save_status` reads `FAILED`, and the pause-panel flag is set. Cites: SL-6.
115. **AC-SL-115 (auto) — same-tick collisions.** GIVEN (a) Save & Quit and an interval save due on the same tick: one `QUIT` generation, no `AUTO` generation, `due_first_T` discarded; (b) an autosave due and `DEAD` entered at the same boundary: no `request_snapshot`, the restore proceeds; (c) `request_restore` arriving while the kernel is `SAVING`: the capture completes, the stub settles the writer without waiting (SL-7), so the new generation is a candidate only if it is `WRITTEN` by then and otherwise its job is abandoned, and the stub selects among the verified candidates *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*. Cites: SL-6, SL-7, F-2, kernel `SAVING` rule.

### F. Death stub, selection and demotion (SL-7, SL-8, T-8, F-3, F-7)

46. **AC-SL-46 (auto) — the stub restores the newest verified generation.** GIVEN a ring of three verified generations and a seat reaching `DEAD`, WHEN the tick boundary runs, THEN in that boundary the stub settles the writer without waiting, pre-checks and stages the newest non-demoted generation (from the hot RAM copy where one exists) and only then issues `request_restore(payload)` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*; the kernel enters `LOADING`, the command queue is flushed `Dropped(RELOAD)`, and the first `RUNNING` tick sees an empty queue. Cites: SL-7, T-8.
47. **AC-SL-47 (auto) — nothing is read before the pre-check passes.** GIVEN the newest generation refused by the pre-check, WHEN the stub runs, THEN it falls back to the next candidate before `request_restore`, and the kernel is touched at most once. Cites: SL-7, SL-11.
48. **AC-SL-48 (auto) — no loadable save.** GIVEN every candidate refused, WHEN the stub runs, THEN `SL_LOAD_NO_LOADABLE_SAVE` is published, the kernel stays untouched, Save/Load issues no pause, no file is deleted, and no autosave of the dead world occurs. Cites: SL-7.
49a. **AC-SL-49a (auto) — the stub never waits on the writer** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*. GIVEN a write job that has reached `WRITTEN` by the `DEAD` boundary, THEN its generation is a candidate for the selection; GIVEN a job still busy (the manual-pump executor stalled in `VERIFYING`), WHEN the stub runs, THEN the executor spy records zero blocking waits or joins on the main thread, the job is abandoned with `SL_WRITE_ABANDONED_AT_DEATH`, `write_epoch` is bumped, `writer_busy` is false and the selection is made among the already-verified candidates in the same boundary. Cites: SL-7, SL-10, T-8.
49b. **AC-SL-49b (auto) — the initial baseline failed.** GIVEN the initial `NEW_GAME` write `FAILED` when `DEAD` is entered, WHEN the stub runs, THEN `SL_NEW_GAME_NO_BASELINE` is raised, the empty campaign directory is removed and no `request_restore` is issued; and GIVEN the baseline job still busy at the `DEAD` boundary, THEN it is abandoned, there is no candidate and the same outcome follows *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*. Cites: SL-18, SL-7.
50. **AC-SL-50 (auto) — `RISKY` step-back and demotion.** GIVEN ring G7 (`T = 40 000`, `RISKY`), G6 (29 000), G5 (18 000), G7 restored, and `T_downed = 41 200`, `DEAD` at 43 000, WHEN the stub selects, THEN G6 is chosen and G7 is renamed `.demoted` and absent from the candidate list even after `campaign.json` is deleted; a second death before any autosave restores G6 again, never G7. Cites: SL-8, F-3.
51. **AC-SL-51 (auto) — a non-`RISKY` generation steps back only inside the fairness window** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*. GIVEN the same ring with G7 untagged and, on a fresh fixture per case, a fall at `T_downed − g.T` = 0, 1 and 599 after the restore, THEN G6 is selected and G7 is renamed `.demoted` (the fairness route, no `RISKY` needed); GIVEN a fall at 600, 1 200, 3 600, 3 601 and 10 000, THEN G7 is restored in all five and nothing is demoted; GIVEN the same fixtures with `W_MIN_WARNING_S` 4 and 20 (windows of 240 and 1 200 ticks), THEN the boundary moves with it (239 and 1 199 step back, 240 and 1 200 do not). Cites: SL-8, F-3, OQ-1.
52. **AC-SL-52 (auto) — the window is measured at `DOWNED`.** GIVEN a `RISKY` g_r with `T_downed − g_r.T = 3 600` (inside) and 3 601 (outside), and `DOWN_TO_DEATH_TICKS` varied across 600, 1 800 and 3 600, WHEN the stub selects, THEN the inside case steps back and the outside case does not, independently of `DOWN_TO_DEATH_TICKS`. Cites: SL-8, F-3.
53. **AC-SL-53 (auto) — no loop when a newer generation exists.** GIVEN a `RISKY` g_r and a newer non-demoted verified generation written after the restore, WHEN the crew dies within the window, THEN the newer generation is restored and nothing is demoted. Cites: SL-8 (b).
54. **AC-SL-54 (auto) — degenerate rings.** GIVEN (a) one candidate that is `RISKY` with a loop, (b) only demoted candidates, (c) no restored generation yet, THEN (a) restores it and logs `SL_STEPBACK_NONE_AVAILABLE`, (b) a second pass restores the newest demoted one, (c) the newest is restored with no loop test. Cites: F-3.
55. **AC-SL-55 (auto) — loss bounds.** GIVEN 10 000 scripted death times generated from `LOSS_FUZZ_SEED` over the scenarios (clean cycle, continuous soft block, `DOWNED` at the cap, a recovery at the cap followed by a second fall on the first retry-grid tick after the refractory, one step-back, a death by `give_up`) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* at the default knobs, run twice — once with `EVENT_TRIGGERS` = {`NEW_GAME`} only and once with the shipped MVP set — plus scenarios with injected write failures (`fail_n` 1 … 6) and a `RISKY` cap generation followed by a second death *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*, WHEN `discard_per_restore` (= `loss_ticks`) is computed, THEN on a healthy writer `min ≥ 49` (`GIVE_UP_MIN_TICKS + 1`; `≥ 1 801` for every timer-path death), `max ≤ 20 400` without a step-back (the recovery-at-the-cap scenario reaches exactly 20 400) and `≤ 24 000` with one, and the mean over uniform death phase of the timer-path deaths with an open gate and no events is within 1 % of 7 200; with events enabled no discard exceeds the interval-only maximum (events can only lower it); with injected write failures the discard exceeds 20 400 by at most the sum of the backoff delays accrued (the healthy-writer bound is not asserted for them); and `net_progress_lost` over the step-back chain is ≤ 39 000. Cites: F-7. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* *Amended conditions:* (a) the mean is asserted over a deterministic enumeration of death phases (every `T_dead` of one interval at stride 60, no random sample, so the check cannot fail on an unlucky seed), equal to 7 200 within one stride; the random scenarios assert only the min/max bounds. (b) Revive-and-fall-again scenarios k = 1 and k = 2 (a seat downed for 1 799 ticks, recovered at the cap, downed again) assert `max ≤ 22 199` and `≤ 23 998` and that I9 does not refuse. (c) The clean-quit scenario: `QUIT` at `T_q`, Continue, death at `T_q + 599` asserts the QUIT generation is restored again (no demotion, loss = `T_dead − T_q`) unless its `risky_cause` contains `VITALS`, in which case it steps back (OQ-52).
56. **AC-SL-56 (auto) — the stub keeps `k_z` and attaches no penalty.** GIVEN a death stub run, WHEN the first `RUNNING` frame is produced, THEN the Pipeline test double reports the pre-load `k_z`, a `CUT`, and no death-screen, lights-out or beat state; no item, stat or counter other than the restored blocks differs from the generation. Cites: SL-7, SL-21.
57a. **AC-SL-57a (auto) — owner failure in `LOADING` is `FAULTED`, not retried in line.** GIVEN an owner `restore()` returning a `<BLOCK>_RESTORE_*` failure on the staged payload, WHEN `LOADING` runs, THEN the kernel enters `FAULTED`, the loader does not retry, and the menu's Continue re-runs the pipeline on the remaining candidates with `restore_reason = FAULT_RECOVERY`. Cites: SL-11, SL-22.
57b. **AC-SL-57b (auto) — failure counter and quarantine.** GIVEN the same generation failing across Continue runs, THEN its failure counter increments per failure and persists across the runs; at `QUARANTINE_AFTER_FAILURES` (2) it moves to `quarantine/` with reason `RESTORE_FAILED` and the next Continue loads the next candidate. Cites: SL-22, T-3.
57c. **AC-SL-57c (auto) — dev-build `FAULTED`.** GIVEN a dev build, WHEN the kernel enters `FAULTED`, THEN the build halts with diagnostics, offers no menu path, and makes no automatic revert from the in-memory pre-restore snapshot. Cites: SL-22.
112. **AC-SL-112 (auto) — no `DOWNED` observed.** GIVEN `DEAD` entered with no prior `DOWNED` observation, WHEN `generation_select` runs, THEN `T_downed = T_dead`, the hard condition has closed the gate at that boundary, and selection follows F-3 with that value. Cites: F-3.
113. **AC-SL-113 (auto) — re-entrancy and attempt bound.** GIVEN `continue_campaign()` called twice while the kernel is `LOADING`, THEN the second returns `SL_LOAD_REENTRANT`, with one `write_epoch` bump, one `restore_epoch` step and one restore. GIVEN a campaign written with `AUTOSAVE_GENERATIONS` 5 and then opened with 3 (so five files remain candidates, SL-3) and `LOAD_MAX_ATTEMPTS` 3 (= `AUTOSAVE_GENERATIONS`, I11), the 3 newest corrupt, THEN exactly 3 pre-check attempts run and `SL_LOAD_NO_LOADABLE_SAVE` is published even though the fourth is intact; with `AUTOSAVE_GENERATIONS` 5 and `LOAD_MAX_ATTEMPTS` 5 the fourth loads; a `LOAD_MAX_ATTEMPTS` below `AUTOSAVE_GENERATIONS` fails boot (I11) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*. Cites: SL-7, SL-11, F-8.
130. **AC-SL-130 (auto, integration) — a hung writer cannot hold the death stub or disable later autosaves** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*. GIVEN the manual-pump writer executor and a job stalled in re-read when a seat enters `DOWNED` (the `finish_or_abort` flag is set) and never pumped, WHEN `DEATH_STUB_WRITE_WAIT_MS` (5 000 ms of injected monotonic time) passes, THEN the job is abandoned (`SL_WRITE_ABANDONED_TIMEOUT`) and at `DEAD` the stub restores the newest already-verified generation with no blocking wait; WHEN a later autosave comes due after the restore with the gate open, THEN a fresh job starts (`writer_busy` is false), writes a `.tmp` whose name carries the new `write_epoch` (`gen_<write_epoch>_<n>.tmp`, different from the stalled job's), lands at an unclaimed ring position and ends `WRITTEN`; WHEN the stalled executor is then pumped to completion, THEN its generation is moved to `quarantine/` as `STALE_EPOCH`, no ring file is modified and the newest generation is unchanged; and GIVEN a job that hangs in `RUNNING` with no seat ever `DOWNED`, WHEN `WRITE_JOB_TIMEOUT_MS` (30 000 ms of injected monotonic time) passes, THEN it is abandoned (`SL_WRITE_ABANDONED_TIMEOUT`), `save_status` reads `FAILED`, `fail_n` is 1, and the next due autosave fires once `next_retry_T` is reached on a fresh job; and GIVEN `AUTOSAVE_GENERATIONS` 2 with the second position claimed by the abandoned job, THEN the write ends `FAILED` with `SL_WRITE_NO_POSITION` and backs off. Cites: SL-10, SL-7, F-9.
137. **AC-SL-137 (auto) — the `DOWNED`-entry command bounds a slow healthy write** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*. GIVEN a job in `WRITING_TMP` with a rename retry pending when a seat enters `DOWNED`, THEN `finish_or_abort` is set, the worker takes no rename retry beyond the first, skips the manifest rewrite and aborts at its first failure with the `.tmp` removed; GIVEN a job that settles 4 999 ms of injected monotonic time after the command, THEN it ends `WRITTEN` and is a candidate; GIVEN one that has not settled at 5 001 ms, THEN it is abandoned and takes no part in the selection. Cites: SL-10.
139. **AC-SL-139 (auto) — a `QUIT` generation steps back only on a `VITALS` cause or inside the fairness window** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*. GIVEN `g_r` a `RISKY` generation of kind `QUIT` with `risky_cause` not containing `VITALS` (a channel open at the quit) restored by Continue, no newer generation, and `T_downed − g_r.T` = 1 200, WHEN the stub selects, THEN `g_r` itself is restored again and nothing is demoted; GIVEN the same with `risky_cause` containing `VITALS`, THEN it steps back as in AC-SL-50; GIVEN a `QUIT` generation of any cause and `T_downed − g_r.T` = 599, THEN it steps back by the fairness route; GIVEN the same ring with `g_r` of kind `AUTO` or `EVENT`, THEN it steps back as in AC-SL-50. Cites: SL-8, F-3.
140. **AC-SL-140 (auto, integration) — the hot copy and the step-back pre-stage** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*. GIVEN a generation `WRITTEN` and the stub running with the hot copy present, THEN the file-system adapter records zero reads during the `DEAD` boundary and `t_read` is 0; GIVEN the hot copy dropped (a cold process, a demoted newest, an abandoned job), THEN the disk chain runs and the selection is identical; GIVEN `g_r` `RISKY`, or `g_r` of any kind with `T_downed − T(g_r)` under 600 ticks *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*, and a bound seat entering `DOWNED`, THEN exactly the one candidate a step-back would select is staged (one read, on the worker), it is discarded on a `DOWNED → IDLE` recovery and re-staged on the next `DOWNED`, and a newer generation written in between replaces the staged candidate. Cites: SL-11, SL-8, F-8. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* *Unfinished pre-stage:* GIVEN a step-back after a fast death (a solo `give_up` 48 ticks after `DOWNED`) with the off-thread decode of the predecessor not finished, THEN the stub falls through to the disk chain, the selection is identical, the `DEAD`-tick hitch is recorded against `DEAD_TICK_HITCH_MS`, and no blocking wait on the worker occurs; with the predecessor's decoded copy ready, THEN the `DEAD` tick only selects and hands over (zero reads, zero decode on the main thread).
134. **AC-SL-134 (auto) — a dead session with no loadable save is not a dead end** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*. GIVEN the death stub with every candidate refused (`SL_LOAD_NO_LOADABLE_SAVE`) and the kernel still `RUNNING` with a dead world, WHEN the host-local API is introspected and driven by the Chart Table test double, THEN `new_game` and the shutdown path are both reachable, `save_and_quit()` returns `SL_QUIT_NO_WRITE_DOWNED` (or the `DEAD` equivalent) and writes nothing, no autosave of the dead world occurs, and no file is deleted. Cites: SL-7, OQ-42.

141. **AC-SL-141 (auto + manual) — the death-restore line** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* *(user ruling D15 (b))*. GIVEN a death-stub restore of a generation with `T = 437 400`, WHEN the first `RUNNING` frame is produced, THEN `restore_reason` reads `DEATH_STUB` and the HUD test double receives line data with playtime 7 290 s (`T div TICK_RATE`), and after a step-back the data is the older generation's; GIVEN Continue (`LOAD_GAME`), `NEW_GAME` or `FAULT_RECOVERY`, THEN no line data is raised; GIVEN UI-7 taking the `FIRST_FRAME_LINE` slot, THEN UI-11 is skipped; GIVEN the shipped string, THEN it passes the AC-SL-86 token lint and contains no death wording; (manual) a retained screenshot of the first `RUNNING` frame after a death shows the line on a Ground plate with no Flesh or Violet pixels and nothing else added by Save/Load (part of the AC-SL-117 set). Cites: SL-7, SL-21, UI-11. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* The manual half captures the HUD layer alone or asserts on the HUD draw list, not a whole-frame diff.
142. **AC-SL-142 (auto) — `restore_epoch` advances once per restore, is durable first, and everything else continues exactly** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review, delegated ruling R1)* *(user ruling D16, OQ-47)*. GIVEN a session saved by `save_and_quit()` at `T = 20 000` with `restore_epoch = e`, WHEN Continue restores it, THEN before the first `RUNNING` tick the header of the restored file reads `e + 1` on disk, `kernel.clock.restore_epoch` reads `e + 1` and `restore_reason` reads `LOAD_GAME`; AND an uninterrupted control kernel given the same injected `restore_epoch` and the restored kernel, run to `T = 21 000` on the same command stream, have equal `state_hash(PERSIST)` and equal persisted counters and seeds, while a second control run at epoch `e` differs from the restored one only in threat draws keyed after the restore (in a window with no threat draw the three are equal); GIVEN a death-stub restore, THEN `restore_reason` reads `DEATH_STUB` on the first `RUNNING` frame and the epoch advanced by exactly 1 (a step-back to an older generation included: `E_seen + 1`, never lower); GIVEN a stub that finds nothing loadable, THEN the epoch did not advance. Cites: SL-21, SL-23.
143. **AC-SL-143 (auto) — a `give_up` death and a death in the pod** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* *(user rulings D7, D60)*. GIVEN a seat `DOWNED` that applies `give_up` at `T_downed + GIVE_UP_MIN_TICKS`, WHEN `DEAD` is entered, THEN the stub runs in that boundary exactly as on the timer path (AC-SL-46), a write job still busy at that boundary is abandoned with no blocking wait (AC-SL-49a) and its late generation lands as `STALE_EPOCH`, the previous verified generation is restored, and `loss_ticks ≥ GIVE_UP_MIN_TICKS + 1` (asserted in AC-SL-55); GIVEN a seat `IN_POD` that goes `DOWNED` and then `DEAD` in the pod, THEN the hard gate was closed while it was `DOWNED`, `request_restore` is issued at the `DEAD` boundary, `web.state` restores the leg, and there is no second code path. Cites: SL-7, SL-10, F-7.
145. **AC-SL-145 (auto, integration) — the three required restore-epoch cases and their failure paths** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review, delegated ruling R1)*. (i) GIVEN one save restored twice (stub then stub, or Continue then Continue, in one process), THEN the epochs are `e + 1` and `e + 2` and the kernel-derived threat draws (a spy on the kernel's `counter_eff` derivation, same `rng_counter`) differ. (ii) GIVEN the process killed after `EPOCH_DURABLE` and a fresh process that Continues the same slot, with `campaign.json` deleted before the relaunch, THEN the epoch advances again (`e + 2`), read from the headers; GIVEN the newest file deleted while a `.bak` or a `quarantine/` header carries a higher value, THEN the new value exceeds it. (iii) GIVEN the same (save bytes, `restore_epoch`) pair run twice to `T + 3 600`, THEN `state_hash` and every draw are identical. (iv) GIVEN the header-only rewrite failing (injected), THEN `SL_RESTORE_EPOCH_NOT_DURABLE` is logged, play proceeds, and the next generation's header carries the value; GIVEN a kill at each step of the rewrite (the fault-injection harness, T-6), THEN recovery finds a verifying file holding the old or the new value and never neither, and two files with one `generation` are ordered by `restore_epoch`. (v) GIVEN `restore_epoch = 2^32 − 1`, THEN the next restore saturates and logs, and no header is written with a value ≥ 2^32. (vi) GIVEN two restores at different epochs, THEN the Graph's physical failure schedule and every persisted counter are identical; only epoch-keyed threat draws differ. (vii) GIVEN a grep of the shipped source and data, THEN `threat_epoch` has zero hits (lint; positive control: a fixture containing it fails). Cites: SL-1, SL-23, T-6, T-8.
146. **AC-SL-146 (auto) — scheduler and ring edge cases** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*. (i) *Pending events are a set.* GIVEN a RAGGED cut at `T = 440 000` with two stubs open, then a weld at `T = 440 900`, WHEN the gate runs on every grid tick, THEN `event_due = 0` until the last stub is capped at `T = 441 800`, one capture fires and both entries clear; in the reverse order (weld, then cut) the same holds. (ii) *A failed write does not reset the deferral clock.* GIVEN `due_first_T = 10 800` and writes failing at 10 800 and 11 100, THEN `due_first_T` is still 10 800 and a soft-blocked save fires at the cap 18 000; only a `WRITTEN` capture or a restore clears it. (iii) *Backoff overflow.* GIVEN `fail_n` = 60 (and 1 000), THEN `next_retry_T = last_fail_T + AUTOSAVE_FAIL_BACKOFF_MAX_TICKS`, the exponent is clamped at 16, no integer overflows and no retry fires on every grid tick. (iv) *Demotion is a worker job.* GIVEN a step-back, THEN the in-memory ring marks `g_r` demoted before `request_restore`, the `DEMOTE` rename runs on the worker (the executor spy records zero main-thread renames), and GIVEN a kill between `request_restore` and the rename, THEN on relaunch `g_r` is the newest again, a death inside the same window demotes it again, and no `.demote_intent` file exists. (v) *Absent soft inputs.* GIVEN no Suit view, THEN `vitals_block` is false and `GATE_INPUT_ABSENT{VITALS}` is logged once per process; the same for `ATTENTION_VIEW` and `RAGGED_BREACH`. Cites: F-1, F-2, F-9, SL-8.
147. **AC-SL-147 (auto, integration) — worker lifecycle and the single-instance lock** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*. GIVEN a worker that raises an error and dies with no terminal status, THEN the next poll reaps it, logs `SL_WRITE_WORKER_DIED`, sets `FAILED`, releases the ring position and does not hold the hard gate for `WRITE_JOB_TIMEOUT_MS`; GIVEN `MAX_LIVE_ABANDONED_JOBS` workers abandoned and alive, THEN the next write ends `SL_WRITE_TOO_MANY_ABANDONED`; GIVEN process exit with an abandoned worker alive, THEN exit does not block and the next open's T-6 handles the `.tmp`; GIVEN a second process opening a campaign whose lock is held, THEN it opens `READ_ONLY` (no generation written, no T-6 deletion or quarantine move, only New campaign and Quit offered) and the first process's files are byte-identical afterwards. Cites: SL-10.
148. **AC-SL-148 (auto) — the writer verifies its own output** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*. GIVEN a snapshot with an untyped-container violation injected (a typed `Array[int]`, a `Vector2i`), WHEN the write runs, THEN `VERIFYING` fails with `SL_WRITE_UNENCODABLE` naming the block, no generation is replaced, and in a dev or test build the capture-time `is_typed()` assertion fires first; GIVEN a file that passed write verification but whose type walk fails at load (injected), THEN `SL_WRITER_DEFECT` is logged, the file is marked `suspect`, the next candidate is tried and no other generation is quarantined. Cites: SL-2, SL-10, SL-11, T-4, T-5 step 19.

### G. Verification, restore fidelity and the kernel contract (SL-12, SL-15, F-4)

58. **AC-SL-58 (auto) — the kernel's G4 through the on-disk file (EXACT).** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: split into (a) the through-disk harness half, unblocked — the compare is taken by the test harness between the last `post_restore()` and tick one, in dev and test builds — and (b) the release-build half, blocked on K-4 (OQ-6): the same compare inside `LOADING`, not passed until the kernel answers. The real `controller.state` and `body.state` fixtures belong to AC-SL-67.)* GIVEN `REBASE_BLOCKS` empty, the kernel's stand-in blocks and the real `graph.state`, a snapshot at `T = 1 000` written to disk, a fresh kernel restored from the file and both run to `T = 1 100`, WHEN `state_hash(PERSIST)` is compared, THEN they are equal, the header hash equals the recomputed hash at the compare, the mode is EXACT and the verdict is `MATCH`. Cites: SL-12, F-4, the kernel's G4 criterion and its restore round-trip criterion, through disk.
59. **AC-SL-59 (auto) — the compare counts only at the right tick.** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: (a) harness half, unblocked; (b) the in-`LOADING` release half, blocked on K-4, OQ-6.)* GIVEN a compare attempted after one tick has run (`kernel.clock.T` ≠ header `T`), WHEN `save_verification` runs, THEN `SL_VERIFY_WINDOW_MISSED` is logged, the verdict is `NONE` and no baseline or `suspect` mark is recorded. Cites: SL-12, F-4.
60. **AC-SL-60 (auto) — a reproduction mismatch plays on and never falls back.** GIVEN an EXACT-mode load and one byte of a restored block altered after restore, WHEN the compare runs, THEN `SL_VERIFY_NOT_REPRODUCED{blocks}` is logged; with `release_mode` true the generation is marked `suspect` in `campaign.json` and the session continues on it; with `release_mode` false the injected `halt` handler is invoked once with the diverging block names; in both modes no other candidate is tried and nothing is quarantined. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: the harness-driven compare asserts the `halt` and `suspect` behaviours above in dev and test builds)* The `release_mode` half is **blocked** on the kernel's answer to K-4 (OQ-6) and is not passed until then. Cites: SL-12, T-3.
61. **AC-SL-61 (auto) — each REBASED cause.** GIVEN, in turn, (1) a v1 golden save restored by a synthetic v2 that migrates `graph.state`, (2) a stripped retired block, (3) a `default_state()` fill, (4) an unknown `hash_algo`, (5) a file containing a `REBASE_BLOCKS` member, WHEN each loads, THEN each yields mode REBASED with the named cause, the load succeeds, the recomputed hash is recorded in `campaign.json` only, the file is not rewritten, and for (1) G4 equivalence holds on continued play; and an additive bump (`format_version + 1`, `min_reader_version` = new, identity container migration, `default_state()` fill) loads a v1 file under a v2 build; a second restore of the same REBASED generation is checked against the recorded baseline (AC-SL-138) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*. Cites: SL-12, SL-14, F-4.
62. **AC-SL-62 (auto) — golden v1 restored by a synthetic v2.** GIVEN the stand-in golden v1 save in `tests/fixtures/saves/v1-standin/` (until the first outside build; the frozen golden in `tests/fixtures/saves/v1/` takes its place then, SL-14) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* and a synthetic v2 migration of `graph.state`, WHEN it is loaded, THEN the migrated state round-trips with an equal `state_hash` after the next autosave, and the loader does not walk the ring. Cites: SL-14, Graph LS-21 golden-save criterion.
63. **AC-SL-63 (lint) — golden saves are immutable.** GIVEN a CI run, WHEN a file under `tests/fixtures/saves/v1/` (the frozen golden) changes without an explicit golden-regeneration marker, THEN the check fails; the stand-in directory is exempt; and a schema-drift fingerprint check fails when the committed block shapes' `registry_epoch` digest changes without a `schema_version` or `format_version` bump (SL-14) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*. Cites: SL-1, SL-14.
64. **AC-SL-64 (auto) — block contract boot assertions.** GIVEN fixtures that (a) lack `supported_versions`, (b) name an unregistered `restore_after` target, (c) mark a `PERCEPTION_OUT` block `persist`, (d) omit `persist_scope` — including a Sanity-shaped fixture (a level block with `persist` and no `persist_scope`) and a Suit-shaped one, (e) have an unkeyed `PER_PLAYER` snapshot, (f) lack `added_in_format`, (g) are on `RETIRED_BLOCKS` and registered, WHEN Save/Load boots, THEN each fails with the matching `SL_BOOT_*` reason (`NO_SUPPORTED_VERSIONS`, `RESTORE_AFTER_UNREGISTERED`, `PERCEPTION_PERSIST`, `SCOPE_MISSING`, `PER_PLAYER_UNKEYED`, `NO_ADDED_IN_FORMAT`, `RETIRED_BUT_REGISTERED`) and the composition root refuses to start naming block and rule. Cites: SL-15.
65. **AC-SL-65 (auto) — generic round trip per block.** GIVEN every `persist` block with a populated fixture factory, WHEN `snapshot → var_to_bytes → bytes_to_var → restore → snapshot` runs, THEN the canonical bytes are equal (restore-idempotence, the kernel's byte-identity rule), a JSON-dump assertion shows 64-bit values as strings, and a block lacking `default_state()` or a fixture factory fails this test; the Graph instance passes its own restore-idempotence criterion. A block listed in `REBASE_BLOCKS` runs this test with a rest-state fixture and its mid-move, mid-channel or pending fixture runs a **characterisation test** that asserts the *current* behaviour (`snapshot(restore(snapshot()))` differs from `snapshot()` in exactly the documented transient fields) and is tagged with the debt ids `OQ-9` / `OQ-39`; CI forbids skipped and expected-fail tests, so when an owner repays the debt the characterisation assertion flips and fails, and is deleted in the same change that removes the block from the list, after which AC-SL-67 is the standing gate *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*. Cites: SL-15 items 7, 10 and 12, K-3.
66. **AC-SL-66 (review, checklist) — the restore-idempotence debt is delivered and tracked** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*. GIVEN the Open Questions section, WHEN reviewed, THEN OQ-9 records the Controller's and Body's answers (PC-17 normalises in `snapshot()`; BDY-31), OQ-39 records the remaining Interaction and Assembly debt addressed to their owners, and `REBASE_BLOCKS` is named as the interim debt with its exit. Cites: SL-12, OQ-9, OQ-39.
67. **AC-SL-67 (auto) — real-block restore fidelity; the merge gate** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*. GIVEN the real `controller.state` on a mid-move fixture (octant set, `run` true, a channel reference, a `STAGGERED` seat with a timer), the real `interaction.state` on a mid-channel fixture (an open door attempt, an in-progress channel, a `last_result`), the real `assembly.state` on a pending-weld fixture and the real `body.state`, WHEN each runs `snapshot → var_to_bytes → bytes_to_var → restore → snapshot`, THEN the canonical bytes are equal for each, and a snapshot written to disk and restored by a fresh kernel verifies `MATCH` in EXACT mode with `REBASE_BLOCKS` empty. This is the merge gate for the Controller, Interaction and Assembly stories; a block still listed in `REBASE_BLOCKS` runs the characterisation test of AC-SL-65 instead and moves to this criterion when it leaves the list, a story whose fixture fails may list its block only with a dated debt entry naming the owner (SL-12), and the list must be empty at the first outside build. Cites: SL-12, SL-15 item 10, OQ-9, OQ-39.
68. **AC-SL-68 (auto) — a reload reproduces the same keyed draw.** GIVEN an RNG stand-in block drawing by `rng_derive(world_seed, stream, counter)` with the counter inside the block, WHEN a snapshot is restored and the same ticks replayed, THEN the sequence of draws is identical to the uninterrupted run. Cites: SL-23, kernel D.10.
96d. **AC-SL-96d (auto) — non-conforming restore return.** GIVEN stub blocks returning null, a bare `true`, a dictionary lacking `reason`, and a `reason` outside the block's declared set, WHEN `LOADING` runs, THEN each is `SL_RESTORE_NONCONFORMING`, the kernel enters `FAULTED`, and no automatic retry occurs. Cites: SL-15 item 5.
119. **AC-SL-119 (auto) — suspect counters.** GIVEN a generation marked `suspect` and a later generation that reproduces, THEN the counter resets; reaching `SUSPECT_THRESHOLD` (3) logs a diagnostic only and changes nothing about loading. Cites: T-3, SL-12.
119b. **AC-SL-119b (review) — a re-signed hand edit loads (accepted exposure).** GIVEN a hand-edited file re-signed with a valid trailer and valid block hashes whose payload decodes validly, WHEN the pre-check runs, THEN it loads; this section records the case as an accepted exposure and no criterion requires a tamper rejection (integrity is against accidental corruption, not tampering — host-owned, no PvP, no leaderboard). Cites: SL-16.
138. **AC-SL-138 (auto, integration) — the REBASED baseline is read back** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*. GIVEN REBASED mode (a `REBASE_BLOCKS` member present) and a first restore of a generation, THEN the verdict is `REBASED_RECORDED` and the recomputed value is stored in `campaign.json` for (`generation`, `build_id`, `registry_epoch`, trailer SHA-256 prefix); GIVEN a second restore of the same generation, THEN the verdict is `BASELINE_MATCH`; GIVEN one byte of a restored block altered, THEN the verdict is `BASELINE_DIVERGED` (release: `suspect`, play on; dev and test: the injected `halt` handler is invoked once; no other candidate is tried); GIVEN `campaign.json` deleted between the two restores, or a different `build_id`, THEN the value is re-recorded and no verdict fails. Cites: SL-12, F-4.

### H. PROFILE store, identity and what is not saved (SL-9, SL-17, SL-20, SL-24, SL-25)

69. **AC-SL-69 (auto) — PROFILE untouched by a world restore.** GIVEN a synthetic PROFILE block mutated after the snapshot, WHEN the death stub restores a generation, THEN the WORLD blocks roll back, the PROFILE block still holds every post-snapshot mutation, and `request_restore` was passed WORLD blocks only; and WHEN the process is restarted and Continue is used, THEN each scope loads whole from its own file. Cites: SL-9, kernel CR-18 (its PROFILE-untouched criterion is the model).
70. **AC-SL-70 (auto) — `profile.sav` rules.** GIVEN (a) zero PROFILE blocks registered and no `profile.sav`, (b) one registered and no `profile.sav` and no `.bak`, (c) a `.bak` only, (d) a profile write killed mid-way, THEN (a) opens with an empty profile, (b) refuses `SL_PRECHECK_MANDATORY_MISSING` for the profile, the world still loads and the profile resets with `SL_PROFILE_RESET`, (c) the `.bak` is used, (d) T-6 recovers and the ring is unaffected; and the file is rewritten only when a capture's PROFILE per-block hashes differ, on `QUIT`, or on an owner notification, never faster than `PROFILE_MIN_WRITE_GAP_TICKS`. Cites: SL-9.
71. **AC-SL-71 (auto) — zero blocks, zero hooks.** GIVEN Save/Load booted, WHEN the registry is inspected, THEN the only registered block is `kernel.clock` plus the test blocks, no hook or command type belongs to Save/Load (`saveload.request_save` is reserved, provisional, and not registered), and any attempt by Save/Load to register fails `SL_BOOT_SAVELOAD_REGISTERS_BLOCK`. Cites: SL-17, the kernel's single-block criterion.
72. **AC-SL-72 (auto) — `PERCEPTION_OUT` is absent from every file.** GIVEN a session with active sanity and a perception-output block registered, WHEN a generation and `profile.sav` are written, THEN neither file contains the perception block or any field describing what the player saw. Cites: SL-24, CR-12.
73. **AC-SL-73 (auto) — identity is the seat.** GIVEN a peer rebinding to a different transport id, WHEN the next generation is written and the previous one loaded, THEN every `PER_PLAYER` key is the same PlayerId, no file contains a peer id, and the binding table is absent from the file. Cites: SL-20, kernel CR-7.
74. **AC-SL-74 (auto) — settings and camera are not in a snapshot.** GIVEN a session with changed key bindings and a non-default zoom, WHEN a generation is written, THEN no settings, binding, camera or zoom field appears in any block. Cites: SL-19, SL-25.
96b. **AC-SL-96b (auto) — scope matrix.** GIVEN a synthetic block for each of `WORLD × SHARED`, `WORLD × PER_PLAYER`, `PROFILE × SHARED`, `PROFILE × PER_PLAYER`, each mutated after the snapshot, WHEN the death stub restores, THEN the two WORLD blocks roll back to their generation values and the two PROFILE blocks keep every post-snapshot mutation; `PER_PLAYER` data is keyed by PlayerId in both. `suit.state`, `sanity.state` and `sanity.ledger` are `PER_PLAYER × WORLD` in the MVP and must roll back (shapes per the designed Suit and Sanity GDDs). Cites: SL-9, SL-15 item 12.
116. **AC-SL-116 (auto) — PROFILE flush before restore.** GIVEN a PROFILE write pending when the death stub fires, THEN the profile is flushed first, `request_restore` receives WORLD blocks only, and no byte of `profile.sav` reaches a WORLD block. Cites: SL-9, SL-7.
123. **AC-SL-123 (auto) — the VS-A replacement keeps the PROFILE rule.** GIVEN a test double standing in for Failure Contract & Re-wake (provisional — undesigned) that restores a generation by the same `request_restore` path with a PROFILE block mutated, WHEN it runs in place of the stub, THEN the PROFILE block is kept and the WORLD blocks roll back (AC-SL-69 with the stub replaced), so the replacement cannot silently regress SL-9. Cites: SL-7, SL-9.

### I. New game, directory and API (SL-18, SL-19, SL-21, T-9)

75. **AC-SL-75 (auto) — new game baseline.** GIVEN `new_game(world_seed)` with a stand-in world builder and an injected entropy source supplying a *sequence* of 16-byte values *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*, WHEN the world is constructed, THEN the campaign directory exists and its 32-character lowercase-hex name equals the first value, `world_seed` is in `kernel.clock`, an initial generation (kind `EVENT`, trigger `NEW_GAME`) is `WRITTEN` at the first boundary after construction, and on construction failure or `AUTOSAVE_FAIL_ESCALATE_COUNT` baseline failures the empty directory is removed and `SL_NEW_GAME_NO_BASELINE` is raised; a second `new_game` whose first draw equals the existing directory's name never overwrites it and takes the sequence's next, different value (orchestrator ruling: OQ-31); and GIVEN two stand-in owners registering `init_world(world_seed)` with `restore_after` B → A, THEN A's step runs after B's, both inside one `WriteScope` and before `post_restore()`, and neither runs on a load from a save (kernel CR-18, K-2; WM-7 is the MVP world build) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*. Cites: SL-18, T-9.
76. **AC-SL-76 (auto) — host-local API surface.** GIVEN the release API, WHEN introspected, THEN it exposes `new_game`, `continue_campaign`, `save_and_quit`, `campaign_info`, `list_saves`, `retire_campaign` and `delete_campaign`, and exposes no `save_now` and no `load(generation)` (a dev build may). Cites: SL-4, SL-21.
77. **AC-SL-77 (auto) — retire before delete.** GIVEN New campaign over an existing campaign, WHEN confirmed, THEN the old campaign no longer appears in `list_saves()`, its files are byte-identical at their new location, and `continue_campaign()` opens the new one; deletion occurs only through the separate `delete_campaign` action. The retire path is an ADR choice and is asserted through the API only (OQ-27). Cites: SL-19.
78. **AC-SL-78 (auto) — quarantine is never auto-deleted.** GIVEN a quarantine folder beyond `SL_QUARANTINE_SIZE_ALARM_BYTES`, WHEN a long session of 100 writes and 10 failures runs, THEN a diagnostics alarm is logged and no quarantined file is deleted. Cites: SL-19, Tuning Knobs.
79. **AC-SL-79 (auto) — `FAULTED` never writes.** GIVEN the kernel in `FAULTED`, WHEN an autosave comes due, a Save & Quit is requested and a snapshot is requested, THEN no file in the campaign directory is created, renamed or modified, and the kernel hands no payload. Cites: SL-22, the kernel's `FAULTED` rule.
80. **AC-SL-80 (auto) — `restore_reason` on the first `RUNNING` frame.** GIVEN each path (`NEW_GAME`, `LOAD_GAME`, `DEATH_STUB`, `FAULT_RECOVERY`), WHEN `LOADING → RUNNING` occurs, THEN the host-local read reports the matching value for the first `RUNNING` frame, and no consumer in the MVP alters hallucination content from `DEATH_STUB`. Cites: SL-21, OQ-8.
81. **AC-SL-81 (auto) — `save_status` and `SaveInfo`.** GIVEN a write, WHEN it progresses, THEN `save_status` reads `IDLE → WRITING → WRITTEN` (sticky, with `written_at_T`) or `FAILED`, and `SaveInfo` (campaign id, generation, kind, `T`, playtime, `game_version`, crew count, `RISKY`, depth label) is computed from SIM blocks at capture, contains no thumbnail field, and its depth label (provisional — derived from the node `ring` of `web.view`) equals the header value; playtime is `T div TICK_RATE` whole seconds (at `T = 437 400` it is 7 290 s; orchestrator ruling: integer floor, OQ-30); `fail_escalated` is false through two consecutive failed writes, true from the third (`AUTOSAVE_FAIL_ESCALATE_COUNT`) and false again after the next `WRITTEN`; and `campaign_info().newest_failed_verification` is true for a ring whose newest generation has a corrupted trailer and false otherwise *(Amended 2026-10-27 — consistency pass)*. Cites: SL-21, T-7.
96e. **AC-SL-96e (auto) — Item DB header fields.** GIVEN `catalogue_version` = 7 and a `catalogue_hash`, WHEN a generation is written and read, THEN both round-trip; a changed `catalogue_hash` alone never refuses a load; an unknown `def_id` at restore surfaces as the owner's named failure (for example `GRAPH_RESTORE_UNKNOWN_DEF`) and is never a Save/Load refusal; GIVEN `catalogue_version` = 9 in the header and 7 in the running catalogue, THEN the pre-check does not refuse and the holder's restore decides. Cites: SL-1, SL-14.

### J. Cross-system criteria
