82. **AC-SL-82 (auto) — Graph through the container.** GIVEN the real `graph.state` at the MVP slice size, WHEN written and restored through disk, THEN the restore order places the `layout.state` stand-in before `graph.state` (kernel order, never Save/Load's), the encoded `graph.state` payload is ≤ 32 KB, restore plus `post_restore` reports success (`GRAPH_RESTORE_*` is not raised), and no packed array travels as a JSON number list. Cites: SL-2, SL-25, S-1, Graph LS-21.
82b. **AC-SL-82b (spike) — Graph cost.** GIVEN the same fixture on the target machine, WHEN run 1 000 times, THEN the snapshot costs ≤ 0.5 ms and restore plus `post_restore` ≤ 2 ms at P99 (the Graph's own criterion re-run through the container). Cites: Graph LS-21, F-5.
83. **AC-SL-83 (auto) — Controller.** GIVEN a Controller test double, WHEN a seat enters `DEAD`, THEN Save/Load issues `request_restore` in that tick's boundary, the Presenter holds the down sheet, no second camera cut is issued, and `DEAD` during a camera cut is handled. Cites: SL-7, the Controller's `DEAD` row.
84. **AC-SL-84 (manual) — Pipeline.** GIVEN an in-engine run, WHEN `LOADING` is entered mid-session, THEN `TRANSIT_HOLD` shows layer-1 black, zoom presses are discarded, and the first `RUNNING` frame is a camera `CUT` at the pre-load `k_z`; retained screenshots of the black frame and of the first `RUNNING` frame are in `production/qa/evidence/`. Cites: SL-7, Pipeline `TRANSIT_HOLD` and C5.
84b. **AC-SL-84b (review) — the routed Pipeline text fix exists.** GIVEN the Open Questions section, WHEN reviewed, THEN OQ-14 carries the row saying the Pipeline's 2.10 traceability entry is built in Failure Contract & Re-wake, not in the stub (this criterion checks the row, not another GDD's text). Cites: OQ-14.
85. **AC-SL-85 (review) — routed amendments are carried as routed.** GIVEN the Open Questions section, WHEN reviewed, THEN K-1, the D.10 note, K-3, K-4, K-5 (K-2 is adopted and OQ-4 is closed), the observation channel, `restore_reason`, the "no clean frame" ordering, the stale-token list and the index label clarification are rows OQ-2 … OQ-15 addressed to their owners, and none is written as an existing rule of another GDD. Cites: Open Questions.
86. **AC-SL-86 (lint + review) — stale tokens.** GIVEN the GDD, WHEN searched for "reload last save", "keyed by peer ID", "single-slot autosave", "P5 snapshot", "respawn", "save point", "game over", "you died", "progress saved", "quicksave", THEN every hit is on a line allow-listed in `tests/lint/save-load-stale-token-allowlist.txt` (negations and the stale-token row) and a reviewer signs the allow-list; AND GIVEN every player-facing string authored for Save/Load (pause panel, load screen, notices), THEN none contains a stale token, an exclamation mark, or a second-person death phrase. Cites: Player Fantasy fixed terms, OQ-14.
95a. **AC-SL-95a (auto) — the observation channel is exactly the granted reads.** GIVEN Save/Load booted with a kernel spy and a registry spy, WHEN a full session runs (autosaves, a death stub, a Save & Quit), THEN its recorded reads are exactly `kernel.clock`, `controller.state.loco_state` per bound seat, the Graph's `read_view.failures()`, the optional Attention SIM view `attention.view`, Suit's `view(player_id).vitals_state` per bound seat, Assembly's `ragged_breach_open()` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*, the event-trigger reads of SL-4 (`POD_ARRIVED`, `last_shape_change_T()`, Interaction's `last_salvage_T()`), and the end-of-tick change notifications; no `PERCEPTION_OUT` view is read; the wall clock is never read inside a decision about sim time (only `created_unix` metadata is written), and the injected monotonic millisecond source is read only by the I/O timeout paths of SL-23 (`SAVE_QUIT_TIMEOUT_MS`, `RENAME_RETRY_DELAY_MS`, `READ_RETRY_DELAY_MS`, `DEATH_STUB_WRITE_WAIT_MS`) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review, delegated ruling R1)* Save/Load makes no read of any epoch: it supplies `restore_epoch` only as the second argument of `request_restore` (SL-23), so a spy sees no `threat_epoch()` or other epoch read and a correct implementation passes. Cites: SL-13, SL-23, SL-24, CR-12.
95b. **AC-SL-95b (lint) — Save/Load never writes the sim.** GIVEN the Save/Load source, WHEN searched, THEN no call to a block mutator or `restore()` appears; the worker module imports no kernel and no block module; `created_unix` is written and never read by a decision. Recommended gate level BLOCKING (guards the kernel-write boundary). Cites: SL-10, SL-13, SL-17.
121. **AC-SL-121 (review) — Attention soft-gate view (blocked).** GIVEN the designed Attention GDD, WHEN reviewed, THEN its SIM read view `attention.view` carries `tell_active`, `tell_remaining` (0 … `TELL_MAX_TICKS`) and `breach_phase`, and a view returning a `PERCEPTION_OUT`-derived value fails AC-SL-95a. Unblocked 2026-10-27 (consistency pass); the Graph half of the tell condition is not blocked (AC-SL-125) *(Amended 2026-10-26 — CD-GDD-ALIGN)*. Cites: SL-5, SL-13, OQ-24.
122. **AC-SL-122 (review) — Emitter and Body restore order (blocked).** GIVEN `emitter.state` with `restore_after` [`layout.state`, `graph.state`, `body.state`, `controller.state`] (all four designed: Layout LY-1, Body BDY-5, Emitter EM-1), WHEN restored through the container with stand-ins, THEN the kernel's order is honoured, and with `body.state` unregistered boot fails `SL_BOOT_RESTORE_AFTER_UNREGISTERED`. Unblocked: `body.state` is registered by Ship Body & Interior Physics (BDY-5; OQ-10 closed). Cites: SL-25, OQ-10.
124. **AC-SL-124 (review) — the "no clean frame" ordering stays recorded.** GIVEN the Perception Filter, Sanity or Pipeline GDD when it lands, WHEN reviewed, THEN OQ-11 is either answered with an owner or still open with no owner, and AC-SL-93 is re-run. Cites: SL-24, OQ-11.
127. **AC-SL-127 (auto) — the Overlay diagnostics channel** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — consistency pass, routed from debug-overlay-true-values)*. GIVEN a dev-tag build with a channel-registry spy, WHEN the composition root runs, THEN Save/Load registers exactly one channel, `save_load`, while the kernel is `BOOTING`, with the eight rows of the Debug Overlay row in Interactions; WHEN a write fails, THEN `pull()` reports `save_status` `FAILED` and the last `SL_*` reason; WHEN `pull()` is called during a full session, THEN Save/Load's recorded kernel and block reads are unchanged from AC-SL-95a; GIVEN a release-tag build, THEN no channel is registered. Cites: debug-overlay-true-values DO-2, DO-12, AC-DO-69; registry `diag_panel_contract`.

### K. Performance, feel and presentation

87. **AC-SL-87 (spike) — capture cost unchanged.** GIVEN the MVP slice (14 blocks, about 86 KiB, with the shipped `hash_algo`) on the target machine *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*, WHEN 1 000 autosaves run, THEN `main_hitch_ms` (= `capture_time_ms + t_file_encode + t_handoff`) is ≤ `SNAPSHOT_BUDGET_MS` (16.7 ms) at P99; `t_file_encode` ≤ `SAVE_ENCODE_BUDGET_MS`; `t_handoff` ≤ `WRITE_HANDOFF_MAX_FRACTION × SNAPSHOT_BUDGET_MS`; `durable_ms` ≤ `WRITE_DURABLE_ALARM_MS`; `worker_ms` ≤ 30 000 ms (`DOWN_TO_DEATH_TICKS × 1000 / TICK_RATE`) at the maximum observed; every exceeded threshold is logged and no write is truncated; and no frame between `WRITING_TMP` and `MANIFEST` has CPU frame work above P99(baseline CPU frame work, same build, no save) + `SAVE_HITCH_BUDGET_MS`; `main_hitch_ms` P99 ≤ `SAVE_HITCH_BUDGET_MS` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*. The measured encoding-overhead ratio, the hash cost (`t_hash` for the shipped H64 and, for the record, a scripted xxHash64 or FNV-1a loop) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings, consistency follow-up)* and the header-size constants (`H0` 500 – 700, `BLOCK_RECORD_BYTES` 180 – 240) are recorded. Cites: F-5, F-6 (orchestrator ruling: the one-frame target applies to the main-thread sum; OQ-33).
88. **AC-SL-88 (spike) — restart time.** GIVEN the death stub on the target machine with an MVP-size generation, WHEN `DEAD` to first `RUNNING` frame is timed over 1 000 runs, THEN `restart_ms` ≤ `LOADING_TARGET_MS` at P99 and the `DEAD`-tick hitch from the pre-check is recorded; if missed, the alternatives of OQ-23 are evaluated. Cites: F-8, SL-11. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* The record also holds log timestamps at three points (the `DEAD` tick, the first black frame, the first `RUNNING` frame), the `DEAD`-tick main-thread hitch against `DEAD_TICK_HITCH_MS` (separate from the black hold), `t_epoch` (the header-only `restore_epoch` rewrite) against `RESTORE_EPOCH_WRITE_MAX_MS`, and the hot-copy RAM; if the epoch rewrite exceeds the 120 ms hold, the hold stretches and the measured P99 goes to the user (SL-23).
89. **AC-SL-89 (spike) — hash and decode cost.** GIVEN the ratified H64 (first 8 bytes of SHA-256 via `HashingContext`) over 100 – 300 KiB, WHEN measured on capture and on restore, THEN `t_hash_capture` and `t_hash_restore` are recorded to `production/qa/evidence/spike-hash.md` and the K-1 / hash-ADR decision (OQ-2, OQ-17) is written beside the number; pass = a decision exists for the measured value, and if `t_hash_capture` exceeds the kernel's 1.5 ms assumption the decision names the mitigation (the per-block cache K-1). Cites: SL-16. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings, consistency follow-up)*
90. **AC-SL-90 (spike) — engine claims.** GIVEN the spike, WHEN run, THEN a table records, per claim, `confirmed` or `amended` (with the amended rule id): `var_to_bytes` int-exactness and signed-`u64` behaviour; typed-container and `StringName`-key encoding; duplicate-key JSON behaviour; native SHA-256 throughput; the worker hand-off and thread file access; the `HashingContext` algorithm list; the close notification and `auto_accept_quit`; `process_mode` ALWAYS polling; Mutex-guarded status publication; the thread join signatures; rename and flush semantics; and peak memory and time to decode a 32 MiB hostile payload (nested arrays, array of nulls, large integer-keyed dictionary) against the ceiling set in OQ-35; the count of [UNV] tags left in the GDD equals the count of still-open spike items (lint). Cites: OQ-16, OQ-35. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* *Spike rows added:* a worker killed by an injected GDScript error is reaped as `SL_WRITE_WORKER_DIED` within one poll; process exit with an abandoned worker still running does not block; with `MAX_LIVE_ABANDONED_JOBS` workers abandoned the next write ends `SL_WRITE_TOO_MANY_ABANDONED`; the exclusive lock is measured under SSD, HDD, antivirus and a OneDrive-redirected `user://`.
91. **AC-SL-91 (manual) — the save is imperceptible.** GIVEN a 30-minute playtest at 60 Hz with a frame-time log, autosaves, at least one event save and exactly one death, WHEN reviewed, THEN (a) no frame within ±2 frames of a capture start has CPU frame work above P99(baseline CPU frame work) + `SAVE_HITCH_BUDGET_MS` (CPU work, not presented intervals, which vsync quantises) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*, and the gate-open and gate-deferring periods are compared as in AC-SL-131 *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*; (b) no sound, text or mark announces an autosave anywhere on the HUD (the failed-write notice stays absent while writes succeed; checked by capture review, not recollection) *(Amended 2026-10-26 — CD-GDD-ALIGN)*; (c) the black hold, read from log timestamps at three points (the `DEAD` tick, the first black frame and the first `RUNNING` frame; no stopwatch) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*, is ≥ 120 ms (the `TRANSIT_HOLD` fade) and `restart_ms` ≤ `LOADING_TARGET_MS`, both values written on the evidence sheet; (d) a screenshot of the first `RUNNING` frame after the death is retained in `production/qa/evidence/` with lead sign-off; it shows the UI-11 record line and nothing else that Save/Load adds *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*. Cites: Game Feel, SL-21.
92. **AC-SL-92 (manual + lint) — presentation guard rails.** GIVEN each screen touched by Save/Load (the pause panel, which shows no save fact; the main-menu Continue / New campaign screen with its newest-record line, UI-4; New campaign over an existing one, UI-5; hold-to-confirm "quit without saving"; the load screen; the corrupt-ring notice, UI-7; `SL_LOAD_NO_LOADABLE_SAVE`; the INCOMPATIBLE message; the `FAILED` notice; the death-restore line, UI-11 *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*, WHEN built, THEN a screenshot of each is retained in `production/qa/evidence/` and shows no thumbnail, no ring or percentage, no death screen; the `FAILED` notice's and the newest-record line's sampled pixel colours are not within the art bible's Flesh (`#D9A58C`, `#B5705C`) or Violet (`#8A3FD1`, `#B07BE6`) entries (sampled RGB and the four hex codes written on the sheet); destructive actions are hold-to-confirm on a linear bar (hold duration in ms written on the sheet and verified by stopwatch; the value is the UX spec's, OQ-32); and a capture sequence across at least 5 autosaves, taken with the gate deferring (an injected tell) and with it open, shows HUD pixels identical outside the notice region, so no HUD element differs between a write, a deferral and neither *(Amended 2026-10-26 — CD-GDD-ALIGN)*. GIVEN the Save/Load source, WHEN linted, THEN it calls no audio-playback API and no viewport-capture API; GIVEN the shipped strings, THEN they pass the AC-SL-86 token lint. Cites: SL-21, SL-24, UI Requirements, art bible 7.4 and HR-14.
93. **AC-SL-93 (manual) — hallucination interplay.** GIVEN a playtest session with a false system state active, WHEN the crew dies and the stub restores, THEN every persisted counter and seed read after the restore (the owners' `rng_derive` counters, via the debug overlay or the stand-in log) equals the generation's, the sanity level read after the restore equals the generation's, and a screenshot of the first `RUNNING` frame (grabbed by the frame-capture tool, not from memory) is retained and shows the filter applied for that sanity level. The criterion deliberately does **not** assert that the false state's onset tick is identical or different between runs: that is Sanity's decision (OQ-37) *(Amended 2026-10-26 — CD-GDD-ALIGN)*. If the first frame is unfiltered, OQ-11 is raised and this criterion is recorded FAILED, not deferred. Cites: SL-23, SL-24, H2 – H4. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* The screenshot is of the HUD layer alone (the HUD canvas layer rendered to its own viewport) or the check asserts on the HUD draw list; whole-frame pixel diffs are not used, because world rendering is not bit-identical between runs.
94. **AC-SL-94 (manual) — kill / Save & Quit / die / Continue playtest.** GIVEN the first build given outside the project and three testers, WHEN each kills the process, Save & Quits, dies and chooses Continue, THEN every one of those campaigns loads (12 of 12, or all attempted if fewer actions were completed), and each tester completes questionnaire items Q1 – Q4 (is the cost of dying fair, 1 – 5; is quit-and-relaunch a cheat, 1 – 5; did anything lose your progress, y/n; did you notice an autosave, y/n). In addition, the playtest build logs and the sheet records, per tester, the six triggers of Edge Cases — **M1** `deaths_per_restored_gen`, **M2** `scout_death_rate` (`T_dead − T_enter` ≤ `SCOUT_DEATH_WINDOW_TICKS` 5 400, `T_enter` = Web Map & Transit's derelict-level `FIRST_BOARDED{derelict_id, T}` at the MVP, OQ-25) **M3** `quit_relaunch_rate` (Save & Quit then Continue within `QUIT_RELAUNCH_WINDOW_S` 120 s), **M4** `quit_in_tell_rate` and **M5** `event_scum_rate` — each against its starting threshold and, where tripped, the named response (retune `AUTOSAVE_INTERVAL_TICKS` once, otherwise escalate to Failure Contract & Re-wake at VS-A). A tripped trigger with no recorded response is an incomplete sheet. The fairness numbers and the questionnaire stay ADVISORY: a tripped trigger opens OQ-19 or OQ-25 work, it does not fail the build; "campaigns load" stays BLOCKING *(Amended 2026-10-26 — CD-GDD-ALIGN)*. Cites: SL-14, SL-23. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* Each tester completes at least 3 kills, 3 Save & Quits and 3 deaths (fewer attempted is recorded as an incomplete sheet, never as a pass), death phases are stratified (at least one death in each third of the interval and one inside the first 600 ticks after a restore), a scripted kill-during-write run is added (the process killed at each write state of AC-SL-24 in a real build), and the build is a `release` export (SL-12 table).
117. **AC-SL-117 (manual) — UI evidence set.** GIVEN the screens listed in UI Requirements, WHEN built, THEN the screenshot set of AC-SL-92 (UI-2 to UI-11, including UI-4, UI-5, UI-7 and UI-11; UI-1 is retired) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* exists in `production/qa/evidence/` with one image per screen and the checks on the sheet pass. This is the retained-screenshot evidence the UI row of the testing standard requires. Cites: UI Requirements.
120. **AC-SL-120 (auto) — formula worked examples run as unit tests.** `autosave_due` (432 000 → 437 400: elapsed 5 400 ≥ 1 800, EVENT; latch at 438 700, due 439 200; the RAGGED withhold case) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*; `autosave_gate` (the FAIR, STRICT, cap, hard, backoff, vitals and refractory examples of F-2); `generation_select` (the F-3 example and its four variants); `save_verification` (the F-4 example, 90 020 B); `write_budget` (F-5: `main_hitch_ms` 6.9, `durable_ms` 22.5; HDD: 306.9; the scripted-hash counter-example fails the `main_hitch_ms` check); `save_bytes_header` (F-6: `header_len` 3 960, `file_bytes` 90 020, header share 4.4 %; 128 blocks → 31 320); `loss_ticks` (F-7: 49, 1 801 on the timer, 20 400, 24 000, mean 7 200, `net_progress_lost_max` 39 000) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*; `load_budget` (F-8: 139.7 ms and 179.1 ms); `ring_position` (F-9: `gen_next` 10, slot 1); `hex64` (F-10). The F-5 and F-6 constants (`H0` 600, `BLOCK_RECORD_BYTES` 240, `B` 14, `t_file_encode` 2.0 …) are **illustrative test inputs, labelled as such, not measurements** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*; the real writer's measured header size must fall in `H0` 500 – 700 and `BLOCK_RECORD_BYTES` 180 – 240 (measured under AC-SL-87). Cites: F-1 … F-10.
131. **AC-SL-131 (manual + auto) — a write is not a tell in the frame-time trace** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*. GIVEN two 30-minute captures at 60 Hz with a frame-time log, one with the gate injected open for every due save (windows of ±2 frames around each capture start) and one with the gate injected closed by a tell for the first half of each retry period (windows of ±2 frames around each retry tick at which the gate refused), WHEN the two frame-time distributions are compared, THEN, on CPU frame work (frame process time, not presented intervals) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*, the P99 of each capture is ≤ P99(baseline CPU frame work) + `SAVE_HITCH_BUDGET_MS` and the two P99 values differ by at most `SAVE_HITCH_BUDGET_MS` / 2 (the Kolmogorov–Smirnov test is dropped: over about 108 000 frames it separates distributions that no player can tell apart, so it proves nothing), with the log retained in `production/qa/evidence/`; a hitch present only on writes fails the criterion because it tells the player when the game saved and when the gate deferred (Pillar 3). Cites: F-5, SL-16, Game Feel 1.
132. **AC-SL-132 (auto, integration; the real tick driver is blocked on K-5, OQ-45) — no catch-up burst after `LOADING`** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*. GIVEN a tick-driver test double whose wall-time accumulator holds five frames of time when `LOADING` ends, WHEN the first `RUNNING` frame is produced after a death-stub restore, after Continue and after a Save & Quit that returns to `RUNNING`, THEN `T` advances by at most 1 on that frame (the accumulator was discarded on entry to `RUNNING`) and the frame log records it. Cites: Game Feel 2, OQ-45. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* The case set also includes `RUNNING → SAVING → RUNNING` (an autosave): `T` advances by at most 1 on the first frame after `SAVING` returns, which settles whether the accumulator is discarded there (OQ-53).
133. **AC-SL-133 (auto) — the pause panel is identical across autosave periods** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*. GIVEN the Chart Table test double reading everything Save/Load publishes while the panel is open, with the gate injected closed and then injected open, across 5 autosave periods (54 000 ticks) in which writes occur, WHEN the pause panel's rendered content (every string and mark, excluding the Save & Quit failure flag during the player's own held quit) is captured at each period, THEN the captures are identical across all periods and both gate conditions, and no save-derived value (playtime, generation, kind, `written_at_T`) appears on it. Cites: UI-2, SL-21.

### Story-type and gate classification (for `/qa-plan`)

*(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* The project's story types are Logic, Integration, Visual/Feel, UI and Config/Data; "Spike", "Playtest" and "Review" are not story types and are mapped below. **Logic** = pure functions and state machines over in-memory bytes and the injected seams, with kernel, Graph and Controller doubles or spies and no real disk. **Integration** = a real temporary directory, a real kernel, Graph or Controller through disk, a spike measurement on the target machine, or a documented playtest.

| Criteria | Story type | Required evidence | Gate (project default) |
|---|---|---|---|
| AC-SL-01 … 05, 07, 08 (auto half), 09, 10 … 15, 17 … 21, 26, 31 … 39, 41 … 44, 44b, 45a … 45c, 46 … 56, 64, 65, 68, 71, 73, 74, 75, 76, 80, 81, 95a, 96, 96c, 96e, 96f, 97 … 107, 112 … 116, 119, 120, 123, 125, 126, 127, 128, 129, 133, 134, 135, 137, 139, 141 (auto half), 144 *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*; 142, 143 moved to Integration *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* | Logic | automated unit test in `tests/unit/save-load/`, file name `save_load_[feature]_test.gd` | BLOCKING |
| AC-SL-16, 22 … 25, 27 … 29, 40, 57a … 57c, 58 … 62, 67, 69, 70, 72, 77 … 79, 82, 83, 96b, 96d, 108 … 111, 118, 130, 132, 138, 140, 142, 143, 145, 146, 147, 148 *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* | Integration | integration test in `tests/integration/save-load/` (a real temporary directory with fault injection, or a real kernel, Graph or Controller through disk) | BLOCKING; the K-4-blocked (b) halves of AC-SL-58 … 60, the end-of-`LOADING` hand-off wait of AC-SL-142 and AC-SL-145 (K-4) and the real tick-driver half of AC-SL-132 (K-5) are BLOCKED, not passed *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* |
| AC-SL-30, 82b, 87 … 90 | Integration (measured on the target machine; formerly "Spike") | measurements in `production/qa/evidence/`; each evidence file records the machine description it ran on, and no reference machine is defined here (X14 stays open, OQ-34) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* | BLOCKING for the "save round-trip with version" spike exit |
| AC-SL-94 | Integration (documented playtest; formerly "Playtest") | notes and questionnaire | ADVISORY for fairness; BLOCKING for "campaigns load" |
| AC-SL-06, 08 (lint half; its auto half is Logic), 63, 86 (lint half), 95b | Config/Data (static) | CI lint pass | **BLOCKING** for the security lints AC-SL-06 and AC-SL-95b *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* (they guard the object-injection boundary and the observation channel); ADVISORY for the rest |
| AC-SL-84, 91, 93, 131 | Visual/Feel | retained screenshot plus lead sign-off; for AC-SL-131 the frame-time logs and test output | BLOCKING |
| AC-SL-92, 117, 141 (manual half) | UI | retained screenshot of each screen touched (UI-2 to UI-11) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* | BLOCKING |
| AC-SL-66, 84b, 85, 119b, 121, 122, 124 (and the review half of AC-SL-86) | Review checklist (formerly "Review"; not story criteria) | the checklist below | not gates; BLOCKED rows are tracked in Gaps |

**Review checklist (not acceptance tests).** Run by a named reviewer at each design review, not by a QA tester per story; the ids are kept stable. (66) OQ-9 and OQ-39 carry the restore-idempotence debt and its exit. (84b) OQ-14 carries the Pipeline's 2.10 text fix. (85) K-1, the D.10 note, K-3, K-4, K-5, the observation channel, `restore_reason`, the "no clean frame" ordering, the stale-token list and the index label are rows addressed to their owners and none is written as an existing rule of another GDD. (119b) the re-signed hand edit is recorded as an accepted exposure. (121) the Attention view carries `tell_active`, `tell_remaining` and `breach_phase`. (122) the Emitter and Body `restore_after` order. (124) OQ-11 stays recorded and AC-SL-93 is re-run when the Perception Filter, Sanity or Pipeline GDD changes. (86, review half) shipped strings carry no stale token.

**CI tiering.** A fast subset runs on every push and PR: the Logic rows at reduced counts (AC-SL-13 at 100 mutations, AC-SL-14 at 100 per block, AC-SL-37 on one seed, AC-SL-55 at 1 000 death times). The full counts run nightly (AC-SL-13 at 1 000, AC-SL-14 at 1 000 per block, AC-SL-37 on 5 seeds of 120 000 ticks, AC-SL-40 at 100 000 ticks, AC-SL-55 at 10 000), and the measured rows run on the target machine at release candidates. A failing test is never skipped to make CI pass.

`qa.level` and `testing.strict` overrides in `project.yaml`, if set, change the gate level of the Logic, Integration, Visual/Feel and UI rows only; at `qa.level: minimal` test evidence is waived but the UI and Visual/Feel screenshots are not.

### Gaps / untestable as written

- **Blocked on undesigned or in-flight GDDs (review criteria cannot pass yet).** AC-SL-67 is now automated and is the merge gate for the Controller, Interaction and Assembly stories (OQ-9 is closed for the Controller and Body; OQ-39 stays open for Interaction and Assembly) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*; AC-SL-124 (no-clean-frame ordering, OQ-11); AC-SL-121 and AC-SL-122 are unblocked now that Attention, Layout and Body are designed, and AC-SL-96b's Suit and Sanity shapes follow the designed GDDs *(Amended 2026-10-27 — consistency pass)*; the `EVENT_TRIGGERS` names beyond `NEW_GAME` (OQ-24).
- **Blocked on the kernel owner.** The (b) halves of AC-SL-58, 59 and 60 (the compare inside `LOADING` in a release build) depend on the verification-window mechanism K-4 (OQ-6), and the real tick-driver half of AC-SL-132 on K-5 (OQ-45); the (a) halves run in the harness and in dev builds now *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*.
- **Resolved by user ruling** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*. The criteria drafted for the OQ-40 package (the pre-`DOWNED` tick, QUIT then Continue, the post-`DOWNED` refractory) are promoted to story criteria as AC-SL-128 and AC-SL-129 (section D); Suit's AC-SE-34 can pass once its side lands. Still open, and not a Save/Load blocker: X14, the reference machine and build type behind every (spike) criterion (OQ-34); OQ-49 (the carrier of the threat re-key epoch) is closed in this GDD *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review, delegated ruling R1)*: Save/Load owns `restore_epoch` (SL-23) and its criteria are AC-SL-142 and AC-SL-145; the sibling refresh is OQ-51.
- **Not decidable from the GDD, carried as Open Questions.** Behaviour of a knob set outside its safe range with no invariant violated — decided: refuse at boot with `SL_BOOT_KNOB_RANGE` (OQ-28, AC-SL-38); the "target machine" definition for every (spike) criterion (OQ-34); the hold-to-confirm duration (OQ-32); hostile-payload decode allocation — the type walk runs after the decode allocates, so a 32 MiB nested payload may allocate far more than 32 MiB before the walk can refuse it; AC-SL-90 measures it and OQ-35 sets the ceiling, or the pre-walk moves to the raw bytes (ADR candidate); the physical encoding of a quarantine reason and the retire path (OQ-27).
- **Decided here and recorded (orchestrator rulings), so the criteria above are verifiable.** Backoff schedule (OQ-26); "session" = process lifetime (OQ-29); playtime = integer floor (OQ-30); duplicate-entropy `new_game` re-draws and never overwrites (OQ-31); the one-frame target applies to the main-thread sum `main_hitch_ms` (OQ-33); a block missing `default_state()` or a fixture factory is a failing AC-SL-65 test, not a boot refusal (OQ-36).
- **Not automatable by design.** "Imperceptible", "reads as a flash or a crash" and fairness are judged by people; AC-SL-91 and AC-SL-94 replace the adjectives with frame-time logs, stopwatch values and a questionnaire, and fairness is data, not a gate.

## Open Questions

Ids are stable and are cited from the sections above; rows are grouped (user decisions first, then amendments routed to other owners, then engine and ADR items, then author decisions recorded here) and never renumbered. "Owner" is who can answer; "Target" is where the answer lands. Nothing in this table is written as an existing rule of another GDD: routed items are requests, and every undesigned dependency is marked provisional wherever it appears.

**User decisions (informed by playtest).**

| # | Question | Owner | Target |
|---|---|---|---|
| OQ-1 *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* | **Narrowed 2026-10-27 — user ruling (design-review decisions): the fairness step-back (X7 (b)) is adopted, so a non-`RISKY` generation is stepped back from when the fall comes within `W_MIN_WARNING_S × TICK_RATE` ticks of the restore (SL-8, F-3); the general form below stays playtest-gated.** The question flagged for the user: should a repeated death restored from a **non-`RISKY`** generation step back to an older generation? The binding rule is "restart from the last autosave", so the default is the newest verified generation; step-back fires only when that generation is `RISKY` and the death came within `DEATH_LOOP_WINDOW_TICKS` of `T_downed` (SL-8, F-3). A non-`RISKY` loop (the same death on the same save, repeatedly) is possible (the fairness variant, a step-back from a non-`RISKY` generation after a death that follows the restore too quickly, is OQ-40 (b)) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*; the cost is that the player may need to change what they do, which is the game. Instrument `deaths_per_restored_gen` and ask only if playtest shows players stuck | user (playtest) | playtest (AC-SL-51, AC-SL-94) |
| OQ-18 | A **pinned, main-menu-only manual slot** as the single fallback if the 3-person playtest finds Save & Quit plus autosave too punishing. Never an in-session save: save-anywhere makes a reload a free hallucination oracle (Pillar 4) and removes the cost of death | user (playtest) | playtest |
| OQ-19 | **Death-cost tuning**: `AUTOSAVE_INTERVAL_TICKS`, `AUTOSAVE_MAX_DEFER_TICKS` and the `LOSS_CEILING_TICKS` diagnostic; the loss window numbers 49 (1 801 on the timer) / 20 400 / 24 000 ticks and the 7 200-tick mean (`loss_ticks`, F-7) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*. "If death turns into a quit, the knob is the interval, then the pinned slot, never save-anywhere". The measurable triggers (M1 to M6) and their named responses are in Edge Cases and AC-SL-94 *(Amended 2026-10-26 — CD-GDD-ALIGN)* | user (playtest) | playtest (AC-SL-55, AC-SL-94) |
| OQ-25 *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* | **Accepted exposures** (Resolved 2026-10-27 — user ruling (design-review decisions): D55 accepts the fault loss window, mean about 5 400, maximum 10 800, ceiling 18 000 ticks; on the narrow recovery-at-the-cap path the refractory can add up to 600, F-7): a process kill (the task manager, power loss; Alt-F4 is not a kill, it raises the window-close request and takes the Save & Quit path, SL-6) rewinds at most `AUTOSAVE_INTERVAL_TICKS + AUTOSAVE_MAX_DEFER_TICKS` (18 000 ticks) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*; Save & Quit then relaunch is a disguised retry — determinism means it buys a retry of inputs and, for up to one interval, a look at the physical world (SL-23). The trigger is no longer open-ended *(Amended 2026-10-26 — CD-GDD-ALIGN)*: metric **M2** `scout_death_rate` (> 0.25 of deaths within `SCOUT_DEATH_WINDOW_TICKS` 5 400 of first boarding a derelict — derelict-level at the MVP) and **M3** `quit_relaunch_rate` (> 0.30 of `QUIT` generations relaunched within `QUIT_RELAUNCH_WINDOW_S` 120 s, or 3 pairs in one 30-minute session) in Edge Cases, measured by AC-SL-94. First response to M2: retune `AUTOSAVE_INTERVAL_TICKS` once (OQ-19). M3 is not an interval problem (a `QUIT` generation is written at the quit): accept the exposure for the MVP and keep the metric. A repeat trip, or M1 together with M2 or M3, escalates to Failure Contract & Re-wake (VS-A), never to deleting the `QUIT` generation (that would contradict SL-8). M4 `quit_in_tell_rate` and M5 `event_scum_rate` join the set *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — consistency pass)* M2 uses derelict-level `T_enter` at the MVP: Web Map & Transit's `FIRST_BOARDED{derelict_id, T}` (the dock room, WM-28); Web Map does not supply compartment-level first entry, and a DIAGNOSTIC-only `FIRST_COMPARTMENT_ENTRY{room_id, T}` log by the Debug Overlay from Body section changes (never persisted) is the recorded refinement if M2 proves too coarse | user (playtest); Failure Contract; Web Map author for `FIRST_BOARDED` | playtest |
| OQ-46 *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* | **Resolved 2026-10-27 — user ruling (design-review decisions): option (b), the creative director's recommendation (D15, D84).** One plain ship-log line on the first `RUNNING` frame after a death-stub restore, through the HUD's `FIRST_FRAME_LINE`, keyed on `restore_reason = DEATH_STUB`, with no death wording (UI-11, SL-7, SL-21). It carries the restored generation's playtime, a SIM fact, and stays inside the art bible's no-death-screen rule and the no-hallucination-content rule (SL-21, SL-24). Option (a)'s Q5 questionnaire item is not added. The HUD owner widens its trigger beyond a fallback load (HS-46) | user (ruled); HUD author | closed |
| OQ-47 *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* | **Resolved 2026-10-27 — user ruling (design-review decisions): D16, threat draws (Attention, Horror) re-key per restore epoch; the Graph's physical failures and every persisted counter replay exactly (Pillars 1 and 5); the re-key must not be steerable by quit or death (OQ-37).** Save/Load's side is fixed in SL-23 and H2: every persisted counter and seed is still restored exactly (AC-SL-68), and, per delegated ruling R1 *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*, `restore_epoch` advances on every restore (SL-23; AC-SL-142, AC-SL-145), the quit-then-Continue reading being a USER DECISION (OQ-50); a death is the price (metric M2); a process kill replays the older stretch exactly (OQ-25). Stated honestly: "not steerable" can mean only that the player cannot pick or predict the new draw and (per delegated ruling R1, a USER DECISION at OQ-50) that a Save & Quit then Continue also re-keys the next threat draw at the cost of the relaunch alone; deliberate deaths remain a priced way to re-roll the next stretch. The carrier is Save/Load's persisted `restore_epoch` and the derivation is the kernel's (OQ-49, closed); neither threat GDD defines its own | user (ruled); Attention and Horror authors; kernel owner | OQ-49 |

| OQ-50 *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review, delegated ruling R1)* | **USER DECISION: does a Save & Quit followed by Continue advance `restore_epoch`?** Delegated ruling R1 says every restore advances it, so a Continue after a Save & Quit re-keys the next threat draws (the Graph's physical failures and every persisted counter still replay exactly), which makes the relaunch a reroll of the next threat draw whose only cost is the relaunch itself. That sits against the recorded D16 wording "must not be steerable by quit" (OQ-37, OQ-47) and against metric M3's premise. Applied as written, provisionally, because R1 binds. Alternative if the user wants quit to buy nothing: a Continue of a `QUIT` generation that was the newest write of the previous process carries the epoch unchanged, while a death and a Continue of any other generation still advance it (R1 case (ii), the fresh-process advance, then holds for autosaves only). Not changed by the delegate because choosing it would reverse part of R1 | user | before the Attention and Horror re-key stories |

(b) Attention (the `restore_epoch` row near L585, C35, AT-8, F-10): call the kernel derivation, drop its ownership of `RESTORE_KEY_STRIDE`, and read "advances on every restore". (c) Horror HE-41 and HE-42: replace `threat_epoch` with the kernel's `restore_epoch` derivation. (d) Pipeline: the black hold lasts until the hand-off. Following user ruling X7 (b) to the letter, a player who quits, continues and falls within 599 ticks loses the deliberate quit-save and the session before it, a loss larger than the 20 400 / 24 000 headline that F-7 never derived. Options: (a) exempt `QUIT` generations without `VITALS` in their `risky_cause` from the fairness route (applied; the RISKY-window route already does this); (b) demote them but keep them as the second-pass candidate; (c) accept the loss and derive it in F-7. Applied (a) because it is the creative director's stated lean (G2); it narrows the wording of X7 (b), "whether or not `g_r` is `RISKY`", for one kind of file, so the user may prefer (b) or (c).
